# Changelog

Changes that are merged but not yet in a release go under **Unreleased**. Newest first.

## Unreleased

- `pull_ticket` saves the issue and its comments in the run folder (#700, part 2 of 2 of #695): `issue.json` (body, number, title, `updatedAt`) and `issue-comments.jsonl` (one comment per line) are written next to the run, so a later step can build the context with `readIssueFiles` without calling `gh`. A failed fetch removes the file and the run goes on; the issue text the step prints is unchanged.
  - **Code.** `blocks/pull-ticket.yaml` (two `gh` lines after the `case` line), regenerated `flows/issue-gitflow.yaml`, `flows/issue-plan.yaml`, `flows/issue-code-daily.yaml` and `tests/fixtures/flows/{github-issue,github-auto,github-pr,issue-deliver}.yaml`. `package.json` gets the `npm run flows` script; `CLAUDE.md` names it.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/pull-ticket-files.test.ts` (the line order in the three shipped flows; the rendered script with both, no, and empty comment variables; stale files removed; `readIssueFiles` reads the result). `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` answers the two new calls only when `FAKE_GH_ISSUE_JSON` or `FAKE_GH_ISSUE_COMMENTS_JSONL` is set (an empty value counts as set).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("A run": the issue files and a failed fetch), `CLAUDE.md`.
- Strict reader for the issue files in the run folder (#699, part 1 of 2 of #695): `readIssueFiles` reads `<run>/issue.json` and `<run>/issue-comments.jsonl` so a later step can build the context from the raw files without calling `gh`. The issue file must be a regular file of at most 16 MiB that matches the issue number and has a valid version; a bad issue file stops the read before the comments. Bad comments (an id that is not a safe integer, a bad `updated_at`, a duplicate id, a body that is neither text nor null, or any comment after the first 10,000) are skipped and counted in `leftComments`. Nothing writes or reads these files yet, so nothing changes for users.
  - **Code.** New `src/context/issue-files.ts` (`ISSUE_FILE`, `ISSUE_COMMENTS_FILE`, `ISSUE_FILE_MAX_BYTES`, `readIssueFiles`; a private `readStrict` opens with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, checks with `fstat` and reads exactly the size, plus one read that must return 0).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/issue-files.test.ts` (valid and skipped comments, the cases of the issue, a FIFO and mode 000 skipped where the system cannot run them, the result passes `selectContext`) and `tests/issue-files-io.test.ts` (file-system failures and a file that grows after `fstat`, with `node:fs` mocked).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is unchanged, because no user-visible behaviour changes yet. The new exports have doc comments.
- Owner-action questions wait for a person (#678, part 5 of 5 of #673): a question with `**Kind:** owner_action` (something only the owner can do, such as a file, an account or access) is no longer answered by auto `/defaults`, and the story waits with no run, no cost and no comment. **Needs you** shows it as "waiting for you — something to do" with the question as the action and an **Answer…** button. The story goes on when a person answers, or when the file named by the question's new optional `**Needs:** file <path>` line is on the develop branch (the default branch without gitflow). A file found once resumes the story at most twice; a file check takes at most 20 requests and 30 seconds per check, and an unsafe path is never checked, so the story waits for a person.
  - **Code.** New `src/queue/owner-action.ts` (`openOwnerActions`, `questionKinds`, `safeRepoPath`, `contentsPath`, `actionText`, the hidden mark `OWNER_FILE_MARK`). `src/queue/watcher.ts` (`canTakeDefaults`, `ownerWait`, `filesThere`, `fileRef`, `noteFile`; the owner hold in both the no-run and the run branch). `src/next-step.ts` (`owner_action` kind, `ownerAction`, `ownerFiles`). `src/your-turn.ts`, `src/turn-actions.ts` and `src/server/turn-actions.ts` (the answer action and the question comment of the item), `src/server/next.ts`, `src/words.ts` (glossary text). `ui/turn-act.js` (**Show question**, **Answer…**), `ui/icons.js`, `ui/dom.js`. `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (the `**Needs:**` line in the question format), regenerated `flows/issue-plan.yaml`, `flows/issue-gitflow.yaml`, `flows/epic-questions.yaml`, `tests/fixtures/flows/issue-deliver.yaml`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/owner-action.test.ts` (the parser, the Needs values, the path rules, the encoding of the contents path, the kinds as `tools/decide-questions.mjs` reads them). New `tests/watch-owner-action.test.ts` (fake `gh`: auto `/defaults` skips an owner_action question and no run happens on later checks; a human answer resumes; a file on develop resumes; the note limit; a directory or a missing file does not resume). `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`FAKE_GH_FILES`, a contents answer per branch). Changed `tests/next-step.test.ts`, `tests/your-turn.test.ts`, `tests/turn-actions.test.ts`, `tests/ui-turn-act.test.ts`, `tests/ui-icons.test.ts`, `tests/blocks.test.ts`, `tests/words.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Needs you", the labels, the glossary row "waiting for you — something to do"), `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` (the `**Needs:**` line).
- The planner decides its own technical questions (#692, part 2 of 2 of #684): when the repository's **Decide technical questions itself** setting is on, `issue-plan`, `issue-deliver` and `issue-gitflow` run `post-plan` on a NEEDS_INFO plan. Technical questions with a recommendation are decided and posted as one "Decided by the Foundry" comment, and the planner plans again without waiting; product, policy and owner-action questions, and technical ones without a recommendation, still wait for you. The planner takes the decisions as answers unless a later comment from a person says otherwise. The loop stops after two replans in a row (a decided title seen before in the issue also stops it): the questions are then posted as a plain comment and the run waits. The waiting text for planner questions now names the Foundry itself, and `$FACTORY_FIRST_DECIDED` is new.
  - **Code.** `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (`planPhase`: the `send_back` step calls `post-plan`, the `DECIDED: went_on` route, `max_visits: 3`, the `plan` prompt line), regenerated `flows/issue-plan.yaml`, `flows/issue-gitflow.yaml`, `tests/fixtures/flows/issue-deliver.yaml`. `tools/decide-questions.mjs` (`FACTORY_NEW_ROUND`: a fresh planner round is never a retry). `src/turn-actions.ts` (`findComment` finds the decided comment of a round). `src/words.ts` (`planner_questions` help text).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/decide-questions-flow.test.ts` (all technical, mixed, a revision then a decision then a replan, a failed post, a posted comment with a failed commit then resume, the setting turned off between a failed post and a resume, the third round gives up). `tests/blocks.test.ts` (the `send_back` lines of all three flows). Fakes: `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`FAKE_GH_COMMENT_MKDIR`), `tests/fixtures/fake-claude.mjs` (`FAKE_REVISE_COUNT`, `FAKE_REVISE_THEN`). `tests/turn-actions.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Questions the Foundry decides itself" under the label pipeline, the setting bullet, the waiting text), `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` (`$FACTORY_FIRST_DECIDED`, `$FACTORY_DECIDE_TECHNICAL` when it is read).
- Post-plan command (#691, part 1 of 2 of #684): `node tools/decide-questions.mjs post-plan <run-dir>` reads a planner text on stdin, decides its technical questions and posts one issue comment: all decided, mixed (the decided section and the questions still asked), or the plain NEEDS_INFO comment when the setting is off or nothing is decided. It records the round in `decided.json` in two steps, so a crash leaves nothing half-written, and exits 3 (all decided), 0 (questions asked or nothing decided), 4 (gave up), 1 (error) or 2 (bad arguments). The loop guard gives up when a decided title came up before in the issue, or after two planner rounds that went on; a plain comment is posted then, and a `gave_up` round is recorded. A retry with the same input posts nothing again, and a changed asked question is posted again. Every `gh` call times out after 60 seconds (`FACTORY_GH_TIMEOUT_MS` overrides it). `scanNode` accepts one plain condition between two `FACTORY_FIRST_*` variables. No flow calls it yet, so nothing changes for users until #692.
  - **Code.** `tools/decide-questions.mjs` (`postPlan`, `main`; the main guard means importing the file runs nothing; `decideRound` stores the planner input). `tests/helpers/comment-scan.ts` (`scanNode` reads one condition between two `FACTORY_FIRST_*` variables; nested ternaries are rejected).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/post-plan.test.ts` (argument and environment checks, the plain comment bytes, mixed, all decided, a rerun, give-up and its retry, A→B→A, a changed asked question, crash recovery, a failed post or commit, a broken comment list, a corrupt record, timeouts). `tests/blocks.test.ts` (the accepted and rejected `scanNode` cases; the tool is in the good-tools list).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` are unchanged, because no flow uses the command yet.
- Policy 4d (#631): the baseline records `path.forbidden` after a step when the run has changed a file matching its `forbidden_paths` (worked out by the engine with git, against the run's starting commit), and `sandbox.required` before a shell step of a user's run without the OS sandbox or a Docker image. Observe only; the guard step and the sandbox are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/policy/baseline.ts` (`baselineControls` reads `forbidden_paths`, `pathRules`, the `stepWithoutSandbox` pack). `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` (`needsFact`, the `!` line for patterns left out). `src/engine/diff.ts` (`changedPaths`, `checkoutRoot`, `runChangedPaths`, `ensurePathsBase`: async git with no lazy fetch, a private index, a 30 s budget and cancel). New `src/engine/step-facts.ts` (`shellSandboxed`, `stepAfterPaths`). `src/engine/state.ts` (`pathsBaseSha`, `pathsTracked`, `pathsBaseLost` on the run summary). `src/engine/execute.ts` and `src/engine/runner.ts` (the `paths` and `sandboxed` facts, the flag at run start, the resume check).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/diff-paths.test.ts` (changed paths, a hostile promisor repository, bad bases, timeouts, cancel, the base rules), `tests/policy-paths-run.test.ts` (in-place, empty-workspace, resume, parallel and user-run flows). Changed `tests/policy-baseline.test.ts`, `tests/policy-hooks.test.ts` and `tests/fixtures/policy-pack.ts` (the cover fixture now has a `paths` rule).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the baseline rules: `path.forbidden`, the sandbox of shell steps, when the result is `policy.input-incomplete`), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Baseline controls", "Step without sandbox fact", "Changed paths").
- Steadier load governor (#693): the governor lowers the cap only on sustained load (the 5-minute load above 2.5 per core, or the 1-minute load above 2.5 for 3 checks in a row), so a spike of a minute or two no longer costs runs. Memory pressure "warning" alone no longer lowers the cap. Critical memory pressure and a 1-minute load above 4 per core still act at once. After a change there is a pause: no step down for 3 minutes and no step up for 10 minutes, except in an emergency. `low` is 1.2 per core (was 1.5), so the gap to `high` is wider. `scf governor status` and Home's health line show how many times the limits changed today; the digest mentions it above 20 changes in 24 hours.
  - **Code.** `src/ops/governor.ts` (`decide` takes the time of the check and new settings `hotChecks`, `settleMinutes`, `downPauseMinutes`; `GovernorState` gains `hot` and `changedAt`, both optional, so an old `state.json` still loads; `changesToday` reads `changes.log` from the end; `MANY_CHANGES`). `src/ops/cli.ts` (status line, digest threshold). `src/server/health.ts` (`governor.changesToday`, information only: it does not change `ok`). `ui/health.js` and `ui/css/pages/shell.css` (the health line).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/governor.test.ts` (spike versus sustained load, emergencies, the pauses, memory pressure, `loadState`, `changesToday` across a 64 kB chunk). The `decide` cases moved out of `tests/ops.test.ts`; `tests/health.test.ts` and `tests/ui-health.test.ts` have one case each.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("The load governor" under Helper processes).

- Delivery 1b-4 (#658, part 4 of 4 of #650): `scf delivery verify <run-id> [--json] [--public-key <file>]` checks the delivery contracts of a run offline against the trusted key. It prints `verified` (exit 0), `not verified` (exit 1, with the first failed check and a path relative to the run folder), `unsigned` or no contract (exit 2). It checks the signatures, the numbering, the `supersedes` links, the latest copy and the evidence chain up to each contract's `count`; events added after the last contract do not fail it. `scf delivery public-key` prints the configured signing key as an `allowed_signers` line (`scf-delivery ssh-ed25519 …`). A bad run id, an unknown run, an unset key or a bad `--public-key` file exits 1 with a message that has no path. `scf delivery validate` is unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `src/delivery/run-verify.ts` (`verifyRunDelivery`, `deliveryVerifyExitCode`, `deliveryVerifyReport`, `readPublicKeyFile`, `deliveryCommand`), which reuses `verifyDeliveryContract` and `evidenceHeadProblem`. `src/cli.ts` routes `delivery verify` and `delivery public-key` to it (only `delivery validate` still goes to `src/delivery/cli.ts`), adds the `--public-key` option and skips the data-folder migration for these reads. `src/delivery/write.ts` exports `readConfiguredSigningKey` and `readBoundedBytes` (the key is read the same way as when a contract is written); `src/delivery/sshsig.ts` exports `parseKeyLine`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/delivery-verify.test.ts` (every failure case of the issue, the valid case with events added later, the command's messages and exit codes, and `ssh-keygen -Y verify` on the written contract when `/usr/bin/ssh-keygen` exists).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Verify a run's delivery contracts offline"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Delivery contract").
- Epic questions can answer `WAITS_FOR` (#690, part 4 of 4 of #675): the `epic-questions` check records "this story waits for #N" in the issue's Foundry part of **Depends on** instead of asking a question. A valid request prints `#n: waits for …` and posts no comment and no label. An invalid one (self, a loop inside the batch or in the repository, landed work, an unknown or closed issue) becomes the last question, with a recommendation to plan the issue now without waiting. The check reads the commits of `HEAD` once; if they cannot be read, no wait is recorded and every request is asked.
  - **Code.** `tools/post-questions` (lazy import of `dist/queue/waits-for.js`, one shared `known` map, `recordWaitsFor`, `waitsForQuestion`; a 64 MiB `gh` buffer). `scripts/build-flows.mjs` and `flows/epic-questions.yaml` (the `WAITS_FOR` bullet and the third output form in the `ask` prompt). A mixed rerun (an already recorded wait plus a new one) is asked, not silent.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/post-questions-waits.test.ts` (the issue's cases, a valid wait with other questions, no `WAITS_FOR` in the batch, `WAITS_FOR: none`, reruns, landed work, a `git log` failure, the prompt text).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Depends on" in the watcher section), `docs/CHANGELOG.md`.
- "This story waits for #N" library (#688, part 2 of 4 of #675): a new library decides whether a request to wait for other issues is valid, and records it in the issue's Foundry part of **Depends on** without storing cycles, loops or stale edits. It parses the last `WAITS_FOR:` line (at most 10 issues, an optional `WAITS_FOR_REASON:`), finds landed work by commit message (`Resolve`, `Merge`, `Fixes`, `closes #N`), and rejects the issue itself, unknown or closed-without-commit issues, work that already landed, issues already in its own part, and direct, indirect or title-only cycles. A limit reached in the cycle walk is a problem, never a pass. The write reads the body fresh, keeps a person's edit made in the meantime, checks the result, and undoes its own numbers when a new cycle appears after the write. Nothing calls the library yet, so no issue changes.
  - **Code.** New `src/queue/waits-for.ts` (`parseWaitsFor`, `landedCommit`, `findCycle`, `judgeWaitsFor`, `issueLookup`, `recordWaitsFor`, `waitsForQuestion`), built on `src/queue/deps.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/waits-for.test.ts` (unit tests on a fake `GhCall`). `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` answers `issue view --json number,title,state,body` and stores `issue edit --body-file -` bodies (documented in its header).
  - **Docs.** No user-visible change yet, so `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is unchanged. The library's exports have doc comments.
- Delivery 2a-2 (#672, part 2 of 2 of #667): `scf delivery validate --events <file>` (and `node tools/delivery-validate/validate.mjs --events <file>`, 256 MiB at most) checks the run's evidence chain against the contract, with the same verdicts as `verifyDeliveryContract`. The event at `count - 1` must carry `evidence.terminalHash`; the first `count` lines are checked in the library's order: canonical JSON objects, the version (a higher version than the script's pinned `EVIDENCE_EVENT_VERSION` is refused), the full event schema with every refinement, `seq`, `runId`, unique ids, and the hash chain. A failure is `evidence: stop evidence-unverified: <reason>` with `fault: "evidence-head-mismatch"` in `--json`. Without `--events` the check prints `skipped (no --events given)`; with an untrusted contract, `skipped (the contract is not trusted)`. Nothing reads or writes a contract yet.
  - **Code.** `tools/delivery-validate/validate.mjs` (the evidence section, a copy of `src/evidence/schema.ts` and what it reaches, as plain predicates; the file is now about 850 lines, past the usual 500-line guide, because the issue asks for one script). `src/delivery/cli.ts` (usage text names `--events`).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/delivery-validate-events.test.ts` (a drift guard that reads the script's constants and compares them with the library's enums; one valid event per type and enum value, chained and anchored, accepted by both sides; version 5 refused by both; a parity matrix of chain faults and schema-invalid events with correct hashes; read errors and output). Changed `tests/delivery-validate.test.ts` (the `evidence` check is now skipped with the new text).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Check a delivery contract in CI"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Delivery contract").
- Delivery 1b-3 (#657, part 3 of 4 of #650): a run that has a pull request in its own repository writes a chain of signed delivery contracts to `<run>/delivery/`. After each top-level step, a new `contract-<n>.json` (and `.sig` when a key is set) is written only when the commit, the branch, a dependency lock, a CI workflow, the pull request or a requirement changed. Each contract `supersedes` the digest of the one before. `contract.json` and `contract.json.sig` always hold the latest one. Evidence growth alone writes nothing. A failure (missing or broken evidence, a key outside `delivery-keys`, a git timeout, a file error) logs one fixed line and the run goes on; no contract is written, and CI must treat a missing contract as a failure. The delivery settings are read again for each step, so a change applies from the next step. Git is read with bounded synchronous calls (10 s in total, no replace objects, no lazy fetch).
  - **Code.** New `src/delivery/write.ts` (`DELIVERY_DIR`, `parsePullRequestUrl`, `deliveryRequirements`, `writeContract`, `deliveryAfterStep`) and `src/delivery/git-facts.ts` (`readCommitFacts`, `DEPENDENCY_LOCK_NAMES`). `evidenceHeadProblem` is extracted from `verifyDeliveryContract` in `src/delivery/contract.ts`, with no change in behaviour. `recordStep` calls `deliveryAfterStep` in `src/engine/execute.ts`. `deliverySettings` is a runner option (`src/engine/runner.ts`), filled from the scheduler's live config (`src/queue/scheduler.ts`).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/delivery-write.test.ts` (unit: write, read, fs failures, bad key and folder, bad evidence, replacement, missing object, timeout, reuse) and `tests/delivery-flow.test.ts` (a flow with two pull requests and a settings change: contracts 1 to 3 are written, contract 4 is not). `tests/delivery-contract.test.ts` is unchanged.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Delivery contract"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Delivery contract").
- Foundry-marked dependencies (#687, part 1 of 4 of #675): the Foundry can keep its own dependencies in a marked part of an issue's **Depends on** section (`<!-- claude-factory depends-on -->` … `<!-- claude-factory depends-on end -->`, with a `- #N` list and an `_Added by the Spaghetti Code Foundry: …_` line). The person's text is never changed. The watcher holds a story on open marked dependencies even with `wait_for_dependencies` off. The 1000-character limit now applies only to the person's text. Nothing writes the part yet, so no existing issue changes.
  - **Code.** `src/queue/deps.ts` (`DEPS_MARK`, `DEPS_MARK_END`, `foundryDependsOn`, `withoutFoundryPart`, `cleanReason`, `mergeDependsOn`, `removeFromDependsOn`; `dependencies()` adds the marked issues). `src/queue/watcher.ts` (`allIssues`, `openFoundryDepsOf`, `openDepsOf`, `blockedBy`, `describeBlocker`).
  - **Tests.** Changed `tests/deps.test.ts` (the part: merge, remove, reason cleaning, the cap, empty and CRLF bodies) and `tests/watch-comment-reads.test.ts` (holds with the setting on and off, resume, new issues, gitflow).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("It waits for another issue" and the `wait_for_dependencies` setting).
- Epic questions decide technical questions per issue (#685, part 6 of 6 of #676): with the repository setting **Decide technical questions itself** on, the up-front check of an epic (`epic-questions`) decides each issue's technical questions that have a recommendation, guards and records each round (source `epic`), and posts only the questions that need the owner, plus a note of what was decided. An issue with only decided questions gets a comment and no label; a mixed issue gets one comment and the label. Before it posts, the tool reads the issue and skips a comment that already carries the round marker, so a retried batch posts nothing twice and commits each round once. If `gh` fails, that issue's round is not committed and the command exits 1. With the setting off, or without a run folder, the comments are the same as before.
  - **Code.** `tools/post-questions` takes an optional run-folder argument; decide mode needs it and `FACTORY_DECIDE_TECHNICAL=1`. It uses `tools/decide-questions.mjs` (`decideRound`, `commitPending`, `checkRound`, `readRecord`). A marker counts only when the account gh acts as wrote the comment. `scripts/build-flows.mjs` and `flows/epic-questions.yaml`: the `post` step passes `{{run.dir}}`. `src/turn-actions.ts` (`parseQuestions` stops at the **Decided by the Foundry** heading, so the last open question does not take the decided section).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/post-questions.test.ts` (all technical, mixed, setting off, no run folder, `gh` failures, a retried partly posted batch, foreign or unknown comment authors, an ambiguous section, bad input). Changed `tests/blocks.test.ts` (the `post` step), `tests/turn-actions.test.ts` (the decided section) and `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`FAKE_GH_FAIL_ON`; labels in the from-log `issue view`).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Epics" under the issue watchers).
- Decided questions record and loop guard (#683, part 4 of 6 of #676): each round in which the Foundry decides technical questions is written to a run record, `decided.json` in the run folder, with its issue, time, source (planner or epic), outcome (`went_on`, `asked` or `gave_up`) and the questions with their decisions. At most 500 rounds. A round is written in two steps: `decideRound` writes only a pending file, the comment is posted, then `commitPending` appends the round once. A retry keeps the same round ID and time, and a failed write exits non-zero. `abandonPending` removes a pending file that does not match the current body. The loop guard (`checkRound`) gives `gave_up` when a decided title was already decided earlier in the same issue, or when a planner round that decides everything follows two planner rounds that went on; an `asked` or `gave_up` round starts the streak again. The run summary carries `decidedQuestions` (admin only; `userRun` leaves it out), and the run page shows a **Decided by the Foundry** card to administrators, hidden when there are no rounds. No flow writes the record yet, so nothing shows until a later part wires it in.
  - **Code.** `tools/decide-questions.mjs` (`decideRound`, `commitPending`, `abandonPending`, `checkRound`, `readRecord`, `roundId`, `normaliseTitle`; `LIMITS` gains `rounds` and `bytes`). New `src/engine/questions.ts` (`readDecidedQuestions`: a strict check, `redactText`, one log line on bad input; it returns nothing when the file is missing or not valid). `decidedQuestions` in `src/engine/state.ts`, set in `recordStep` in `src/engine/execute.ts`. `decidedItems` and `overviewPanel` in `ui/run-tabs.js`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/decide-record.test.ts` (round-trip at every limit, pending and commit cases, the guard) and `tests/questions.test.ts` (the reader, redaction, and that `LIMITS` matches the tool). Changed `tests/decide-questions.test.ts`, `tests/run-outputs.test.ts` and `tests/ui-run-tabs.test.ts` (the admin card, and no card without admin).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("The run page", the admin display).
- Delivery 1b-1 (#655): a run that opens or finds a pull request records its URL as the run output `pull_request` (`run.json` → `outputs`). The `open-pr` block and the daily release's `release_pr` step report it through the new `tools/pr-output <run-dir> <url>`, which takes the last line of the URL, checks it against the GitHub pull request form and merges it into `outputs.json`. The tool always exits 0 and leaves an existing file that is not valid outputs as it was. A failed `gh pr create` still fails the step. Nothing reads `pull_request` yet; the delivery contract that uses it is a later part of #650. Shipped `issue-code-daily` and `daily-pr` do not report it yet.
  - **Code.** New `tools/pr-output` (plain Node, about 40 lines; uses `writeRunOutputs` and `readRunOutputs` from `dist/engine/run-outputs.js`). `blocks/open-pr.yaml` (the existing-PR branch and the create branch, which captures the output of `gh pr create`). `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (`release_pr`); the generated `flows/release-daily.yaml` and `tests/fixtures/flows/{github-pr,github-auto,chore,ci-fix}.yaml`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/tool-pr-output.test.ts` (the tool, and the `open_pr` shell text run under `/bin/sh` with a fake `gh`). Changed `tests/deliver.test.ts` (`release_pr` calls the tool).
  - **Docs.** `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` ("Outputs").
- Repository setting **Decide technical questions itself** (#680, part 1 of 6 of #676): an admin can switch, per repository, whether the Foundry may decide technical questions itself. It is **on** by default, so **existing repositories switch on after the upgrade** (their records have no `questions` setting, which counts as on); turn it off in the repository's Edit dialog to be asked every question. Every step gets `$FACTORY_DECIDE_TECHNICAL` (`1` or `0`); any error gives `0`, so the run asks. Nothing decides anything yet; this part only stores the setting and passes it on. `decidesTechnical` and `decideTechnicalFor` in `src/auth/repo-settings.ts`, `decideTechnicalEnv` in `src/engine/runner.ts`, the checkbox in `ui/admin-repos.js` and `ui/repo-admin-sections.js`. Tests in `tests/repos-admin.test.ts`, `tests/ui-admin-repos.test.ts` and `tests/ui-repo-admin-sections.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (settings) and `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` (environment table).
- Question kinds and the decide library (#682, part 3 of 6 of #676): the planner, revision and epic-questions prompts ask for `**Kind:** technical | product | policy | owner_action` under each `**Q<n>. …**`, and a technical question gets a `**Recommendation:**` or `none`. A new pure library `tools/decide-questions.mjs` (`readQuestions`, `decideQuestions`, `LIMITS`, `DECIDED_HEADING`) splits a question comment into the questions the Foundry can decide (a technical question with a recommendation, at most 20 per round, title 300 / text 4000 / decision 1000 characters) and the ones it must ask. Ambiguous input decides nothing. Nothing is posted to GitHub yet. Counts (`countQuestions`) and the questions a user sees (`questionsOf`, `withQuestions`) leave out the decided section that starts at `DECIDED_HEADING`; a new report kind `decided` gives `FACTORY_FIRST_DECIDED`. Comments without a kind count as before.
  - **Code.** New `tools/decide-questions.mjs`. `DECIDED_HEADING` and `beforeDecided`, `countQuestions` and the `decided` report kind in `src/next-step.ts`; `questionsOf` in `src/server/user-view.ts`; `DECIDED_HEADING` and `withQuestions` in `ui/run-tabs.js`; the question format in `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (`QUESTION_FORMAT`, `QUESTION_KINDS`). The four generated flow files are regenerated.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/decide-questions.test.ts` (parsing, ambiguity, limits, and a check that `DECIDED_HEADING` matches the TS constant). Changed `tests/next-step.test.ts`, `tests/user-view.test.ts`, `tests/ui-run-tabs.test.ts` and `tests/blocks.test.ts` (the prompt wording).
  - **Docs.** `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` ("Question kinds"). `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is unchanged: nothing a user sees changes until a later part posts the decided section.
- Replan output (#681): a jump back to `pull_ticket` or a resume no longer lets an old round's output win. `PICK_PLAN`, `send_back`, `split_gate`, `create_split` and `claim_areas` use `plan_review`, `revise_gate`, `revise_plan` or `force_split` only when that step ran after the latest `plan`; otherwise the output is empty, so a new NEEDS_INFO draft is posted, not an old revision's questions or a READY verdict that `send_back` rejects. The rule comes from the run history (visit order), through the new engine env var `FACTORY_RAN_<STEP>` (`SCF_RAN_<STEP>` alias) set by `stepEnv` from `ranOrder`. Flows without these steps are unchanged. `ranEnvName` in `src/engine/template.ts`, `ranOrder` and `stepEnv` in `src/engine/execute.ts`, the shared `FRESH_PLAN` snippet in `scripts/build-flows.mjs`; `FAKE_PLAN_COUNT` and `FAKE_ISSUE_PLAN_THEN` in `tests/fixtures/fake-claude.mjs`.
- Schedule runs (the daily release and report) get their own lane (#679): they use no story slot, go before queued stories and do not wait for a helper's cap; one works at a time on the whole machine, and a spent daily budget still holds them. A daily schedule run belongs to the day it was queued for (`chore_day`), so one that starts after midnight no longer takes the next day's turn. `isChoreRun` in `src/queue/scheduler.ts`, `tickChore` in `src/queue/watcher.ts`; tests in `tests/scheduler-fair-use.test.ts` and the new `tests/chore-day.test.ts`.
- Dependencies (#674): with `wait_for_dependencies` on, a stopped, waiting or paused run is not resumed while an issue under its **Depends on** is still open. Every resume path checks this first: answered, auto `/defaults`, approved or rejected, usage limit, daily budget, owner limit, interrupted, and paused on a release. A held run gets no auto `/defaults`, does not count against `max_per_tick`, and is listed as a `dependency` hold with its blockers. When the dependencies are done, it resumes with the reason "its dependencies are done". A dependency is done when it is closed, has a `dependency_done_labels` label, or (only when the flow has a `develop_branch`) has the watcher's done label. The done label is a completion signal, not proof that the code is in develop: a gitflow hotfix can finish while develop is behind. The monitor does not report a held run's waiting label as unlisted. Part 1 of 5 of #673.
  - **Code.** `src/queue/deps.ts` (`DoneRule`, `dependencyDone`; `openDependencies` takes a `DoneRule` or the old list of labels). `src/queue/watcher.ts` (`doneRule`, `canTakeDefaults`, `holdForDeps`, `depHeldBefore`, the checks in the working-label, needs-info and waiting-label branches, and the up-front questions path, which checks dependencies before `/defaults`). `src/monitor/unlisted.ts` (skips dependency-held issues).
  - **Tests.** Changed `tests/deps.test.ts`, `tests/watch-comment-reads.test.ts` (a new describe block for the resume rules, with the fake `gh`) and `tests/monitor-unlisted.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Depends on" in the settings table and in "It waits for another issue").
- Delivery 2a-1 (#671): `scf delivery validate` (and `node tools/delivery-validate/validate.mjs`, which needs only Node.js) checks a delivery contract in CI against the checked-out commit. It checks the contract and its signature, the commit, the dependency locks, the CI workflows, the required checks and the marker of a pull request comment, and prints one line per check. Exit 0 means no stop, 1 a stop and 2 a usage or read error. The evidence-chain check comes in part 2 of #667. Nothing reads or writes a contract yet.
  - **Code.** New `tools/delivery-validate/validate.mjs` (plain Node ESM, `node:` imports only, with copies of the contract and signature rules), `src/delivery/cli.ts` (`deliveryCommand`, which runs the script) and the `delivery` dispatch and usage line in `src/cli.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/delivery-validate.test.ts` (a parity matrix against `verifyDeliveryContract`, the git checks, the checks and comment inputs, the output, the exit codes and the CLI).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Check a delivery contract in CI"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Delivery contract").
- Stale runs and a self-update that waits (#670): the server frees the `.running` markers and area locks of runs that are not active and not live in another process, at start and every minute. A new version or settings restart keeps the watchers running during the wait (a data-folder move still stops them). Stuck runs (waiting for a code area, or quiet for 30 minutes) are named in Home and the Health line; when every active run is stuck, the update interrupts them, waits up to 60 s and installs. A stopped shell step stops its whole process group. The `orphan-lock` finding clears at the next check.
  - **Code.** `sweepStaleRunning` and the interrupt constants in `src/engine/running.ts`; the start sweep and the timer in `src/server/server.ts`; the lock wait file in `tools/area-lock`; new `src/server/stuck-runs.ts`; `stuck` and `interrupt` in `src/self-update.ts` (wired in `src/cli.ts`); `Scheduler.interrupt` in `src/queue/scheduler.ts`; `groupKill` in `src/steps/process.ts` and `src/steps/shell.ts`; `resolved` in `mergeFindings` (`src/monitor/findings.ts`), `locksFreed` in `src/monitor/monitor.ts` and `src/queue/watchers.ts`; `WatcherManager.drain()` removed (`src/queue/watchers.ts`, `src/cli.ts`, `src/server/api-restart.ts`).
  - **Tests.** Changed `tests/running.test.ts`, `tests/scheduler-drain.test.ts`, `tests/monitor.test.ts`, `tests/monitor-findings.test.ts`, `tests/monitor-work-detectors.test.ts` and `tests/repo-watchers-run.test.ts`. New `tests/self-update-stuck.test.ts` and `tests/process-group.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Self-update", the restart rows, the monitor's `orphan-lock`, and the cancel note under "When something goes wrong").
- Evaluations 1b (#661): `scf eval safety` runs every `evals/safety/*/suite.yaml` offline with fake `claude` and `gh` programs and an isolated config. It prints each suite's pass rate, cost, tokens, time, fix loops, policy conformance and evidence completeness, and exits 1 when a case fails, a suite cannot run or there is no suite. The first suite, `injected-instructions`, checks that orders in an issue or CI log are labelled untrusted or held, that only read tools are used, and that no `OBEYED-…` text is printed. A suite can declare `programs:` (`claude`, `codex`, `gh`), relative to the suite file; `scf eval` and `scf eval safety` take `--claude-bin`, `--codex-bin` and `--gh-bin`, and a program on the command line wins. A suite folder may hold a `flow.yaml`. A new `untrusted:` control checks the label of a step (with `signals`) or a held content hash, and fails closed. A smoke test in `npm test` runs one case of each safety suite. Part 2 of 4 of #158.
- Evaluations 1a (#660): an eval case can declare control checks with a `controls:` block: expected policy decisions, allowed tools, maximum privileges (and `sandbox: required`), evidence fields that must be present and a verifying manifest, and regular expressions that the output must not match. Each result gets `controls` with the first mismatch, a secret is never printed, `passed` also needs the controls, and the summary adds `policyConformanceRate` and `evidenceCompletenessRate`. `scf eval` prints a controls table and the first mismatch of each failed result; the Dashboard shows a **Controls** column. Part 1 of 4 of #158.
  - **Code.** New `src/eval-controls.ts` (the strict schema `ControlsExpectSchema`, `readControlsInput`, the pure `evaluateControls`). `runEval` and `summarize` in `src/evals.ts`, `controlsReportText` in `src/evals.ts`, the controls table in `src/cli.ts`, the column in `ui/dashboard.js`. `fixtureChain` in `tests/fixtures/evidence-runs.ts` takes an optional event version.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/eval-controls.test.ts` (synthetic run folders: each check with a match and a mismatch, a missing or broken evidence stream, a manifest that does not verify, unscannable output sources, the fixed order of mismatches). Changed `tests/evals.test.ts` and `tests/ui-dashboard.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Control checks", under "Evals").
- Policy 4c (#630): the baseline also records output, time and budget overruns at `step.after`. The new rules are observed only, so nothing is stopped. Part 3 of 5 of #144.
  - **Rules.** After `credential-scope-exceeded`, the baseline has `output-limit-exceeded` (a warn when a step's output is over its limit), `timeout-exceeded`, `budget-step-exceeded`, `budget-run-exceeded` and `budget-daily-exceeded` (deny). Each rule exists only when its limit is in effect. Steps with the same limit share one rule, in chunks of 100. Budget rules need `cost_limits`, and the daily rule needs `daily_budget_usd`. Every shell step has the 20,000-character output limit by default. A baseline with more than 500 rules is `policy.invalid`.
  - **Scopes.** Steps of a sub-flow are judged by that sub-flow's own limits, at every boundary. If a sub-flow's baseline cannot be built, its steps that sign in or write are denied. A failed root baseline is kept for every step.
  - **Cap.** `runBaseline` refuses a baseline with more than 500 rules (`PolicySchemaError`), so the run fails closed instead of building a partial pack.
  - **Facts.** `step.after` always gives `outputBytes`, `elapsedMs`, `stepCostUsd` and `runCostUsd`, and gives `dailyCostUsd` when a daily budget is in effect.
  - **Elapsed time.** `elapsedMs` is the process time of the final attempt, not the whole step, and `outputBytes` counts bytes (not characters). The meter is also filled when a process is rejected, so a rejected call keeps its output size.
  - **Code.** `src/policy/baseline.ts` (the rules and the cap), `src/engine/policy-limits.ts` (new: the limits of a flow, `scopedPacks`), `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` (`subFlow`, scoped baselines at every boundary), `src/steps/process.ts` (`ProcessMeter`), `src/steps/shell.ts` (`DEFAULT_MAX_OUTPUT`), `src/steps/claude.ts` and `src/steps/codex.ts` (the meter), `src/agents/run.ts` (the meter is reset for each attempt), `src/engine/execute.ts` and `src/engine/runner.ts` (the facts and `dailyCostUsd`).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/policy-limits.test.ts`. Changed `tests/policy-baseline.test.ts`, `tests/policy-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/policy-hooks-run.test.ts` (shell and sub-flow timeouts, output limit, agent retry), `tests/process-log.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/policy-pack.ts` and `tests/fixtures/fake-claude.mjs` (the `CLAUDE_SLOW_ONCE` marker).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Policy packs in observe mode"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Baseline controls", "Construction failure", "Facts").
- The monitor reports an open issue whose waiting label Needs you does not list for longer than two checks of its watcher (`waiting-unlisted`, #622).
- Delivery 1b-2 (#656): new `delivery` section in `config.yaml` (`signing_key_path`, `required_checks`, `artifacts`, `provenance`, and per-repository `repositories`), with four controls in Settings (a **Delivery** card). Nothing reads these settings yet; the delivery contract that uses them is a later part of #650. A key in `<data folder>/delivery-keys` is never readable by a sandboxed step, even when it is rotated during a step, and a run's `delivery` folder is not writable by a step.
  - **Code.** `src/config.ts` (`deliveryNames`, the `delivery` schema). `src/settings/describe.ts` and `src/settings/exceptions.ts` (the four controls and the per-repository exceptions). `src/engine/os-sandbox.ts` (`DELIVERY_KEYS_DIR`, `RUN_DELIVERY_DIR`; the key read-deny comes after every allow rule, and the run's `delivery` folder is write-denied). `ui/admin.js` (the **Delivery** card).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/config-delivery.test.ts`. Changed `tests/os-sandbox.test.ts`, `tests/os-sandbox-run.test.ts` (a key added during a sandboxed step cannot be read), `tests/settings-describe.test.ts`, `tests/settings-inventory.test.ts`, `tests/ui-settings.test.ts` and `tests/watch-park.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Delivery settings and the signing key").
- Policy 5 (#145): the run log and the evidence view say why an action was allowed, warned, denied or sent for approval. Each new decision has a line `  Why: <reason> What to do: <next action>`, in plain words. Repeated identical decisions keep their own log line and add `· seen N times, first at <time>, now <time>`, so no count or time is hidden; the evidence view groups them with the count, the first and last time and the steps. A user sees the outcome, the reason, the reason code, the policy id and version, and the next action; the rule and the pack's hash are for administrators only. A run with no decisions, an old run and a run with a damaged event record each get a clear answer. Block mode is still not available: `enforce` is refused when a pack is loaded.
  - **Code.** New `src/policy/explain.ts` (pure: `NEXT_ACTIONS`, `nextActionCode`, `explanationLine`, `isExplanationLine`, `logNote`, `groupPolicyDecisions`; at most 100 groups and 10 step names each). `logDecision` in `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` replaces `report` and keeps the count per run start (at most 1,000 keys). `policyDecisionsOf` in `src/server/evidence-view.ts` (`policyDecisions` on the answer of `GET /api/runs/:id/evidence`). `src/server/user-view.ts` rebuilds the policy and `Why:` lines for a user. `ui/run-evidence.js` shows **Policy decisions**.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/policy-explain.test.ts` and `tests/policy-compat.test.ts` (old runs with no decisions, a run still going, a damaged or cut event stream, old flows and policy-pack version changes). Changed `tests/policy-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/policy-fail-closed.test.ts` (fail-closed privileged actions), `tests/evidence-api.test.ts`, `tests/user-view.test.ts` and `tests/ui-run-evidence.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Policy packs in observe mode": the baseline's ownership, rule precedence, observe and block mode, what you see), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Policy hooks in observe mode").
- Tool isolation (#150): text from outside the flow is data. Issue text, files, command output, logs, other agents' output and saved learnings are labelled, bounded, scrubbed and wrapped before an agent or step reads them. A step's output over 200,000 characters is withheld, not cut. Secrets are removed, and policy tags and chat markers (such as `<|im_start|>`) are neutralised. In the prompt, the text sits in an `<untrusted-data>` block, and the system prompt says it is data. A shell step or a sub-flow `vars` value that uses such text is refused (`steps.<id>.output`, `task`, `run.history`, `vars.ci_workflow`, `vars.ci_url`); use the `$FACTORY_*` environment variables. A `system_prompt` with such text fails with a fixed message. A step that would read content a person has not approved stops the run as **waiting for approval**; only a person resuming the run from that step approves it. A held child of a parallel step stops the run and cannot be skipped by `on_failure`. An optional classifier can only raise the risk, quarantine content or require approval; it never grants access, and an invalid verdict, an error or a timeout quarantines the content. The classifier runs only after the existing denials.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/untrusted.ts` (trust by path, unknown paths are untrusted; the label with source, trust class, content type, size, sha256, consumers, risk and signals; `boundUntrusted`, `envelope`, `boundAgentPrompt`, `classifyBound`, `boundEnv`, `renderTrusted`). `template.ts` gets `placeholders()` and an `each` callback for `render`. `execute.ts` refuses untrusted placeholders in shell and sub-flow values, bounds the step environment, and records a `label` on each step. `agents/run.ts` bounds the prompt after the tool profile resolves. `runner.ts` takes an `outputClassifier` option (tests only) and stops a held top-level step. `state.ts` adds `label` and `held` on the step record, and `heldContent` and `approvedContent` on the run.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/untrusted-output.test.ts` (unit tests with adversarial fixtures from `tests/fixtures/untrusted.ts`: hidden orders in files, issue text, command output, logs and inter-agent messages) and `tests/untrusted-flow.test.ts` (end-to-end: a repository file through a shell step, a refused `system_prompt`, a refused shell placeholder, hold and approve, a held parallel child, Codex). Changed `tests/engine.test.ts`, `tests/codex-resume-flow.test.ts` and `tests/repo-broker.test.ts` (the prompt holds the data block).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Text from outside the flow", under "Approvals").
- Watchers (#623): an epic with the trigger label is skipped, not built. An issue is an epic with the `epic` label, or with a **Stories** heading that lists another issue; a bare `- [ ] #N` checklist and an issue with its own `**Epic:**` or `Parent:` line are not. The epic starts no run and asks no questions, and shows the new status **epic — its stories are built one by one** (remove the `epic` label or the list to undo a wrong match). A stopped run of the epic that asks questions is parked, and its status labels are removed (`done` is kept); a run or queued job for the issue in any flow is left alone. Part 3 of 4 of #616.
  - **Code.** New `storiesIn` and `isTrackingEpic` in `src/queue/issue-list.ts` (`toRecord` shares the same `valid` rule and regexes). `tickIssues` in `src/queue/watcher.ts` skips the epic after the priority step and checks `issueBusy` again after the label edit; `setParked` is checked. New `epic` kind in `src/next-step.ts`, `src/words.ts`, `src/board.ts` (no column) and `ui/icons.js` (disabled).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/watch-epic.test.ts` (fake `gh`: two checks, no run, one status comment; needs-info with a stopped asking run). Changed `tests/issue-list.test.ts`, `tests/words.test.ts`, `tests/board.test.ts`, `tests/next-step.test.ts` and `tests/ui-icons.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Epics" in "How issue watchers use labels", and the status row in "Words the Foundry uses").
- Watchers (#624): an owner can park a story. Reply **/park** to a question, or answer a "not a coding task" stop with **keep it open**, **do not build**, **don't build** or **park it**. Only someone with write access can park; others' replies are ignored and count as answers. Parking removes the trigger and status labels, posts a comment with the marker `<!-- claude-factory run=<id> parked -->` and marks the run parked, so the Foundry stops building and asking. When work for the issue is queued or running again, or the labels cannot be removed, nothing changes and the park is tried again at the next check. A "not a coding task" stop is no longer answered with `/defaults` (auto_defaults); the **send_back** comment for it says how to park. A UI resume of a parked run clears the parked mark and runs; the labels stay off.
  - **Code.** New `src/queue/park.ts` (`wantsPark`, `notCodeStop`, `parkedComment`, `PARK_PHRASES`). `parkOnRequest` and `mayDecide` in `src/queue/watcher.ts`, called in the up-front, locked, late-answer and needs-info branches; `setParked` checks queued and running work first. New `clearParked` in `src/queue/scheduler.ts` and `clearRunParked` in `src/engine/state.ts`, for a park that could not be finished. `PARK_HINT` and `FACTORY_PARK_HINT` in `src/next-step.ts`; the hint is added to the NOT_CODE `send_back` in `scripts/build-flows.mjs`, and `flows/issue-gitflow.yaml` and `flows/issue-plan.yaml` are regenerated.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/park.test.ts` and `tests/watch-park.test.ts` (phrases, `/park`, read-only logins, auto_defaults, held lock, a resume at the same time, a given-up check, custom status labels, a UI resume of a parked run). Changed `tests/next-step.test.ts`, `tests/pipeline.test.ts` (the hint) and `tests/fixtures/flows/issue-deliver.yaml`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Park with a reply", "Parked stories", the troubleshooting entry for a parked story).
- Watchers (#654): one **Needs you** item per issue, even when several watchers list it. The issue is matched by repository and number, case ignored (`Acme/App#4` is `acme/app#4`). The item kept is an ordinary listing before an extra one, then one that needs you, then the first watcher's. Your turn adds no separate run item for a story that a waiting-label listing already covers, so a stopped question run of an old flow no longer shows next to it. Two failed runs give the first watcher's item; a failed run beats a starting hold. The Board counts a differently cased live run or queued job as the same card. Part 4 of 4 of #621.
  - **Code.** New `IssueEntry` type and `oneIssueEach` in `src/server/next.ts` (used by `collectNext`, so Your turn, the Board and `allNext().issues` get one record per issue); `extra` is carried on the issue entry. `labelStories` built from all watchers before the dedupe in `src/server/your-turn.ts`. Case-insensitive keys in `src/server/board.ts` and `src/board.ts`; `onTurn` in `src/clarity.ts` compares the repository case-insensitively.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/next-dedupe.test.ts`. Changed `tests/watch-needs-you.test.ts` (scenario 9, two watchers in both orders), `tests/server.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/scenarios.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Your turn").
- Watchers (#653): every open issue with a needs-info or waiting-approval label is listed under **Needs you**, also past the first 100 issues of the trigger list, next to another status label, and without the trigger label unless its run is parked. Such an issue is never relabeled, commented on, started or resumed. A needs-info issue shows its number of questions when its comments can be read: at most 20 reads per check, each result kept for 10 minutes; waiting-approval issues are not read. When the issue list is cut, the label query reads at most 10 pages per label per check (`waitingListPages`) and continues from where it stopped. Part 3 of 4 of #621.
  - **Code.** New `extraWaitingIssues`, `EXTRA_READS_PER_CHECK` and `EXTRA_READ_MEMO_MS` in `src/queue/waiting-issues.ts`; new `listOpenIssuesByLabelPages` and a shared page reader in `src/github.ts`; `TrackedIssue.extra`, the page cursor and read memo, `addExtras` and `waitingFromLabels` in `src/queue/watcher.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/watch-needs-you.test.ts`. Changed `tests/github-open-issues.test.ts`, `tests/waiting-issues.test.ts` and `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`FAKE_GH_LIST_LIMIT`, and `labels=` and `state=open` filtering in the paged REST handler).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Your turn").
- Watchers (#652): a waiting label (needs-info or waiting-approval) makes an issue show as waiting for you, whatever the label order and whether or not another status label is next to it. The watcher does not relabel, comment on, resume or start such an issue unless its run really waits that way. A lone waiting-approval label with no run, or with a failed or succeeded run, is not relabeled; the issue is listed from its label. A run of a removed or retired flow is never linked, so no answer or `/approve` resumes it. A stopped run that asks questions reads `(asks questions)` in the activity line. Part 2 of 4 of #621.
  - **Code.** Changed `src/queue/waiting-issues.ts` (`waitingLabelOf`, `waitsAs`, `openQuestion`, `isPaused` and `steppedAsideFor` moved here; `watcher.ts` re-exports `steppedAsideFor`) and `src/queue/watcher.ts` (mixed labels, the lone-label guards, the `one_at_a_time` lock uses `waitsAs`, the activity line).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/watch-waiting-labels.test.ts`. Changed `tests/waiting-issues.test.ts`, `tests/watch.test.ts`, `tests/deliver.test.ts` and `tests/watch-comment-reads.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("How issue watchers use labels").
- Watchers (#651): a comment read has a time limit (15 s each, 2 min per check). A read that fails or times out changes nothing: no label, no `/defaults`, no run, no status comment update. The issue is listed from its label. An issue whose read timed out is not read again for 10 minutes. Part 1 of 4 of #621.
  - **Code.** New `src/queue/waiting-issues.ts` (`commentReads`, one reader per check). Changed `src/queue/watcher.ts` (the five read sites, `Hold.byLabel`, the quiet list) and `src/queue/status-comment.ts` (`StatusView.quiet`, `statusTargets` skips quiet issues).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/waiting-issues.test.ts` and `tests/watch-comment-reads.test.ts`. Changed `tests/status-comment.test.ts`.
- Policy 4b (#629): the baseline records protected branches (with the hotfix exception), pushes to another repository, and a user's run with the machine credential, all in observe mode. The baseline is built per run from `protected_branches`, `secret_scan` and the run's repository, so `packSha256` differs between configs. A baseline that cannot be built counts as `policy.invalid`. Part 2 of 5 of #144.
  - **Rules.** In order: `branch-hotfix-exception` (allow, only for the shipped hotfix flow's `push_main`), `branch-protected` (deny, `branch.protected`), `secret-detected` (only when `secret_scan` is not off), `identity-missing`, `repository-not-allowed` (deny, `repository.not-allowed`, only when the run's repository is known), then the existing sandbox and workspace rules and `credential-scope-exceeded` (deny, `credential.scope-exceeded`, at `repository.access` for a user's `machine` credential). Protected branches are split into chunks of 100 patterns, so none is dropped.
  - **Patterns left out.** Patterns with `?`, `[`, `]` or `\`, or over 255 characters, are not in the baseline. Each run logs one `!` line for them; the hook still blocks them, so such a push is recorded as `policy.input-incomplete`.
  - **Pushes without a repository.** A push to a remote that is not on GitHub is asked against the pack without the repository rule, so it is neither denied nor input-incomplete for that rule.
  - **Facts.** `shippedFlow` is a run fact (`isShippedFlow`, true only for an unchanged built-in flow), given to every boundary. The per-step overrides are removed.
  - **Code.** Changed `src/policy/baseline.ts` (`baselineControls`, `baselinePack`, `runBaseline`, `BASELINE_VERSION`; `BASELINE_PACK` is unchanged), `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` (`baselineEntry`, `runPolicy` builds the baseline per run), `src/engine/guards.ts` (`isShippedFlow`), `src/engine/execute.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/policy-baseline.test.ts`. Changed `tests/policy-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/policy-hooks-run.test.ts`, `tests/hotfix-guards.test.ts` and `tests/policy-compose.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Policy packs in observe mode"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Policy hooks in observe mode").
- Versions of the components (#639): `scf components [kind]` prints the version and SHA-256 of each flow, policy pack, tool profile, model route set and context selector in the Foundry home, so a promotion can name exactly what was tested. Part 2a of #159. **No run behaviour changes**; listing changes nothing in the data folder.
  - **Command.** `scf components [flow|policy-pack|tool-profile|model-routes|context-selector]` prints `COMPONENT <kind> <id> v<version> sha256:<hash>` lines. Built-in flows and the built-in baseline pack end with ` builtin`. A bad file gives one `! <kind> <id>: <reason>` line and exit 1; the valid components still print. `scf components record [kind]` gives a new hash of a counted component its version; it writes only `promotions/versions.json`, under the data-folder lock, and writes nothing when nothing is new. Listing does not run the data-folder migration.
  - **Versions.** A flow's version is its `publish.version` when published, else `0`; its hash leaves `publish.version` out. A policy pack's version is `policy.version`. Tool profiles, model routes and context selectors are counted: the version is the place of the hash among the different hashes seen, so A, B, A gives 1, 2, 1. An unrecorded counted component prints `v-` and ` unrecorded`.
  - **Hashes.** Every hash is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON (sorted keys), so key order and YAML comments never count. Policy packs and tool profiles use `policyPackSha256` and `toolProfileSha256`, the hashes runs already record. Model routes hash `router.rules`, `router.fallback` and `router.fallback_on` after defaults. A context selector hashes the six settings `selectContext` reads.
  - **Tool descriptions.** `ToolProfileSchema` has an optional `descriptions` map (`<kind>.<operation>` to text, at most 1000 characters, each name must be a granted tool). It is part of the hash only when it has an entry, so a profile without it keeps its hash. `toolProfileRef` agrees with the component hash.
  - **Files.** New folders `tool-profiles/<id>.yaml` and `context-selectors/<id>.yaml` in the Foundry home (both optional). `promotions/versions.json` (strict schema, at most 4 MiB, mode 0600 in a 0700 folder). An invalid or linked `versions.json` or `promotions` folder is refused and never overwritten.
  - **Code.** New `src/promotion/components.ts` (pure), `src/promotion/home.ts` (reads the home folders, fixed problem reasons), `src/promotion/versions.ts` (`readVersionBook`, `recordVersions`) and `src/promotion/cli.ts`. Changed `src/cli.ts` (the `components` command, which skips `prepareDataHome` for listing), `src/context/select.ts` (`ContextSelectorSettingsSchema`), `src/engine/policy-store.ts` (`writeAtomic` is exported), `src/tool-profile/schema.ts` and `src/tool-profile/resolve.ts` (`descriptions`).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/promotion-components.test.ts`, `tests/promotion-home.test.ts` and `tests/promotion-cli.test.ts` (the built `scf` as a process: listing leaves an explicit home unchanged, and two concurrent `record` runs keep both entries).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the command line table and "Versions of the components").
- Tool isolation 4c (#635): an agent step never signs in to Git or GitHub, in any run. Agent steps get no `GH_TOKEN`, no other token variable, no ssh agent or keys, no `gh` login and no credential helper; they get an empty `gh` folder of their own, no prompts and `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=file` (network git only through the broker). **Breaking for custom flows**: an agent step that used `gh` or pushed or cloned over the network with the bot's login must move that work to a shell step or to `repo_ops`.
  - **Who keeps what.** An admin's agent step on the machine's HOME keeps the global git config and the bot's commit name (`GIT_AUTHOR_NAME`, `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL`), but not the bot's token. A user's run is unchanged: its agent steps are isolated as before, and shell steps keep `https:file` and the bot's identity. The clean Codex folder of an admin run ends with `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=file` too.
  - **Bot token.** `engine.botEnv()` is fetched up front, and the bot's token is given, only for a flow with a shell or sub-flow step (a sub-flow counts because its steps are not known yet). A flow with only agent steps and no bot token variable now runs; a shell step still fails at that step.
  - **Profile.** Every agent step has `credential: "none"`, and the policy's `repository.access` check for an agent is asked with `none`. A new optional `repoCredential` (`machine` or `repository`) on the tool profile keeps the broker's ceiling for `repo_ops`; without it the old mapping applies, so other profiles and their hashes are unchanged. The hash of an admin agent step changes.
  - **Provider key.** A provider whose `api_key_env` is a token variable (for example a GitHub token for GitHub Models) can no longer hand that token to an agent, also in an admin run. **Upgrade:** give that provider its own variable.
  - **Config count.** `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` from `agent_env` that is negative, not a number or too large no longer cancels the helper reset: `nextConfigIndex` (`src/engine/isolation.ts`) uses the number of contiguous `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_n` entries instead. `isolationEnv` uses it too.
  - **Code.** New `agentNoSignInEnv` and `nextConfigIndex` in `src/engine/isolation.ts`; new `botIdentityEnv` in `src/engine/guards.ts` (`identityEnv` builds on it). Changed `src/agents/run.ts`, `src/agents/codex-home.ts`, `src/agents/tool-gate.ts`, `src/tool-profile/legacy.ts` (`repoCredential`) and `src/engine/runner.ts` (`flowUsesBot`).
  - **Tests.** `tests/isolation.test.ts`: unit cases for the three functions, including a real `git config --get-all credential.helper` check for valid and bogus counts; admin-run cases for no tokens, the `gh` folder removed after the run, `agent_env` unable to put a variable back, and an agent-only flow without the bot token. `tests/codex-isolation-flow.test.ts`: the admin Codex login, private and personal-folder cases. `tests/tool-conformance.test.ts` and `tests/tool-profile.test.ts`: `credential: "none"` and `repoCredential`. `tests/policy-fail-closed.test.ts`: an agent under an unusable mandatory policy is denied at `agent.write`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("What an agent step can reach"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Runs that never use the machine's login").
  - **Limit.** In an admin run without an OS sandbox this is the environment only: an agent that unsets the variables could still read `~/.config/gh` or the keychain. The user's OS sandbox holds that.
- Tool isolation 4b (#634): an agent step can ask the engine for repository operations through a request file. `repo_ops: [commit, status]` on a `claude` step lets the agent propose operations (`clone`, `fetch`, `branch`, `commit`, `push`, `pull-request`, `status`); the engine checks and carries them out after the step succeeds, so the agent never runs them with a credential. Part 2 of 3 of #149. **Only steps with `repo_ops` change**; shell steps, `repo_access` and the built-in flows behave as before.
  - **Flow.** `repo_ops` is an optional, unique, non-empty list of `REPO_OPERATIONS`. Validation refuses it on a `skill_role: reviewer` step and on a step listed in a `parallel` step.
  - **Profile.** `legacyToolProfile` takes `repoOps`: each git operation is a `git` grant, `pull-request` is `github` `pull-request.write`, target `*`, credential `repository` for an isolated step and `machine` otherwise. Without `repo_ops` the profile and its hash are unchanged.
  - **Request file.** The agent gets `FACTORY_REPO_REQUESTS` (and `SCF_REPO_REQUESTS`) pointing at `<runDir>/repo-requests/<step>-<visit>.jsonl`, removed before every try. The file is read once after the final try, only when the step succeeded: a plain file, at most 64 KiB and 20 requests, then removed. Requests go to the broker in order and stop at the first one that is not `done`.
  - **Results.** Each result is logged as `· repo <op>: done|failed|refused (<reason>)` and added to the step output under `## Repository operations`. A failed or refused request fails the step; a policy denial ends the run. A line that is not JSON is `invalid-request`.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/repo-requests.ts` (`repoRequestsFile`, `repoRequestsEnv`, `drainRepoRequests`, `applyRepoRequests`). Changed `src/agents/run.ts`, `src/agents/tool-gate.ts` (`stepToolSettings` passes `repoOps`), `src/tool-profile/legacy.ts`, `src/flow/schema.ts` (`stepRepoOps`, the checks), `src/engine/runner.ts` (a `repo_ops` step is checked before its requests run), `src/engine/os-sandbox.ts` and `src/steps/{claude,codex}.ts` (the request folder is writable).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/repo-requests.test.ts` (drain limits, a link refused, file removed, order, stop at the first refusal) and `tests/repo-broker-flow.test.ts` (commit and status carried out, a push refused without `repo_ops`, an unlisted operation refused, a policy denial ends the run, `repo.operation` evidence). Changed `tests/tool-profile.test.ts`, `tests/os-sandbox.test.ts` and `tests/fixtures/fake-claude.mjs` (a `REQUEST` line).
  - **Docs.** `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` (`repo_ops` and the request file format), `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the flow section on repository operations).
- Delivery 1a (#649): a versioned, signed delivery contract format, built and checked as pure functions. Part 1 of 2 of #162, part of #133. **Library only: nothing in a run writes a contract yet**, so runs behave as before.
  - **Contract.** `DeliveryContractSchema` (`src/delivery/schema.ts`, version 1, strict): the run, sequence and supersession, the pull request, the commit, the dependency locks and CI workflows at the commit (SHA-256 and git blob id), required checks, the evidence head (count and terminal hash), artifacts, provenance and the fixed list of seven stop conditions. Lists are sorted and each key appears once.
  - **Build and verify.** `buildDeliveryContract` (`src/delivery/contract.ts`) is pure: the same facts give the same canonical bytes and digest in any order. `verifyDeliveryContract` never throws and returns the first fault: `unreadable`, `wrong-version`, `signature-missing`, `signature-bad`, `wrong-key`, or `evidence-head-mismatch`.
  - **Signature.** OpenSSH `SSHSIG` (Ed25519, SHA-512, namespace `scf-delivery-contract`) in plain Node, in `src/delivery/sshsig.ts`. `readSigningKey` takes only an unencrypted Ed25519 key (OpenSSH or PKCS#8 PEM). A signature made here is accepted by `ssh-keygen -Y verify`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Delivery contract").
  - **Tests.** New `tests/delivery-contract.test.ts` and `tests/delivery-sshsig.test.ts`. The `ssh-keygen` checks are skipped when `/usr/bin/ssh-keygen` is missing.
- Policy 4a (#628): each rule of a policy pack can have its own `mode` (`observe` or `enforce`), so one control can move to block mode without moving the whole pack. Pack format version 2; version 1 packs are still read. Part 1 of 5 of #144. **Enforce is still refused when a pack is loaded or restored**, so nothing is blocked yet.
  - **Format.** `PolicyRuleSchema` has an optional `mode`. `POLICY_PACK_VERSION` is 2, and `POLICY_PACK_VERSIONS` is `[1, 2]`. `parsePolicyPack` reads version 1 with `PolicyPackV1Schema` (no rule `mode`; a rule `mode` in a version 1 pack is refused). A version 1 pack keeps its exact `policyPackSha256`: it is hashed as written, never rewritten to version 2. Version 3 is refused.
  - **Decisions.** A rule's decision, and its `policy.input-incomplete`, takes the rule's `mode`, else the pack's. `policy.no-rule-matched` takes the pack's `mode`. The decision version is not bumped.
  - **Loading.** `enforceProblem` (`src/engine/policy-hooks.ts`) refuses a pack when the pack or any rule is `enforce`, in `loadOne` and in the last-known-good restore (`src/engine/policy-store.ts`), with the rule id in the detail.
  - **Baseline.** `baselinePack` is version 2 and writes `mode: "observe"` on every rule. Its decisions do not change.
  - **Docs.** `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Policy contracts"), `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Pack format").
  - **Code.** Changed `src/policy/schema.ts`, `version.ts`, `parse.ts`, `evaluate.ts`, `baseline.ts`, `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` and `src/engine/policy-store.ts`.
  - **Tests.** Changed `tests/policy-contracts.test.ts` (version 1 reading and hash, rule mode over pack mode, unknown rule field and mode refused, version 3 refused), `tests/policy-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/policy-restore.test.ts` and `tests/policy-compose.test.ts`.
- Tool isolation 4a (#633): a repository broker on the server carries out repository operations (`clone`, `fetch`, `branch`, `commit`, `push`, `pull-request`, `status`) with the run's own target, the narrowest credential and policy. Part 1 of 3 of #149. **No agent calls it yet**, so runs behave as before. **An older build cannot read version 4 evidence** (a run with a `repo.operation` event), so keep this build for those runs.
  - **Request.** Strict, discriminated on `op`; unknown fields are refused. A request never names a repository, URL or remote: the target is the run's `github_repo`, and a missing or placeholder one is refused (`no-target`). Branch names follow `git check-ref-format --branch`.
  - **Credential.** The narrowest one, never above the grant. A repository's own sign-in only (`repository`): a GitHub App token limited to the repository and to the operation's permissions, a stored token as it is; deploy keys and missing sign-ins are refused. `machine` also falls back to the bot, only for a run that is not isolated. Local operations get none.
  - **Git settings.** Before an operation in a checkout, any repository git setting outside a fixed allowlist refuses it (`unsafe-git-settings`), so an agent-written `http.proxy`, `include.*`, `url.*.insteadOf` or `core.fsmonitor` has no effect. Processes use argument lists, HTTPS only, prompts off and an empty `gh` config folder.
  - **Policy and evidence.** Asks `repository.access` (and `push` for a push) before running; a denial refuses with `policy`. Every outcome is one `repo.operation` event (evidence version 4; the migration only changes `version`). Telemetry maps it to `tool.called`, and a done push also emits `delivery.push_attempted`. Output never holds a token.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/repo-broker.ts` and `src/engine/repo-request.ts`. Changed `src/engine/repo-access.ts` (`appTokenAccess` passes `permissions`), `src/github-app.ts` (`installationTokenInfo` takes `permissions`, part of the cache key), `src/evidence/{schema,version,parse,record}.ts` and `src/telemetry/envelope.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/repo-broker.test.ts` (fake `exec`: argv and env of every operation, each refusal, app permissions, the settings allowlist, policy through a mandatory outage, no token in output; a real temporary repository for commit, branch and status). Changed `tests/evidence-schema.test.ts`, `tests/evidence-chain.test.ts`, `tests/evidence-stream.test.ts`, `tests/telemetry-envelope.test.ts` and `tests/github-app.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Repository broker", and the evidence formats).
- Telemetry 3d (#620): the repository view has an **Outcomes** section for a GitHub repository, for its owner and for an admin. It shows the outcome measures of that repository for 7, 30 or 90 days, with a flow filter, the same pairs and definitions as the Dashboard. A user never sees cost, returning users, or the model and policy filters; a pair with admin-only measures says how many are left out. If the card cannot load, it shows "Could not load the outcomes." with Retry; the rest of the page still works. Part 4 of 4 of #157.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Outcomes on the repository view" in the repository section; the Outcomes chapter is unchanged).
  - **Code.** Changed `ui/outcomes.js` (`outcomesSection` takes `repo`, `admin` and `embedded`; new `adminOnlyText`; a group counts its `hidden` measures) and `ui/repo-view.js` (`githubOf`, the Outcomes section, `ctx.viewerAdmin` and `ctx.part`). `ui/outcomes.js` does not import `ui/dashboard.js`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-repo-outcomes.test.ts` (user and admin sessions). Changed `tests/ui-outcomes.test.ts`, `tests/ui-repo-view.test.ts` and `tests/ui-repo-admin-sections.test.ts`.
- Policy 3c-2 (#626): an active break-glass turns a mandatory-policy outage denial into an audited `warn` (`policy.break-glass`), so privileged steps go on, recorded and logged. Part 2 of 2 of #583. **An older build cannot read a run with a `policy.break-glass` decision**, so keep this build for those runs.
  - **Effect.** While `scf policy break-glass` is active, a privileged step whose mandatory pack cannot be loaded or evaluated (`policy.unavailable` or `policy.invalid`) goes on. Rule decisions, the repository and optional layers, and denials with no recorded decision are unchanged. A denial whose decision cannot be recorded in the evidence stays a denial. Without a break-glass, a reader that throws or returns nothing, the outage still denies.
  - **Log and evidence.** The run logs `! policy: break-glass active until <expiresAt> (<reason>) — <pack> <code> allowed`, and the policy line ends with `— break-glass: the step goes on`. The evidence has a `policy.decided` event with `outcome: warn`, `reasonCode: policy.break-glass`, version `unknown` and no `packSha256` or `rule`. It verifies.
  - **Schema.** `policy.break-glass` is in `ENGINE_REASONS`, `NO_RULE_REASONS` and `NO_PACK_REASONS`. `PolicyDecisionSchema` accepts it only as `warn` in `enforce` mode. The decision version is not bumped.
  - **Server watch.** New `breakGlassWatch` in `src/engine/policy-break-glass.ts`, run at startup and every 60 s (unref'd, cleared in `close()`). While a break-glass is active it logs `! policy: break-glass active until …` at the first tick, for each new record, and every 15 minutes. On expiry it writes the `expired` audit line and logs `policy: break-glass ended (expired)`. When the record is gone it logs `policy: break-glass ended`.
  - **Code.** Changed `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` (`RunPolicy` option `breakGlass`, the private `outage`, `report` returns whether the decision was recorded, `runPolicy` passes `activeBreakGlass`), `src/evidence/record.ts` (`put` and `policyDecided` return whether the event was recorded), `src/policy/reasons.ts`, `src/policy/schema.ts`, `src/engine/policy-break-glass.ts` and `src/server/server.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/policy-break-glass-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/policy-break-glass-watch.test.ts` and `tests/policy-break-glass-run.test.ts` (end to end: active, expired and invalid). Changed `tests/policy-contracts.test.ts` (stable list, schema, old-build refusal) and `tests/policy-hooks.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Break-glass", and the break-glass rows of the command table), `docs/DESIGN.md` (the reason list, "Break-glass" in "Policy hooks in observe mode").
- Telemetry 3c (#619): an **Outcomes** card on the Dashboard shows the ten outcome measures for 7, 30 or 90 days, each speed or cost measure next to the measures to read it with. Part 3 of 4 of #157.
  - **Card.** Filters for repository, flow and window; model route and policy version for an admin only. A change of filter loads `GET /api/outcomes` again and redraws only the card. Each row shows the value (`—` when there is none), how many it counts, how many runs were left out, the owner, the decision and a "Definition and exclusions" disclosure with the server's text.
  - **Failure.** If the answer cannot be loaded, the card shows "Could not load the outcomes." with Retry; the rest of the Dashboard still works.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Outcomes": the ten definitions and their exclusions, the same text as `OUTCOME_DEFINITIONS`).
  - **Code.** New `ui/outcomes.js` (`outcomesSection`, `outcomeGroups`, `outcomeTable`, `outcomeValueText`, `countText`). Changed `ui/api.js` (`api.outcomes(params)`) and `ui/dashboard.js` (the card after "Cost per day"; `renderDashboard` waits for it).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-outcomes.test.ts`. Changed `tests/ui-dashboard.test.ts` (the outcomes answer and failure) and `tests/ui-a11y-equivalents.test.ts` (the card's audit and captions).
- Policy 3c-1 (#625): `scf policy break-glass` starts, ends and audits a time-limited break-glass record. Part 1 of 2 of #583. **The break-glass has no effect on runs yet**: no step is allowed to go on because of it. Part 2 will use it during a mandatory-policy outage.
  - **Start.** `scf policy break-glass --reason "<text>" --for <n>m|<n>h` writes `policy-break-glass.json` in the data folder (file 0600, written by a temp file and one rename). The reason is required, up to 200 characters, with no control characters or newline and no credential-like text. The duration is required, `1m` to `4h`. A start while a break-glass is active is refused. A start over an expired file first writes the expiry end line. If the start line cannot be written, the file is removed again.
  - **End.** `scf policy break-glass --end` removes the file and writes the end line with `ended`. An expired file gets `expired`, and an invalid file or link is removed with `invalid`. A folder at the path is refused, and a missing record prints `✘ no break-glass is active` and exits 1.
  - **Expiry.** The first reader after the expiry time (`activeBreakGlass`, on the data folder) writes exactly one `policy-break-glass-end` line with `expired` and removes the file, under the auth lock.
  - **Audit.** New actions `policy-break-glass-start` (`target: expiresAt`, `detail: reason`) and `policy-break-glass-end` (`target: expiresAt`, `detail: ended|expired|invalid`), with `by: "cli"`. The reason is only in the audit line and log lines, never in run evidence. The data-folder move copies `policy-break-glass.json` byte for byte.
  - **No API and no config.** There is no API route and no `config.yaml` key. The record can only be changed on the server machine.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/policy-break-glass.ts` (`readBreakGlass`, `activeBreakGlass`, `startBreakGlass`, `endBreakGlass`, `endExpiredBreakGlass`). Changed `src/engine/policy-cli.ts` (`policyCommand`, `POLICY_USAGE`), `src/cli.ts`, `src/engine/policy-store.ts` (`writeAtomic`, `randomTmp` and `writeTmp` exported), `src/auth/audit.ts`, `ui/audit.js` and `src/home-migrate.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/policy-break-glass.test.ts` (the checks, start, end, the expiry line, every read state and the CLI output). `tests/ui-audit.test.ts` counts 43 actions.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the command table), this entry.
- Telemetry 3b (#618): `GET /api/outcomes` answers the outcome measures for one window, with filters and access rules. Part 2 of 4 of #157.
  - **Query.** `repo`, `flow`, `model` (`agent:provider:model`), `policy` (`id@version`) and `days` (a whole number from 1 to 90, default 30). A wrong value answers 400. `model` and `policy` are admin only: a user who sends them gets 403, and a malformed value gets 400 first.
  - **Answer.** `{ window: { from, to, days }, filters, values: { repos, flows, models?, policies? }, measures, definitions }`. `values.repos` and `values.flows` come from the runs the caller may see, as on `/api/run-filters`. `models` and `policies` are only for an admin.
