  - **Who sees what.** An admin sees every run. A user, and an admin using view-as, sees only their own runs on repositories still in their list (`listRepos`). For them there is no `cost_per_successful_run` and no `returning_users`, and `definitions` holds only the measures they may see.
  - **Model and policy.** A run matches `model` when one of its steps ran on that model, and `policy` when one of its decisions came from that `id@version`.
  - **Load.** Briefs in memory narrow the runs by owner, repository, flow and window before any `run.json` is read. The runs are read one by one, yielding to the event loop every 25, and their evidence goes through `OutcomeFactsCache`.
  - **Code.** New `src/server/api-outcomes.ts` (`parseOutcomeFilter`, `visibleBriefs`, `narrowBriefs`, `outcomeDefinitionsFor`, `outcomesFor`, `outcomeRoutes`). Changed `src/engine/state.ts` (`loadRunAsync`), `src/queue/scheduler.ts` (`getAsync`), `src/server/api-runs.ts` (`checkedRunAsync`), `src/server/server.ts` (route registered), `src/server/permissions.ts` (rule `GET outcomes`, user `yes`) and `src/remote/areas/index.ts` (no command line yet, so `GET outcomes` is API only).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/outcome-filter.test.ts` (the query parsing, the 400 texts, the brief narrowing and the visibility) and `tests/server-outcomes.test.ts` (an admin, a user, a user with a removed repository, view-as, and the 400 and 403 cases). `tests/permissions.test.ts` has the example for the new rule.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the API table row), this entry.
- Telemetry 3a (#617): ten outcome measures computed from run history and evidence (`src/outcomes.ts`, `src/outcome-facts.ts`). Part 1 of 4 of #157. No UI yet.
  - **Measures.** Issue to pull request, first-pass test success, merge rate (pushed work), rework after delivery, policy blocks, evidence completeness, resumes, approval latency, cost per successful run and returning users. Each has a unit, a category, an owner, the decision it informs and the measures to read it with (`pairedWith`). Only `returning_users` is admin-only, and it gives counts, never an account.
  - **Delivered.** A run delivers when it succeeded and a `push` policy decision was not `deny`. So merge rate measures pushed work: a merge done by hand on GitHub is not seen. Installs without push policy decisions have no delivery data, so issue-to-PR time, rework and merge rate are empty there.
  - **Window.** Runs count by the time they ended, work items by the end of their first delivery. Rework looks at later runs up to the end of the window. `excluded` always counts runs.
  - **Cache.** `OutcomeFactsCache` reads each `evidence.jsonl` at most once per run folder, run id and `updatedAt`, and only after the run has settled (2 minutes). It keeps small facts, never the events. Files over 16 MiB and links are not read. The manifest and incomplete marker are checked on every call.
  - **Code.** New `src/outcomes.ts` (`OUTCOME_DEFINITIONS`, `computeOutcomes`, `outcomeText`) and `src/outcome-facts.ts` (`outcomeFactsOf`, `OutcomeFactsCache`). Changed `src/stats.ts` (`workKey` is exported) and `src/monitor/slo-input.ts` (`evidenceFiles` is shared with the SLO input; no change in behaviour).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/outcomes.test.ts` (the catalogue, each measure on fixed runs, the window and the bad input) and `tests/outcome-facts.test.ts` (the facts from events, the cache). The SLO input tests still pass.
  - Docs: `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Outcomes"), this entry. The user guide does not change: users see nothing new yet.
- Policy 3b-2 (#615): a last-known-good copy of each mandatory overlay, and `scf policy restore <id> [--force]` to put it back. Part 2 of 2 of #582, after #614.
  - **Copy.** Each run start saves every valid `policy.mandatory` overlay to `policies/last-known-good/<id>.json` in the data folder (folder 0700, file 0600, written by a temp file and one rename). The baseline, repository packs and optional packs are not saved. An invalid pack keeps the earlier copy. A copy that cannot be saved logs `! policy <id>: the last-known-good copy could not be saved` and the run goes on.
  - **Restore.** `scf policy restore <id> [--force]` puts the copy back as `policies/<id>.json`. It refuses, and writes nothing to `policies/`, for an invalid id, `foundry.baseline`, a missing copy, a linked or non-file copy, a copy whose SHA-256 does not match, a copy over 1 MiB compact, a folder at `<id>.json`, or a valid current file without `--force`. A replaced file is kept as `<id>.json.replaced-<time>`, and an earlier backup is never overwritten.
  - **Audit.** New action `policy-restore`: `result: "ok"` on a restore, `result: "failed"` on a refusal after the lock is taken. A refused id writes no line.
  - **Output.** `✔ restored policy <id> (sha256 …)`, then `kept the replaced file as policies/<name>` when a file was kept. A refusal prints `✘ <reason>` and exits 1.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/policy-store.ts` (`saveLastKnownGood`, `readLastKnownGood`, `restorePolicy`) and `src/engine/policy-cli.ts` (`policyCommand`, `POLICY_USAGE`). Changed `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` (`readPolicyFile` is exported and used by `loadOne`; `runPolicy` saves the copies), `src/cli.ts`, `src/auth/audit.ts` and `ui/audit.js` (`policy-restore`).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/policy-restore.test.ts` and `tests/policy-restore-seams.test.ts`. `tests/ui-audit.test.ts` counts 41 actions.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("When the mandatory policy cannot be read", the command list), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Policy hooks in observe mode"), this entry.
- Policy 3b-1 (#614): a mandatory policy that cannot be read or evaluated stops the steps that hold a sign-in or write. Part 1 of 2 of #582, after #581.
  - **Upgrade note.** A missing, unreadable, linked, invalid, too large or `mode: enforce` mandatory overlay (`policy.mandatory`) now **denies privileged steps**. Before, the run went on. Until the file is fixed, every step that signs in to the repository or writes with an agent fails. Check `policies/<id>.json` before you upgrade an install that uses `policy.mandatory`.
  - **Privileged steps.** `repository.access` with the machine's login or a repository token (`credential: machine` or `repository`), `agent.write` and `approval.consume`. Everything else is not stopped: `run.start`, `step.before`, `step.after`, `push`, `run.complete`, `repository.access` without a sign-in and the workspace's `repository.access`. Isolated read-only agent steps and isolated shell steps without `repo_access` still run.
  - **Denied steps.** The run ends `failed` and `on_failure` is not followed. The error and the log line say `mandatory policy <id> cannot be used (<code>): <boundary> is denied`. A `step.denied` event (`denial.kind: "policy"`, version `unknown`) is written, and the evidence still verifies. A shell step is refused before its push log and bot token are set up; an agent step before it starts, or before a session that writes. An approval that cannot be used records no decision.
  - **Packs in the other layers** (`policy.repositories`, `policy.packs`) never deny, and a valid mandatory pack that denies still only observes.
  - **Log lines.** An enforced decision ends `— enforced: the step is stopped`. A mandatory pack that cannot be loaded ends `— recorded as <code>; this mandatory policy denies steps with a sign-in and writing agents`; the other layers keep `; the run goes on`.
  - **Code.** `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` (`privilegedBoundary`, `deniedReason`, `PolicyDenied`, `PolicyAnswer.denied`, `mandatoryUnusable`; `repository()` returns the answer), `src/evidence/record.ts` (`stepDenied`), `src/engine/execute.ts` (`refuseByPolicy`), `src/agents/run.ts` (the denial at `repository.access` and `agent.write`), `src/engine/runner.ts` (the denial at `approval.consume`; no bot token fetched at start when a mandatory pack is unusable).
  - **Tests.** Extended `tests/policy-hooks.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/policy-pack.ts` (`linkPack`). New `tests/policy-fail-closed.test.ts` (the four failures on a shell and an agent step, the other layers, a worktree run, approval) and `tests/policy-fail-closed-isolation.test.ts` (isolated and stored-token steps).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Policy packs in observe mode", "When the mandatory policy cannot be read"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Policy hooks in observe mode"), this entry.
- Telemetry 2b: five SLO detectors with owner, threshold, window and next action (#613). Part 2 of 2 of #156, part of #133.
  - **Detectors.** `policy-service-failure`, `evidence-finalization`, `step-latency-p95`, `repeated-tool-denial` and `abnormal-output`. The Problems page shows the owner, threshold, window and next action of each.
  - **Settings.** Five new `monitor` keys with defaults an admin can change.
  - **Local files only.** They read `run.json` and the evidence files, never telemetry, so telemetry off or an unreachable collector changes nothing. Evidence files over 16 MiB are not read.
  - **Code.** New `src/monitor/slo-detectors.ts` (the five detectors, `p95`, `median`) and `src/monitor/slo-input.ts` (the local input, cached by run folder and `updatedAt`). Changed `src/monitor/detectors.ts` (`Slo`, `Detector.slo`, `measures` and `evidence` in `DetectorInput`), `src/monitor/work-detectors.ts` (`ALL_DETECTORS`, `detectorInfo(config)`), `src/monitor/monitor.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/settings/describe.ts`, `src/server/api-monitor.ts` and `ui/problems.js` (Owner, Window and Next action columns).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/monitor-slo-detectors.test.ts` (each detector below, at and above its threshold, outside its window, and with missing data; the telemetry-off case). Changed the detector, settings, Problems API, Problems UI and watch tests.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the detector table, the `config.yaml` example, "The Problems page"), this entry.
- Telemetry 2a: each ended run is measured in fixed terms (#612). Part 1 of 2 of #156, part of #133.
  - **Measures.** A catalogue of 12 measures, each with a unit, an owner (`admin` or `repository owner`, never an account) and the detector or setting it informs: duration, queue time, retries, tokens, cost, denials, policy outcomes, context size, tool latency, interruptions, approval latency and pull-request result. A test fails when an entry has no owner or no decision.
  - **One event.** A `run.measured` telemetry event is written when a run ends (succeeded, failed or cancelled), after the evidence is final. It holds only numbers (a new strict `measures` object), and a measure without a source is left out. A run that is only stopped or waiting gets none. It is best effort and never changes the run.
  - **Tool latency.** The time between each `tool_use` and its `tool_result` is measured for Claude Code steps. The `tool.called` event gets `durationMs`; a call with no result is sent at the end of the step without it. The step record gets `tools` (calls, total and largest time in ms).
  - **Context size.** The step record gets `contextTokens`: the largest input of one model turn (input, cache read and cache creation tokens). Claude steps only.
  - **Telemetry version 2.** `TELEMETRY_VERSION` is 2. Version 1 events are still valid.
  - **Code.** New `src/monitor/measures.ts` (`MEASURES`, `measureRun`, `readRunEvents`, `runMeasuresTelemetry`, `emitRunMeasured`). Changed `src/steps/claude.ts` (`streamMeter`, `ToolTimes`), `src/agents/run.ts`, `src/engine/state.ts`, `src/engine/execute.ts`, `src/engine/runner.ts` (`finish`, `cancelWaitingRun`, `cancelStoppedRun`) and `src/telemetry/envelope.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/measures.test.ts` (fixed run and event data: missing evidence, an old run, a cancelled approval, a resumed run). Changed `tests/telemetry-envelope.test.ts`, `tests/telemetry-privacy.test.ts`, `tests/telemetry-run.test.ts`, `tests/telemetry-otlp.test.ts`, `tests/telemetry.test.ts`, `tests/agents.test.ts` and `tests/fixtures/fake-claude.mjs` (`usage` and `tool_result` lines).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Lifecycle telemetry"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Telemetry"), this entry.
- A schedule watcher can build release files by itself: with `release_build: true` ("Build release files after each release"), each new commit on main is built once by `release-build` when the repository has a recipe (#589). Part of #588.
  - **Option.** `release_build` on a schedule watcher (default `false`). A watcher of another source is refused with "only a schedule watcher builds release files". The form shows the checkbox only for a schedule.
  - **Checks.** The watcher reads the head of the branch (the `mainBranch` setting, else `main`) at every check, and a schedule watcher with the option checks at least every 5 minutes. A new commit with a recipe (the file at the commit, or the stored recipe) queues one `release-build` run for the newest commit on the branch. The run takes the commit it resolves. A new commit without a recipe is recorded and nothing is built. The first check only remembers the current head.
  - **Never twice.** A commit is recorded when it is claimed by an automatic run, skipped or seen without a recipe. A recorded commit is never built again, also after a restart, after a switch off and on, or after main moved back to it. A run that finds a recorded commit ends with `skipped: this commit was handled before` and builds and uploads nothing. A failed automatic build is not retried; start it by hand. Queued runs are never cancelled: several moves before a build starts give one build, of the newest commit.
  - **Queue.** A queued job carries a submission id. After a crash, the next check finds out whether the job was accepted and queues the commit again only when it was not.
  - **Since and Your turn.** A succeeded automatic build shows under "Since you last looked" as "release files uploaded" (with "… N times" for several), linking to the draft release. A failed one shows in "Your turn" as "Build failed — open the run", linking to the run.
  - **Record.** One state file per repository and branch, and one small file per recorded commit, in `release-builds/` of the data folder. The folder name is a hash of the repository (case-insensitive) and branch. A broken state file is read as "no state", so the next check starts again from the head and records it (no build).
  - **Code.** New `src/queue/release-trigger.ts` (`checkReleaseBuild`, `releaseCheckEvery`, `mainBranchOf`, `releaseBuildLock`), `src/release/build-claims.ts` (the state and the commit records), `src/release/auto-build.ts` (`isWatcherReleaseBuild`, the run variables), `src/engine/release-claim.ts` (the claim after `resolve`). Changed `src/queue/watcher.ts` (`tickSchedule` runs the chore and then the check; `tickChore` is the old body), `src/config.ts`, `src/engine/execute.ts`, `src/since.ts`, `src/server/your-turn.ts`, `src/settings/describe.ts`, `ui/watcher-form.js`, `tools/release-build` and `tools/release-upload` (a `skip` plan ends the run with a line).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/release-trigger.test.ts` (first check, queue, same head, no recipe, stored recipe, restart, a broken state, replacement while queued, a failed queue, two checks at once, a failed read of main, names, the interval) and `tests/release-claim.test.ts`. Extended `tests/since.test.ts`, `tests/server-your-turn.test.ts`, `tests/ui-watcher-options.test.ts`, `tests/release-build-flow.test.ts`, `tests/os-sandbox-run.test.ts` and `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`git/ref/heads/*` and the recipe file).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Sources", the options table, "Build release files on this machine", "Since you last looked", "Your turn"), this entry.
- Tool isolation 3 — Claude and Codex get the same tool profile (#148). Part of #133.
  - **One profile per step.** An agent step works out its tool profile once, before an agent is picked, and checks it again for every try, so a retry or a fallback to the other agent gets the same profile. Today's flows keep their settings: their profile is recorded and not changed.
  - **The `· tools:` line.** One line per try, for example `· tools: generated profile 3f2a9c1b8d0e on Codex: partial — Codex cannot hold … (recorded only)`. It shows the first 12 characters of the profile's hash, never its id, version or rules.
  - **Evidence.** A new `step.profiled` event for each try: the profile reference (id, version, SHA-256), the source, the agent, the outcome, what the agent cannot hold and what the settings allow more than the profile. Evidence event version 3; version 2 lines are still read. The same try is also a `step` telemetry event.
  - **Outcomes.** `enforced`: the agent holds the profile. `partial`: a generated profile that the agent cannot hold in full; it is recorded, not enforced. `refused`: the step does not start, with no retry and no other agent.
  - **Declared profiles fail closed.** A profile passed to the engine as `toolProfile` (in code, not in the config) is saved in `run.json` and refused before launch when the settings allow more than it, when the agent cannot hold it, when it has rules with no meaning in a profile, when a data class is below `restricted`, or when the agent would load the personal setup. The mapping to the settings only narrows: it never turns on Claude's sandbox (which would auto-allow Bash), and never widens Codex's sandbox.
  - **Unchanged.** Runs of today behave as before. Their profile only adds the `· tools:` line and the evidence event.
  - **Code.** New `src/agents/tool-gate.ts` (`resolveStepProfile`, `gateTarget`, `stepToolSettings`, the refusal texts) and `src/tool-profile/session.ts` (`toolSession`, `mapSessionSettings`, `mappedExcess`, `adapterFit`, `declaredToolProfile`, `snapshotToolProfile`). Changed `src/agents/run.ts` (the gate for each try; the helpers moved to `tool-gate.ts` and re-exported), `src/agents/codex-home.ts` (`prepareCleanCodex` takes the profile), `src/engine/runner.ts`, `src/engine/execute.ts` and `src/engine/state.ts` (`RunOptions.toolProfile`, `RunSummary.toolProfile`, `Engine.toolProfile`), `src/evidence/schema.ts`, `version.ts`, `parse.ts`, `record.ts` (version 3 with a migration from 2), `src/telemetry/envelope.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/tool-conformance.test.ts` (the settings of today and declared profiles against both agents, the mapping invariants, the refusal texts) and `tests/tool-gate-flow.test.ts` (a generated profile, retry then fallback, a refused Codex step, a narrower profile, resume). Changed the evidence, Codex home, Codex isolation, skill and telemetry tests for the new event and version.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Codex and your personal setup", "Evidence of a run"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Tool profiles", "Evidence formats"), this entry.
- Policy 3a (#581): a built-in baseline policy, layers of packs, and the most restrictive decision wins. Still observe only: nothing is stopped. Part of #133, after #142.
  - **Baseline.** `foundry.baseline` is built into the Foundry, its version is the Foundry's package version, and it is asked at every boundary of every run. Its rules: a push with secrets found is denied (`secret.detected`), a push without a committer identity is denied (`identity.missing`), a user's agent write without a sandbox is denied (`sandbox.required`), and an in-place run by a user is denied (`workspace.admin-only`). It cannot be replaced, and `policy.packs`, `policy.mandatory` and `policy.repositories` refuse its id.
  - **Layers.** New `policy.mandatory` (admin overlays, asked after the baseline), `policy.repositories` (packs per `owner/name`, asked for runs of that repository) and `policy.packs` (optional). Packs are files in `<data folder>/policies/`. New `src/policy/compose.ts` (`combineDecisions`, `layeredPackIds`): the most restrictive outcome wins, a tie goes to the higher layer, and a repository pack can only make things stricter.
  - **Log and evidence.** Each live-log line names the layer and marks the winning decision with ` · wins`. Every run writes one baseline line and one `policy.decided` event per boundary, also when no packs are configured.
  - **Push hook.** The pre-push hook writes the number of secrets found and whether a committer identity exists to `FACTORY_PUSH_LOG`. The hook still refuses protected branches and any non-zero scan result as before. The scan output now goes to stderr when the scan ends.
  - **Code.** New `src/policy/baseline.ts` and `src/policy/compose.ts`. Changed `src/config.ts`, `src/settings/exceptions.ts`, `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` (`RunPolicy.at` returns `{decisions, combined}`, `runPolicy` returns a `RunPolicy`, `drainPushLog`, `observesPushes`), `src/engine/guards.ts`, `src/engine/runner.ts` and `src/engine/execute.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/policy-compose.test.ts`. Changed the policy, secrets, evidence, settings and telemetry tests. The evidence tests now read the non-policy events for positions, and the check step prints a sentinel that does not contain "found".
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Policy packs in observe mode"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Policy hooks in observe mode"), this entry.
- Telemetry 1b (#155): push attempts, Claude Code tool calls and watcher events (#585). Part 2 of 3 of #155, part of #133.
  - **Events.** `delivery.push_attempted`, `tool.called` (the tool's name only), `watcher.checked`, `watcher.failed` (no error text), `watcher.queued` and `pull_request.feedback`. `tool.called` comes from Claude Code steps only; Codex steps give none.
  - **Push log.** `pushLogFor` also returns the push log when telemetry is on and the protected-branch or secret-scan hook is installed anyway. Hook installation is unchanged. A push is recorded as an attempt, so a refused push is recorded too.
  - **`scf watch`** writes telemetry, like `runFlow` and `resumeRun`.
  - **Code.** `tmId` is exported from `src/telemetry/envelope.ts`. New `emitLiveTelemetry(kind, name, fields)` in `src/telemetry/telemetry.ts` for events without an evidence line. Changed `src/engine/execute.ts`, `src/steps/claude.ts` (`onTool` option), `src/agents/run.ts`, `src/queue/watcher.ts` and `src/cli.ts`. Every label is checked with its schema before it is written; a bad label is left out, not the event.
  - **Tests.** `tests/telemetry-run.test.ts` (tool calls, pushes, the push log) and new `tests/telemetry-watcher.test.ts` (watcher events, a long watcher id, `scf watch --once` with the fake `gh`).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Lifecycle telemetry"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Telemetry"), this entry.
- Release builds 4 (#593): `scf release-build init` suggests a `.factory/release-build.yaml` from the build files of a repository. Part 4 of 4 of #588.
  - **Command.** `scf release-build init --repo <owner/name>` makes a shallow clone in a temporary folder (deleted afterwards) and prints the suggested recipe to stdout. With `--write` it creates the branch `release-build-recipe`, commits the file and opens a pull request with `gh pr create`. `--write` refuses, changes nothing and exits 1 when the file exists, `.factory` is not a folder, nothing was detected, the branch already exists on GitHub or the default branch is unknown. It never pushes to the default branch.
  - **Detection.** Plain rules, no agent: `package.json` (`npm pack`), `pyproject.toml` or `setup.py` (`python -m build`), `build.gradle(.kts)` and `pom.xml`, `go.mod`, `Cargo.toml`, and `Dockerfile` (image tarball, `requires: [docker]`). The package-manager choices mirror `tools/detect-commands`. Every guessed line has a `#` comment, and the version is always `{commit_date}-{sha}`. With nothing detected it prints a commented template and says so.
  - **Code.** New `src/release/init.ts` (`suggestRecipe`, `detectBuilds`) and `src/release/init-cli.ts` (`releaseBuildCommand`). Changed `src/cli.ts` (`--write` option, `release-build` case, usage line).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/release-init.test.ts` (the rules per kind, a mixed repository, unsafe names, the template) and `tests/release-init-cli.test.ts` (print mode, `--write` against a bare remote and the fake `gh`, the refusals, a race on the branch, interrupts, a failed pull request, a failed clone, a `.factory` symlink).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Suggest a recipe" and the command table), this entry.
- Release builds 3 (#592): a release-build recipe can be stored in the Foundry settings of a repository, for a repository that should not contain a Foundry file. Part 3 of 4 of #588.
  - **Settings.** New optional `releaseRecipe` in `src/auth/repo-settings.ts` (YAML text, at most 16 384 characters; CRLF becomes LF, trailing spaces are trimmed, control characters other than tab and line break are refused). `{}` or an empty value clears it. Stored settings without it load unchanged.
  - **API and admin.** `PUT /api/admin/repos/:id/settings` checks the recipe with the recipe schema and refuses an invalid one with a 400 and a `releaseRecipe: …` message. Admin only (403 for others); the audit line names the changed keys, never the text. The admin page (All repositories) and the repository page (new section "Release recipe") show and edit it. `scf admin repo-settings` gains `--release-recipe`, so it does not clear the recipe.
  - **Lookup.** New `findReleaseRecipe(githubRepo, commitDir)` in `src/release/recipe.ts` is the one check for "this repository has a recipe": the file at the commit wins, else the stored recipe. `hasReleaseRecipe` wraps it for part 4 (#589). The stored recipe is matched by `owner/name` in any letter case, with or without `.git`; different texts under two accounts are an error. A git error while reading the file fails the run; it never falls back to the stored recipe.
  - **Flow.** `release-build` prints `recipe: .factory/release-build.yaml` or `recipe: stored in the Foundry settings`, and `plan.json` gets `recipeSource`. With no recipe, the message says whether the repository is registered in the Foundry. `tools/release-build` reads the stored recipe from `dist/auth/repo-settings.js` and the repository store.
  - **Code.** `src/release/build.ts` no longer reads the recipe itself. Changed `src/auth/repo-settings.ts`, `src/server/api-repos.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts`, `src/remote/areas/people.ts`, `ui/admin-repos.js`, `ui/repo-admin-sections.js`, `scripts/build-flows.mjs` and `flows/release-build.yaml`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/release-recipe-stored.test.ts`; extended `tests/release-build-flow.test.ts`, `tests/repos-admin-api.test.ts`, `tests/ui-admin-repos.test.ts`, `tests/ui-repo-admin-sections.test.ts` and `tests/permissions.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Where the recipe comes from", the settings list and the API table).
- Release builds 2 (#591): `release-build` uploads the built files to a **draft** GitHub release of the recipe's tag, prunes old drafts and can be started by hand. Part 2 of 4 of #588.
  - **Upload.** Step `upload` (`repo_access: true`, the only step with the `gh` login) finds the draft by listing the releases, creates it with `--target <sha>` if there is none, and replaces same-named files. A published release with the tag fails the run with nothing changed. Several drafts with the tag: the newest is used, with a warning. Names on the release are made safe (`/` becomes `-`).
  - **Pruning.** After a successful upload, the oldest drafts of the recipe are deleted down to `keep`. Only drafts whose tag fully matches the recipe's tag pattern and whose notes carry the recipe's marker line are candidates. Published releases and hand-made or other drafts are never deleted.
  - **Lock.** One `release-build` works at a time (area `@release-build`, all repositories), the others wait in the queue.
  - **Start.** `scf run release-build --repo owner/name [--ref …]` sets `github_repo` and `ref` (`--repo` takes a folder as before). Through the server a user may build only the default branch (`mainBranch`, else `main`); a refusal is a 403 that names the ref.
  - **Result.** The run summary lists the files with sizes and the draft's link. `run.json` gets `outputs` (new `src/engine/run-outputs.ts`, a step writes `outputs.json`) with `release_url`, `release_tag` and `release_files`.
  - **Code.** New `src/release/upload.ts` (`ghRunner`, `uploadRelease`, `pickPrune`, `stageFiles`), `src/run-target.ts` (`runTarget`), `src/engine/run-outputs.ts`, `tools/release-upload`. Changed `src/release/recipe.ts` (`tagPattern`, `releaseMarker`), `src/cli.ts`, `src/engine/state.ts`, `src/engine/execute.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts` (`checkReleaseRef`, a rule for `POST /api/runs`) and `src/server/api-runs.ts`, `scripts/build-flows.mjs` and `flows/release-build.yaml`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/release-upload.test.ts` (fake `gh` runner: existing draft, published tag, pruning, several drafts, name rules), `tests/run-outputs.test.ts` and `tests/run-target.test.ts`. Extended `tests/release-build-flow.test.ts` (end to end against the fake `gh`, lock and `repo_access` checks), `tests/permissions.test.ts` (a non-default ref refused for a user) and `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`gh release list/view/create/upload/delete`, state next to `$FAKE_GH_LOG`).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Build release files on this machine": upload, drafts, pruning, start by hand; the `POST /api/runs` row; the `scf run` row), this entry.
- Release builds 1 (#590): build release files on the Foundry machine from `.factory/release-build.yaml`, with no upload yet. Part 1 of 4 of #588.
  - **Flow.** New built-in flow `release-build` (`flows/release-build.yaml`, generated by `scripts/build-flows.mjs`): clone, resolve the ref to a commit, read the recipe at that commit, build. Outputs in `release/` of the run folder. Not a coding flow, so it runs in parallel with others.
  - **Recipe.** `version`, `tag`, `prerelease`, `keep`, `timeout_minutes`, `builds` and `assemble`, with placeholders `{commit_datetime}`, `{commit_date}`, `{sha}` and `{version}` in UTC. Each build has `name`, `on`, `arch`, `requires`, `run` and `artifacts`. Unknown keys and unsafe values are refused with a message that names the key.
  - **Builds.** One after the other, each in its own `git worktree` through `tools/test-slot`, with `timeout_minutes`. A build that does not fit the machine is skipped with a summary line. A missing `requires` command fails that build before anything runs.
  - **Environment.** Build and assemble commands get a short environment: `PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`, a fixed list of toolchain variables and the `FACTORY_RELEASE_*` variables. No GitHub token, no bot token, no provider key and no name that looks like a secret.
  - **Code.** New `src/release/recipe.ts`, `src/release/host.ts`, `src/release/build.ts`, `tools/release-build`. Changed `scripts/build-flows.mjs`, `tools/test-slot` (new `FACTORY_TEST_SLOT_STRICT=1`: exit 75 instead of running without a slot) and the flow lists in `tests/schema.test.ts` and `tests/repo-access.test.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/release-recipe.test.ts`, `tests/release-build.test.ts` and `tests/release-build-flow.test.ts` (a Node package, a static-site zip and a per-platform binary through the same flow).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Build release files on this machine").
- A public website, spaghetti-code-foundry.com, in `website/`.
  - **Pages.** Home (with FAQ), Features (with the screenshots), 21 how-to guides in three groups, the complete user guide split into 22 pages (one per chapter; the four longest parts of chapters 6 and 7 get their own page), and the flow format, design, security (threat model) and lessons-learned documents. Every GitHub anchor of the guide keeps working on the site.
  - **Built from the docs.** `node website/build.mjs` (or `npm run website`) renders `docs/` and `website/content/` into `website/dist/` with a small Markdown renderer (`website/lib/markdown.mjs`, no new dependency). It exits 1 on a broken internal link, a missing anchor or image, or invalid how-to front matter.
  - **SEO.** A title, description, canonical URL and Open Graph and Twitter tags on every page; structured data (WebSite, SoftwareApplication, FAQPage, HowTo with steps, TechArticle, BreadcrumbList); `sitemap.xml` with dates from git, `robots.txt`, `llms.txt`, a 404 page, image sizes against layout shift, no web fonts.
  - **Reading.** Light and dark themes, search over every page and section (`search-index.json`), "on this page" navigation, copy buttons, diagrams drawn from the guide's Mermaid blocks, and a layout for phones.
  - **Publishing.** `.github/workflows/website.yml` builds and deploys it with GitHub Pages on every push to `main` that changes the docs or the site; `website/static/CNAME` names the domain.
  - Tests: new `tests/website.test.ts`.
- Telemetry 1c (#155): an optional OTLP/HTTP logs exporter for lifecycle telemetry, off by default. Part 3 of 3 of #155, part of #133.
  - **Off by default.** Set `telemetry.otlp.enabled: true` and `telemetry.otlp.endpoint` in `config.yaml`. Nothing is sent otherwise. The local file (#584) is written as before, whether or not the exporter is on.
  - **Endpoint.** `http:` or `https:` only, with no user, password, query or fragment. Plain `http:` is allowed only for `localhost`, `127.x.x.x` and `[::1]`; any other host needs `https:`. `timeout_ms` (100 to 60000, default 5000) limits each request. There is no authentication: use a collector on the same machine or a network you trust.
  - **What is sent.** The same value-free events as the local file, as OTLP logs. Repository names and account ids (UUIDs) are sent as they are. Failed and denied events are marked WARN.
  - **Batches.** Up to 50 events per request, one request at a time, a 2 s timer for a short batch, a queue of 1000 (the oldest is dropped and counted). A request is never retried. A slow or failing collector never slows down or stops a run.
  - **Log lines.** One `! telemetry: the OTLP collector …` line when sending starts to fail, and one `· telemetry: the OTLP collector accepts events again` line on recovery.
  - **Flushed on exit.** The CLI, `watch` and `serve` (on Ctrl+C, SIGTERM and server close) send what is queued before they exit, for at most 3 s. A batch that cannot be sent in time is dropped and counted.
  - **Config and Settings.** New `telemetry.otlp` section in `config.yaml`; not in Settings. Known gap: `src/supervise.ts` restarts with a synchronous exit, so the last unsent batch can be lost there. Endpoint authentication is a follow-up.
  - **Code.** New `src/telemetry/otlp.ts` (`otlpLogsRequest`, `OtlpExporter`). Changed `src/config.ts` (`OtlpSchema`, `isOtlpEndpoint`), `src/telemetry/telemetry.ts` (exporter per config, `exporter` counters `exported` and `failed`, async `closeTelemetry(ms)`, `exitAfterTelemetry`), `src/cli.ts`, `src/server/server.ts` and `src/settings/exceptions.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/telemetry-otlp.test.ts` (request shape, disabled, failures, shutdown, overflow, batches, `fetch` options, no secret in the body), with `fetch` stubs and no fixed waits. Config cases in `tests/telemetry.test.ts`. Changed `tests/settings-inventory.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Lifecycle telemetry", the collector example), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Telemetry"), this entry.
- Telemetry 1a (#155): one strict, value-free lifecycle event per run, step, model, policy, tool, delivery, pull request and watcher fact, written to a local daily file (#584). Part 1 of 3 of #155, part of #133.
  - **What is written.** Labels, ids, numbers, times and outcomes only. The event has no free-text field and refuses unknown fields, so a prompt, an output, tool arguments or an error text cannot be stored. A label that holds a credential or a configured sensitive pattern is dropped and named in `redacted`. An event whose id or run id changes under the check is dropped.
  - **Where.** `<data folder>/telemetry/events-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`, one line per event, named by the UTC day. The folder is 0700 and the files 0600. Links and folders owned by someone else are refused. Files older than `telemetry.keep_days` are removed.
  - **Config.** New `telemetry` section in `config.yaml`: `local` (default `true`) and `keep_days` (1 to 365, default 30). Not in Settings.
  - **Evidence.** Each evidence event is mirrored with the same time. A stopped evidence stream still gives telemetry, without the `evidence` link. Telemetry never changes a run or its evidence.
  - **Drops.** An event that is invalid, private or cannot be written is dropped and counted. Each reason is logged once as `! telemetry: …`, in fixed words with no values.
  - **Code.** New `src/telemetry/envelope.ts` (`TelemetryEventSchema`, `telemetryFromEvidence`, `watcherRef`), `src/telemetry/privacy.ts` (`privateTelemetry`), `src/telemetry/local.ts` (the daily file, `sweepTelemetry`) and `src/telemetry/telemetry.ts` (`configureTelemetry`, `emitTelemetry`, `flushTelemetry`, `closeTelemetry`, `resetTelemetry`, `telemetryStats`). Changed `src/config.ts` (`telemetry`), `src/evidence/record.ts` (`repositoryOf` exported; `RunRecorder` takes the repository and mirrors each event), `src/engine/runner.ts` and `src/server/server.ts` (configure on load and reload), `src/settings/exceptions.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/telemetry-envelope.test.ts`, `tests/telemetry-privacy.test.ts`, `tests/telemetry.test.ts` (modes, links, sweep, defaults) and `tests/telemetry-run.test.ts` (event order, ids against `evidence.jsonl`, `local: false`, a stopped evidence stream). Changed `tests/settings-inventory.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Lifecycle telemetry"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Telemetry", data folder table), this entry.
- Evidence on the run page (#139). Part 6 of #133, after #138. The **Evidence** tab starts with an **Evidence record** section: the verification status (Verified, Not verified, Not finished or No record), then the result, the flow and its version, the commits, the approvals, the tests, the pull request and the files. A user sees a count of files per state and no paths; an administrator also sees the models, the recorded policy denials and every file path. A failed check names the event or file that does not verify, says what is wrong and gives the next step. **Export evidence** downloads `evidence-<run-id>.json`: the same answer as `GET /api/runs/:id/evidence`, so it holds no transcript content. The section works at 360 px and with the keyboard, and its status is text.
  - **Code.** New `ui/run-evidence.js` (`evidenceRecordModel`, `evidenceExport`, `evidenceRecordSection`). Changed `ui/api.js` (`evidence`), `ui/run-tabs.js` (`evidencePanel({ record })`), `ui/runs.js` and `ui/user/runs.js`. No server or permission change.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-run-evidence.test.ts` and `tests/browser/evidence.spec.ts` (360 px, keyboard and download, as an administrator and as a user); changed `tests/ui-run-tabs.test.ts` and `tests/ui-user-runs.test.ts`.
- Policy 2 — evaluate policy in observe mode at the lifecycle boundaries of a run (#142). Part of #133, after #141 and #135. Packs named in `config.yaml` are asked at every boundary of a run. Nothing stops: each decision is logged and recorded, and the run's result does not change.
  - **Config.** `policy.packs` in `config.yaml` lists policy ids (up to 20, no duplicates). A pack is read from `<data folder>/policies/<id>.json`, a plain file of at most 1 MiB. Only `mode: observe` is accepted; a pack in `enforce` mode is reported as `policy.invalid`. The setting is not in Settings. With no packs, nothing is read and nothing is logged.
  - **Boundaries.** `run.start` (before the workspace is made, also on an early refusal), `repository.access` (before a workspace is made, and before a step starts in the repository's checkout), `step.before` and `step.after` (each visit of a step, also the children of a parallel step and the steps of a sub-flow), `agent.write` (before a write-capable agent step), `push` (for each push a step makes), `approval.consume` (when an approval is used) and `run.complete` (only for a run that succeeded). The boundaries are asked in the engine, so built-in and custom flows both get them, and a flow cannot leave them out.
  - **Pushes.** With packs configured, the pre-push hook is installed even when no branch is protected. It writes each pushed branch to a file of the step (`FACTORY_PUSH_LOG`), and the engine reads it when the step ends. The hook never fails a push.
  - **Unavailable packs.** A missing, unreadable, linked, invalid or enforce-mode pack is recorded as a `policy.unavailable` or `policy.invalid` decision and logged as `! policy <id>: …`. The run goes on as it would without the pack.
  - **Record.** Every decision is a `policy.decided` evidence event (carries the decision, and the step when the boundary is about a step) and a live-log line `· policy <id> <version> · <boundary>: <outcome> (<reason>)`. When a decision cannot be recorded at all, the evidence is marked incomplete, as with any other evidence failure. The run goes on.
  - **Evidence version.** `EVIDENCE_EVENT_VERSION` is 2. A build from before this change cannot read a `policy.decided` line, so it refuses the evidence of such a run.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/policy-hooks.ts` (`RunPolicy`, loading of the packs, the pushes log). Changed `src/engine/runner.ts` (`run.start`, `repository.access`, `approval.consume`, `run.complete`, the policy passed to the engine), `src/engine/execute.ts` (`step.before`, `step.after`, `beginStep`, `pushLogFor`), `src/engine/guards.ts` (the pre-push hook logs pushes; `protectedBranchEnv` takes `observe`), `src/agents/run.ts` (`agent.write`, the repository access of an agent step), `src/evidence/record.ts` (`policyDecided`, `incomplete`), `src/evidence/schema.ts`, `src/evidence/version.ts`, `src/evidence/parse.ts`, `src/config.ts` (`policy.packs`) and `src/settings/exceptions.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/policy-hooks.test.ts` (loading, facts, the `RunPolicy` contract, the config, the event), `tests/policy-hooks-run.test.ts` (one case per boundary, with a real run; the order of events; a built-in flow; a run whose decisions are all `deny` ends as it would without packs), and the test pack `tests/fixtures/policy-pack.ts`. Changed `tests/evidence-schema.test.ts`, `tests/evidence-chain.test.ts`, `tests/evidence-stream.test.ts` and `tests/settings-inventory.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Policy hooks in observe mode", "Evidence formats"), `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Policy packs in observe mode", after "Evidence of a run"), this entry.
- Codex runs are isolated from your personal Codex setup and from undeclared integrations (#147, part 2 of #133). Every Codex step of a run the server or a watcher starts gets a clean per-run Codex folder (`<run folder>/home/.codex`, made 0700) with no `config.toml`, MCP servers, plugins, hooks, skills, `AGENTS.md`, `.env`, prompts or command rules of the person. Session state stays, so a resumed session still works. Local CLI runs follow `isolate_agents` as before.
  - **Sign-in.** With a Codex login (no `CODEX_API_KEY`, not a local model), only the `auth.json` file is copied into the clean folder for one session and removed afterwards. A token that Codex refreshed is written back to your personal file only when that file is unchanged. The record of the loan is kept by the server in `<FACTORY_HOME>/codex-logins/`, outside the run folder. A login kept in the keychain, a linked file or a missing login refuses the step. A copy that cannot be returned stops the run until it is returned.
  - **MCP servers.** A clean session starts with no MCP server (`mcp_servers={}`), so no personal or undeclared server is reachable. Codex does not yet get servers from the ToolProfile: the profile's MCP grant is not used for Codex.
  - **Push.** A Foundry command rule forbids `git push` in every clean session, and Codex's own sandbox has no network (`sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=false`). Pushing is done by the flow only. A clean session with `bypassPermissions` and no sandbox is refused, because it could push.
  - **Code.** New `src/agents/codex-login.ts`. Changed `src/agents/codex-home.ts` (`cleanCodexHome`, `prepareCleanCodex`, `codexMustIsolate`, `codexPushAllowed`), `src/agents/run.ts`, `src/engine/isolation.ts` (`noMachineSignIn`), `src/engine/runner.ts` (a lent login left by an interrupted run is returned first), `src/steps/codex.ts` (`clean`, `noNetwork`).
  - **Tests.** Changed `tests/codex-home.test.ts`, `tests/codex-isolation-flow.test.ts`, `tests/agents.test.ts`, `tests/agent-sandbox.test.ts`, `tests/os-sandbox-agent-run.test.ts`, `tests/skill-codex-flow.test.ts`, `tests/setup.ts` and `tests/fixtures/fake-codex.mjs`. The isolation flow test writes a fake personal `config.toml` with an MCP server named `canary` and checks that it never reaches the session.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Codex and your personal setup"), this entry.
  - Not checked against a real Codex CLI: the rules syntax, which files carry configuration and the network setting. Check them once before release.
- TypeScript and Node.js skill suite, effect part (#578, part 2 of #574). `evals/skill-gate/typescript-node/suite.yaml` gets an `instructions` section (`repeat: 4`, not mandatory) with five sample tasks for `scf skills effect`: `parse-stock-event`, `config-validation`, `graceful-shutdown` and `report-stream-errors` on `fixtures/ts-service`, and `await-notifications` on `fixtures/js-package`. Each task has an offline check (a `node --input-type=module` one-liner, exit 2 on a self-timeout and 1 on a failure) and `network: false`. The fixtures gain `src/stock.ts`, `src/shutdown.ts` and `src/report.ts`, and `test/service.test.ts` gets tests for `applyEvent`, `formatRow` and the report happy path. Tests in `tests/skill-suite-typescript-node.test.ts` run each check on the unchanged fixture, on a reference solution and on a typical wrong one. The cost is 5 × 4 × 2 = 40 sessions. The checks need Node 22.18 or later. No code under `src/` changes.
- Java and Spring skill suite, part 2 of 2 of #573 (#580): effect scenarios for `scf skills effect`. `evals/skill-gate/java-spring/suite.yaml` gets an `instructions` section (`mandatory: false`, `repeat: 4`) with four scenarios: `cancel-rules` and `optimistic-locking` on `fixtures/orders-service`, and `single-load` and `log-load-failure` on the new `fixtures/cache-library`, a plain Java library built with Gradle (`java-library`, Java 17 toolchain) and tested by its own `main` class. Each check is an offline behaviour test run with `sh -c` and the JDK; `orders-service` gets a stand-in `org/springframework/stereotype/Service.java` and `jakarta/persistence/Version.java`. The suite still reads as `ok`. A repository test (`tests/skill-effect-java-spring.test.ts`) runs without a model: every check fails on the unchanged fixtures, also when the keywords are only in comments, and passes on a reference solution; it is skipped with a warning when no JDK is found. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("The Java and Spring effect scenarios") lists the scenarios, the cost (scenarios × `repeat` × 2) and the JDK requirement. No code under `src/` changes.
- Engineering-practice skill suite, testing area (#575, part of the engineering-practice suites). New `evals/skill-gate/engineering-practice/suite.yaml` (schema 1) with `selection`, `conflicts`, `context` and `tools` (the same limits as `evals/skill-gate/suite.yaml`), and an `instructions` section (`mandatory: false`, `repeat: 4`) with five scenarios for `scf skills effect`: fix a repeated-product bug and add a regression test, add unit tests for a rounding helper, test-drive a planned shipping rule, fix a stock bug with a regression check, and add unit checks for a reorder rule. Each scenario has `policy: { network: false }` and an offline check. The check of a test-quality scenario plants known faults in the source one at a time; the checks of the two bug fixes put the original bug back. The submitted tests must fail on each of them.
  - **Scope.** The suite covers the testing practice only: test writing, test-first development and regression tests for a bug. A skill is offered when its id or capability is `testing`, or when the task names it; the two bug-fix scenarios also name `debugging`, so a skill with that capability is offered in those two. Planning, spec-driven work, debugging, code review, simplification, documentation and ADRs, git workflow, security hardening and verification each need their own suite. Until then, skills of those areas stay unscored by this suite.
  - **Selection.** Nine cases: six offered (a Node.js and a Java task each for regression testing, test-first testing and unit testing) and three not offered (rename a package, bump a version, add a licence).
  - **Fixtures.** `evals/skill-gate/engineering-practice/fixtures/js-cart` (a Node.js ESM package, tested with `node --test`) and `fixtures/java-stock` (a Java 17 library with plain `*Check` classes, no JUnit). Each has a known bug, an untested part and a planned feature noted in a comment. No network, no credentials, no symbolic links, no `.claude/` or `.mcp.json`. Each fixture's own tests pass on a clean checkout. The Java scenarios need `javac` and `java`.
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-suite-engineering-practice.test.ts` and `tests/skill-suite-engineering-practice-java.test.ts` (skipped without `javac`), with shared helpers in `tests/engineering-practice-helpers.ts`. They check the suite shape, the selection scores, the fixture hygiene and, for each scenario, that the check fails on the unchanged fixture and on wrong solutions and passes on a reference solution. No model is called. No code under `src/` changes.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the suite, under "Score and gate an import"), this entry.
- TypeScript and Node.js skill suite, gate part (#577, part 1 of #574). New `evals/skill-gate/typescript-node/suite.yaml` (schema 1) with `selection`, `conflicts`, `context` and `tools`, so `scf skills score` and `scf skills gate` can score TypeScript and Node.js skills. Its eight selection cases offer a skill for the TypeScript service and the JavaScript package and not for the Java library. No `instructions` section yet (part 2). Three fixtures under `evals/skill-gate/typescript-node/fixtures/`: `ts-service` (Node 22.18 or later, type stripping), `js-package` and `java-library` (checked with `javac` and `java`). Tests in `tests/skill-suite-typescript-node.test.ts`. No code under `src/` changes.
- Java and Spring skill suite, part 1 of 2 of #573 (#579): `evals/skill-gate/java-spring/suite.yaml` scores Java and Spring Boot skills with `scf skills score` and `scf skills gate`. Nine selection cases: seven are offered on the new `fixtures/orders-service` (a small Spring Boot service with a JPA repository, tests and a Maven `pom.xml`), two are not offered on `fixtures/web-tool` (a Node.js package). The fixtures have no network access, credentials or links. A repository test checks the suite and the fixtures without calling a model, and compiles and runs `orders-service` with the JDK when one is installed. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("The Java and Spring Boot suite"). No code change.
- Skill catalogue description for an imported skill (#576). An entry in `skill-selections.yaml` can set `catalogue_description`: a short description of 1–160 characters for one reviewed skill, used in the catalogue instead of the package's own text when the skill is in the catalogue. The upstream package, its digest and its provenance do not change.
  - **Where it applies.** The override is tied to the skill's `digest`; an upstream update drops it until the entry is reviewed again. It reaches runs only through a published release: `release publish` writes it as `catalogueDescription` (`source: skill-selections`) into the release entry, and the release digest covers it. Releases without the field are unchanged.
  - **Checks.** `scf skills selections check` prints a `PROBLEM` for an override whose source is not enabled with an approved commit, or whose skill is not staged with that digest, and refuses an empty, over-long, control-character or credential-like text. The entry is refused by the same rules when the file is read.
  - **Score and gate.** `score` and `gate` judge `context` on the override, and `selection` for the candidate and its imported peers. The record stores the digest of the scored text; `gate` and `release publish` fail with `description-changed` when the override was edited after scoring.
  - **Display.** The catalogue shows the override and says where it came from. `scf skills` prints a `DESCRIPTION <id>@<version> skill-selections <release>: <text>` line for each shown override; the run record keeps up to 20 of them and "Why these skills" lists them as plain text.
  - **Compatibility.** An older Foundry refuses a release that carries `catalogueDescription`, because its manifest schema is strict. An invalid `skill-selections.yaml` now also stops `score` and `gate` with a `PROBLEM`; before, they never read the file.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/catalogue-description.ts`. Changed `src/skills/selections.ts`, `src/skills/cli.ts`, `src/skills/evaluate-cli.ts`, `src/skills/evaluation-score.ts`, `src/skills/release.ts`, `src/skills/release-publish.ts`, `src/skills/registry.ts`, `src/skills/catalogue.ts`, `src/skills/run-catalogue.ts`, `src/server/skill-selection-view.ts`, `src/engine/state.ts`, `ui/run-skills.js`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-catalogue-description.test.ts`. Changed `tests/skill-score-cli.test.ts`, `tests/skill-evaluation-score.test.ts`, `tests/skill-release-publish-cli.test.ts`, `tests/skill-release.test.ts`, `tests/skill-registry-release.test.ts`, `tests/ui-run-skills.test.ts` and `tests/helpers/skill-release-v2.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Skill selections", "Score and gate an import", "Skill catalogue").
- `skill-sources.yaml`: three more sources enabled after the owner's review on 2026-10-17 (community, MIT): `superpowers` (`obra/superpowers` at `bb92a777`, 8 paths), `mattpocock-skills` (`mattpocock/skills` at `49dd158d`, 9 paths) and `claude-code-java` (`decebals/claude-code-java` at `0d98fe9b`, 15 paths). Skills the checks refuse stay unpromoted; the source entries only allow the import.
- AI help actions 3b: in the Ask SCF drawer you review, confirm or cancel a suggested change, and see its result (#568). Part 2 of 2 of #240, after #567. Behind `help.actions`, which is **off by default**. Nothing happens until you review the change and confirm it.
  - **Review.** A card that the server can carry out has **Review the change**. The dialog shows the server's preview in six rows: action, now, what changes, impact, undo and who may do it. A high-impact change has a red confirm button. Cancel, Escape and the cross send the cancel call, so the token is dropped at once.
  - **Result.** After a confirm, the drawer draws the returned result with its word (**Done**, **Queued**, **Partly done**, **Not done**) and what to do next. The result is shown at once and read back with the conversation; when it was not stored, it still shows until you leave the conversation. A reply that became stale is ignored, and an error (403, 404, 409, 410) shows in the drawer's error line.
  - **Prompt.** When `help.actions` is on, the prompt lists the role's action ids (`resume-run`, `cancel-run`), says how to bind them (`source` and `evidence` name the run's `state` source) and says that an action runs only after the person confirms. The `changes-state` limit line says to propose the action instead. With it off, the prompt is unchanged.
  - **API.** `ui/api.js` has `prepareHelpAction`, `confirmHelpAction` and `cancelHelpAction`, all through `req` (CSRF and the preview rules are unchanged). No new route and no permission row.
  - **Code.** Changed `src/help/turn.ts` (`turnPrompt` takes `{ actions }`), `src/server/help-evals.ts`, `ui/api.js`, `ui/ask.js` (`reviewAction`, an injectable `review`), `ui/ask-model.js` (`previewRows`, `previewDanger`, `outcomeOf`, `outcomesFor`, `explainActionError`) and `ui/ask-view.js` (`reviewDialog`, `outcomeNodes`). No CSS change.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-api-help.test.ts` (the real adapter with a stubbed `fetch`: method, URL, body and CSRF header), `tests/ui-ask-review-model.test.ts` and `tests/ui-ask-review.test.ts` (review then cancel, confirm, a stale reply, a 409). Changed `tests/help-turn.test.ts`, `tests/help-evals.test.ts` and `tests/help-evidence.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (chapter 14: the card, "Reviewing a change in the drawer", the result words), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Ask SCF: confirmed actions"), this entry.
- AI help actions 3a-2: Ask SCF can carry out an approved resume or cancel of one of your runs, through the API (#570). Part 2 of 2 of #567, part of #240. Behind `help.actions`, which is **off by default**. Nothing changes without a fresh, single-use confirmation, and the result is read back from the run. The drawer's confirm button comes with #568.
  - **Routes.** `POST /api/help/conversations/:id/actions` with `{ message, index }` answers `{ token, expiresAt, preview }`. The preview is the server's own words: the action, the run, what is now, what changes, the impact and its level, whether and how it can be undone, and who may do it. Nothing changes except an in-memory grant. `…/actions/confirm` with `{ token, message, index }` answers `{ outcome, stored }`. `…/actions/cancel` with `{ token }` answers `{ cancelled }`; a later confirm with that token answers 409. Each route has a rule in the permissions table.
  - **Tokens.** A token is kept only as a sha256 hash. It works once, for 5 minutes, and only for the account, conversation, reply and place it was made for. A token used with another index, conversation or account is burned. At most 5 tokens per account and 1000 in all; a new preview replaces the older one for the same action. Tokens are in memory, so a restart ends them.
  - **Re-checks.** A confirm is refused, with nothing changed, when the token is unknown, used, expired or does not fit; when the run changed since the preview (409); when Ask SCF, `help.actions` or the account no longer allows it (503 for a switch that is off, 403 for an account that may not); when the rule of the same button on the run's page refuses (your own runs only, an admin too, as for the run's page); or when the action no longer applies. A resume first waits for the same gate as the run's page. Each refusal is audited.
  - **State version.** A hash of the run's status, history, answers, next step, waiting step, resumes and finish time, and of its place in the queue (queued, active, and the identity of the queued job). A job that was taken out and queued again counts as a change. The scheduler has a new read-only `queuedJob(runId)`.
  - **Results.** Decided from the fresh state, never from a promise that settled. A resume is `done` (resumed; read back), `queued`, `start-failed`, `missing` or `unconfirmed`. A cancel is `dequeued` (the queued job was taken out), `stopped` (the run is marked cancelled), `stopping` (still active after 5 seconds), `not-done` (the run ended on its own first, or was not cancelled) or `missing`; a run that was neither queued nor active is `done` or `not-done` by its status. Each result has fixed words and a recovery line, and `seen` is the status the server saw.
  - **After the action.** Once the re-checks have passed, a failure while the action runs or is read back (a queue write, a rejected wait) does not give a 500. The token stays used up, the server reads the state again, and the answer is the result that state gives, or `unconfirmed` when nothing can be read.
  - **Outcomes.** A finished answer keeps its newest 10 outcomes (`outcomes`, a strict schema in `src/help/outcome.ts`). `stored` is `false` when the conversation was deleted meanwhile, or when the outcome or the audit line could not be written.
  - **Audit.** A `help-action` line for every confirm that ran (`<action>; run <id>; <result>`) and for every confirm that was refused (`refused: <code>`). A line holds no token and no model text. Preview and cancel write no line.
  - **Downgrade.** An older version cannot read a `help.json` that holds an outcome (its schema is strict). Keep this build until the conversations with outcomes are no longer needed, or take `help.actions` out of `config.yaml` and do not use the feature.
  - **Code.** New `src/help/grants.ts`, `src/help/outcome.ts` and `src/server/help-actions.ts`. Changed `src/server/api-runs.ts` (the resume route is split into `jobMetaFor`, `checkResume`, `startResume`, with `cancelRun`; its answers do not change), `src/queue/scheduler.ts` (`queuedJob`), `src/help/store.ts` (`addOutcome`, `outcomes`), `src/server/api-help.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts`, `src/auth/audit.ts`, `ui/audit.js` and `src/remote/areas/index.ts` (the three routes are API-only).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/help-grants.test.ts`, `tests/help-actions.test.ts`, `tests/help-actions-run.test.ts` (with the real scheduler), `tests/help-actions-api.test.ts` (HTTP with `FAKE_HELP`) and `tests/helpers/help-actions.ts`; changed `tests/help-store.test.ts`, `tests/help-governance.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts` and `tests/ui-audit.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Ask SCF: confirmed actions"), `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (chapter 14, the permission rows, the `help-action` audit row), this entry.
- AI help actions 4: Hand off from Ask SCF to existing pages (#241). Part of #226.
  - **Targets.** Under a finished answer, **Continue on another page** offers the pages the answer can be taken to. Everyone gets **Run details** (for a run the answer is about) and **Refinement** (a new session from the answer, and one per draft). Administrators also get **Problems** (for a finding) and **Your turn on Home** (for a status answer or a next step).
  - **Preview.** Before anything happens, the drawer shows what is copied: the title, the text, the repository (only one the caller still owns) and a **Link back** to the conversation, which only you can see. Navigation copies nothing. Cancel changes nothing.
  - **Record.** A confirmed hand-off is kept in the conversation (where it went, the page and the kinds of thing copied, never the copy), up to the last 50. The target is never changed by the hand-off. A refinement session is created only when you press its own **Start session**.
  - **Checked again.** The server works out the offers again for the account at confirm. The offer, its page and its copy must be the ones shown, or the answer is 409 and nothing is written. Every source of the answer is checked again before its line is copied.
  - **`fromHelp` on refinement sessions.** A session started from an answer keeps the id of your conversation (owner only, never in the text). Its owner sees **Open the conversation**. Administrators and "View as user" never see it.
  - **Routes.** `GET` and `POST /api/help/conversations/:id/handoffs` (API only). `POST /api/refinement` accepts `fromHelp` (400 for a value that is not a UUID; another account's or a deleted conversation is left out silently).
  - **Code.** New `src/help/handoff.ts` and `src/server/help-handoff.ts`, `ui/ask-handoff.js`. Changed `src/help/store.ts` (`addHandoff`, the `handoffs` field), `src/refinement/store.ts` (`fromHelp`), `src/server/api-help.ts`, `src/server/api-refinement.ts` (`githubNames` exported, `view()` hides `fromHelp` in View as user), `src/server/permissions.ts`, `src/remote/areas/index.ts` (the new routes are API-only), `ui/api.js`, `ui/ask.js`, `ui/ask-view.js`, `ui/refinement.js` and `ui/refinement-new.js`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/help-handoff.test.ts`, `tests/help-handoff-api.test.ts` and `tests/ui-ask-handoff.test.ts`; changed `tests/help-store.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/ui-ask.test.ts` and `tests/ui-refinement.test.ts`.
  - **Downgrade.** `help.json` may hold a `handoffs` field and refinement sessions may hold `fromHelp`. An older build refuses such files (its schemas are strict).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Take an answer to another page" in chapter 14, the "What it does not do" table, the permission rows), this entry.
- AI help actions 2b: read-only checks in Ask SCF, with their result stored in the conversation (#566). Part 2 of 2 of #239, part of #240 and #241. Two offers run a check on your click: **Read it again** (`refresh-evidence`, every kind of subject) and **Test the connection** (`test-repository`, repositories). Neither needs a new permission, and neither runs during a turn or asks for a confirmation.
  - **Checks.** A check reads through the same snapshot as the answers (`helpSnapshot`), or calls the existing repository test and then reads the repository again. Its result is a `check` message in the conversation, written by the server: a title, an outcome (`read`, `passed` or `failed`) and at most 30 lines of at most 300 characters each (the marker ` …` included). The title is at most 500 characters. Values that are too long are cut, and the check is checked again before it is stored. A check of an administrator's view is marked with that role.
  - **Later answers.** The two newest checks of a conversation are given to the next answer as sources, but only about things your account can see now. A check older than 15 minutes is marked as possibly out of date.
  - **Refusals.** Before any remote call, a check answers 409 with a fixed sentence when the conversation is not open, a reply is still pending, the conversation is full (100 messages) or another check runs in it. If the conversation changes while the test waits (a question, an archive, a delete), the test still ran, so the audit line says `failed` with `not stored: <reason>`, and the answer is 409 (or 410 for a deleted conversation). Nothing is restored. 404 and 403 use the sentences of the offers.
  - **Authorization.** A check is refused, with nothing contacted, for another account's repository, for a repository whose GitHub App allow-list does not hold it, for a user testing a repository whose method is `none` (409), for a user who was lowered from administrator, for another account's run, repository, queue entry or refinement, and for a user who asks about an `issue`.
  - **Audit.** `help-action` gets the detail `<type>; <outcome>` (for example `test-repository; passed`).
  - **Data.** `help.json` gets the optional `check` message (version 1 is unchanged; older files still load). An older build cannot read a conversation that holds a check.
  - **Code.** Changed `src/help/actions.ts` (`READ_CALLS`, `CheckSchema`, `checkText`, `checkSources`), `src/help/store.ts` (`addCheck`, the `check` role), `src/help/turn.ts`, `src/server/help-evidence.ts` (`runOffer` with an optional test function), `src/server/api-help.ts`, `src/server/api-repos.ts` (exports `testRepo`), `src/server/permissions.ts`, `src/remote/areas/help.ts`, `src/remote/areas/index.ts`, `ui/ask.js`, `ui/ask-model.js` and `ui/ask-view.js`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/help-checks-api.test.ts`; changed `tests/help-actions.test.ts`, `tests/help-store.test.ts`, `tests/help-turn.test.ts`, `tests/help-contract.test.ts`, `tests/help-offers-api.test.ts`, `tests/ui-ask-model.test.ts` and `tests/ui-ask.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("What a reply can hold", "Conversations", the permission row), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Ask SCF: checks"), this entry.
- AI help actions 3a-1: Ask SCF binds a proposed resume or cancel of a run to the server's evidence (#569). Part 1 of 2 of #567, part of #240. Behind `help.actions`, which is **off by default** and is set by an administrator. Nothing is carried out yet: the reply only proposes the action.
  - **Actions.** `resume-run` (reversible, undoable) and `cancel-run` (high impact, not undoable). Both are for the asker's own runs, for administrators and users alike; an administrator gets no exception. Each has a fixed effect text written by the server. A kept action shows that text, never the model's.
  - **Checked by the server.** `checkReply` keeps an action only when its id is on the role's list, its source is a cited, offered state source that is not uncertain, the server tied that source to a run, and the impact text claims no result (for example "The run was resumed."). Anything else is dropped with one of three fixed words. Duplicates are kept once.
  - **Binding.** The server sets the run a source is about only when the run is fresh, the asker owns it and no conflict source is present. The model never names a run. A run of another account is never bound, not even for an administrator.
  - **Switch.** `help.actions` (boolean, default `false`) is in `config.yaml` and on the Ask SCF card of Settings. With it off, every proposed action is dropped. The prompt still says `"actions": []`, so the model is not asked to offer any yet.
  - **Downgrade.** An older version refuses `help.actions` in `config.yaml`, and it cannot read a `help.json` that holds a bound action. Take the setting out of `config.yaml` before you go back.
  - **Evals.** `scf eval help` re-checks each kept action from outside (`actionProblems`): an action not on the role's list, a source that was not offered or is uncertain, a run that differs from the one the source is about, an impact text that is not the server's, an `outcomes` key, or a plan step with an action not on the list. Each problem is an `unsafe-action` failure that blocks a release. Two new scenarios in the `action` group, `action-bound` and `action-false-execution`.
  - **Code.** Changed `src/help/contract.ts` (`ACTIONS`, `AboutSchema`, `checkReply`), `src/help/turn.ts` (`Source.about`, `Prepared.targets`), `src/server/help-evidence.ts` (`sourceOf`, `stateSources`), `src/config.ts`, `src/settings/describe.ts`, `src/server/help-evals.ts` and `src/server/help-eval-scenarios.ts`.
  - **Tests.** Changed `tests/help-contract.test.ts`, `tests/help-turn.test.ts`, `tests/help-evidence.test.ts`, `tests/help-evals.test.ts`, `tests/settings-describe.test.ts` and `tests/ui-admin-sections.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Ask SCF: the contract", and "Ask SCF: confirmed actions"), `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the `help.actions` setting under "Ask SCF for administrators"), this entry.
- AI help actions 2a: safe navigation offers in Ask SCF answers. A reply may carry up to 3 `offers`: buttons that open a run, a repository or your item under Your turn on Home. The address always comes from the server (`subjectPage`, always `#/…`, the id encoded); model text never becomes an address. The button asks `POST /api/help/conversations/:id/actions` with `{message, offer}`, which checks the role, the conversation, the message and the offer, then checks again the permission rule of the underlying call, and answers `{type, to}`. Nothing is stored, so it works in every state except deleted. Refusals: 404 when the thing is gone or no longer visible, 403 when it is not allowed now, 410 for a deleted conversation. Each click writes a `help-action` audit line. Part 1 of 2 of #239 (#565).
  - **Registry.** New `src/help/actions.ts`: `OFFERS` lists `open-run` (both roles), `open-repository` (both roles) and `open-turn-item` (admins; opens Home only while the run or issue is on it, so a dismissed item counts as gone and a merged release item counts). Each row has its roles, its subject kinds, its effect (`navigation`) and the call checked at the click, so read-only checks can be added in part 2.
  - **Checked reply.** `checkReply` keeps an offer only when its type is in the registry for the role, its evidence is a state source offered in that turn, and the subject kind fits. Other fields the model wrote are dropped; the title is the server's own. Replies stored before this change still parse.
  - **Subjects.** `sourceOf` gives each state source a `subject` (kind and id), which `prepareTurn` keeps out of the model's view and in `Prepared.targets`. `LINKS` moved into `subjectPage`. `SUBJECT_KINDS` equals `SNAPSHOT_KINDS` (tested).
  - **Your turn.** A grouped release item keeps its server-only `members` (never in the public data).
  - **Drawer.** Offers are cards with an Open button. A second click while one is running sends nothing. A page the browser does not accept shows "This page cannot be opened here."
  - **Code.** New `src/help/actions.ts`; changed `src/help/contract.ts`, `src/help/turn.ts`, `src/server/help-evidence.ts`, `src/server/api-help.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts`, `src/your-turn.ts`, `src/auth/audit.ts`, `src/remote/areas/index.ts` (the route is API-only), `ui/api.js`, `ui/ask.js`, `ui/ask-model.js`, `ui/ask-view.js` and `ui/audit.js`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/help-actions.test.ts` and `tests/help-offers-api.test.ts`; changed the contract, turn, evidence, Your turn, permissions, audit and UI tests.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the offers in "What a reply can hold", the audit row, the permission table), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Ask SCF: offers"), this entry.
- AI help actions 1: Ask SCF can give a step-by-step plan (#238). Part of #226. A reply may now carry a `plan` of at most 8 ordered steps, and each step is one of six kinds: explanation, open a page, read-only check, your input, reversible change or high-impact change. Every change step must say what you should see, what a failure looks like and what to do then. Steps are kept safest first, and a check stays next to the high-impact step it belongs to.
  - **Checked by the server.** `checkPlan` keeps only what the rules allow. A cited source must have been offered, a page must be one the role may open, and an action must be on the server's list. A step whose title or instructions say that something already worked is dropped. A destructive word (delete, remove, reset, overwrite, force, close, cancel and similar) raises a step to high-impact; the server can raise a step's level but never lower it.
  - **Demoted steps.** A change that the evidence does not support becomes a "Check first. Change nothing yet." step with fixed text and no action; the model's title is kept as `proposal`. A high-impact step stays only with a confirmed reply and a diagnosis the server did not doubt.
  - **Contract.** `plan` is a new, optional part of a reply. `ActionDef` has a required `impact` (`read-only`, `reversible` or `high`); a registered action must set it. A reply without a plan is stored and shown as before. The prompt describes the plan and tells the model never to say that a step worked.
  - **Drawer.** Each step is a card with its kind. A high-impact step always shows a danger pill. "I did this" is kept in the browser tab only and says "Ask SCF has not checked the result." Nothing is stored on the reply, and no step shows a result before a verified read-back (#239 and #240).
  - **Evals.** `scf eval help` re-checks plans from the outside and counts a non-empty plan as useful. Four new `plan` scenarios.
  - **Code.** New `src/help/plan.ts`; changed `src/help/contract.ts`, `src/help/turn.ts`, `src/server/help-evals.ts`, `src/server/help-eval-scenarios.ts`, `ui/ask.js`, `ui/ask-model.js`, `ui/ask-view.js` and `ui/css/pages/ask.css`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/help-plan.test.ts`; changed the contract, turn, store, API, eval, UI model and UI drawer tests, and the CSS baseline.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the Plan row in "What a reply can hold"), this entry.
- Ask SCF quality 2: feedback, quality numbers, a pause and a pilot stage (#245). Part of #226.
  - **Feedback.** Under each finished answer: **Helpful** or **Not helpful**. Not helpful offers six fixed reasons (no free text), and the reasons are saved with **Save reasons**. `POST /api/help/conversations/:id/feedback` (`scf ask feedback <id> <message> helpful|not-helpful|none`, `--reasons '["incorrect"]'`); `none` takes the vote back. It answers 404 for another account's conversation (checked before the body), 410 for a deleted one, 409 for a message that is not a finished reply, and 400 for a bad body. It works while Ask SCF is paused. The vote and reasons are stored on the reply in `help.json`. Nothing is sent to a model, and a vote gives no consent to use conversations for training.
  - **Resolved.** Only "This solved my problem" counts as resolved: it closes the conversation with `resolved: true` (`scf ask complete --resolved`), once, and only for an active conversation with a finished answer. Closing without saying so counts nothing.
  - **Quality numbers.** New `help-stats.json` in the data folder: one row per UTC day with counts (turns, retries, answers, sources offered and cited, limited answers, stopped, abandoned, resolved, helpful and not helpful with reasons, failures and refusals by code) and latency buckets (under 5, 15, 30, 60, 120 seconds, and longer). It holds no account, conversation, question or answer, and a strict schema refuses anything else. Rows are kept 90 days. A failed write is logged in fixed words and never fails a turn or a request. The action counters (`actionsRun`, `actionsFailed`) are kept for #239 and #240 and stay at 0 until then.
  - **Usage.** `GET /api/help/usage` (`scf ask usage`) adds `rollout` (the stage, whether it is paused, the audience, the number of pilot and disabled accounts) and `quality` (the last 7 days summed, and the daily rows). Administrators only.
  - **Pause.** `help.paused: true` (the **Pause Ask SCF** setting; default `false`) is the emergency stop. New questions, retries and page-context checks answer 503 with the paused sentence, and a question that waits in the queue fails as "not set up" without a model call. Reading, export, delete, archive, restore, complete and feedback keep working, and the Ask SCF button stays. `enabled: false` is still "never set up": no button. Refusals are counted as `paused`.
  - **Pilot stage.** `help.audience: pilot` lets administrators and the accounts in `help.pilot_accounts` (account ids, at most 1,000; `config.yaml` only) ask. `disabled_accounts` still wins. The stages are off, paused, administrators, pilot and everyone. Old settings read as before.
  - **Restarts.** A turn that a restart ended is counted once, as `interrupted`, at the first start after it.
  - **Drawer.** The thread redraws without losing focus, unsaved reason ticks stay, and a paused notice disables **Send** and **Try again**. A 503 counts as paused only with the exact paused sentence; Send checks again with the server, so unpausing needs no reload.
  - **Downgrade.** An older build refuses `help.json` once any message carries feedback (its schema is strict), and refuses the `help` keys `paused` and `pilot_accounts` and `audience: pilot`. Before you go back, remove those from `config.yaml`; a conversation with feedback needs a build with this change to be read.
  - **Code.** New `src/help/telemetry.ts`. Changed `src/config.ts`, `src/help/store.ts`, `src/help/turn.ts`, `src/server/api-help.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts`, `src/remote/areas/help.ts`, `src/settings/describe.ts`, `src/settings/exceptions.ts`, `ui/api.js`, `ui/ask.js`, `ui/ask-model.js`, `ui/ask-view.js`, `ui/css/pages/ask.css`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/help-telemetry.test.ts` and `tests/help-rollout.test.ts`. Changed `tests/help-store.test.ts`, `tests/help-turn.test.ts`, `tests/help-governance.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/settings-describe.test.ts`, `tests/settings-inventory.test.ts`, `tests/ui-admin-sections.test.ts`, `tests/ui-ask.test.ts`, `tests/ui-ask-model.test.ts` and `tests/fixtures/ui-style-baseline.css`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (chapter 14: "What Ask SCF knows", "Rate an answer", the pause and pilot settings, "Rolling out Ask SCF"; the permission rows; the `scf ask` line of the command table) and `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Ask SCF: feedback and quality numbers").
- Skill deduplication 5d: explain on the run page why a skill was loaded.
