The Skills card of a run has a **Why these skills** list: the winner of each exclusive capability and why it won, its companions, up to 5 rejected skills with their reason, the copies collapsed into one, and the project pins and owner overrides applied. Only the skills a run involves are named, never the catalogue. Lists are capped at 20 capabilities and 20 groups of copies. Admins also see digests, who set a pin or override, and the pins commit; users do not. The planner prompt is unchanged, and no route or permission is added. Older runs show the card as before. Part 4 of 4 of #212 (#564).
  - **Code.** Added `src/server/skill-selection-view.ts`. Changed `src/server/skill-view.ts` (the optional `selection` field of `RunSkillView`) and `ui/run-skills.js`.
  - **Tests.** Changed `tests/skill-view.test.ts` and `tests/ui-run-skills.test.ts`; added `tests/skill-selection-view.test.ts`.
- Skill deduplication 5c: complementary providers. A skill can list in `skill.yaml` the exclusive capabilities it may be loaded with another provider for (`composable`, at most 32 slugs, default `[]`, each one also in `capabilities`). A provider ranked after the winner is a companion only when the winner and the provider both list the capability as composable. Companions load after the winner, in rank order, each with its own `foundry-skill` attribution, and only while the budget allows. A companion that does not fit is `over-budget` (resolver) or `omitted` (payload) and never displaces the winner. A provider without `composable` is never loaded with the winner, and a provider that loses another exclusive capability stays out. The run record lists `companions` per capability. Built-in skills are unchanged. **Older Foundry versions refuse a `skill.yaml` with `composable`** (the schema is strict), so upgrade them before you publish such a skill. Part 3 of 4 of #212 (#563).
  - **Code.** Changed `src/skills/schema.ts`, `normalize.ts`, `normalize-rules.ts`, `package.ts`, `collisions.ts`, `mirrors.ts`, `canonical.ts`, `canonical-facts.ts`, `resolve.ts`, `run-canonical.ts` and `run-catalogue.ts`.
- Skill deduplication 5b: exclusive capabilities. A capability listed in `skills.selection.exclusive` (at most 200 slugs, default `[]`) has one winner in a run. The run offers, locks and loads only the canonical provider; a requested loser is left out, and a tie or a loser the run needs stops the step instead of loading both. With `[]` runs behave as before. Part 2 of 4 of #212 (#562).
  - **Stops.** A live step stops with `skills not resolved:` (the catalogue) and the lock with `skill selection is blocked:`, before the agent starts. Owner overrides come from `skill-overrides.yaml` in the data folder; project pins from `.claude-factory/skill-pins.yaml` on the default branch. An invalid or expired choice stops the step too.
  - **Lock.** `skill lock: <id> left out: not-canonical` is logged for each dropped request. Two providers of an exclusive capability are never in one lock. The decision is saved in `run.json` as `summary.skillSelection` (the winner, `decidedBy`, up to 5 rejected skills with their codes and levels, and a count of the rest), not in `skill-lock.json`.
  - **Drafts.** The plan-review context uses the same check: a tie refuses the review block instead of loading both.
  - **Catalogue.** The catalogue's record gets an optional `canonical` field with the same compact shape.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/run-canonical.ts` (`liveCanonicalDeps`, the shared check and the compact record); changed `src/config.ts`, `src/settings/describe.ts`, `src/skills/run-catalogue.ts` (uses the shared check), `src/skills/choices.ts` (the wording of an invalid overrides file), `src/agents/run.ts`, `src/engine/skill-lock.ts`, `src/engine/state.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-run-canonical.test.ts`; `tests/engine-skill-lock.test.ts`, `tests/skill-review.test.ts`, `tests/skill-run-catalogue-run.test.ts`, `tests/settings-describe.test.ts` (setting count), `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`; config defaults in `tests/skill-resolve.test.ts` and `tests/skill-registry.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Exclusive capabilities" under "Skill selection", the config defaults).
- Skill lifecycle: deprecate, quarantine, retire or revoke a skill version with `scf skills lifecycle` (record in `skill-lifecycle.jsonl`). New runs never select quarantined, retired or revoked versions (reason code `ineligible`, no fallback to another version). Locked runs follow the lifecycle policy, and a run locked to a key that a release revokes now stops with "is revoked" instead of "is missing". `scf skills allow-run` lets one run go on with a quarantined version. Admin API routes `GET`/`POST /api/skills/lifecycle` and `POST /api/skills/lifecycle/allow-run`. Part 4 of 5 of #207.
  - **Record.** Append-only JSON lines, each with `seq`, `at`, `by`, `reason`. A missing file means every version is active. A record that cannot be read fails closed: selection and locked runs with skills stop, and `scf skills` names the line.
  - **Size.** The record is capped at 4 MiB; a change that would pass the cap is refused before it is written.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/lifecycle.ts` (record, fold, `effectiveLifecycle`), `src/skills/lifecycle-cli.ts`, `src/server/api-skills.ts`; changed `src/skills/registry.ts`, `resolve.ts`, `resolve-rules.ts`, `unresolved.ts`, `run-lock.ts`, `src/engine/skill-lock.ts`, `src/skills/cli.ts`, `src/cli.ts`, `src/server/server.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts`, `src/server/skill-view.ts`, `src/remote/areas/index.ts` (the new routes are API-only), `ui/run-skills.js`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Skill lifecycle" in "Trust", the reason-code table, the permissions table), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("The skill path").
- Skill deduplication 5a: exact duplicate skills reach the model once. Two skills whose description and instructions are the same text (for example one upstream skill in two catalogue releases under different ids) share one section in the `<foundry-skills>` block, and a review block shares one section for the same description and `REVIEW.md`. Part 1 of 4 of #212 (#561).
  - **Payload.** The first copy in load order keeps the section; its opening tag gets `also="<id@version>,…"` for the copies. The copies stay in `loaded` and are listed in a new `collapsed` list, never in `omitted`, and a copy never blocks a mandatory skill. The budget counts the text once.
  - **Resolution.** A copy of an admitted skill costs 0 tokens, so it cannot cause `over-budget`. Its decision and its lock entry stay.
  - **Catalogue.** One entry per text. The kept copy is the best tier, then the lowest id, then the lowest version. Copies with the same text and the same safety profile are merged before the canonical selection. The run record gets `mirrors` (at most 50 collapsed copies, `id@version`) and `mirrorsOmitted`; the log says `n mirror(s) collapsed`.
  - **Lock log.** Each collapsed copy gets a `loaded once` line. `skill-lock.json` and its version are unchanged.
- Skill source checks on a schedule: the server runs the due checks of `scf skills update --due` from a catalogue clone set in `skills.update_check` (`folder`, off by default; `every`, `1h` by default, `5m` to `24h`). Problems show on the Problems page as `skill-source-check`. Editable by admins on the Skills card of Settings. Part 2 of 2 of #553 (#560).
- Skill libraries runtime 1e: `scf skills release propose <catalogue>@<version>` opens a pull request in the deployment repository that approves a release (#558). Part 5 of 5 of #207.
  - **Config.** New `skills.releases.deployment: {repository, file}`. `repository` is `owner/name` or `host/owner/name`; `file` is a relative path to a `.yaml` or `.yml` config file that holds `skills.releases.approved`. Checked by `ConfigSchema` (`DEPLOYMENT_REPOSITORY_RE` in `src/config.ts`).
  - **Edit.** The file is read from `origin/<default branch>`. One entry (catalogue, version, digest, and `key` and `signature` with `--key` and `--signature`) is appended to `approved`; every other byte and every older entry stays. The edit is checked before pushing: only the new entry is added, the YAML is valid and the whole config passes the schema.
  - **Pull request.** Pushes `skill-catalogue/<catalogue>-<version>` and opens a pull request whose body has the change report against the newest approved version of that catalogue (added, removed, changed, deprecated, canonical selection). Refuses before pushing when that older release is neither in the store nor named by an `artifact` in the local config.
  - **Source.** `--artifact <absolute folder>` reads an unpublished release from a local folder, checked like `adopt`; nothing is written to the store. Origin must be the configured repository. With `skills.releases.keys` set, `--key` and `--signature` are required, and the signature is checked against the digest before pushing.
  - **Output.** `CHANGES …`, then `OK <url>`, `OPEN <url>` (no second pull request), `UNCHANGED` (already in the file) or `PROBLEM …` with fixed text (exit 1). A branch it pushed is deleted on failure, only while it still holds its commit.
  - **Local state.** Nothing changes in the local config, the data folder or the release store.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/release-deployment.ts` (`releaseBranch`, `appendApprovedEntry`, `checkApprovedEdit`, `releasePullRequest`, `normalRepository`); `src/skill-import/release-propose-cli.ts` (`releaseProposeCommand`); `src/skills/release-cli.ts` (`loadReleaseDir`, `artifactFolder`); `src/skills/source-update.ts` exports its splice helpers; `src/cli.ts` (options and dispatch). `src/settings/describe.ts` and `ui/settings-skills.js` have the two new settings.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-release-deployment.test.ts` (unit), `tests/skill-release-propose.test.ts`, `tests/skill-release-propose-failures.test.ts` and `tests/helpers/skill-propose-world.ts` (a bare remote and the fake `gh`); `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`FAKE_GH_CREATE_THEN_FAIL`); `tests/settings-describe.test.ts` (the setting count).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Propose the release to the deployment repository" in "Publish a catalogue release").
- Internal: the sweeper for scheduled skill source checks (`src/server/skill-update-sweep.ts`). It runs the `--due` logic in the server process and shows `PROBLEM` outcomes on the Problems page under the new detector `skill-source-check`. Not switched on yet (#559). Part 1 of 2 of #553.
- Skill source update: `scf skills update --due [--all]` checks every enabled source whose cadence is due (#552). Part 3 of 4 of #205.
  - **Due.** A source is due when it was never checked, its last check ended with `PROBLEM`, or the last check is at least 1 day (`daily`), 7 days (`weekly`) or 30 days (`monthly`) old. `manual` is never due; `--all` ignores the cadence. `--due` cannot take a source id, `--owner`, `--reviewer` or `--licence`.
  - **Outcomes.** Each source gets the outcome of `scf skills update <id>` (`UNCHANGED`, `OPEN`, `UPDATED`, `OK`, `PROBLEM`), prefixed with its id. One failing source does not stop the others. A `CHECKED` line counts the outcomes. Exit 1 on any `PROBLEM` or an interrupt.
  - **List.** Read from `skill-sources.yaml` on `origin/<base>`, not from the working copy, so a clone behind origin needs no `git pull`.
  - **Reviewer.** New optional `update.reviewer` (a GitHub user or team) in `skill-sources.yaml`, validated by `scf skills sources check`. An unattended run names it as the reviewer of a new commit of an enabled community source and says the review is pending in the pull request. Without it: `PROBLEM … needs update.reviewer`, nothing opened. A licence that is not clear is `PROBLEM … needs --licence`.
  - **Record.** The time and outcome of each finished check are written atomically to `skill-update-checks.json` in the data folder (schema 1). A missing or unreadable file means never checked and prints one `NOTE`.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/update-schedule.ts` (`isDue`, `nextCheck`, `readUpdateChecks`, `recordUpdateCheck`), `src/skill-import/update-due.ts` (`updateDue`), `src/skill-import/update-common.ts` (shared lines and reviewer helpers); `src/skill-import/update-cli.ts` (`run` returns a typed outcome, `updateSource`); `src/skill-import/catalogue-git.ts` (`requestReviewer`, `pullRequestReviewers`); `src/skills/sources.ts` (`update.reviewer`); `src/skills/source-update.ts` (`reviewPending`, `missing`). Tests: new `tests/skill-update-schedule.test.ts`, `tests/skill-update-schedule-io.test.ts`, `tests/skill-update-due.test.ts`; `tests/skill-sources.test.ts`, `tests/skill-source-update.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`--add-reviewer`, `pr view --json reviewRequests,reviews`).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Skill library sources", "Check every due source" with cron and launchd examples, command table).

- Skill source update: a re-run refreshes the open pull request instead of only reporting it (#551). Part 2 of 4 of #205.
  - **Refresh.** While the pull request for `skill-update/<id>-<commit12>` is open, `scf skills update <source-id>` rebuilds its title and body from the proposal on the branch and edits it with `gh pr edit --body-file -`. It prints `UPDATED <url> for <id>@<commit>` when the text changed and `OPEN …` when it did not. It never opens a second pull request and never pushes the branch again. A failed edit prints one fixed `PROBLEM` line and leaves the pull request as it was.
  - **Supersedes.** Open pull requests of the same source for older commits are listed in the new body as `Supersedes (these stay open and are not changed)`. They are not closed, merged or edited. Nothing merges or approves: the tests check the fake `gh` never gets a merge or approve call.
  - **Re-run checks.** A re-run runs the same working-copy and origin check as a new run, and reads the tracked ref from the branch. A locally changed `skill-sources.yaml` is now a `PROBLEM` on a re-run too. A review claim is only added for an enabled community source.
  - **Code.** `src/skill-import/catalogue-git.ts` (`openSourcePullRequests`, `pullRequestText`, `editPullRequest`); `src/skill-import/update-cli.ts` (`refresh`, `proposalText`, `baseState`); `src/skills/source-update.ts` (`updateBranchPrefix`, the `supersedes` option). Tests: `tests/skill-update-cli.test.ts`, `tests/skill-update-failures.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` (`pr edit`, `pr view`, `pr list --head --limit`).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Update a source": Supersedes, Re-run, Output).
- Skill libraries runtime 1b: a verified approved release is the approval (#555). Part 2 of 5 of #207.
  - **Source.** Packages of an approved, verified (and, with keys, signed) release in `skill-releases/` load with source `catalogue`, trust `approved` and pin `pinned`, because the release lists their digest. They need no entry in `skills.lock.json`. Administrator folders (data folder, `skills.roots`) still need hand pins. A hand pin with another digest for the same `id@version` is still a `mismatch`.
  - **Planner.** `buildSkillCatalogue` and `runSkillCatalogue` only offer catalogue packages from releases that passed every check; a refused release adds no candidates.
  - **Canonical.** `catalogue` ranks as `scf-curated` (`CANONICAL_REGISTRY_TIER`), so the administrator's own folders still win a capability.
  - **Run locks.** New locks record `source: "catalogue"`. Older `skill-lock.json` files with `"admin"` for the same packages still read and verify, because `verifyRunSkillLock` matches by key and digest. A run locked to an older approved release still resumes after a newer one is approved.
  - **Output.** `scf skills` and `scf skills release list` show the `catalogue` source. `release list` no longer prints `PIN` lines; each approved release line ends with ` (source catalogue)`. The UI shows "approved catalogue".
  - **Code.** `SkillSourceKind` in `src/skills/registry.ts` (`buildSources` and the pin rule in `discoverSkills`); `RunSkillLockEntrySchema` and `buildRunSkillLock` in `src/skills/run-lock.ts`; `sourceOf` in `src/engine/skill-lock.ts`; `src/skills/overlap.ts`, `src/skills/profile-canonical.ts`, `src/skills/review-cli.ts`, `src/server/skill-view.ts`, `releaseList` in `src/skills/release-cli.ts`.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-release-catalogue.test.ts`; `tests/skill-release-retained.test.ts`, `tests/engine-skill-lock.test.ts`, `tests/skill-view.test.ts` and `tests/ui-run-skills.test.ts` updated.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Approval is the pin" in "Trust", the trust table, "Publish a catalogue release").
- Skill libraries runtime 1c: adopt an approved release from a local artifact; never fetch during a run (#556). Part 3 of 5 of #207.
  - **Config.** `skills.releases.approved[].artifact` is an optional absolute local path to a schema-2 release folder. A URL, a network path, a relative path, a control character, a path over 4096 characters or a path inside `.claude`/`.codex` is refused. Check: `artifactPathProblem` in `src/skills/schema.ts`.
  - **Adopt.** `scf skills release adopt [<catalogue>@<version>]` copies each approved release missing from `skill-releases/` (or the named one) after `verifyRelease`, the catalogue, version and digest match, and a valid signature from `skills.releases.keys`. It writes once with `publishRelease`. Output: `ADOPTED`, `PRESENT`, `REFUSED <reason>` or `MISSING-ARTIFACT`; exit 1 on any refusal. A refused artifact writes nothing. Personal agent folders and the release store are never a source. Code: `releaseAdopt` in `src/skills/release-cli.ts`; the dispatch in `src/skills/cli.ts`; `readReleaseContent` (reads with `readRegular`, re-checks parent folders for links) in `src/skills/release-store.ts`.
  - **Missing release.** An approved release that is not in the store gives a `root-missing` problem in `scf skills` that says to run `scf skills release adopt`; a credential-looking name is left out of the text. A matching exact release in `skills.roots` does not trigger it. `unstoredApproved` in `src/skills/release-store.ts`, used by `discoverSkills`.
  - **No network.** `adopt`, discovery, run start and resume open no network connection. `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` covers the new modules.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-release-adopt.test.ts`; `tests/skill-registry-release.test.ts`, `tests/settings-describe.test.ts`, `tests/skill-cli.test.ts` updated.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Trust", "Adopt a release" bullet under "Trust").
- Skill source update: the pull request shows what changed since the approved commit (#550). Part 1 of 4 of #205.
  - **Section.** `scf skills update <source-id>` adds a "Changes since `<old commit>`" section to the pull request body, between the header and the review report. It lists each skill root as added, removed, changed or unchanged; per changed skill the files (bounded, "… and N more"), name, version, category, capabilities, licence status and licence ids, scan blocked and finding counts, and the evaluation verdict of the old digest and whether a record exists for the new digest. Its own byte budget keeps the body within `PULL_REQUEST_BYTES`.
  - **Canonical skills.** The capabilities of added or changed skills are checked against the canonical winners of the catalogue (the `scf skills overlap` selection). A change that adds a `CONFLICT` is marked.
  - **Verdict.** The verdict line names the failing parts (`security`, `licence`, `evaluation`, `review`) and says that nothing is promoted until the gates pass.
  - **Old commit.** When the old commit is not in quarantine, it is staged first with the checks of `scf skills import`. If that fails, the section says it could not be compared and the pull request is still opened. A first import has no section.
  - **Output.** A `CHANGES` line is printed before the `REVIEWED` line.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/source-change.ts` (`compareImports`, `sourceChangeMarkdown`; pure), `src/skill-import/update-changes.ts` (staging of the old commit), `src/skills/overlap-staged.ts` (`overlapStagedSkills`, `catalogueProblems`, shared with `scf skills overlap`). `src/skills/source-update.ts` adds `attentionParts` and the change section to `updatePullRequest`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Update a source").
- Skill libraries runtime 1a: signed catalogue approvals (#554). Part 1 of 5 of #207. A publisher's key can be required, so a release with the approved digest from the wrong publisher is refused.
  - **Config.** `skills.releases.keys` lists up to 10 trusted `{id, ed25519}` entries (`ed25519` is base64 of the 32-byte raw public key). An approved entry may have `key` (a key id) and `signature` (base64 of 64 bytes), and both or neither. The config is refused for an unknown key, a key id listed twice, a key that is not 32 bytes, or only one of `key` and `signature`. Code: `keys` in `src/config.ts`.
  - **Check.** When `keys` is not empty, an approved release with no signature, or one that does not verify, is refused as `unsigned` (`root-refused`: "is not signed by a trusted key" or "signature does not verify"), and none of its packages load. This holds with `allow_unreleased` too. With no keys, nothing changes. The signed text is `scf-skill-catalogue-approval-v1`, then the catalogue, version and digest, one per line; it is checked with `node:crypto` only. A digest that is not the approved one is still `mismatch`. Code: `src/skills/release-signature.ts` (`approvalSignatureProblem`, `approvalSignatureState`, `releaseSignatureToken`), `matchRelease` in `src/skills/release.ts`.
  - **Verify and list.** `scf skills release verify` and `scf skills release list` print `SIGNED <key>`, `UNSIGNED` or `BAD-SIGNATURE` when keys are set. Output is unchanged without keys.
  - **Sign.** `scf skills release sign <catalogue>@<version> --key <private-key-file>` signs a stored release with a PEM Ed25519 private key and prints `APPROVE <catalogue> <version> <digest> key <id> signature <b64>`. It checks the new signature before printing. It never prints key material or paths, and refuses a key file that is a link, is not a regular file, or is readable by group or others, and a key that is not listed in `skills.releases.keys`. Code: `releaseSign` and `readSigningKey` in `src/skills/release-cli.ts`; the dispatch in `src/skills/cli.ts`; the help line in `src/cli.ts`.
  - **Upgrade.** Once keys are set, sign every approved entry first, including the entries that locked runs use. Otherwise those runs stop resuming (see "Signed approvals" in the User Guide).
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-release-signature.test.ts`; `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`, `tests/skill-registry.test.ts`, `tests/skill-release.test.ts` and `tests/settings-describe.test.ts` updated.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Trust", "Publish a catalogue release").
- Skill libraries runtime 1d: `scf skills release diff` shows what a newer catalogue release changes, offline (#557). Part 4 of 5 of #207.
  - **Command.** `scf skills release diff <catalogue>@<old> <catalogue>@<new>` reads two stored releases from `skill-releases/` and prints one line per change: `ADDED`, `REMOVED`, `CHANGED`, `DEPRECATED` and `CANONICAL`, then `DIFF <c>@<old> <digest> -> <c>@<new> <digest>: <n> changes` (or `no changes`). Both releases must be of the same catalogue. Exit 0; a missing, invalid or unloadable release prints `PROBLEM …` and exits 1. Approval is not needed, and nothing is written. The output is the same for the same input.
  - **Changes.** `ADDED` and `REMOVED` are ids only in one release. `CHANGED` is an id with another version or digest, showing both. `DEPRECATED` is a skill revoked in the new release, with its day and reason, and `(already revoked)` when the old release had it revoked too. `CANONICAL` is a capability that more than one package of either release provides, whose canonical winner changed (`id@version`, or `conflict:<code>`, or `none`).
  - **Markdown.** `catalogueChangesMarkdown` renders the same changes as Markdown for a pull request or comment, within a byte limit (60 000 bytes by default). It shows ids, versions, digests, capabilities, days and conflict codes only, never skill text or a reason. The command does not print it yet.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/release-changes.ts` (`catalogueChanges`, `catalogueChangeLines`, `catalogueChangesMarkdown`, `releaseEntry`); `releaseDiff` and `loadStored` in `src/skills/release-cli.ts`; the dispatch and `SKILLS_USAGE` in `src/skills/cli.ts`; the help line in `src/cli.ts`. `boundedList` in `src/skills/source-update.ts` is exported for reuse.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-release-changes.test.ts` (in-memory manifests for each kind of change, the canonical rules and the Markdown limit, plus one CLI run with two stored releases).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Publish a catalogue release").
- Skill scanner and licence check: two more false alarms (#549).
  - **Quoted examples.** A prompt-injection phrase in quotation marks, introduced by a cue ("e.g.", "such as", "ignore", "refuse", "detect", "watch for", "treat as data") in the same sentence, with no negation and no instruction before the quote, is the `low` finding `prompt-injection.quoted-example` and does not block. An instruction ("Ignore previous instructions and …", "Follow this example: …") stays `high`. `scf skills scan` prints `FINDING … <path>:<line>: <evidence>`. Code: `quotedExample` in `src/skills/scan.ts`; `FIXED_RULES.quotedExample`, `QUOTED_EXAMPLE_CUE`, `QUOTED_EXAMPLE_DIRECTIVE` and `QUOTED_EXAMPLE_NEGATION` in `src/skills/scan-rules.ts`. Other finding ids do not change.
  - **Licences.** The text is recognised however it is indented or wrapped, also on one line, with owner notices of up to 7 words, however they are wrapped. Apache 2.0 is recognised with the `[yyyy]` placeholder and `https` addresses (`LicenceFullText.placeholder` and `wordForms`, a whitespace-tolerant cut). A changed word is still not recognised.
- Skill libraries publishing 1c: retained approved releases resolve for locked runs. Every release in `skill-releases/<catalogue>/<version>` that is listed in `skills.releases.approved` is read, newest version first within a catalogue. A run locked to an older approved version still resumes after a newer one is published and approved. Part 3 of 3 of #204 (#541).
  - **Discovery.** `skillSources` and `discoverSkills` add one admin source per approved release, labelled `release <catalogue>@<version>`, read after the data folder and before `skills.roots`. A store folder must hold exactly the schema-2 manifest its name says, or it is refused (`root-refused`). Store releases have their own budget, so they do not use up the room of the other roots. A `skills.roots` entry inside `skill-releases/` is skipped with a `NOTE`.
  - **Active and retained.** `findSkill` still returns active entries only, so a shadowed package is never offered to the catalogue or chosen for a new plan. `selectSkill` and `verifyRunSkillLock` resolve it by exact `id@version` and digest when it is pinned. Nothing in `resolve.ts`, `run-lock.ts` or `src/engine/skill-lock.ts` changed.
  - **Unapproved folders.** A store folder that is not in `skills.releases.approved` is not loaded and gets a `NOTE` (not a problem). Nothing deletes a release.
  - **List.** `scf skills release list` (new `releaseList` in `src/skills/release-cli.ts`) reads each stored release with its approval state and the run locks in the runs folder that reference one of its packages (`readRunSkillLock`, read only). It prints a `PIN` line for each approved package that is not pinned. `src/skills/cli.ts` dispatches it and lists it in `SKILLS_USAGE`.
  - **Pins.** Approval never pins or edits `skills.lock.json`; the owner pins a new package with `scf skills pin`. This is the decision in #541, so the trust model is unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/skills/release-store.ts` (`listStoredReleases`, `storeSources`, `releaseStorePath`, newest first); `src/skills/registry.ts` (`buildSources`, the per-source budget and the store check); the shared helpers are moved to `src/skills/registry-util.ts` and the error classes to `src/skills/registry-errors.ts`, re-exported from `registry.ts`.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-release-retained.test.ts` (a run locked to `x@1.0.0` stays valid after `c@1.1.0` is approved, a new plan picks `x@1.1.0`, removing `c@1.0.0` from `approved` gives `missing`, plus the notes and `release list`); `tests/skill-release-publish-cli.test.ts` updated.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Trust", "Publish a catalogue release").

- `skill-sources.yaml`: `addyosmani-agent-skills` enabled after the owner's review on 2026-10-15 (community, MIT, at `aeac41d4`): 16 engineering-practice skills, all promotable, no overlap with the catalogue. `source-driven-development` is left out until the scanner stops reading its defensive "ignore previous instructions" rule as an attack.
- Skill scanner: fewer false refusals of harmless skills (#548).
  - **File tests.** The upload rule is case-sensitive now, so the shell test `-f` is no longer read as curl's `-F`, and `-d`, `-F` and `-T` after `[`, `[[`, `!`, `&`, `|` or `test` are not uploads. So `[[ -f .env ]]` no longer raises `unsafe-network.upload-secret-file`. Real uploads of a credential-like file are still `high`: `curl -F "f=@.env"`, `curl -T .env`, `--data`/`--upload-file` with `.env`, `scp .env …` and `nc … < .env`.
  - **Web addresses.** `scf skills scan` and `scf skills promote check` print one `ADDRESS <skill> <mentioned|called> <path>: <address> (<why>)` line per web address. A file under `references/` or `evals/`, `metadata.json`, Markdown and other data files only mention an address; they no longer exclude a skill. A script, a file with a code extension, a file under `scripts/` or a file starting with `#!` that holds an address still excludes it as `holds a web address in code`. Addresses are shown with user, password and credential-like query values hidden, and cut at 80 characters with a short hash.
  - **Code.** `src/skills/import-exclusions.ts` (`addressUse`, `webAddresses`, `addressLines`, `shownAddress`), `src/skills/import-review-rules.ts` (`READ_ONLY_FOLDERS`, `CODE_EXTENSIONS`, `WEB_ADDRESS_LIST_RE`), `src/skills/scan-rules.ts` (the upload rule), `src/skills/scan-staged.ts` (`addresses` per skill). `scf skills scan` and `promote check` print the lines; no scan finding or stored report changes.
- `skill-sources.yaml`: two sources enabled after the owner's review on 2026-10-15: `spring-agent-skills` (community, Apache-2.0, 6 skills, reviewed at `f2287316`) and `anthropic-skills` (verified, Apache-2.0, `frontend-design`, `webapp-testing` and `mcp-builder` only, at `dbd4588f`, with `licence.reviewed`). Both pass `scf skills promote check`. Anthropic's `docx`, `pdf`, `pptx` and `xlsx` are left out (not open source); `skill-creator` and `claude-api` are refused by the scanner and size limits.
- Skill libraries publishing 1b-3: `scf skills release publish <catalogue>@<version>` publishes a schema-2 release in the catalogue repository from the skills in `skill-releases.yaml` that were staged, promoted and evaluated after review, and writes it once to `skill-releases/<catalogue>/<version>`. Revoked skills need only a loadable package and an honest evaluation verdict, so a bad skill can always be revoked. Config, pins and `skills.lock.json` are never changed. Part 3 of 3 of #540 (#546).
- Skill effect 1: `scf skills effect` runs a skill's sample tasks with and without the skill on the same model, and stores whether it helps, has no measurable effect or harms (#542). Part 1 of 2 of #456.
  - **Command.** `scf skills effect <source-id> <skill-id> [<suite-file>]` for a staged skill, or `scf skills effect <id@version> [<suite-file>]` for an approved, pinned skill. Only the `instructions` scenarios where the skill is offered run, with `repeat` runs per arm.
  - **Verdict.** A two-sided Fisher exact test on the pass counts. `helps` or `harms` when p ≤ 0.05 and the pass rates differ by at least 0.2; otherwise `no-measurable-effect`. A skill that never loads in its with-arm is `never-selected`.
  - **Output.** `RUN`, `SKIPPED`, `EFFECT` and `STORED` lines; `PROBLEM` and `INTERRUPTED` on refusal or interrupt, which store nothing.
  - **Records.** New `skill-effects/<id>/<digest>/effect--<seq>.json` (`schema: 1`), written through the shared store publish path. `readNewestEffect` re-checks the newest record and never falls back to an older one. Nothing reads these records yet.
  - **Store.** `writeEvaluationRecord` now uses the shared publish step; the existing store and score tests are unchanged.
  - **Runner.** `runOutcome` in `src/skill-eval/run.ts`; `scoreRun` is built on it. An arm can run with skills off.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/effect.ts` and `src/skill-eval/effect.ts`; `agentPreflight` and `blockedReason` exported from `src/skill-eval/score.ts`; `foundryVersion` exported from `src/skills/evaluate-cli.ts`; the command in `src/skills/cli.ts`; the help line in `src/cli.ts`.
  - **Example.** `evals/skill-effect/kafka/` holds a 5-scenario Kafka suite and a kafkajs fixture.
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-effect.test.ts` (pure logic, store, command syntax, example set) and `tests/skill-effect-agent.test.ts` (sandbox runs with the fake `claude`, refusals, interruption).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Measure the effect of a skill"); `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Skill effect (#456)").
- Skill effect 2: the effect verdict decides promotion, and `scf skills` shows it (#543). Part 2 of 2 of #456. **`scf skills gate` is stricter now:** a skill without a recorded effect verdict, or with a "no measurable effect" or "harms" verdict, fails where it passed before, so run `scf skills effect` and add a decision where one is needed.
  - **Decisions.** `skill-evaluations.yaml` takes an optional top-level `effect_decisions` list. Each entry has `skill`, `digest`, `accept` (`no-effect` or `not-measured`), `owner`, `reviewed` (not in the future) and `note` (required). Bad values and duplicates are `PROBLEM` lines. A file without the key reads as before.
  - **Gate.** `scf skills gate` reads the newest effect record of each skill and its digest. `helps` adds nothing. `harms` always fails (`effect-harms`), and an unreadable or invalid record fails (`effect-invalid`). `no-effect` fails (`effect-no-effect`) unless an `accept: no-effect` decision exists for the digest; no record fails (`effect-not-measured`) unless an `accept: not-measured` decision exists. A used decision prints a `DECISION` line with its owner. An invalid `skill-evaluations.yaml` now makes the gate print `PROBLEM` lines and exit 1.
  - **Promote check.** `scf skills promote check` prints an `EFFECT` line per skill and refuses with `REFUSED <path>: effect (<code>)` on the same rules. Licence, finding and evaluation refusals still come first.
  - **List.** `scf skills` ends each skill's digest with `effect helps|no-effect|harms|not-measured|invalid`, from the newest record of its digest. `invalid` is shown when the record cannot be read; it does not change the exit code.
  - **Not changed.** `release publish` does not apply the effect rule yet. The evaluation reason codes and the evaluation records are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `effectPromotion`, `effectState` and `effectCheckLine` in `src/skills/effect.ts`; `parseSuiteRecords` and `checkDecision` in `src/skills/suite-records.ts`.
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-effect-promotion.test.ts`, the "gate, effect" and "promote check, effect" blocks of `tests/skill-score-cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-promote-check-cli.test.ts`, and `tests/helpers/effect-records.ts`. No model is used.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Score and gate an import", "Check a promotion", "Measure the effect of a skill"); `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Skill effect (#456)").
- Skill libraries publishing 1b-2: release store. `publishRelease` in `src/skills/release-store.ts` writes a schema-2 release to `skill-releases/<catalogue>/<version>` once: built in a private `.tmp-` folder, checked with `verifyRelease`, then moved into place in one step; an existing version is never replaced (`unchanged` or `refused`). New `foundryPackageFiles` (#545). Part 2 of 3 of #540.
- Skill libraries publishing 1b-1: `skill-releases.yaml` and `scf skills releases check`, offline and read-only (#544). Part 1 of 3 of #540.
  - **File.** `skill-releases.yaml` in the catalogue repository lists the catalogue versions to publish (`schema: 1`, `repository`, up to 200 `releases`). Each release has `catalogue`, `version`, 1 to 500 `skills` (`{source, id}`), an optional `review` (`by`, `on`) and an optional `revoked` list (`id`, `version`, `on`, `reason`). Unknown keys, duplicate ids and duplicate `catalogue`@`version` pairs are refused.
  - **Reader.** `readReleaseRequests` refuses a symbolic link, a file that is not regular, a file over 256 KiB and YAML with aliases. Its problems are `{where, message}` and never repeat file content.
  - **CLI.** `scf skills releases check [<file>]` prints one line per release (`<catalogue>@<version> <n> skills, <k> revoked, reviewed by … on …` or `NOT REVIEWED`), then `OK <n> releases for <repository>` (exit 0), or `PROBLEM <where>: <message>` (exit 1). A missing file is a `PROBLEM`. It runs without the data folder.
  - **Not changed.** Nothing reads the file at run time. Publishing (#540) is not in this change, so an unreviewed entry is valid here only.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/release-requests.ts`; `releasesCheck` in `src/skills/release-cli.ts`; the dispatch and `SKILLS_USAGE` in `src/skills/cli.ts`; the help line in `src/cli.ts`. `REPO_RE` and `isDay` are exported from `src/skills/release.ts`.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-release-requests.test.ts` (a valid file, each refusal, the reader, the CLI output and exit codes); a case in `tests/cli.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Publish a catalogue release").
- Skill libraries publishing 1a: release manifest 2 with file digests, provenance, licences, attribution, evaluation summary and revocation; offline `scf skills release verify` (#539). Part 1 of 3 of #204.
  - **Manifest.** `catalogue-release.json` accepts `schema: 2`. Each skill adds `files`, `provenance`, `licences`, `attribution` (kept in `.attribution/<id>/`), `evaluation` (`suite`, `record`, `verdict`) and an optional `revoked`. The v2 digest uses the header `scf-skill-catalogue-release-v2`. Schema 1 is unchanged.
  - **Verify.** New `verifyRelease(root)` in `src/skills/release-verify.ts` checks every listed file (presence, regular file, size, SHA-256), each package digest, and that no unlisted file is in a package or in `.attribution/`. It follows no link, opens no network connection and starts no process.
  - **Discovery.** `discoverSkills` runs `verifyRelease` on every schema-2 release; a failure refuses the root (`root-refused`). A skill with `revoked` in an approved schema-2 release is never registered and gets a `revoked` note.
  - **CLI.** `scf skills release verify <dir>` prints `VERIFIED …` or `PROBLEM …` and `APPROVED` or `NOT-APPROVED`. New `src/skills/release-cli.ts`; the dispatch is in `src/skills/cli.ts`, the help line in `src/cli.ts`.
  - **Not changed.** No config field and no API route. The evaluation verdict does not block loading; the publish gate (#540) decides what may be published.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-release-verify.test.ts` and `tests/helpers/skill-release-v2.ts`; cases in `tests/skill-release.test.ts` and `tests/skill-registry-release.test.ts`; `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` covers the new modules.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Trust", "Release manifest 2").
- Community Spring source 4b: `scf skills compare <source-id>` prints `PROMOTABLE <skill>` or `REFUSED <skill>: <codes>` for each candidate of a `trust: community` source (#538). Part 2 of 2 of #521.
  - **Exit rule.** For a community source, `compare` exits 1 when a candidate is refused or an entry did not normalize (`REFUSED <folder>: not-normalized`, printed before `COMPARED`), as well as on a conflict, a blocking collision or a `PROBLEM`. Most community imports exit 1 until review, licence evidence and fixture records exist. This is intended (fail closed); scripts that use `compare` will see it. Other trust tiers keep the old output and exit rule.
  - **Inputs.** The licence evidence of the staged import, `skill-scan-exceptions.yaml` (an invalid file is a `PROBLEM`) and `skill-profile-evaluations.yaml` (an invalid file is a `PROBLEM`). Nothing is installed, pinned or offered to runs, and the command still only reads.
  - **Fixture records.** Read from the `kind: fixtures` records of `skill-profile-evaluations.yaml` (#524), matched on the skill id and the exact digest. A `passed` record satisfies the fixture check, a `failed` one gives `fixtures-failed`, and no record, an expired one or one for another digest gives `fixtures-missing`. The #503 gate records are not used.
- Skill source connector 4: `scf skills profiles evaluate <source-id>` recommends one canonical skill per overlapping exclusive capability, and the guide has a Supabase example (#525). Part 4 of 4 of #222.
  - **The recommendation.** For each `exclusive` capability that the source declares and at least two skills provide, it prints `CANONICAL <capability>: <id@version> <origin> (<level>)`, or `CONFLICT` or `NONE`, then a `REJECTED … : <code>` line for each other provider. Providers are the staged skills and the active built-in and administrator skills. A staged skill that failed a gate is never recommended; its failed gates are shown as `gates=…`. A capability in `high_risk` is marked `high-risk`. Only validated ids, versions, codes and the selector's message are printed.
  - **Exit code.** 1 for a `CONFLICT`, a blocked skill, a `PROBLEM` or a catalogue that could not be read in full. A recommendation alone does not change it.
  - **Command name.** The issue asked for `scf skills evaluate`; the recommendation is part of `scf skills profiles evaluate`. The plain `scf skills evaluate` is unchanged.
  - **Example.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Example: Supabase agent skills") has a `skill-sources.yaml` entry for `https://github.com/supabase/agent-skills` and the matching `skill-source-profiles.yaml` entry. `ref.approved`, the folder names and the licence are placeholders to check against the reviewed commit; the upstream could not be checked (no network). The repository ships no real `skill-sources.yaml`, commit id or credential.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/profile-canonical.ts` (builds the candidates from the part-3 results and formats the lines). `src/skills/profile-evaluate.ts` also returns the version, category and risk of each candidate. `src/skills/profile-evaluate-cli.ts` reads the catalogue only when the source declares an exclusive capability, and `src/skills/cli.ts` supplies it. Runs and `run-catalogue.ts` are unchanged.
  - **Not done.** Nothing is promoted, pinned or loaded by this command, and no run reads the recommendation. The Supabase folder names and licence are not checked against the upstream repository.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-evaluate-canonical.test.ts` with the synthetic fixture `tests/fixtures/skill-sources/supabase-like/`; `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` lists the new module.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Canonical skill per capability", the Supabase example, the command table).
- Skill evaluation 1d-3: `scf skills score <source-id> [suite] --agent` runs the `instructions` scenarios in the OS sandbox, with repeats and paired baselines (#536). Part 3 of 3 of #504.
  - **What it does.** For each staged skill that may be scored, every scenario runs `repeat` times with the candidate, and again with each baseline from `gateChecks` (at most 4; more is a `PROBLEM`). Each baseline is also scored offline with the same suite. The arms run one after another. The record gets `dimensions.instructions`, an `agent` block (`sandbox: os`, `network: not-host-limited`, `outbound`, `repeat`, `runs`, `baselines`), `runtime.agent` and `runtime.model`, and the evidence `agent-package` (the digest of the candidate as a Foundry package). One `RUN` line per run and a `NOTE` line about the network.
  - **Scoring.** A run scores 1 when the fixture, the work and the check succeed and the skill was active; otherwise 0, with a finding (`agent-failed`, `check-failed` or `skill-not-loaded`). A broken fixture step or an unreadable fixture gives `PROBLEM <id>: …; no instructions`.
  - **Isolation.** Each arm runs in a new temporary data folder that holds only its own skill, pinned in its own `skills.lock.json`. The real `skills/` and `skills.lock.json` are unchanged. Every run has no GitHub token, an empty `gh` folder, strict MCP config, a short environment and a private `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `HOME`. The model key is the only credential. The work step uses `dontAsk` and gets only the tools of its scenario policy. The real data folder is denied for reads.
  - **Refusals.** Printed as `PROBLEM …` before anything starts, with exit 1 and nothing written: no sandbox, the sandbox switched off (`SCF_USER_SANDBOX` or `FACTORY_USER_SANDBOX`), a suite without `instructions`, an agent that is not Claude, no model key, or a fixture that differs from the suite digest.
  - **Blocked candidates.** A candidate with promotion blockers or a licence that is not `allowed` prints `SKIPPED <id>: …; no agent run`. It gets the offline record only.
  - **Interruption.** On SIGINT, SIGTERM or an error, every job is cancelled and awaited, the folders are removed and the environment is restored. Then `PROBLEM the evaluation was interrupted` is printed and no record is written. Deadlines: fixture 120 s, work `timeout_sec`, check 300 s.
  - **Without `--agent`** the output and the record are unchanged. `scf skills evaluate … --agent` is refused with a message that points to `score --agent`.
  - **Code.** New `src/skill-eval/run.ts` (the process-wide folder switch, the in-memory flow, the evaluation config, the run scoring and the arm loop), `src/skill-eval/package.ts` (the candidate and baseline packages), `src/skill-eval/fixture.ts` (the fixture snapshot, refused when it holds a `.git` entry) and `src/skill-eval/score.ts` (the refusals, the arms and the record). `src/skills/evaluate-cli.ts`: `scoreImport` is split into `prepareScoring` and `writeScores`. `src/engine/state.ts`: `StepRecord.accessFailed`. `src/cli.ts` and `src/skills/cli.ts`: the `--agent` option.
  - **Limits.** Outbound is limited to the model's port, not its host (Seatbelt cannot filter by host). The model key is visible to the agent. macOS only.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-evaluate-agent.test.ts` (the refusals, a run without `--agent`, a blocked candidate, the fixture code and, on macOS with `sandbox-exec`, the runs with `fake-claude.mjs`: a new candidate, an upgrade with a paired baseline, the environment, the real config, the timeout and the cancellation). `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` allows `src/skill-eval/score.ts` to import `registeredCandidate`. `tests/skill-score-cli.test.ts` is unchanged.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Run the scenarios in the sandbox"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("Agent runs of the scenarios").
- Skill source connector 3: `scf skills profiles evaluate <source-id>` shows the gates a staged import must pass before promotion, per skill (#524). Part 3 of 4 of #222.
  - **The command.** `scf skills profiles evaluate <source-id>` is offline and read-only: it writes nothing, promotes nothing and takes no lock. It prints `PASS` or `BLOCKED` per staged skill, with the reasons by gate (`declared`, `licence`, `security`, `requirements`, `evaluation`), then `NEEDS` lines and `MISSING` lines for profile skills that are not staged, and `EVALUATED <n> skills …: <p> pass, <b> blocked, <m> missing`. It exits 1 when a skill is blocked or a `PROBLEM` is printed. The name is `profiles evaluate`, not `evaluate`: `scf skills evaluate` already reports a source before import.
  - **Gates.** Declared: a staged skill with no profile entry is `BLOCKED`. Licence: the stored evidence must pass `evaluateLicence`; without evidence the reason says to import again. Security: the skill must normalize and have no open high finding, the same decision as `scf skills scan`. Requirements: every tool or connector the package needs must be in the profile's `requires`; declared names are printed under `NEEDS`, and no credential or project value is read or printed. Evaluation: each kind in the profile's `evaluations` needs a `passed` record for the skill id and exact digest, with an owner, a date and an expiry not in the past. A failed record at that digest wins over a passed one; a record for another digest is `recorded for another digest`.
  - **Records.** New `skill-profile-evaluations.yaml` (`schema: 1`, up to 500 `evaluations`), read from the current folder. Each record has `skill`, `digest`, `kind`, `verdict` (`passed` or `failed`), `owner`, `reviewed`, `expires` and an optional `report`. The lifetime is at most 365 days after `reviewed`, the same as scan exceptions. Wildcards and unknown keys are refused. An invalid file prints `PROBLEM skill-profile-evaluations.yaml …` lines and nothing is evaluated. The file is not the suite record file of #504 (`skill-evaluations.yaml`) and not the scored record of `evaluation.ts`.
  - **Shared scan.** `scanImport` and the new command use one helper, `src/skills/scan-staged.ts`; the output of `scf skills scan` is unchanged.
  - **YAML warnings.** `skill-sources.yaml`, `skill-source-profiles.yaml` and `skill-scan-exceptions.yaml` are read with the YAML library silent, so a warning cannot print a source line that may hold a credential. Errors are still reported as `PROBLEM` lines. This also applies to `scf skills scan` and `sources check`.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/profile-evaluate.ts` (the pure gate function, `evaluateProfileCandidates`), `src/skills/profile-evaluate-cli.ts` (reads the files and prints), `src/skills/profile-evaluations.ts` (parses, reads and looks up the records) and `src/skills/scan-staged.ts`. `dayNumber` is exported from `scan-exceptions.ts`.
  - Tests: `tests/skill-profile-evaluate.test.ts` (one case per gate and blocking reason), `tests/skill-profile-evaluations.test.ts` (file validation, the 500-record limit), `tests/skill-profile-evaluate-cli.test.ts` (the lines on a temporary home), and cases in `tests/cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Evaluate the profile gates" under "Skill library sources" and the command table).
  - Not done: nothing is promoted by this command, and no run reads the records yet.
- Skill evaluation 1d-2: `instructions` scenarios in the suite, and paired baselines in the record and the gate (#535). Part 2 of 3 of #504.
  - **The suite.** An optional `instructions` map: `mandatory` (default false), `repeat` (3–20, default 3) and `scenarios` (1–20). Each scenario has `id`, `repo` (a fixture folder), `task`, `check`, `policy` (`shell`, `network`, `filesystem`) and `timeout_sec` (60–3600, default 900). Scenarios × repeat is at most 200; ids are unique across the suite. A fixture that holds `.claude/` or `.mcp.json`, or a symbolic link, is refused. The suite digest of a suite without `instructions` is unchanged; with it, the digest covers each scenario, `{mandatory, repeat}` and a content hash of each fixture.
  - **The record.** An optional strict `agent` block: `sandbox` (`container` or `os`), `network` (`not-host-limited` or `host-limited`), `outbound` (at most 10 host names), `repeat`, `runs` (at most 1000) and `baselines` (at most 4, each with `dimensions.instructions`). A record with `agent` is refused as `record-invalid` when its runs and its `instructions` scores disagree, a run names an unknown scenario, or run ids repeat. A record without `agent` keeps its digest.
  - **The gate.** A baseline paired in the candidate's own `agent` block (same skill id and digest) is used for every dimension, so no stored baseline record is needed. A stored record of an older suite does not affect it. Without a pair, the #503 behaviour is unchanged. `instructions.mandatory` makes `instructions` mandatory, and `minSamples.instructions` is repeat × scenarios.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/evaluation-instructions.ts` (parsing, fixture hash, the `agent` schema and its checks, the gate policy). `src/skills/evaluation-suite.ts` exports the shared helpers. New constants in `src/skills/evaluation-rules.ts`: `EVALUATION_INSTRUCTIONS_LIMITS`, the sandbox, network and arm lists, and the finding codes `check-failed`, `agent-failed`, `skill-not-loaded` and `network-not-host-limited`. `schema` stays 1.
  - **Not done.** `scf skills score` does not run the scenarios yet, so it writes no `instructions` scores and no `agent` block. A suite with `instructions.mandatory: true` therefore gives `dimension-missing:instructions` until the runner of part 3 (#504) writes them.
  - Tests: `tests/skill-evaluation-suite.test.ts`, `tests/skill-evaluation.test.ts` (hand-written records: a paired regression `[1,1,1]` → `[0,0,0]`, a paired baseline without a stored record, a stored record of an older suite, mandatory and optional), `tests/skill-score-cli.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("The suite file and its instructions section" under "Score and gate an import").
- Community Spring source 4a: a pure promotion check per candidate skill, the fixture collection and the trust and review fields of the promotion manifest (#537). Part 1 of 2 of #521.
  - **Check.** New `src/skills/community-promotion.ts`: `communityPromotionCheck` gives one decision per skill (`promotable` or `refused`) with fixed, unique codes (`COMMUNITY_PROMOTION_CODES`), sorted by skill id. A skill is promotable only when it has no code. It covers the source (enabled, `trust: community`), the review and the approved commit, the licence (through `promoteImport`), the scan with its exceptions, the version support, the collisions and canonical conflicts with the built-in skills, and the fixture result. A fixture result that is missing or not `passed` or `failed` is refused. `communityPromotionLines` formats `PROMOTABLE` and `REFUSED` lines. Nothing is installed, pinned or offered; `scf skills compare` is unchanged.
  - **Manifest.** `PackageProvenance` gets two optional fields: `trust` (always set from the source) and `review` (`by`, `on`, `commit`, only when the source has a review). `schema` stays 1; older manifests stay valid.
  - **Fixtures.** `tests/fixtures/skill-sources/community-spring/` (MIT `LICENSE`, diagnosis, review and operations skills, a stale Spring Boot 2 skill and a `spring-boot` skill that collides with the built-in) and `tests/fixtures/skill-sources/community-spring-projects/` (Maven Spring Boot 3 on Java 17, Gradle Spring Boot 3 on Java 21, plain Java and Node projects).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-community-promotion.test.ts` (one case per code, the fixed order, the input unchanged, both line formats) and `tests/skill-community-spring.test.ts` (the collection and the projects: offers, the stale refusal, the collision and missing fixtures); cases in `tests/skill-promotion.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`: the manifest fields `trust` and `review` are in "Check a promotion".
- Skill evaluation 1d-1: evaluation limits for sandboxed agent runs (#534). Part 1 of 3 of #504.
  - **What it does.** One process-wide limit (`setEvaluationLimits` and `evaluationLimits` in `src/engine/eval-limits.ts`) holds every sandboxed step tighter than a normal run: outbound TCP only to the listed ports, the listed folders unreadable, and a Claude agent with only the listed tools. It is unset by default; normal runs never set it, and their sandbox profiles and agent command lines are unchanged.
  - **Outbound.** Each entry is `*:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, with a port from 1 to 65535. Anything else (a host name, port 0, a newline) is refused with `an outbound rule must be *:<port> or localhost:<port>`, and the old limit stays. The OS sandbox profile adds `deny network-outbound (remote ip "*:*")`, then one `allow network-outbound (remote tcp "<entry>")` per entry, after the unix-socket rules and the mDNSResponder exception.
  - **Read denies.** Each `denyRead` folder (an absolute path, made real with `deepReal`) joins the broad read denies, before every allow exception.
  - **Tools.** A Claude agent step under the limit gets `--tools <list>`, `--settings {"disableAllHooks":true}` (merged with `sandbox` when that is on), `--strict-mcp-config` and `--setting-sources project,local --disable-slash-commands`. Without the limit the arguments are unchanged. `noTools` wins when both are set.
  - **Code.** `src/engine/eval-limits.ts` (new); `src/engine/os-sandbox.ts` (`SandboxPaths.denyRead` and `outbound`, filled by `stepSandboxPaths`); `src/steps/claude.ts` (`ClaudeRunOptions.tools`); `src/agents/run.ts` passes the tools from the limit. No config field and no run-level plumbing.
  - Tests: `tests/os-sandbox.test.ts` (`evaluation limits`: no network lines without the limit, the order with `*:443`, `localhost:11434`, the refused entries, the `denyRead` order and parent folders, set and reset) and `tests/agents.test.ts` (`buildClaudeArgs` with and without `tools`). The tests read the profile text and the arguments; they do not run Seatbelt or Claude Code.
- Refinement: an architect run no longer waits for a free place behind story runs.
  - **Now.** Architect runs of refinement sessions take no slot: the scheduler starts them past `concurrency` and past a helper's cap (load governor, usage limits), before the other queued jobs (`slotsInUse` in `src/queue/scheduler.ts`). The account's lock still lets one architect run per account work at a time; blocked accounts and account limits still hold them. They are never marked `capped` in the queue.
  - **Why.** A person waits for an architect run, and it only reads. On a busy machine it waited behind story runs for a long time.
  - Tests in `tests/scheduler-fair-use.test.ts`; `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Epic page: one epic's stories, chains, chart, pace, critical path and forecast history (#489). Part 7 of 7 of #408.
  - **What.** New page `#/epics/<id>?repo=<name>`, reached from the name of each epic row on the Epics page (an issue epic keeps its "On GitHub" link). It shows the expected finish with its reason, what it leaves out and how it moved; the critical path; the stories in build order; the dependency chains; a chart of stories and done stories per UTC day with a table; the pace and the median story time. It has loading, empty, error, no-permission and stale states. Below 768 px the chain graph scrolls sideways. The user display (`/user/#/epics/<id>`) shows the same page.
  - **Navigation.** The page is registered as `epic` under the Epics page; its breadcrumb and back link keep the repository (`keepRepo` in `ui/ia.js`).
  - **Data.** Everything comes from `GET /api/repos/:id/epics/:issue` (`api.epic` in `ui/api.js`). No count or forecast is calculated in the browser; no server change.
  - **Code.** New `ui/epic-page.js` (the page and the chart, drawn as plain SVG with a table next to it) and `ui/epic-page-model.js` (pure: chart points and rows, forecast history lines, critical path text, story list, cycle notes, pace text). `chainGraph` and `chainLayout` in `ui/graph.js` draw the chains; `renderEpic` is wired in `ui/app.js`; the page is in `src/help/context.ts` and in `ui/css/pages/work.css`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-epic-page-model.test.ts` (chart points, history lines, critical path text) and `tests/ui-epic-page.test.ts` (the page states); new `tests/browser/epic-page.spec.ts`. Updates to `tests/ui-ia.test.ts`, `tests/ui-routers.test.ts`, `tests/ui-auth.test.ts`, `tests/ui-epics.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/flow-app.ts` and `tests/fixtures/ui-style-baseline.css`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`: the Epics section covers the page, how the forecast is made and what it cannot know. `docs/ui-redesign/inventory.md` and `docs/ui-redesign/prototypes.md` list the page.
- Fix: test runs an agent starts by a wrapper's path go through the test slots and run at low priority too (`./gradlew test`, `./mvnw verify`, `sh ./gradlew …`, an absolute path) (#533).
  - **Covered.** `./gradlew` and `./mvnw` with a test goal (`test`, `check`, `build`, `verify`, `integrationTest`, `connectedCheck`, `install`, or a task path such as `:app:test`) take a slot, like `gradle test` and `mvn verify` do. Any other goal (`assemble`, `dependencies`, `--version`, `compileJava`) runs unchanged. A nested call inside a slot takes no second slot.
  - **How.** A wrapper ends in `java` with the Gradle or Maven main class, so `tools/agent-bin/java` (first on the `PATH`) passes the call to `_gate.mjs`, which uses the same `BUILD_TEST` rule as `gradle` and `mvn`. A wrapper finds its JDK through `JAVA_HOME`, so `testGateEnv` (`src/agents/run.ts`) points `JAVA_HOME` at a stand-in JDK in `<data>/agent-java` (`javaStandIn` in new `src/agents/java-gate.ts`). Its `bin/java` calls the gate with the real `java`; every other file is a symlink to the real JDK. Without a JDK to stand in for, the `PATH` shim alone applies.
  - **Priority.** A gated Gradle run gets `-Dorg.gradle.priority=low` (in `gatedArgs` of `_gate.mjs`), so its daemon runs at low priority too, also inside a slot. A call that sets a priority keeps its own.
  - **Not covered.** Other test scripts started by path (`./scripts/test.sh`) and steps in the OS sandbox. The wait limit (`FACTORY_AGENT_TEST_WAIT_SEC`) and "run anyway" are the same as for the other gated commands.
  - Tests: new `tests/agent-java-gate.test.ts` (the launcher parsing, the stand-in JDK, the gate on `java`); cases in `tests/agent-test-gate.test.ts` and `tests/test-slot.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Skill source connector 5: Confluent agent skills — example declaration, `skill-evaluations.yaml` and the evaluation gate in `scf skills promote check` (#513). Part 5 of 5 of #218.
  - **Example declaration.** `docs/examples/skill-sources.confluent.yaml` (shipped in the package) is a disabled entry for `confluentinc/agent-skills`. Its paths, capability names and version ranges are placeholders to check against the upstream commit you review; `ref.approved` is empty until the owner sets it. Nothing here was checked against upstream.
  - **Selections for capabilities.** `scf skills overlap` and `promote check` read `skill-selections.yaml`. A reviewed selection gives capabilities to a skill that declares none, when its digest matches the staged skill. A skill with its own capabilities is not changed.
  - **Evaluations.** New optional `skill-evaluations.yaml` (`schema: 1`, up to 500 records) holds the owner's results for two suites, each at one digest: `messaging-semantics` (text rules for exactly-once, ordering, keys, duplicates, retries and dead letters) and `compatibility` (the declared versions against the project's versions). An absent file means no records. A bad file prints `PROBLEM` and exits 1. Records refuse credential-like text and print only the rule.
  - **Codes.** Messaging: `exactly-once`, `ordering`, `keys`, `duplicates`, `retries`, `dead-letters`. Compatibility: `incompatible`, `no-declared-versions`, `stale`, `unchecked`. Records: `not-recorded`, `other-digest`, `recorded-failed`, `profile`.
  - **Gate.** `promote check` refuses a path with `REFUSED <path>: evaluation (<suite>: <codes>)` until both suites pass for its digest. The suites are needed for a skill with a `kafka-` or `confluent-` capability (also one added by the source scope), and for a skill without capabilities from a `confluent` provider source.
  - **Lines.** `EVALUATION <source>/<skill> <suite> <digest>: passed` or the codes, with `at <path:line>` for messaging. `overlap` prints `VERSIONS <skill>: <technology> <range>, …` for each skill with a declared version.
  - **Licence.** The Confluent Community License is `rejected` (it was `ambiguous` before). A licence file that names it is refused for every source; an Apache-2.0 declaration over it is `ambiguous`.
  - **Help.** The `promote check` help line in `src/cli.ts` and `src/skills/cli.ts` names evaluations.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/suite-rules.ts` (data only), `src/skills/suite-records.ts` (the parser and safe reader of the records file) and `src/skills/suite-checks.ts` (the effective skill, the required suites, the checks and the lines). `src/skills/overlap.ts`, `src/skills/cli.ts`, `src/skills/licence-rules.ts`, `src/cli.ts` and `package.json` (the example in `files`).
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-suites.test.ts`, `tests/skill-source-confluent.test.ts` (the example, staging, the gate, records, failures, a changed skill, licence and overlap), `tests/fixtures/skill-layouts/confluent.ts`; `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` covers the new modules. The existing overlap and promote check tests are unchanged.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Confluent skills", "Check a promotion", "Overlap with the catalogue", the command table).
  - Not changed: flows, run files, `skills/kafka`. Nothing is promoted by this change; the Confluent entry stays disabled until the owner approves a commit.
- Skill source connector — Vercel TypeScript and frontend skills (#220). Part of #196.
  - **Connector.** New connector `vercel-agent-skills` for `https://github.com/vercel-labs/agent-skills`: `trust: verified`, `licence.allow` only `MIT`, `paths` at `skills` or `agent-skills` or below. An entry still needs `ref.approved`; the connector approves nothing.
  - **Reviewed profile.** `VERCEL_PROFILE` in `src/skills/frontend-rules.ts` selects four folders in the groups `frontend` and `typescript`. Each folder has a `reviewed` list of digests (shipped empty), required `evaluations` (`skill-gate`, none recorded yet) and an optional `hold`. `web-design-guidelines` is on hold: it fetches its rules from a remote address when it runs. `vercel-deploy-claimable` is not selected and is reported as `NOT-SELECTED`.
  - **Profile replaces upstream.** For a source with a profile, folders are selected before normalization. The profile's capabilities, technologies and category replace the upstream values, and a `scope` on such a source is refused (`PROBLEM`; a `TypeError` in code). A skill is blocked while its declaration is unreviewed, its evaluation is not passed, it is on hold, or its body is over `maxSkillTokens` (5000).
  - **Evidence.** `scf skills evaluate <source-id> --repo <dir>` reads the versions of `next`, `react`, `react-native` and `typescript` from the `package.json` dependencies of that folder. An exact version gives its value, a caret (`^18.2.0`) gives the major version and a tilde (`~18.2.0`) the major and minor, and a range such as `>=18`, `latest` or `workspace:*` gives `unknown` (`unchecked`). Different versions give `unknown`. A scan that hit a limit or skipped an unreadable entry stops with a `PROBLEM`.
  - **Output.** New lines `CONNECTOR` (declaration, evaluation, hold, size), `NOT-SELECTED` and `MISSING`. An import with nothing staged prints `REFUSED`, `NOT-SELECTED` and `MISSING` lines and `EVALUATED 0 skills`. Any blocked skill, missing selected folder or refusal exits 1.
  - **Overlap.** A blocked skill does not win a capability; the built-in skill does.
  - **Not changed.** Nothing is promoted, pinned, loaded or written. No API route and no permission rule is added.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/frontend-rules.ts` (data only) and `src/skills/frontend-connector.ts` (pure selection, rule and evidence functions). Changed: `src/skills/source-evaluation.ts` (`TechnologyOrigin` gains `profile`), `src/skill-import/evaluate-cli.ts` (`--repo`), `src/skills/connectors.ts`, `src/skills/repo-profile.ts` (a dependency declared twice with different versions is `unknown`; an unreadable dependency file makes the scan incomplete), and the help lines in `src/cli.ts` and `src/skills/cli.ts`.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-frontend-connector.test.ts`; cases in `tests/skill-source-evaluation.test.ts`, `tests/skill-evaluate-cli.test.ts`, `tests/skill-connectors.test.ts` and `tests/skill-cli.test.ts`. Baseline tolerance in `tests/test-slot.test.ts`, `tests/agent-test-gate.test.ts` and `tests/skill-import-limits.test.ts` (a nice value of 20; a 2 s stall window).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Evaluate a source", "Vercel agent skills", "Connectors" and the command table) and `docs/DESIGN.md` (the skill path, after "Canonical selection").
- Settings 8: every setting has a control or a reviewed exception (#500). Part 11 of 11 of #407 (every setting can be changed in the interface).
  - **The rule.** Every key of `ConfigSchema` (down to the leaves, watcher options included) and every variable of every shipped flow must have a control in the interface or a reviewed exception. `tests/settings-inventory.test.ts` fails otherwise, and the message names the key and says what to add.
  - **Exceptions.** `src/settings/exceptions.ts` is the one list: each key has a reason, and an entry for a key that no longer exists, or that now has a control, fails the test. Four entries today: `help.disabled_accounts`, `help.sensitive_patterns` and the two `providers.*.price` fields.
  - **Controls.** The Ask SCF card (`generatedSection` in `ui/admin-sections.js`) draws every `help.*` setting that has a generated control, with the same save and error rules as the other cards. `ui.redesign`, `default_model` and the `router.*` fallback settings are described too. `ITEM_SETTINGS` in `src/settings/describe.ts` lists the fields of records (`watchers`, `providers`, `router.rules`) with the module that edits each one.
  - **Flow variables.** A variable of a shipped flow needs a `var_info` entry or `set_by_run: true`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`: the Ask SCF switch and its settings are in Settings, and section 7 has a short paragraph on the start-up settings. `docs/DESIGN.md` describes the rule.
  - Tests: new `tests/settings-inventory.test.ts`; cases in `tests/settings-describe.test.ts`, `tests/ui-admin-sections.test.ts` and `tests/ui-settings.test.ts`.
- Skill source connectors 4: `scf skills update <source-id>` opens a review pull request and never promotes (#508). Part 4 of 4 of #215.
  - **What it does.** It resolves the upstream commit of an enabled source (its `ref.track`, or HEAD) or of a connector that has no entry yet, stages it in quarantine with the checks of `scf skills import`, builds the review report of #507 and opens a pull request in the repository that holds `skill-sources.yaml`. The pull request changes only that file: `ref.approved` (and `review`) of the entry, or one new entry for the connector, with comments and other entries kept byte for byte. Nothing is approved, promoted, pinned or released until the pull request is merged.
  - **Output.** `UNCHANGED` when the upstream commit is already `ref.approved`, and nothing is opened. `OPEN <url>` when a pull request for the same source and commit is open. `OK opened <url>` otherwise. A failure is one fixed `PROBLEM` line and exit 1; a branch made by this run is deleted, and a branch that cannot be deleted is named in the `PROBLEM`.
  - **Refused.** A `disabled`, `quarantined` or `retired` source; a connector whose repository another entry already uses; a `skill-sources.yaml` in the folder that differs from the default branch of `origin`.
  - **Code.** New `src/skill-import/update-cli.ts` (the command) and `src/skill-import/catalogue-git.ts` (the local git and `gh` calls, argument arrays only). `src/skills/source-update.ts` holds the branch name, the YAML edit and the pull request text (pure). `stageSource` in `src/skill-import/cli.ts` and `reviewStaged` in `src/skills/review-cli.ts` are shared with `import` and `review`. `resolveTrack` in `src/skill-import/ls-remote.ts` reads a named branch or tag; `resolveHead` is unchanged. `src/cli.ts` routes the command.
  - **Not changed.** No API route, so `src/server/permissions.ts` is not touched. No skill lock, release, catalogue or built-in file is written.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-update-cli.test.ts`, `tests/skill-update-failures.test.ts`, `tests/skill-source-update.test.ts` (the YAML edit and the pull request text), `tests/helpers/skill-update-world.ts`; cases in `tests/skill-ls-remote.test.ts`, `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` (the git commands each file may start) and `tests/cli.test.ts`. Only the fakes are used: a local bare repository as upstream and as catalogue remote, and `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Update a source" and the command table).
- Settings 6c: approved releases and tools in the Skills card (#529). Part 3 of 3 of #495 (Settings 6).
  - **Approved releases.** `skills.releases.approved` is a row editor: each row has inputs for catalogue, version and digest (each with an aria-label naming the row and the field), a **Remove** button, and **Add a row**, disabled at 50 rows and when the page is read-only. Empty rows and fields are left out of the value, and values are trimmed. A server issue of a row is shown as `Row N, <field>: …`. Its hint has the help sentence, the default and when it takes effect; the "one per line" text is gone from this setting only. No stored data changes.
  - **Tools.** New group "Tools a skill may ask for" with `skills.tools.shell`, `skills.tools.network`, `skills.tools.filesystem` and `skills.tools.missing`, each with its default, help and when it takes effect.
  - **Confirmation.** Turning `skills.tools.network` on adds "Skills that ask for network access may pass the plan gate." Setting `skills.tools.missing` to `degrade` adds "A skill whose tool is not available no longer stops the run; it runs without that tool." Shell off and file access `read` only make things stricter, so they ask nothing.
  - **Every skills setting has a control.** No `skills.*` setting is left only in `config.yaml`, so none goes into the exceptions list of Settings 11 (#500).
  - **Code.** `ui/kit/forms.js`: the record branch of `settingControl` is the row editor (`recordRows`, built with the kit's `textInput`, `iconButton` and `button`); `recordOf` is gone. `ui/kit/core.js`: `settingHint` drops the format text for records. `ui/kit/forms.css`: `.scf-rows` and `.scf-rows__row`. `src/settings/describe.ts`: the new help of `skills.releases.approved`. `ui/settings-skills.js`: the tools group and the `RISKS` entries. The kit gets no new export; the gallery's "List of records" example shows the rows.
  - Tests: `tests/ui-kit-forms.test.ts` (rows, Add and Remove, trimming, the limit, disabled, error, hint), `tests/settings-describe.test.ts`, `tests/ui-settings-skills.test.ts` (the tools group, the risk sentences, `Row N` placement), `tests/ui-settings.test.ts` and `tests/ui-a11y-admin.test.ts` (names of the row inputs and buttons).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (the Skills paragraph of section 7 covers approved releases, tools and the two new risks).
- Epics view: one row per epic with progress, growth, the expected finish and what holds it up (#488). Part 6 of 7 of #408.
  - **What.** New page `#/epics` with a progress bar and text per row, the finish as a date range in the browser's time zone, "Too early to say", sorting by expected finish, "No epic" last, how fresh the data is, and loading, empty, error and no-permission states. Reads `GET /api/repos/:id/epics`.
  - **Navigation.** "Epics" is a main destination for admins and users; `MAX_PRIMARY` is 8. Saved views work on the page.
  - **Code.** `ui/epics.js`, `ui/epics-model.js`; `ui/ia.js`, `ui/views.js`, `ui/api.js`, `ui/auth.js`, both routers and `index.html` files; styles in `ui/css/pages/work.css`.
  - Tests: `tests/ui-epics-model.test.ts`, `tests/ui-epics.test.ts`; `ui-ia`, `ui-routers`, `ui-views`, `ui-palette`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` has a new section "Epics".

- Test runs no longer swamp the machine: they work at low priority, and the tests an agent starts itself count against the test slots.
  - **Low priority.** `tools/test-slot` runs its command with `nice` 10 (`FACTORY_TEST_NICE`; `0` = normal priority) and sets `FACTORY_IN_TEST_SLOT=1` for it. A slots folder that cannot be made no longer stops the tests: they run without a slot.
  - **Agent test gate.** New `tools/agent-bin`: small `npm`, `npx`, `pnpm`, `yarn`, `bun`, `bunx`, `pytest`, `py.test`, `python`, `python3`, `gradle`, `mvn`, `go` and `cargo` commands, first on the `PATH` of every agent step that is not in the OS sandbox (`testGateEnv` in `src/agents/run.ts`). A test command (`isTestCommand` in `_gate.mjs`) runs through `tools/test-slot` with the machine's slots folder; any other command runs the real one unchanged. Inside a slot nothing is gated again, so `npm test` calling `npx vitest` cannot wait for itself. An agent waits at most 300 s (`FACTORY_AGENT_TEST_WAIT_SEC`), then runs anyway.
  - **Why.** Agents ran the full suite on their own, outside the slots, next to the flows' own test steps: the load reached 6 per core, pages took seconds, and a story's implement step ran out of time.
  - **Not covered.** A wrapper started by path (`./gradlew test`) and steps in the OS sandbox.
  - Tests: new `tests/agent-test-gate.test.ts`; `tests/test-slot.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- A parked story no longer shows as waiting for you: removing its labels parks a stopped run (#531).
  - **What it does.** A run that stopped with questions (`send_back` or `ask_for_info`) whose issue no longer has the watcher's trigger label is parked at the next check. The overview (`scf board next`, Home, the Board) shows "parked — not tracked by a watcher" with action "Nothing", and **Needs you** leaves it out too. The watcher only confirms the label with GitHub for such runs, at most 10 checks per tick, and remembers a "not parked" answer for 10 minutes.
  - **Trigger label back.** When the trigger label is added again, the parked run is replaced by a new run; the overview follows the new run. A stopped run of a story that keeps its label is unchanged ("waiting for you — questions").
  - **Cancel a stopped run.** `scf runs cancel` and `POST /api/runs/:id/cancel` (and the Cancel button of a stopped run) now cancel a stopped run. It is saved as cancelled with the reason `cancelled by user while stopped`, shown as "cancelled — nothing to do". Nothing continues it, and the watcher removes its status label without starting or resuming anything. A cancelled run that was really interrupted while working still resumes at the next check as before. Refinement sessions are not cancelled this way.
  - **Code.** `src/engine/state.ts` (`parkedAt`, `setRunParked`, `STOPPED_CANCEL_REASON`), `src/engine/runner.ts` (`cancelStoppedRun`), `src/queue/scheduler.ts` (`cancel` of a stopped run, `setParked`), `src/queue/watcher.ts` (`parkUntracked`, the parked and cancelled-while-stopped cases), `src/next-step.ts` and `src/board.ts` (kind `parked`, `ended` on cancelled), `src/words.ts` (the two new status words), `src/remote/areas/work.ts` (help and message of `runs cancel`), `src/refinement/architect.ts` (a paused session is cancelled as before). `ui/home.js`, `ui/icons.js`, `ui/run-header.js` and `ui/runs.js` show the new kind and the Cancel button of a stopped run.
  - **Not changed.** Parking labels, `wait_*` stops (a release or a code area), and runs that fail or wait for approval.
