  - Tests: new `tests/parked-story.test.ts` (the overview and Needs you for a parked story), `tests/watch-parked.test.ts` (the watcher parks, the label comes back, a story with its label keeps "questions", the cancelled run), and `tests/cancel-stopped.test.ts` (cancelling a stopped run from the command line and the API); cases in `tests/next-step.test.ts`, `tests/board.test.ts`, `tests/words.test.ts`, `tests/ui-next.test.ts`, `tests/ui-run-header.test.ts`, `tests/ui-user-runs.test.ts` and `tests/remote-work.test.ts`. `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` gets `FAKE_GH_LABEL_FILTER` (an `issue list --label` answers only the labelled issues). `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` has the glossary rows, "Parked stories" under the watcher labels, the Cancel bullet, the "Why is nothing happening?" line and the API row.
- Settings 6b: a refused skills save shows each problem at its control (#528). Part 2 of 3 of #495 (Settings 6), part 7 of 11 of #407.
  - **The API.** A refused `PUT /api/config` still answers 400 with `error` starting `invalid config: `, and now also `issues`: up to 50 `{ path, message }` entries with dotted paths. No stored secret (such as the Slack webhook) appears in `issues` or `error`. Older clients keep working.
  - **The card.** Each `skills.*` issue is shown under the control with the longest described key that is its path or a dotted prefix of it (`skills.catalogue.include_x` does not match `skills.catalogue.include`). A list entry shows `Line N: …` (`Row N, <field>: …` for a record). Issues outside the skills keys stay in the banner. A successful save clears all field messages, and each holder is redrawn from the current value.
  - **Code.** `src/server/config-view.ts` adds `ConfigIssue` and `configIssues(e)` (undefined when `e` is not a `ZodError`); `src/server/api-admin.ts` passes the issues as `HttpError`'s `extra`. `ui/settings-skills.js` adds `issueKey`, `issueText` and `showIssues`; `ui/admin.js` calls `showIssues` on a save and on an error.
  - **Docs.** The `PUT config` rule in `src/server/permissions.ts` and its row in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` describe `issues`; the Skills paragraph of section 7 says where the messages appear.
  - Tests: `tests/config-view.test.ts` (paths, the plain error, the cap of 50), `tests/audit-actions.test.ts` (the include/exclude conflict and the webhook check), `tests/ui-settings-skills.test.ts` (`issueKey`, `issueText`, the blank-line regression) and `tests/ui-settings.test.ts` (a 400 with issues shows each message in its field).
- A story that fixes failing tests can run: the red baseline stops only when a failing test is not named in the issue (#530).
  - **What it does.** An issue lists the tests it is meant to fix under a `## Failing tests to fix` heading, one per line, as `Class.method` (a class name alone covers its tests). When the tests fail before any change and every failing test is named, the run goes on instead of stopping at `baseline_failed`. The run log says `BASELINE: going on`, and the report on the issue has a "Tests before the change" section. A failing test that is not named still stops the run, and the stop lists the tests not named.
  - **Green baseline.** When the tests pass before the change, the named tests are noted as already passing. Nothing else changes.
  - **Merge rule.** A run that went on is checked after the change (`test_develop` and `test_main`, and the hotfix path of `issue-gitflow`): the named tests must pass, and every test that passed before must still pass. A test that failed before and is now missing also fails the check.
  - **Failure summary.** A `baseline_failed` stop is always a code problem with fixed text ("The tests failed before any change…") and the failing tests. No model call, so it never reads as an environment failure.
  - **Code.** New `tools/baseline-tests` (modes `red`, `green`, `verify`, `purge`) reads the JUnit XML reports; `scripts/build-flows.mjs` adds the `fixed_tests` and `fix_fixed_tests` steps and the note; `src/failure.ts` and `src/failure-explain.ts` give the fixed text. `flows/issue-gitflow.yaml`, `flows/issue-code-daily.yaml` and `tests/fixtures/flows/issue-deliver.yaml` were edited by hand to match the script; `node scripts/build-flows.mjs` must show no difference.
  - **Not changed.** Flaky tests (one retry as before), the release flow's baseline on `main`, and a repository without JUnit XML reports (the run stops and says so).
  - Tests: new `tests/baseline-tests-tool.test.ts` (the four cases, the green case, class names, a test deleted after the change) and `tests/baseline-red-flow.test.ts`; cases in `tests/failure-summary.test.ts` and `tests/failure-explain.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` has the section "Stories that fix failing tests".
- Settings 6a: the Skills card on the Settings page — skill sources, catalogue, selection, review checks and skills that cannot be used (#527). Part 1 of 3 of #495 (Settings 6), part 6 of 11 of #407.
  - **The card.** New **Skills** card after **Safety**, with five groups: "Where skills come from", "Catalogue for the planner", "Selection", "Review checks" and "Skills that cannot be used". It has one control for each `skills.builtin`, `skills.roots`, `skills.repository`, `skills.releases.allow_unreleased`, `skills.catalogue.*`, `skills.selection.*`, `skills.review.*` and `skills.unresolved.*` key, built by `settingControl` from `GET /api/settings`. `releases.approved` and `skills.tools.*` come in part 3.
  - **Save.** The PUT body keeps every other skills key unchanged, including `releases.approved` and `tools`. Before the save, a confirmation dialog lists one sentence per change that lowers safety; **Cancel** sends nothing and says "Not saved.", **Save anyway** saves. Risky changes: `skills.repository` turned on, `skills.releases.allow_unreleased` turned on, a folder added to `skills.roots`, any `skills.unresolved.{unknown,missing,untrusted,conflict,oversized}` set to `warn`, and a term removed from `skills.unresolved.high_risk`. A value that was already risky when last saved, a removed folder and turning a risky value back off ask nothing. After a successful save the baseline moves to the saved values.
  - **No descriptions.** When the server sends no skills descriptions, the card says "The skills settings could not be described by the server." and saving keeps `skills` unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `ui/settings-skills.js`: `SKILL_GROUPS`, `valueAt`, `withValue` (pure; the input is never changed), `RISKS`, `riskyChanges(saved, draft)` and `skillsSection(descriptions, skills, { disabled })`. `ui/api.js` has `api.settings()`; `ui/admin.js` loads it with the config and asks for the dialog before the PUT. The module is in `CLEAN` in `tests/ui-inline-styles.test.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-settings-skills.test.ts` (helpers, `riskyChanges` cases, one control per key with ranges and hints); `tests/ui-settings.test.ts` (the card, the save body, the dialog with Cancel and "Save anyway", no dialog for a non-risky change); `GET /api/settings` added to the stubs in `tests/ui-a11y-admin.test.ts` and `tests/ui-settings-states.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (section 7 "Skills" and one sentence in "Skill sources").
- Epics API: the epics of a repository and one epic, read from the saved records and the watchers, without a call to GitHub (#487). Part 5 of 7 of #408.
  - **Routes.** New `src/server/api-epics.ts`: `GET /api/repos/:id/epics` gives one row per epic and "No epic", sorted by expected finish (finished epics last, with when and how long), with counts, growth, the forecast range and reason, and what holds each up. `GET /api/repos/:id/epics/:issue` gives one epic: its stories in build order, the chains, the critical path, the series per day, the pace, the median story time and the forecast per day. An epic that is a label has `id` `l-` and the base64url of the label, so a label with `/`, `%` or spaces can be opened; an issue epic has `#<number>`. Both routes are wired after `repoRoutes` in `src/server/server.ts`.
  - **Access.** The owner of a repository gets it, an admin gets all. Anyone else gets 404, as the other `repos/:id` routes do. Rules are in `src/server/permissions.ts`, examples in `tests/permissions.test.ts`. Both routes are listed as API-only in `src/remote/areas/index.ts`.
  - **No GitHub call.** The routes read the saved issue list (`readIssueList`), the saved forecasts and the watchers' records, and the story states from the same sources as the board (`boardSources` in `src/server/board.ts`, split out of `boardFor`). A non-admin gets user words, and a hold that a watcher reports with admin words is replaced by the run's own record (`forUser` in `collectNext`). `tests/api-epics.test.ts` fails if the fake `gh` is called.
  - **Freshness and no data.** The answer says when the issue list was read and how old it is. A repository without an enabled issues watcher, or with no issue list read yet, answers "no data" with the reason (`NO_DATA_WORDS`).
  - **What holds it up.** `holdUps` in `src/epics/view.ts` names the kinds `person` (a story waits for an answer or a plan approval, each with its own instruction), `failed` (a failed story at the head of a chain), `limit` (a usage limit), `no-slot` (no free slot) and `not-handed` (stories not handed to the Foundry). The words are the ones of `src/next-step.ts`.
  - **Daily forecast.** A route saves the day's forecast for each unfinished epic, once per UTC day. New `saveMissingForecasts` in `src/epics/forecast-store.ts` checks "no entry for today" inside the lock, so a saved day is kept when two requests race. It waits up to 500 ms for the lock and does not block the event loop. If the save fails, the route logs it (`diagLog`) and still answers. `saveForecasts` is unchanged.
  - **Pure view.** `epicsView(input)` and `epicView(input, key)` in `src/epics/view.ts` do the work and read no file and no clock (`now` is in the input). The route only loads the input. `toStored` is exported from `src/epics/forecast-store.ts`; the duration text comes from `span` in `src/estimate.ts`.
  - **Not changed.** No secret and no issue body text is in the answers. No page calls the routes yet (the Epics view and the epic page come in #489).
  - Tests: new `tests/epics-view.test.ts` (sorting, counts, growth, the hold-up kinds, the no-data cases, the label ids, no issue body in the answer) and `tests/api-epics.test.ts` (access for the owner, another user and an admin, no-data for a repository without a watcher, the daily save once and when a save fails, one label epic, the 404 and 400 cases, the fake `gh` log stays empty); new cases in `tests/epics-forecast-store.test.ts` (`saveMissingForecasts`, a held lock) and `tests/permissions.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` has the two rows of the permission table.
- Skill source connector 4: `scf skills overlap` says, for each capability a staged source shares with the catalogue, which skill is canonical or where the conflict is (#512). Part 4 of 5 of #218.
  - **Command.** New `scf skills overlap <source-id>` in `src/skills/cli.ts` (`overlapImport`). It reads the staged import, normalizes it, applies the source profile and compares the result with the registered skills (built-in and administrator). Offline and read-only: nothing is written, loaded, pinned or run. No run reads its report.
  - **Lines.** `CANONICAL <capability>: <id@version> (<level>)` for the winner, then one `REJECTED <id@version>: <code>` per loser. `CONFLICT <capability>: <tie|mandatory|winners-overlap> <ids>` when no single skill can win. `NONE <capability>: no skill can be used` when every candidate is rejected. `NEW <capability>: <id@version>` for a capability only the source declares. A skill with a provider prints `provider <name>` on its line. The last line is `OVERLAP` with the counts: shared, new, vendor-only and conflicts.
  - **Pure part.** New `src/skills/overlap.ts`: `overlapReport(staged, registered, policy)` builds the report and `overlapLines` prints it. It reuses `collisionCandidate` and `classifySkillCollisions` (`src/skills/collisions.ts`), `canonicalFromCollision` and `canonicalFromRegistered` (`src/skills/canonical-facts.ts`) and `selectCanonicalSkills` (`src/skills/canonical.ts`). The exclusive set is every capability with two or more providers. The high-risk terms are `skills.unresolved.high_risk`.
  - **Scope.** A vendor-scoped capability is shared only with a registered skill of the same provider, never with a portable one. The `OVERLAP` line counts the capabilities the profile kept vendor-only. A capability that is portable for one staged skill and vendor-scoped for another is a `PROBLEM` (`scope-collision`).
  - **Gates.** The licence verdict of the import (with `licence.reviewed` applied) and the scanner decide the gates. A skill that fails the licence or safety gate is `REJECTED` with that code and can never be canonical.
  - **Exit codes and output.** 0 when there is no conflict and no `PROBLEM`, otherwise 1. The output is the same for any input order and holds only slugs, versions, fixed codes and levels; a damaged staged import prints a fixed text.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-overlap.test.ts` (pure: a community skill loses to a built-in skill on trust tier, a tie on a high-risk capability is a conflict, a vendor-only source with no overlap, gate failures, order and printed text) and new `tests/skill-overlap-cli.test.ts` (the command, its exit codes and its problems). `tests/skill-cli.test.ts` has the usage case. Fixture skill: `tests/fixtures/skills/overlap/review-guide`.
  - `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` now lists `overlap.ts` as a file that may import `source-skill-evaluation.js`, and `registeredCandidate` is exported for it.
  - `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`: "Overlap with the catalogue" and the command table.
- Epics 4: the expected finish of each epic as a range, and one saved forecast per epic per day (#486). Part 4 of 7 of #408.
  - **What it does.** New pure module `src/epics/forecast.ts`: `forecastEpic` returns the expected finish of one epic as a range (`earliest`, `expected`, `latest`), with the one or two drivers that move it most, or "too early to say". `forecastEpics` does this for every epic of a model and also returns the daily pace of the last 14 days (`pace`) for the page. No files, no clock (`now` is a parameter), no server.
  - **Rule.** Epic pace = repository pace × (this epic's stories left ÷ all stories left). Expected days = stories left × split growth ÷ epic pace. The lower bound is the longest remaining chain × the median story time. The range widens with the day-to-day spread of the pace and with a small done share, and each end is at most a factor of three from the expected finish.
  - **Pace.** `paceDays` counts the done stories in 14 windows of 24 hours ending at `now`. Stories closed in the future or with a bad time are ignored.
  - **Split growth.** `splitGrowth` gives the parts per story. A story is a part only when its `partOf` names another story of the same epic; a `partOf` that names the epic issue is plain membership. The growth applies to the stories left that are not parts yet, and is never below 1.
  - **Median.** `medianRunMs` in `src/estimate.ts` is the median working time of a run that built a story, from the same history as the run estimate. Only the `issue-gitflow` and `issue-code-daily` runs count; runs that only split an issue are left out (`buildHistory` now keeps them apart as `built`). Undefined below `MIN_SAMPLES`.
  - **Too early.** Fewer than `MIN_SAMPLES` stories of the repository finished in the 14 days gives "too early to say"; an epic with no stories is `no-stories`. A finished epic has no forecast.
  - **Left out.** Every forecast names what it leaves out: stops for usage limits and for a person's answer.
  - **Store.** New `src/epics/forecast-store.ts`: `saveForecasts` keeps one forecast per epic per UTC day in `<data>/epic-forecasts/<repo>.json`. A save on the same day overwrites that day; other days and epics are kept. History starts on the day of the first save and is never back-filled; up to 732 days per epic are kept. The file is written through a temporary file and a rename. Read, merge and write run under a lock file: a save waits up to 10 s, takes over a lock older than 30 s, and otherwise fails with a clear error. `readForecasts` and `forecastHistory` read it; `dropForecasts` removes it.
  - **Not used yet.** No route, page or command calls these modules yet; the route and the page come in #487 and #489.
  - Tests: new `tests/epics-forecast.test.ts` (more stories left later, faster pace earlier, the chain floor, too little history, the range width, a finished epic, split growth), `tests/epics-forecast-store.test.ts` (same-day overwrite, held and stale lock, a failed write keeps the history) and `tests/estimate-median.test.ts` (story flows only, the sample threshold). `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is not changed, because no user-visible behaviour changed yet.
- Settings 5: the monitor's thresholds and limits on the Problems page, with range, default, help and when a change takes effect, and **Back to default** (#494). Part 5 of 11 of #407.
  - **Thresholds.** The Detectors table shows one number field per threshold of each detector (`self-update` and `detector-failed` still say "none"). Each field shows its help, default, allowed range and "Takes effect at once.", built from the descriptions of `describeSettings()`.
  - **Monitor settings.** New section on the Problems page, between the Mutes and the Detectors: **Bug stories** (`report_to` with a warning that bug stories and their evidence are written to that repository, `report_limits.per_day`, `report_limits.per_check`, `cooldown_minutes`, `fix_wait_days`) and **Circuit breaker** (`breaker.new_findings`, `breaker.within_minutes`, `breaker.failed_fixes`). The section is not drawn when the answer has no `settings`.
  - **Checks.** A value outside the range, a fraction where a whole number is needed, an empty number or a `report_to` that is not `owner/repo` is not sent. The error line names the field label and the range and marks the input `aria-invalid`. `settingProblem` in `ui/problems.js` does the check; the server still refuses through zod.
  - **Back to default.** New admin route `POST /api/settings/reset` (`{ key }`, rule in `src/server/permissions.ts`): it removes the key from `config.yaml` through the new `resetConfigText` and `removeYamlKey` in `src/config.ts` and `src/config-edit.ts`. A parent left empty is removed too; the comment on the removed line goes with it, comment lines above it stay. The route refuses with 409 for a stale `X-Config-Version`, 400 for an unknown key, and refuses the removal when any other setting would change. It writes an audit line when something is removed. The button is enabled only when the key is stored in `config.yaml` (`stored`), so an explicitly stored default can be reset too.
  - **API.** `GET /api/monitor` returns `settings`: every `monitor.*` description with its `value` and `stored`. `settingValue` in `src/settings/describe.ts` is the exported former `valueAt`. The monitor keys are marked `control: "problems.js"`, so later parts do not build a second editor for them.
  - **Tests.** `tests/config-save.test.ts` (`resetConfigText` cases, a flow-style comment case), `tests/settings-describe.test.ts` (`control`, `settingValue`), `tests/monitor-problems-api.test.ts` (`settings`, the reset route and its 409 and 400 cases), `tests/ui-problems.test.ts` (fields, the range and whole-number messages, the owner/repo check, Back to default, the section, an answer without `settings`), `tests/permissions.test.ts` and `tests/ui-a11y-admin.test.ts`.
- Settings 7: "Restart when no story is running" for settings that need a restart (#496). Part 7 of 11 of #407.
  - **Page.** After a save that changed a setting marked "after a restart" (for example `server.listen`), the Settings page says "A restart is needed" with the running and the saved value. Under the supervisor it offers **Restart when no story is running**; otherwise it says to stop and start the server by hand. The box is refreshed every 5 seconds.
  - **Waiting.** The restart works like a new version: the queue and the watchers drain, no new runs start, and when no run is active the server exits with code 75 and the supervisor starts it again. While it waits the box shows the reason, the active runs and **Cancel the restart**; cancelling resumes the queue and the watchers. A new-version or data-folder restart takes over a settings wait, and then the wait cannot be cancelled.
  - **Routes.** New `src/server/api-restart.ts`: `GET`, `POST` and `DELETE /api/restart`, admin only, with rules in `src/server/permissions.ts` and examples in `tests/permissions.test.ts`. `POST` answers 409 when the server is not supervised (`FACTORY_SUPERVISED` is not `1`) or no saved setting needs a restart.
  - **Audit.** Asking writes `restart-request` (target `server`, detail: the changed settings); cancelling writes `restart-cancel`. Both are in `EVENT_ACTIONS` (`src/auth/audit.ts`).
  - **Code.** `RestartState` (`src/server/next.ts`) has the reason `settings`. `src/cli.ts` sets `ctx.settingsRestart`; `startServer` alone sets none, so the server tests never exit the process. `WatcherManager.sync()` starts no watcher while a restart waits, and `health.ts` counts only the active runs during a drain. `Scheduler.resume()` ends a drain.
  - Tests: new `tests/api-restart.test.ts` (the wait, cancel, the takeover, the audit lines, the 409 cases and the exit, without a real restart); cases in `tests/scheduler-drain.test.ts`, `tests/next-step.test.ts`, `tests/ui-settings.test.ts` and `tests/permissions.test.ts`; `tests/ui-audit.test.ts` now expects 39 action choices.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (section 7 "Restart when no story is running", the API table and the audit list).

- Community Spring source 2: the Java and Spring versions of a project are read from its build files, and a skill that declares a stale version is never chosen (#519).
  - **What is detected.** `buildRepoProfile` reads the Java version from `pom.xml` (`java.version`, then `maven.compiler.release`, then `maven.compiler.source`) and from `build.gradle` / `build.gradle.kts` (`JavaLanguageVersion.of`, then `sourceCompatibility`). Spring Boot comes from a Maven `<parent>` or `<plugin>`, a Gradle plugin `org.springframework.boot`, or a Boot BOM (`platform` / `enforcedPlatform`). Spring Framework comes from its `org.springframework:` dependencies and BOMs. The Maven and Gradle versions come from `distributionUrl` in the wrapper files `.mvn/wrapper/maven-wrapper.properties` and `gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties`. Only plain versions are kept: `latest`, `3.+` and `^3.2` give no version, and `2.3.4.RELEASE` is `2.3.4`. A URL with a user name or password is never stored, only the version.
  - **Five names.** `java`, `spring-boot`, `spring-framework`, `maven` and `gradle`. `projectTechnologies` in `src/skills/project-technologies.ts` returns them in that order. Other spellings are aliases (`jdk`, `openjdk`, `springboot`, `spring`, `spring-core`, `mvn`, `apache-maven`, `gradle-wrapper`, `gradlew`) and map to a name in `src/skills/technology-rules.ts`.
  - **Modules.** With `affectedModules`, a module uses the build folders in or above it, nearest first. Modules with different versions give one entry per version, so the range has to fit all of them. A Boot 2 module below a Boot 3 root is seen as Boot 2 for that module.
  - **Supported lines.** `SUPPORTED_LINES` in `src/skills/technology-rules.ts`: Java 17, 21 and 25; Spring Boot 4.0 and 4.1; Spring Framework 7.0; Maven 3.9 and 4.0; Gradle 8.14 and 9. The date of the last review is `TECHNOLOGY_SUPPORT_REVIEWED`. Java 8 and 11 are not listed.
  - **Stale.** `supportStatus` gives `stale` when a declared version fits none of the supported lines of its technology, `unchecked` when a declared range cannot be read, `supported` otherwise, and `generic` when the skill declares none of the five names. A stale declaration is `incompatible` in every canonical selection, with or without project evidence. An import whose skill is stale gets the outcome `stale` in its evaluation, and it is not chosen even when it is the only skill for its capability.
  - **Version ranges.** The range code moved to `src/skills/version-range.ts` (`parsePartial`, `rangeSatisfied`, `rangeAdmitsLine`). `rangeSatisfied` is still exported from `canonical-facts.ts`. Names are compared after alias mapping.
  - **Limits.** Technology findings come last under the per-kind limit and are the first to be dropped when the profile is too large, so existing findings keep their place. Spring dependency versions that are not plain are dropped.
  - **Not changed.** The table is data: the owner changes it by pull request, and each release line is listed only while the owner accepts it for new work. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (Skill library sources) describes the user-visible rule.
- Settings 4: the missing Settings page controls, and the start-up settings in view (#493). Part 4 of 11 of #407.
  - **Controls.** The Agents card has `isolate_agents` (switch), `failure_summary.enabled` (switch) and `failure_summary.model` (text). The User runs card has `step_env.pass` and `step_env.agent_pass` (list editors) and `sandbox.user_read` (list editor of absolute paths). Each shows its default, one sentence on what it does and when it takes effect, taken from the description of #492.
  - **Refused at the field.** A value the server would refuse cannot be saved. The message is shown at the field and says what is wrong: a name that starts with `FACTORY_`, `SCF_`, `GH_`, `GITHUB_`, `GIT_` or `SSH_`, a name that is not a variable name, a relative path in `sandbox.user_read`, or more than the allowed number of items. The rules are the ones of `src/config.ts` (`STEP_ENV_NAME`, `STEP_ENV_REFUSED`, the absolute-path check); `ui/admin-sections.js` repeats them and a test pins them.
  - **Hand-built sections.** Network, audit retention, budget and capacity, protected branches, hotfixes, secret scan, notifications, bot identity, GitHub App, sandbox image and user runs, and self-update show the default, the help and "takes effect" under each control, from the same description. The values and the saved file are unchanged.
  - **Set at start-up.** A read-only card lists the start-up settings (data folder, port, repository, and the Claude Code, Codex and GitHub CLI programs) with their value and how to change them.
  - **View as another account.** The new controls are read-only there, as the rest of the page is (`lockReadOnly`).
  - **Code.** New `ui/admin-sections.js` (`settingProblem`, `describedSections`, `startupSection`, `hintFor`, `hinted`) keeps `ui/admin.js` below the line limit. `ui/kit/forms.js` and `ui/kit/core.js` have the shared control and hint helpers; `src/settings/describe.ts` covers the keys of the new cards. No server route or setting changed.
  - Tests: new `tests/ui-admin-sections.test.ts` (refusal rules, absolute paths, item limit, the start-up card, the rules pinned against `src/config.ts`); cases in `tests/ui-settings.test.ts`, `tests/ui-settings-states.test.ts`, `tests/ui-a11y-admin.test.ts` and `tests/settings-describe.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` section 7 and the step variables and read paths are updated.
- Skill sources: a source profile keeps portable and vendor-specific capabilities apart (#510). Part 2 of 5 of #218.
  - **Profile.** A `skill-sources.yaml` entry takes an optional `profile`: `provider` (a slug, required), `portable` (a map of upstream capability to catalogue capability, at most 64) and `products` (default provider products with version ranges, 1 to 16). A source without `profile` loads and behaves as before; `schema` stays 1. `parseSources` in `src/skills/sources.ts` checks it, and the result is `SkillLibrarySource.profile` with `portable` and `products` sorted by upstream name.
  - **Apply.** New pure module `src/skills/source-profile.ts` with `applySourceProfile(skill, source)`. A capability named in `portable` becomes the mapped slug; every other one becomes `<provider>-<capability>` (not doubled when it already starts with the provider). A skill with any vendor-scoped capability gets the provider block (`package.provider`), with the profile's products when the skill declares none. A skill that declares another provider is a problem. `profileSkill` returns the problems instead of throwing. Only `package.capabilities` and `package.provider` change: `normalizeSkill` is unchanged, the provenance keeps the upstream values, and the digest and fingerprint do not move. The result does not depend on input order, and the input is not mutated.
  - **Slug problems.** A slug longer than 64 characters, or two upstream names that land on one slug, is a problem that names the skill and the capability. Nothing is dropped silently; `applySourceProfile` throws `SourceProfileError` with all problems.
  - **Check.** `scf skills sources check` prints the provider and the number of portable mappings on the source line (`provider <name> (<n> portable)`), and refuses a `portable` mapping to an invalid slug, a duplicate upstream key and a `profile` without `provider`.
  - **Not used yet.** No import or scan calls `applySourceProfile`; the profile only checks and describes the source.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-source-profile.test.ts` (vendor prefixing, portable mapping, mixed skills, slug collisions, absent profile, provider conflict) and cases in `tests/skill-sources.test.ts` (parser problems, sorting, the 64-mapping limit).
  - `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`: "Skill library sources" ("Source profile").
- Community Spring source 3: `scf skills compare` compares a staged import with the built-in `java` and `spring-boot` skills (#520). Part 3 of 4 of #224.
  - **What it does.** New command `scf skills compare <source-id>`: one `SKILL` line per imported skill with its tier (`vetted-community` when the source is enabled and reviewed at the approved commit, otherwise `discovery-only`), `EXCLUDED` for a stale or incompatible skill, `NO-OVERLAP` for a skill with no mapped capability, a `COLLISION` line per collision, and exactly one `RECOMMEND` or `CONFLICT` line per capability shared with a built-in skill. Offline and read-only: nothing is written, loaded or run. `COMPARED` ends the report.
  - **Pure module.** New `src/skills/compare.ts`: `compareWithBuiltins` takes the source, the normalized skills, the registered built-in skills and the owner overrides, and returns a `CompareReport`. `compareLines` prints it in a fixed order. Built-in packages are adapted to collision members (`builtinCollisionCandidate`, source `builtin`). `classifySkillCollisions` and the canonical selection are reused unchanged; the built-in skill wins on trust tier over a `vetted-community` skill unless an override says otherwise.
  - **Capability table.** `COMPARE_BUILTINS`, `COMMUNITY_CAPABILITY_RULES` and the term lists in `src/skills/community-rules.ts` map upstream tags, keywords and capability names to built-in capability names (for example `spring-boot` + `transactions` → `spring-boot-transactions`, `java` → `java-language`). Used for the comparison only; no package or digest changes.
  - **Command.** New `src/skills/compare-cli.ts` (`compareStaged`, `loadBuiltins`), wired in `src/skills/cli.ts` (`SKILLS_USAGE`, `ALLOWED`) and in `src/cli.ts`, where it runs without a data folder like `assess`. `slugify` in `src/skills/normalize.ts` is now exported for reuse.
  - **Exit codes.** 0 when there is no conflict and no blocking collision; 1 for a conflict, a blocking collision or a `PROBLEM` (for example no staged import, a source without `ref.approved`, or an unreadable built-in skill). The same input gives the same output in any order.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-compare.test.ts` (pure: mapping, stale and incompatible exclusion, tiers, recommendations and conflicts, order independence), new `tests/skill-compare-cli.test.ts` (staged fixture, no network); cases in `tests/cli.test.ts` (help) and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` (the new modules read no network).
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (section "Compare an import", and the command table).
- Skill source connector 4: `scf skills selections evaluate` reports which selected skills of a staged import are eligible or held back, and how they overlap (#517). Part 4 of 4 of #221.
  - **Command name.** The issue asked for `scf skills evaluate <source-id> [<selections-file>]`, but that name is already the git-fetching command of #219. The offline command is `scf skills selections evaluate <source-id> [<selections-file>]`, next to `scf skills selections check`. `scf skills evaluate` is unchanged.
  - **What it does.** Offline and read-only. It reads `skill-sources.yaml`, the staged import with its licence evidence, `skill-selections.yaml`, `skill-scan-exceptions.yaml`, `skill-overrides.yaml` (when present) and the skill registry. It writes nothing and starts no process. Nothing reads its result during a run.
  - **Output.** One `ELIGIBLE <id> <scope> <product>@<version> sdk <name>@<version>` or `HELD <id>: <reason codes>` line per skill, then one `OVERLAP` line per collision, then `EVALUATED <n> skills from <repository>@<commit>: <m> eligible`. A missing licence file, a bad input file or a tampered import is a `PROBLEM`; a `PROBLEM` for missing licence evidence asks you to import again. Credential-like text is shown as `(hidden)`.
  - **Exit codes.** 0 when the evaluation ran, even when skills are held. 1 for a `PROBLEM` or a usage error.
  - **Held, not failed.** A skill that needs a CLI, MCP server, credential or service, or that the import excluded (MCP or plugin configuration, network use), is `HELD … needs-unavailable-tools` and never stops the command.
  - **Overrides.** Only owner overrides with `scope: all` apply. Project pins are not read, and an expired override is noted and not used.
  - **Not changed.** `evaluateSourceSkills`, the collision and canonical rules, `scf skills scan` and the existing `scf skills evaluate` keep their behaviour. The shared readers (`stagedImport`, `loadExceptions`, `hidden`, `plural`, `filesOf`) moved unchanged from `src/skills/cli.ts` to new `src/skills/staged-cli.ts`, which keeps `cli.ts` under 500 lines.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/evaluate-cli.ts` (`selectionsEvaluate`), wired in the `selections` branch of `skillsCommand`; help line in `src/cli.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` "Evaluate an import" has a worked `microsoft/skills` example. The example source entry is a placeholder: the owner verifies the upstream licence and commit. No approved Microsoft commit ships with the Foundry.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-selections-evaluate-cli.test.ts`; additions to `tests/cli.test.ts` (help line, data folder not moved) and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` (only `cli.ts` imports the new module; no write or stage calls in the new modules).
- Skill source connector 2: source profiles (#523). Part 2 of 4 of #222.
  - **File.** New `skill-source-profiles.yaml` at the repository root, shipped with `schema: 1` and `sources: []`. An empty list is valid. It is not in the package `files`.
  - **Parser and reader.** New `src/skills/source-profiles.ts` with `parseSourceProfiles` and `readSourceProfiles`. It checks each source's skills (name, class `portable` or `vendor`, capabilities, `high_risk`, `requires` and `evaluations`) and the limits (200 sources, 100 skills per source, 32 capabilities per skill, 16 items per list). The file is read with the same safety rules as `skill-sources.yaml`. A capability cannot be used by both a portable and a vendor skill.
  - **Output safety.** A message repeats only a valid slug or a plain name. Credential-like values and URLs with user info are never printed; the existing `shownValue` is not used for this.
  - **Command.** `scf skills profiles check [<file>]` lists each skill and then `OK <n> skills in <m> sources, <k> exclusive`, and exits 0. Otherwise it prints one `PROBLEM` line per error and exits 1. It works offline and does not need the data folder. `profiles` is also a read-only command in `src/cli.ts`.
  - **Not used yet.** Nothing reads the file during an import or a run.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-source-profiles.test.ts` (parser, reader, output safety for each field position); new cases in `tests/skill-cli.test.ts`; one spawned case in `tests/cli.test.ts`.
- Skill source connector 3: one verdict per imported skill: selection, licence, provider scope and overlaps (#516). Part 3 of 4 of #221.
  - **What it does.** New pure module `src/skills/source-skill-evaluation.ts` with `evaluateSourceSkills`, which takes the source, the normalized skills, the selections, the licence status per skill root, the blocked skill ids from the scan, the registered skills and a `CanonicalPolicy`. Each skill gets `eligible` and a sorted list of fixed reason codes (`SOURCE_SKILL_REASONS`). `src/skills/source-evaluation.ts` re-exports it. No file system, process or clock; no run calls it yet.
  - **Selection.** A skill with no selection is `not-selected`; a selection whose digest differs from the skill is `selection-stale`.
  - **Licence.** A skill whose licence status is not `allowed`, or is unknown, is `licence`.
  - **Requirements.** Declared requirements become connectors on a copy of the package (`selectionConnectors`), and the risk is raised to at least `medium`. The input skill is never changed. Such a skill is `needs-unavailable-tools` and never wins.
  - **Undeclared Azure needs.** New data-only `src/skills/provider-rules.ts` lists Azure markers: the `az` and `azd` CLIs, Azure identity classes such as `DefaultAzureCredential`, portal and service host names, and Azure MCP tool names. A marker in a skill declared `portable` is `provider-content-in-portable`; a marker whose requirement is not declared is `undeclared-requirement`. Reports name the rule, never the text.
  - **Metadata.** Product and SDK become `technologies`, and the declared category and capabilities are added, so the compatibility and collision checks see them.
  - **Overlaps.** Imported and registered skills go through `classifySkillCollisions` and `selectCanonicalSkills` unchanged. The result lists each collision id, class, reason and shared capabilities. A registered skill that is the same artifact as an imported one is not reported as a collision.
  - **Provider rule.** A `provider` skill cannot win a capability that an eligible `portable` candidate also provides, unless `policy.pins` or `policy.overrides` names it. Otherwise it is `provider-without-policy` and the selection runs again.
  - **Order and bounds.** The result is the same for any input order and is bounded by `COLLISION_LIMITS.candidates`.
  - **Not changed.** `canonical.ts`, `collisions.ts`, their rule files and `canonical-facts.ts`.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-source-skill-evaluation.test.ts` (every reason code, the Cosmos DB skill against built-in `cassandra`, pin and override, order independence, input immutability); `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` guards the new modules. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is not changed, because no user-visible behaviour changed.
- Settings 3: one description of every setting (#492). Part 3 of 11 of #407.
  - **Description.** New `src/settings/describe.ts`: `describeSettings()` lists 60 settings, each with its label, help text, kind (`number`, `yes-no`, `text`, `list`, `choice`), range, default, whether it is optional, and when a change takes effect. The ranges and defaults are read from the settings schema (`src/config.ts`), so the description and the validation cannot disagree; tests check them against the schema. `unsupportedSettings()` names the keys the description does not cover (`watchers`, `providers`).
  - **Route.** `GET /api/settings` (admin only, rule in `src/server/permissions.ts`) answers the description and `startup`: the home folder, port, repository, Claude Code path, Codex path and GitHub CLI path, with their values. No secret is returned. The route is also listed as API-only in `src/remote/areas/index.ts`.
  - **Claude Code is found without blocking.** `resolveClaudeBinAsync` in `src/steps/claude.ts` replaces `resolveClaudeBin`. One `claude --version` is run and shared by the settings route, the providers check (`src/agents/health.ts`) and agent steps (`src/agents/run.ts`); a failed check is remembered and `claude` is used from the PATH. Only the first call waits for the child process; the server keeps answering. `FACTORY_CLAUDE_BIN` skips the check.
  - **Settings control.** New `settingControl` in `ui/kit/forms.js` builds one form control from a description. It gives a number, switch, text, list, list of records or choice field, with the hint and error text. It is shown in the UI gallery (`ui/gallery/registry.js`).
  - **Not done.** No page uses the description yet (parts 4 to 6); only the keys named in the issue are described.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/settings-describe.test.ts` (schema, completeness, numbers, choices and list limits against the schema, and that no secret is returned); `tests/claude-bin.test.ts` rewritten for the async resolver, including a shared slow probe and an overlap with a health request; cases in `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/ui-kit-forms.test.ts`; the gallery count in `tests/ui-gallery.test.ts` is now 33. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` has the new row in the permission table.
- Skill source connector for Anthropic's mixed-licence skills: `licence.reviewed` forces a re-review when any licence or notice file of the repository changes, and provider assumptions are shown (#217). Part of #196.
  - **What it does.** New connector `anthropic-skills` (`https://github.com/anthropics/skills`: root `skills`, adapter `agent-skills`, trust `verified`, licence `Apache-2.0`, track `main`, no commit) with `licenceReview: "required"`. A source of that repository needs a matching `licence.reviewed`, and so does any source that sets one.
  - **Licence review.** New pure module `src/skills/licence-review.ts`. Its digest covers every licence and notice file that can apply to a staged skill (path, kind, content hash or problem) and the decision for every skill folder, so adding, changing or removing a root `LICENSE` changes it, also when every skill keeps its own licence. The commit id and the repository key are not part of it. `promoteImport` refuses a path with status `review` when `licence.reviewed` is missing or does not match. `skill-sources.yaml` takes `licence.reviewed` (a `sha256:` digest, checked by `parseSources`).
  - **Check.** `scf skills promote check` prints `LICENCES <digest> <n> licence and notice files: <state>` and `ASSUMES <id>: <codes>` lines. It does not print a new command.
  - **Provider assumptions.** New pure module `src/skills/provider-assumptions.ts`: `claude-tools`, `mcp-servers`, `claude-surface` and `anthropic-api`, found in the declared tools, `compatibility` and the instructions. Recorded as `SkillProvenance.provider.assumes` (absent when none, and in provenance written before #217). They declare only: an imported `allowed-tools` still grants nothing, and the tool profile is unchanged.
  - **Not used yet.** No run reads `licence.reviewed` or the assumptions.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-licence-review.test.ts`, `tests/skill-licence-review-cost.test.ts`, `tests/skill-provider-assumptions.test.ts` and `tests/skill-source-anthropic.test.ts`; additions to `tests/skill-sources.test.ts`, `tests/skill-promotion.test.ts`, `tests/skill-connectors.test.ts`, `tests/skill-promote-check-cli.test.ts`, `tests/skill-source-evaluation.test.ts`, `tests/skill-ls-remote.test.ts` and `tests/skill-cli.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Skill library sources", "Check a promotion") and `docs/DESIGN.md` ("The skill path").
- Skill source connectors 3: `scf skills review` prints the licence, attribution and collision report of a staged import (#507). Part 3 of 4 of #215.
  - **What it does.** `scf skills review <source-id> [--markdown]` reads `skill-sources.yaml`, the staged import and its licence evidence, and prints one report. Per skill: id, upstream path, name, digest, fingerprint, category, the licence verdict with reasons, the attribution files with SHA-256, the author notice lines and the scan result (blocked or not). Then the collisions, then the skills grouped by SCF category. Offline and read-only: nothing is promoted, pinned, locked, released or written. The output is deterministic: the same input gives the same bytes.
  - **Collisions.** `classifySkillCollisions` runs over the imported skills and the registered built-in and administrator skills (`registeredCandidate` builds a candidate for a registered skill). Only collisions with an imported member are printed, each with its `col-…` id, class, reason, blocking flag, members and shared capabilities. An upstream skill whose name equals the id of an existing skill is listed as `same name`, even when the content differs. Repository skills are not compared. More than 1,000 skills prints a `PROBLEM` and no collision is checked.
  - **Categories.** A skill with a declared category keeps it. Otherwise the category is guessed from the hyphen-bounded parts of its name (`REVIEW_CATEGORY_TERMS`, then the ids of existing skills), else `general`. Each category lists the existing skills of that category, so a candidate can be compared with them.
  - **Trust.** Every skill is shown as `community`, never higher, and as `not-promoted`.
  - **Exit codes.** 1 when a skill is blocked, a licence is not `allowed`, a blocking collision exists or a `PROBLEM` is printed; else 0. Credential-like text shows as `(hidden)`.
  - **Markdown.** `--markdown` prints the same facts as a Markdown document for a pull-request body. It is cut at 60,000 bytes with a note saying how many lines were left out. Without a staged import it prints only the problems.
  - **Code.** New pure module `src/skills/import-review.ts` (`reviewImport`, `reviewLines`, `reviewMarkdown`; limits and rules in `import-review-rules.ts`). `src/skills/review-cli.ts` reads the inputs. The staged-import reader moved from `src/skills/cli.ts` to new `src/skills/staged-import.ts`, shared by `scan`, `promote check` and `review`.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-import-review.test.ts` (a mirror of a built-in, a same-name skill, a capability overlap, a missing licence and a control); cases in `tests/skill-cli.test.ts`, `tests/cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Review an import"), `docs/DESIGN.md` ("The skill path").
- Epics 3: build order, dependency chains and the critical path (#485). Part 3 of 7 of #408.
  - **What it does.** New pure module `src/epics/chains.ts`: `buildChains` takes an epic's stories (from `src/epics/model.ts`) and the saved issue records (`IssueRecord`), and returns the build order, the chains as a graph (nodes and "depends on" edges), the outside dependencies, the cycles and the critical path. No server, no GitHub, no files, no clock. The depends-on numbers come from the records; no issue text is read.
  - **Build order.** A story comes after everything it depends on; ties go to the lower issue number. The order is the same for any input order.
  - **Graph.** Edges only join stories of the epic. A dependency outside the epic is named (`outside`, with its title when known) and not followed. A done story is a node like any other. Each node has its level (0 = depends on nothing in the epic), and the critical edges and nodes are marked, so the UI can draw the graph without another calculation.
  - **Critical path.** The longest chain of stories, counting only stories not yet done. `remaining` is how many of them are still to do, and `at` is the first not-done story on the path. Done stories may sit in the middle of the path. An epic with everything done has an empty path.
  - **Split originals.** A dependency on a split original is followed to its parts (also a part of a part). A part never waits for its own original. A dropped story is ignored.
  - **Cycles.** A cycle does not hang or throw: its stories are reported in `cycles` and marked with `CYCLE_WORDS` ("depend on each other"), and each of them counts once on the path.
  - **Not used yet.** No route, page or command calls the module yet.
  - Tests in new `tests/epics-chains.test.ts`: no dependencies, one chain, a diamond, two chains of equal length, a done story in the middle, a cycle, a split original, and more. No user-visible behaviour changed, so `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is not changed.
- Skill source connectors 1: a reviewed definition for GitHub's awesome-copilot collection, and `scf skills connectors` (#505). Part 1 of 4 of #215.
  - **What it does.** New pure module `src/skills/connectors.ts` with `SOURCE_CONNECTORS` (one connector, `awesome-copilot`: root `skills`, adapter `agent-skills`, trust `community`, licence `MIT`, track `main`, no commit), `connectorFor`, `connectorProblems` and `connectorSource`.
  - **Rule.** `scf skills sources check` (and `import` and `scan`) prints `PROBLEM` and exits 1 for an entry whose repository is the connector's repository, whatever its `id` and however the address is spelled, when it selects a path other than `skills` or a folder below it, or has another trust, adapter or licence. `ref.approved` must still be a full commit id.
  - **Existing files.** A `skill-sources.yaml` entry for `github/awesome-copilot` with other paths, trust or licences is now refused. Entries for other repositories and the existing files are otherwise unchanged.
  - **Command.** `scf skills connectors` lists each connector (id, repository key, roots, adapter, trust, licences, track) and `OK 1 connector`. It takes no operands, reads no files and does not need the data folder.
  - **Not used yet.** Nothing fetches from the connector yet; `connectorSource` is for the import step of part 4.
  - Tests in new `tests/skill-connectors.test.ts`; cases in `tests/skill-sources.test.ts`, `tests/skill-cli.test.ts` and `tests/cli.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Skill provider requirements: visible in skill metadata and selection evidence (#509). Part 1 of 5 of #218.
  - **Block.** `skill.yaml` takes an optional `provider` block: `name` (a slug), `products` (a list of `{ name, version? }`, at most 16) and `tools` (a list of slugs, at most 16). A skill without it is portable; existing packages load unchanged. `SkillProviderSchema` and `SKILL_PROVIDER_LIMITS` in `src/skills/schema.ts`.
  - **Imported skills.** The normalizer reads the block from `skill.yaml`, then frontmatter, then `metadata`, and keeps it in `SkillProvenance.provider` (optional; `schema` stays 1). `provider: acme` (the name only) is accepted. A malformed block rejects the skill with a reason (for example "provider: more than 16 products"); a duplicate product or tool is refused too.
  - **Evidence.** A catalogue entry of a skill with a provider block gets the line `needs provider: <name> (<products>; tools: <tools>)`, for example `needs provider: acme (kafka 3.7; tools: kafka-admin)`. It is cut to 100 characters, and at most three products and three tools are named, then "+N more". The line is kept when an entry is shortened, so a vendor-specific skill is never shown as portable. `providerEvidence` in `src/skills/catalogue.ts`.
  - **`scf skills`.** A skill with a provider block shows `provider <name>` after its digest.
  - **Collisions.** `MemberSettings` (`src/skills/collisions.ts`) includes the provider block, so two approved copies that differ in it are `mirror.settings-differ`.
  - **Text only.** A provider block grants no connector, shell or network access.
  - **Tests.** Extended `tests/skill-schema.test.ts`, `tests/skill-normalize.test.ts`, `tests/skill-catalogue.test.ts`, `tests/skill-collisions.test.ts`, `tests/skill-package.test.ts` and `tests/skill-cli.test.ts`.
  - Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Write a skill", "Skill catalogue", "Skill collisions").
- Skill source connectors 2: the import keeps licence and notice files in quarantine (#506). Part 2 of 4 of #215.
  - **What it does.** `scf skills import` collects the licence and notice files of the staged skill roots from the same commit (`collectLicenceEvidence`, while the tree is still open) and writes them into the quarantine folder of that import, byte for byte. `licence.json` records each file's path, kind, size and SHA-256; the files live in `licence/<upstream path>`. Nothing is followed, loaded or run.
  - **Reader.** New `readStagedLicence(source, commit, { home })` in `src/skills/import-stage.ts` returns `LicenceEvidence` with the same `repositoryKey` and `commit` as `import.json`. It refuses a changed, missing or extra evidence file. An import staged before this change reads as `none`, with a message to import again.
  - **Replacing an old import.** The first import of a commit that has no licence files replaces the old folder. The old folder is kept as `.old-<commit>` until the new one is verified; the next import restores or removes it. A different import of the same commit still gives `conflict`.
  - **Unchanged.** `import.json` keeps its form and `schema: 1`. A second import of the same commit is still `(unchanged)`. `scf skills scan` still reads old and new imports the same way.
  - **Limits.** `LICENCE_LIMITS`: 200 files, 256 KiB per file, 4 MiB in total. More than that stages nothing and prints `PROBLEM`.
  - **Not done.** No command reads the stored evidence yet: `readStagedLicence` has no caller outside the tests, and `scf skills scan` does not use it.
  - **Code.** New pure module `src/skills/import-licence.ts` (`prepareLicenceEvidence`, `parseLicenceRecord`, `evidenceFromRecord`). `publishImport` takes the optional `licence`; `import-plan.ts` exports its name helpers.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-import-licence.test.ts` and `tests/skill-import-licence-stage.test.ts`; additions to `tests/skill-import-cli.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Import a source into quarantine").
- Skill source connector 3: the import keeps licence evidence, and `scf skills promote check` reports licence and provenance per path (#511). Part 3 of 5 of #218.
  - **Import.** `scf skills import` reads the licence and notice files that can apply to the approved paths (`collectLicenceEvidence`) from the same commit, and stages them under `licences/` next to `files/`. `import.json` gets two optional fields, `retrievedAt` (the UTC time of the fetch, recorded at import time) and `licenceFiles` (`path`, `sha256`, `bytes`, or `problem` for a file that cannot be read or staged). The import prints `LICENCE FILES <n> staged, <m> not readable`.
  - **Reader.** `readStagedImport` proves the staged licence files match the record in both directions, as it does for skill files. A record written before this change still reads; its licence evidence is empty, so every path is refused as `missing` by the check.
  - **Check.** New `scf skills promote check <source-id> [<exceptions-file>]` in `src/skills/cli.ts` (`promoteCheck`, next to `scan`). It runs `normalizeImport`, the scanner with exceptions (`promotionBlockers`) and `promoteImport`, and prints `PROMOTABLE <id> <path> <licences>` or `REFUSED <path>: <status> (<reasons>)` per skill path, then `CHECKED`. Exit 1 when a path is refused or a `PROBLEM` is printed. Offline and read-only: nothing is written, loaded, pinned or run; no network and no child process.
  - **Licence texts are untrusted.** They are never printed, and no link in them is followed.
  - **Not changed.** `evaluateLicence`, `promoteImport` and `collectLicenceEvidence` are reused unchanged. Nothing promotes a skill yet.
  - Tests: `tests/skill-import-evidence.test.ts`, `tests/skill-import-cli.test.ts`, new `tests/skill-promote-check-cli.test.ts`, `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`, `tests/cli.test.ts`.
  - `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`: "Import a source into quarantine" and new "Check a promotion".
- Skill selections: `skill-selections.yaml` and `scf skills selections check` (#515). Part 2 of 4 of #221.
  - **File.** `skill-selections.yaml` next to `skill-sources.yaml` records the selected skills of a source: product and SDK version, `portable` or `provider` scope, the category, capabilities and what each skill requires (CLIs, MCP servers, credentials, services). It is changed by pull request. Nothing reads it in a run, and it grants nothing.
  - **Command.** `scf skills selections check [<file>]` is offline and needs no data folder. It prints one line per entry and `OK <n> selections`, or `PROBLEM <where>: <message>` lines and exit 1. A missing file is a `PROBLEM` for the command.
  - **Checks.** Unknown keys are refused. Slugs, digests, owners and dates follow the source and exception rules; a provider scope needs `product` and `sdk`, and a portable one refuses `provider`. `requires` holds at most 16 names in total, and a name may be listed once. At most 500 selections. No wildcards. Credential-like text and control characters are never printed.
  - **Connectors.** `selectionConnectors` returns the sorted connector slugs `cli-…`, `mcp-…`, `credential-…` and `service-…` of one selection.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/selections.ts` (`parseSkillSelections`, `readSkillSelections`, `selectionConnectors`), wired into `src/skills/cli.ts` and the help text in `src/cli.ts`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-selections.test.ts`; new cases in `tests/skill-cli.test.ts`, `tests/cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** "Skill selections" in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` under "Skill library sources".
- Skill libraries: a community source stays off until the owner records a review (#518). Part 1 of 4 of #224.
  - **Review.** A source may have a `review` map in `skill-sources.yaml`: `by` (a GitHub user or team), `on` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, not in the future), `commit` (the full commit id) and `licence` (one of `licence.allow`). Unknown keys are refused. `parseSources` and `readSources` in `src/skills/sources.ts` check it; they take an optional `today` for the future-date rule.
  - **Rules.** An enabled `trust: community` source is invalid without `review`, and when `review.commit` is not `ref.approved`. The shipped list `src/skills/community-rules.ts` holds `github.com/jiangxianze/spring-agent-skills`; a source with that repository and another trust is invalid, and the message names `trust`.
  - **CLI.** `scf skills sources check` prints `reviewed by <by> on <on>` for a reviewed source. `scf skills import` and `scf skills scan` refuse a source whose review is missing or stale through the existing invalid-source path (`PROBLEM`); nothing is fetched or read.
  - **Not changed.** `schema` stays 1. Files without `review` and without community-enabled sources load as before. `collisionCandidate` still gives `approved: false` for a disabled source.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` "Skill library sources" has the `review` field and a disabled example for the candidate source; `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md` "Skill integrity" lists what is checked and what is not.
  - **Not done.** No enabled entry for the candidate repository is shipped; the review itself is for the owner.
- Skill evaluation 1c: `scf skills score` stores one evaluation record per staged skill, and `scf skills gate` prints which skills may be promoted (#503). Part 3 of 4 of #203.
  - **Name.** The issue asked for `scf skills evaluate`, but that name is taken by the connector report (#219). The record writer is `scf skills score <source-id> [<suite-file>]`; the gate is `scf skills gate <source-id> [<suite-file>]`, as the issue says. Nothing that existed changes.
  - **Suite.** Both commands read the suite file given as the second operand, or `skill-evaluation.yaml` in the current folder (`EVALUATION_SUITE_DEFAULT`). A missing or invalid suite is a `PROBLEM` and exit 1. The gate compares the suite digest of each record with the suite it reads, so a record made with another suite fails. The shipped `evals/skill-gate` suite is an example only.
  - **Score.** `scf skills score` reads the staged import as `scf skills scan` does (`import.json` must match), runs `scoreEvaluation` for every skill and writes one record each. It prints `FINDING`, `SCORED` and `EVALUATED` lines. The mode (`new`, `upgrade` or `duplicate`) and the baseline are chosen by `gateChecks` in `evaluate-cli.ts`.
  - **Gate.** `scf skills gate` reads the newest record of every skill, and of the current canonical skill with the same digest where one exists. It prints `PASSED <id>@<version> <mode>`, `FAILED <id>@<version> <mode>: <reason codes>` and `PROBLEM` lines, then `GATED` with the counts. It exits 1 when a skill fails or a `PROBLEM` is printed. A missing, unreadable, invalid, other-digest or other-suite record is `FAILED`, never passed. Only an enabled source is gated.
  - **Store.** New `src/skills/evaluation-store.ts`: records go to `<data folder>/skill-evaluations/<source>/<commit>/<id>--<10-digit number>.json`. A record is written to a `0600` file in a `0700` folder, synced, and published by an exclusive link to the next free number, so an earlier record is never replaced and a new run adds one. The newest record is the highest number, not the clock. `readNewestRecord` reads one skill of one import; `findNewestRecordOf` finds the baseline by id and package digest, because a registered skill has no source or commit. The folders are checked for links, mode and realpath, as the quarantine is. A record is limited to 2 MiB and a folder to 5,000 files (`EVALUATION_STORE_LIMITS`).
  - **Nothing else changes.** The records live outside the quarantine, so a staged import still reads as valid. A failed or interrupted `score` changes nothing but the evaluation folder; `skills.lock.json`, the skill folders, `skill-sources.yaml` and the quarantine are unchanged.
  - **Offline.** Both commands call no model and start no process. `gate` only reads; `score` writes only below `skill-evaluations/`. In `src/cli.ts` both are read-only commands, so they run without moving the data folder.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/evaluate-cli.ts` (`scoreImport`, `gateImport`, `gateChecks`) and `src/skills/staged-import.ts` (now also holds `loadExceptions`). `EVALUATION_SUITE_DEFAULT`, `EVALUATIONS_DIR` and `EVALUATION_STORE_LIMITS` are in `src/skills/evaluation-rules.ts`. `evaluationGate` takes an optional baseline `id`, so an upgrade by capability compares with the approved skill's record.
  - Tests: new `tests/skill-score-cli.test.ts` (the suite, one record per skill outside the quarantine, a second run keeps the first, every gate mode, the FAILED cases, registry problems, and a failed score that is byte-for-byte the same outside `skill-evaluations/`) and `tests/skill-evaluation-store.test.ts` (modes and layout, the sequence, links, realpath and wrong modes, lookup by digest); cases in `tests/cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` "Score and gate an import" and the command table in section 9.
  - **Not done.** No run, flow or promotion reads the records yet: `gate` prints the verdict, and nothing sets `CanonicalCandidate.evaluation` or promotes a skill. The suite is not run with a model.
- Skill evaluation 1b: the suite file, and offline scoring for selection, conflicts, context size and tool boundaries (#502). Part 2 of 4 of #203.
  - **Suite file.** `evals/skill-gate/suite.yaml` (`schema: 1`) lists scenarios per dimension: `selection` (a repository fixture, a task, and `expect: offered` or `not-offered`), `conflicts` (`with: all` or a list of skill ids), `context` (`max_instruction_tokens`, `max_description_chars`) and `tools` (a policy of `shell`, `network` and `filesystem: read | write`). `parseEvaluationSuite` in new `src/skills/evaluation-suite.ts` refuses an invalid suite with the place and the reason, like `skill-sources.yaml`. Unknown keys are refused, and a message never repeats a credential-like value.
  - **Limits.** At most 100 scenarios and 50 per dimension; a suite file is at most 256 KiB; a task is at most 2,000 characters; a path is at most 200 characters. `EVALUATION_SUITE_LIMITS` in `src/skills/evaluation-rules.ts` holds them all.
  - **Digest.** `evaluationSuiteDigest` is stable: it covers the suite and the fixture folders of its selection scenarios. `readEvaluationSuite` reads the file and those folders with fixed limits on files, entries, depth and total bytes.
  - **Scorers.** New `src/skills/evaluation-score.ts`: `scoreSelection`, `scoreConflicts`, `scoreContext`, `scoreTools` and `scoreEvaluation`. Each is pure over input that is already read. Selection is the share of scenarios where the candidate is offered exactly when it should be, counting positive and negative scenarios. Conflicts fail on `version-reuse` or `id-conflict` with an approved skill; a fork or capability overlap with the canonical skill is a finding and marks the comparison as an upgrade or duplicate. Context checks the instruction tokens and the catalogue description against the suite's limits and the skill's declared budget. Tools checks every needed tool against the policy, and fails when a high scan finding is open.
  - **Record shape.** `scoreEvaluation` returns the suite digest and scenario inputs, one sample list per dimension, the sorted findings (codes in `EVALUATION_FINDING_CODES`), the mode and the baseline, in the shape of the part 1 record.
  - **Read only.** The candidate's files are never opened, and its own `evals/` folder is never read. The candidate is scored from its package without files.
  - **Example.** `evals/skill-gate/suite.yaml` with the fixtures `evals/skill-gate/fixtures/kafka-consumer` and `rest-service` scores one built-in skill end to end.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-evaluation-suite.test.ts` and `tests/skill-evaluation-score.test.ts`. No agent, no server, no model.
  - **Not done.** Nothing calls the suite or the scorers yet: no command or run reads `suite.yaml`, stores a record or sets `CanonicalCandidate.evaluation`. So no user-visible behaviour changed, and `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is not changed.
- Skill source connector 1: `collectImportEvidence` can keep an unreadable licence file as a problem (#522). Part 1 of 4 of #222.
  - **What it does.** Licence evidence is kept by #511 (`licence.json`, `licence/`). When a caller passes `recoverable` and git cannot read one licence or notice file (`read-failed`), that file is listed with the problem `cannot be read`. `scf skills import` does not pass it: it still stops with the fixed PROBLEM line and stages nothing.
  - **Code.** `collectImportEvidence` in `src/skills/import-evidence.ts`, used by `scf skills import` and by `stageImport` (option `recoverable`).
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-import-read.test.ts`.
  - **Not done.** Nothing reads the evidence yet: no licence check runs on a staged import, and `promoteImport` is not called. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` has a short note under "Import a source into quarantine".
- Epics 2: which stories belong to an epic, and the counts (#484). Part 2 of 7 of #408.
  - **What it does.** New pure module `src/epics/model.ts`: `buildEpics` takes the saved issue records (`IssueRecord` from `src/queue/issue-list.ts`), the watchers' trigger labels and the run states the Foundry holds, and returns the epics. No server, no GitHub, no files, no clock (`now` is a parameter).
  - **Membership.** A story belongs to an epic through `**Epic:**`, `Parent: #N`, an `epic: …` label, or the story list of the epic issue. Parts of a split story belong to the epic of the story they replace, also for a part of a part. A story that matches no epic is listed under "No epic".
  - **States.** `done` (closed, or with a done label), `working`, `waiting`, `your_turn`, `failed` (the run states), `split` (a closed original that has parts) and `dropped` (closed as not planned, without parts). Split and dropped stories are shown but left out of the totals. A story without the watcher's label is `waiting` and counted as "not handed to the Foundry yet".
  - **Growth.** Stories at the start, stories now and how many were split, as text: "21 at the start, 95 now (16 were split)". "At the start" = the stories that existed when the epic issue's first day ended, counting originals and not their later parts.
  - **Series and finish.** A per-day series of "stories in the epic" and "stories done", rebuilt from the created and closed dates. A finished epic gives when it finished (the latest close among done, split and dropped stories) and how long it took. A done story without a close date (done by label) leaves the finish time unknown.
  - **Not used yet.** No route, page or command calls the module yet; the watcher's tracked states come in part 5.
  - Tests in new `tests/epics-model.test.ts`. No user-visible behaviour changed, so `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is not changed.
- Skill evaluation 1a: the evaluation record and the promotion gate, pure (#501). Part 1 of 4 of #203.
  - **Record.** New `src/skills/evaluation.ts` and `src/skills/evaluation-rules.ts`. `parseEvaluationRecord` checks an `EvaluationRecord` (`schema: 1`): the candidate (id, version, digest, fingerprint, source, repository key, commit), the suite digest and the inputs of each scenario, the runtime (Foundry, and agent and model when a model was used), `evaluatedAt`, the score of every sample per dimension, findings and evidence references. `evaluationRecordDigest` is stable: key order and the order of scenarios, findings and evidence do not change it.
  - **Credentials.** A record that holds credential-like text is refused with `record-credential`. The message names the rule, never the text.
  - **Gate.** `evaluationGate` returns `passed` only when every mandatory dimension meets its threshold, `promotionBlockers` is empty and the licence is `allowed`. Modes: `new` (absolute thresholds), `upgrade` (thresholds and not worse than the baseline record) and `duplicate` (two candidates for one capability; the clearly better one is named, otherwise "no clear difference").
  - **Defaults.** `selection` ≥ 0.9; `conflicts`, `context` and `tools` = 1 (every sample must be 1); `instructions` ≥ 0.8, optional unless the suite makes it mandatory. A regression needs a drop larger than the sample spread and than 0.1. `instructions` needs at least 3 samples. A suite can only make these stricter.
  - **Reason codes.** Every failure carries a code such as `dimension-missing`, `digest-mismatch`, `suite-mismatch`, `safety-blocked`, `licence-not-allowed`, `too-few-samples`, `below-threshold`, `baseline-missing`, `regression` or `worse-duplicate`. A missing mandatory dimension, a record for another digest and a suite other than the expected one each fail closed.
  - **Outcome.** `evaluationOutcome(result, candidate?)` gives the `"passed" | "failed"` value of `CanonicalCandidate.evaluation`. For a duplicate result the candidate must be named; without it the outcome is `"failed"`.
  - **Pure.** The time is an input (`evaluatedAt`); no file system, process, clock or network is used, and the inputs are not changed.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-evaluation.test.ts`; `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` covers both new files.
  - **Not done.** Nothing calls it yet: no command, run or flow runs a suite, stores a record or sets `CanonicalCandidate.evaluation`. `promoteImport`, `SkillProvenance` and `CanonicalCandidate` are unchanged.
- Skill libraries: the OpenHands extensions source, with import review rules (#214). Part of #196.
  - **Source.** `skill-sources.yaml` at the repository root declares `openhands-extensions` (`https://github.com/OpenHands/extensions`, folder `skills`, licence `MIT`, adapter `agent-skills`). It ships `status: disabled` with no `ref.approved`: no commit is approved yet, so nothing is fetched. The owner enables it by pull request with a full commit id.
  - **Exclusions.** New `src/skills/import-exclusions.ts` and rules in `src/skills/import-review-rules.ts`. An imported skill is refused for promotion (`excluded`) when it needs a connector (an MCP tool), needs network access, uses a web or service command or a web call in its scripts, ships `.mcp.json`, `plugin.json` or a `.plugin/` folder, or declares `mcp_tools`. The exception file does not lift an exclusion.
  - **`scf skills scan`.** A skill with an exclusion prints `EXCLUDED <skill>: <reasons>`, counts as blocked and makes the command exit 1. Its `OK` line is not printed.
  - **Promotion.** `promoteImport` in `src/skills/promotion.ts` puts an excluded skill in `refused` with status `excluded`, before the licence check. Nothing calls it yet.
  - **Evaluation.** New `evaluateImport` in `src/skills/import-evaluation.ts` compares imported skills with existing ones: the review areas (`code-review`, `documentation`, `development-workflow`), capability overlaps with the winner of each shared capability, collisions that block a skill, and the outcome `new`, `duplicate` or `blocked`. It is pure and reads no file. Nothing calls it in the CLI yet.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-source-openhands.test.ts` (the source declaration, and an in-memory fixture that runs import, scan, evaluation and promotion), `tests/skill-import-exclusions.test.ts` and `tests/skill-import-evaluation.test.ts`; `tests/skill-scan-cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-promotion.test.ts` extended. No network, no real upstream content.
  - **Not done.** The real import, the review of the upstream content and the choice of the commit are for the owner. Upstream plugins and live integrations are excluded by rule, not by an approval list.
- Skill source connector 1: Superpowers workflow assessment, `scf skills assess` (#216). Part of #196.
  - **Command.** `scf skills assess <source-id>` reads the approved commit from `skill-sources.yaml` and the staged import in quarantine, then prints `CONFLICT`, `CANDIDATE`, `EXCLUDED` and `ASSESSED` lines per skill. Offline and read-only; it needs no data folder and takes no exception file. Exclusions exit 0; only a `PROBLEM` (no staged import, no workflow profile, unreadable built-in flow) exits 1.
  - **Profile.** `src/skills/workflow-rules.ts` (data only) holds the finding codes, their effect (`exclude` or `report`), the text rules and the Superpowers profile for `github.com/obra/superpowers`: six individual skills (TDD, debugging, verification, requesting and receiving code review, writing plans), each with its tools, roles, capability, built-in flows and token budget.
  - **Assessment.** New `src/skills/workflow.ts` (pure: no file system, process or clock). The declaration is the ceiling: upstream tool requests, connectors, `allowed-tools`, scripts, dependencies or text that asks for more (subagents, worktrees, slash commands, its own delivery flow, skipped approval, orchestration, another skill) exclude the skill. Nothing is adapted or merged. A declared flow counts only when a Claude step really receives the skill and gives every declared tool. Every exclusion code is kept, even when the findings are cut at 50 per skill.
  - **Flow steps.** `flowSkillSteps` in `src/skills/tool-policy.ts` lists a flow's Claude steps with their role, skill mode and tools; `flowTools` is unchanged.
  - **Provenance.** `SkillProvenance.workflow` (optional, so older provenance stays valid) can hold a promoted skill's declaration. Nothing writes it yet.
  - **Refactor.** `scf skills scan` and `assess` share the start of the command (`stagedImport` in `src/skills/cli.ts`); `scan` prints the same as before.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-workflow.test.ts`, `tests/skill-workflow-cli.test.ts` and `tests/fixtures/skill-workflow.ts`; `tests/cli.test.ts` (help, data folder not moved) and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` (no process, no network in the command).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` has "Assess an import" and the command table row.
  - **Not done.** Nothing promotes a candidate: `workflowBlockers`, `declaredPackage` and `workflowProvenance` are for a later promotion step to call. Adapted copies of upstream skills are out of scope (#216 question Q1, option a).
- Skill source connector: `scf skills evaluate` reports what a source offers before anything is imported (#219). Part of #196.
  - **Command.** `scf skills evaluate <source-id> [<technology>@<version> ...]` fetches the approved commit of an enabled source into a temporary git folder and prints `SKILL`, `COMPATIBILITY`, `TOOLS`, `ACCESS`, `LICENCE`, `NOTICE`, `REFUSED`, `BLOCKED`, `INTEGRITY`, `RECOMMEND`, `CONFLICT`, `NONE` and `EVALUATED` lines. It writes nothing: no quarantine, no catalogue, no file. Up to 8 technologies; a technology must look like `oracle-database@19`.
  - **Compatibility.** The given project technologies are compared with each skill's version ranges (`compatibility`, from `technologyCompatibility`). An incompatible or unchecked skill fails the command.
  - **Source scope.** New optional `scope` in a `skill-sources.yaml` entry: up to 8 `capabilities` and up to 8 `technologies` (name to version range such as `">=19"`). It states what upstream does not: a range the skill declares itself wins. Parsed and checked by `parseSources` in `src/skills/sources.ts`.
  - **Access and licence.** Each skill shows what it asks for (`ACCESS`); importing grants nothing. Licence is checked per skill path, and the attribution files of each allowed skill are listed (`NOTICE`), so the notices are kept.
  - **Overlap.** Capabilities that an imported skill and an installed skill both provide go through the canonical selection (`selectCanonicalSkills`): a `RECOMMEND` line names the winner and why, a `CONFLICT` or `NONE` line says why no skill can be chosen. Exit 1 on a conflict.
  - **Exit codes.** 0 when every skill passes; 1 when a skill is refused, blocked, has an unsafe licence or incompatible compatibility, an installed skill fails the integrity check, or a `PROBLEM` is printed.
  - **Code.** New `src/skill-import/evaluate-cli.ts` and `src/skills/source-evaluation.ts` (`evaluateSource` and `evaluationLines`, pure: no file system, process or clock). `gitSentence` in `src/skill-import/cli.ts` is exported for reuse.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-source-evaluation.test.ts`, `tests/skill-evaluate-cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-sources-scope.test.ts`; additions to `tests/cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`.
  - **Not done.** Oracle-specific content (the Oracle database, SQL tooling, OCI, APEX and GraalVM domains from `oracle/skills`) is not added: this change is the connector and the report. The evaluation does not write an approved artifact, so the catalogue artifact keeps only what a later promotion step writes. Live cloud access is not checked, and nothing is granted by an import.
- Skill source connector 1 — Prisma database skills (#223). Part of #196; depends on #199, #200, #201, #202, #457, #458, #472, #473, #474.
  - **Profile.** New `src/skills/connector-rules.ts` (data only): a reviewed profile for `github.com/prisma/skills`. It maps five upstream skills (`prisma-cli`, `prisma-client-api`, `prisma-orm-setup`, `prisma-postgres`, `prisma-upgrade-v7`) to their own capability slugs and to the general guidance they share (`sql`, `postgresql`). Each skill declares its ORM version range, supported engines, runtime, the CLI or generated-client requirements, and the evaluations it needs.
  - **Connector.** New `src/skills/connector.ts` (pure, no file system, process, network or clock). `applyConnector` maps the normalized skills of the source; a skill outside the profile, or one whose companion skill is missing, is not mapped. `connectorCandidate` builds a canonical candidate with licence, provenance, safety and compatibility facts. Nothing is installed or executed on import; CLI and generated-client needs are only declared.
  - **Migrations are high risk.** `prisma-migrations` and `prisma-upgrade` are high-risk capabilities.
