A skill that needs them, or whose text names a guarded command (`migrate dev`, `migrate deploy`, `migrate reset`, `db push` and similar), needs the passed evaluations `migration-no-data-loss`, `migration-no-production-reset` and `migration-review-before-apply`. The upgrade skill also needs `upgrade-breaking-changes` and `upgrade-rollback-plan`. A missing or failed evaluation fails the safety gate.
  - **Versions and engines.** The ORM range, the runtime range and the engines take part in compatibility selection. An ORM version outside the project's range, or an engine the project does not use, is incompatible. A range the project cannot prove is `unchecked`, not compatible.
  - **Reviewed commits.** The profile lists the upstream commits it was checked against. It has none yet, because the upstream repository could not be read when it was written. So every Prisma skill fails the safety gate until a commit is reviewed and added.
  - **Withheld.** A skill that fails its safety gate is left out of the catalogue even when it offers no exclusive capability. The `withhold` option of `runSkillCatalogue` does this; a mandatory skill that is withheld stops the step with a message that names it. No live run passes the connector yet.
  - **Scan report.** `scf skills scan prisma` also prints, for a source with a profile, one line per mapped skill: `CONNECTOR <connector> <skill>: specific=… general=… orm=… engines=… runtime=… needs=… risk=… evaluations=… reviewed=yes|no`. It prints `CONNECTOR … unmapped` for a skill the profile does not name or whose companion is missing, and `CONNECTOR … absent` for a profile skill not in the import. The scan stays offline.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-connector-prisma.test.ts` (profile, mapping, migration evaluations, versions and engines, reviewed commits, licence and provenance) and `tests/skill-connector-catalogue.test.ts` (the withheld seam); additions to `tests/skill-scan-cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
  - **Not done.** Reviewed upstream commits and the evaluation producers; live runs that pass the connector; installing or running the Prisma CLI or a generated client.
- Watchers 10 — the watcher options that were missing in the form (#499). Part 10 of 11 of #407.
  - **Controls.** The watcher form (`ui/watcher-form.js`) has controls for `auto_defaults`, `precheck_flow`, `priority_labels`, the five `status_labels`, `wait_for_dependencies`, `dependency_done_labels`, `remove_on_done`, `comment_on_failure`, `status_comment`, `one_at_a_time` and `pause_while_pr_open`. They are grouped under "Labels", "Dependencies" and "Comments", collapsed by default, in new `ui/watcher-options.js`. Only issues watchers show them; a monitor watcher never sends `precheck_flow`.
  - **Clearing.** `pause_while_pr_open` and `precheck_flow` can be changed and cleared. A cleared option is removed from the stored watcher (`null` on save), never saved as empty text. A list keeps its stored array when its text is untouched, so a label with a comma survives.
  - **Errors.** A value the server refuses is shown at its own field, found by `fieldOf` in the form, with the server's message; the group that holds that field opens.
  - **Summary.** The watcher page lists the options that differ from their defaults (`optionNotes`).
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-watcher-options.test.ts`; additions to `tests/ui-watchers.test.ts` and `tests/repo-watchers-api.test.ts`; `tests/ui-user.test.ts` adjusted. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
  - **Server.** No server change was needed: `watcherShape` and `RepoWatcherSchema` already accept these options.
- Refinement: the architect can be asked in several sessions at once; the runs wait in line.
  - **Before.** While the architect was queued, running or paused for one session, asking in another session of the same account answered 409 ("the architect is reading for another of your sessions"). The person had to come back later.
  - **Now.** The ask is accepted. All architect runs of one account share one scheduler lock (`architectLock` in `src/refinement/architect.ts`), so one works and the others wait in the queue in the order they were asked. One run per session stays (409). A paused run of another session no longer refuses a new one.
  - **Page.** A queued session says how many of the account's other sessions are first (`behind` in the architect's state).
  - Tests in `tests/architect.test.ts` and `tests/ui-refinement-architect.test.ts`; `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Skill libraries safety 2c: `scf skills scan` prints the owner's security report of a staged import (#474). Part 3 of 3 of #202.
  - **Command.** `scf skills scan <source-id> [<exceptions-file>]` reads the approved commit from `skill-sources.yaml`, checks the staged import in quarantine against `import.json`, then runs `normalizeImport`, `scanSkill`, `applyExceptions` and `promotionBlockers`. It prints `FINDING`, `BLOCKED`, `OK`, `REJECTED` and `SCANNED` lines, and `PROBLEM` for a refusal. Exit 1 when a skill is blocked or a `PROBLEM` is printed. Offline; nothing is written, loaded or run.
  - **Reader.** New `readStagedImport(source, commit, { home })` in `src/skills/import-stage.ts`: it follows no link, checks modes and proves the folder matches `import.json` both ways. A changed file, a link or an extra file is refused, and nothing is scanned.
  - **Not normalized.** A skill that normalization rejects is still scanned and printed under its folder name, with `BLOCKED <folder>: not normalized (<reasons>)`. Credential-like text is printed as `(hidden)`.
  - **Exception file.** `skill-scan-exceptions.yaml` next to `skill-sources.yaml`; a missing default file means no exceptions, a named one that is missing is a `PROBLEM`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-import-read.test.ts` and `tests/skill-scan-cli.test.ts`; `tests/cli.test.ts` (help, syntax, old data folder untouched); `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts` (no process, no network in the command).
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` has "Scan an import" and the command table row.
  - **Not done.** Nothing promotes a skill yet: `promotionBlockers` is for a later promotion step to call.
- Settings 2: the audit log says what changed from what to what, and the Slack webhook is never shown (#491). Part 2 of 11 of #407.
  - **Settings lines.** `PUT /api/config` writes one `settings-change` line per changed setting, `<key.path>: <old> -> <new>` (for example `monitor.stuck_run.extra_minutes: 30 -> 60`), all under one lock (`auditActions` in `src/auth/audit.ts`). A missing value shows as `(not set)`. Watchers in `config.yaml` are named `watchers.<id>.<option>` (`watchers.<id>#<n>.<option>` when an id is used twice).
  - **Watcher lines.** `PUT /api/admin/repos/:id/watchers/:wid` writes one `repo-change` line per changed option, `watcher <id>: <option>: <old> -> <new>`. This replaces the old `disabled` / `enabled` / `changed a, b` wording.
  - **Masked.** `MASKED_KEYS` in `src/auth/audit-changes.ts` is the one list: `notify.slack_webhook`, `notify.command` and `agent_env` (on any path) say only `changed`. A watcher's `owner` and `task` also say only `changed`.
  - **Limits.** A value is cut at 80 characters and ends with `…`. A detail is at most 2,000 characters (`DETAIL_MAX`, was 500). A name too long for the limit keeps its start and end, with a hash between. A line the schema refuses is skipped; the other lines of that save are still written.
  - **Webhook.** `GET /api/config` and `PUT /api/config` leave out `notify.slack_webhook` and answer `notify.slack_webhook_set` (true or false). On `PUT`, a key left out keeps the stored webhook, `null` removes it and a string sets it; the `_set` marker is ignored. `scf settings set` keeps the webhook when it is left out; only `null` removes it. `config.yaml` keeps `notify.slack_webhook`, so no migration is needed (`src/server/config-view.ts`).
  - **Settings page.** Shows "set" or "not set", an empty field to enter a new address, and **Remove the Slack webhook**. A save that leaves the field empty keeps the stored webhook.
  - **Broken file.** A `config.yaml` that is not valid YAML answers 400 with its line number, never a quoted line of the file. A file that is valid YAML but not valid settings is reported by key path and rule (`ConfigIssuesError` in `src/config.ts`).
  - **Code.** New `src/auth/audit-changes.ts` (diff, masking, cutting) and `src/server/config-view.ts` (webhook handling). Changed: `src/auth/audit.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/server/api-admin.ts`, `src/server/api-repos.ts`, `src/repos/watchers.ts`, `src/remote/areas/setup.ts`, `ui/admin.js`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/audit-changes.test.ts` and `tests/config-view.test.ts`; extended `tests/audit-actions.test.ts` and `tests/ui-settings.test.ts`.
- Skill libraries safety 2b: reviewed exceptions, dispositions and the promotion gate (#473). Part 2 of 3 of #202.
  - **Exception file.** `skill-scan-exceptions.yaml` (`schema: 1`), read by `readScanExceptions` and checked by `parseScanExceptions` in `src/skills/scan-exceptions.ts`. One entry suppresses exactly one finding of one package: `finding`, `skill`, `digest`, `reason`, `owner`, `reviewed` and either `expires` or `review: required`. There are no wildcards. A new upstream version has a new digest, so its exceptions no longer apply.
  - **Limits.** An exception lasts at most 365 days after `reviewed`; `reviewed` must not be in the future. At most 200 entries. A problem names its entry and field, and never repeats a credential-like value. Unknown keys are refused.
  - **Dispositions.** `applyExceptions` gives every finding one of `open`, `accepted`, `expired` or `review-required`. An expired exception (its date is before today) and `review: required` do not unblock. A secret finding is always `open`: an exception cannot accept it. `today` is a parameter; the code reads no clock.
  - **Gate.** `promotionBlockers` lists the high findings that are not `accepted`. Medium and low findings never block; they keep their disposition.
  - **Provenance.** `SkillProvenance.safety` (optional, so `schema` stays 1 and older provenance stays valid) holds the report digest (`scanReportDigest`) and, per finding, its id, severity, disposition and the exception that decided it. `safetyProvenance` refuses a decision that no longer matches its report.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-scan-exceptions.test.ts` (pure unit tests). The only existing file changed is `src/skills/normalize.ts` (the optional field).
  - **Not done.** Nothing calls it yet: no command, run, flow or UI reads the exception file, and no run writes `safety` to provenance.
- Settings 1: a save changes only the keys that changed in `config.yaml`, and a stale save is refused (#490). Part 1 of 11 of #407.
  - **In place.** `editYamlText` in new `src/config-edit.ts` and `editConfigText` in `src/config.ts` edit the file text: comments and key order stay, only changed keys are written, and the result is parsed again and must equal the body before anything is written. Lists are aligned, so a comment on an untouched item survives. A list too long to align (over 250,000 comparisons) is refused, not saved.
  - **Defaults.** A key put back to its default is removed, unless it has a comment; a parent left empty is removed too. A key left out of the body is removed.
  - **Version.** `configVersion` is the sha256 of the file text. `GET /api/config`, `GET /api/watchers` and `GET /api/monitor` send `x-config-version`. `PUT /api/config` with a stale `x-config-version` answers 409 "the settings changed since you loaded them — reload first" and writes nothing; a PUT without the header is not checked, so `scf settings set`, scripts and open tabs keep working. A PUT answers with the new version.
  - **Reads from the file.** `GET /api/config` reads `config.yaml` from disk, not the running settings. The `file` field of a monitor row in `GET /api/watchers` and the thresholds of `GET /api/monitor` come from the file too.
  - **Writes only on change.** `saveConfig` writes nothing when the text is the same. A bad body is refused with 400 and the file bytes stay the same.
  - **Pages.** Settings, Watchers, Problems and Models send the version their page was drawn with. Models keeps it per drawn form, so a reload during a save cannot hand an old save a newer version. A 409 on Settings marks no field.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/config-save.test.ts`; route cases in `tests/watcher-move-server.test.ts`; `tests/ui-auth.test.ts`, `tests/ui-a11y-admin.test.ts`, `tests/ui-models.test.ts`, `tests/ui-watchers.test.ts` and `tests/ui-problems.test.ts` extended.
  - **Limits.** Comments on a key that is removed, inside a list item that is removed or replaced, or inside a value that changes shape are still lost. Whitespace may be normalised on the first changing save. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Watchers keep a small record of every issue per repository (#483). Part 1 of 7 of #408.
  - **What it does.** Each issue watcher check reads the whole issue list once (open and closed, 100 per GitHub call, at most 20 pages = 2,000 issues). Watchers of one repository share that read. The record is saved in `issue-lists/` in the data folder: number, title, state, labels, links (epic, parent, part of, epic label), dependencies and stories. No issue text is stored.
  - **Limit.** A list with more issues is saved as `cut`; the dependency check then sees only the issues in the record. The limit is `issueListPages` on the watcher.
  - **Fallback.** A failed read keeps the previous file and falls back to the old open-issues call; the reason is logged. A malformed issue from GitHub fails the whole read.
  - **Dependency check.** Uses the same list. Late writes are dropped when the watcher stopped, a newer check started or the repository's record was removed.
  - New `src/queue/issue-list.ts`; `listAllIssues` in `src/github.ts`; `titleIndex` in `src/queue/deps.ts`. Tests in `tests/issue-list.test.ts` and `tests/watch-issue-list.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- A sixth helper process, `scf housekeeping` (every 3 hours, with `run|report|install|uninstall|status` like the others).
  - **Memory record.** Each check writes the memory of the system's file-change tracker (`fseventsd`) and of the Foundry server to `housekeeping/memory.log`. Above 8 GB for the tracker or 6 GB for the server it sends one notification that day and the digest names it. Reason: on a machine up for days the tracker once grew to 56 GB and starved the runs of memory.
  - **Nightly clean-up.** At the first check after 03:00 it removes the workspaces of runs that ended more than 2 days ago (as `scf clean --older-than 2`): run records, logs and branches stay; active and paused runs are not touched. A clean-up that fails is tried again at the next check.
  - New `src/ops/housekeeping.ts`; tests in `tests/ops.test.ts`; `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Skill deduplication 4 — owner overrides and project pins (#211). Part of #196.
  - **What it does.** New `src/skills/choices.ts` (pure, no file system, process or clock) and `src/skills/choice-rules.ts` (limits, codes and words). `parseProjectPins` and `parseSkillOverrides` check the YAML; `planSkillChoices` turns the entries in effect into pins and overrides for the canonical selection; `checkSkillChoices` and `choiceStop` report what is wrong. Precedence is as before: a project pin beats an owner override.
  - **Project pins.** `.claude-factory/skill-pins.yaml` (`schema: 1`, `pins:`), read from the default branch with git plumbing (`src/skills/project-pins.ts`, `src/engine/local-git.ts`), never from the work tree, so a work branch cannot pin its own skills. One pin per capability.
  - **Owner overrides.** `skill-overrides.yaml` (`schema: 1`, `overrides:`), scope `all` or a list of repositories. A repository-scoped override beats an `all` one for the same capability.
  - **Record.** Every entry needs `capability`, `target` (`id@version`), `by` (a GitHub user or team), `reason` (no control characters, no credential-like text), `from` and at least one of `expires` or `review_by`. `review_by` marks an entry as due for review without ending it. Each entry gets a digest (`sha256:` of the entry as validated) that the output and the run record show.
  - **Checks.** A target that is expired, not exclusive, missing, not the active version, not usable, revoked, quarantined, unapproved, or that fails the safety, licence, tools or compatibility check fails clearly with a code (`CHOICE_PROBLEMS`). A choice can rank an eligible candidate higher; it cannot bring back a rejected one. Removing the entry returns the capability to the default policy.
  - **Run record.** `RunCatalogueRecord` gets `choices` (applied choices with their effect, `selected` or `superseded`, and the digest), `choicesOmitted` and `choicesSource` (the pins commit and blob). No free text is stored. Existing run files without these fields load as before. `StepRecord.skillCatalogue` has the same fields.
  - **Run seam.** `runSkillCatalogue` takes an optional `canonical.choices` dependency. With it, an unreadable, invalid or unchecked choice stops the step (`blocked`) before any agent starts; without it, nothing changes. No run supplies it yet, so runs do not read either file.
  - **CLI.** `scf skills overrides check [<file>]` and `scf skills project-pins check [<file>]`: offline, print each entry with its digest, `OK <n> entries` and exit 0; exit 1 for a problem or an expired entry. Work with a file only; no data folder needed.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-choices.test.ts` and `tests/skill-project-pins.test.ts`; additions to `tests/skill-run-catalogue.test.ts`, `tests/skill-cli.test.ts` and `tests/skill-no-network.test.ts`.
  - **Not done.** Live runs reading the owner overrides and project pins (the run seam above is not yet fed), and the reviewed config that holds the overrides (#207).
- Skill deduplication 3 — one canonical skill per exclusive capability (#210). Part of #196.
  - **What it does.** New `src/skills/canonical.ts` (pure, no file system, process or clock) and `src/skills/canonical-rules.ts` (data only). `selectCanonicalSkills` picks at most one winner for each exclusive capability and gives the same result for any input order. `canonicalKey` is the stable lexical key: `id@version fingerprint`.
  - **Precedence.** In this order: project pin (`pins`), owner override (`overrides`), trust tier, compatibility (version, then name, then generic), evaluation (passed, then not run, then failed), specificity (fewer capabilities wins), approved release freshness (newer wins), then the lexical key. `CANONICAL_LEVELS` lists them.
  - **Trust tiers.** Best first: `local` (an administrator folder, a local owner choice), `scf-curated` (built-in and first-party), `official-vendor` (verified), `vetted-community`, and `discovery-only` (repository skills and anything unapproved). `CANONICAL_SOURCE_TIER` and `CANONICAL_REGISTRY_TIER` map the existing sources to them.
  - **Excluded before ranking.** Discovery-only, quarantined, revoked and incompatible candidates, a failed safety, licence or tools gate, and compatibility that was not checked. Each one gets a reason code (`CANONICAL_REJECT_CODES`).
  - **High-risk ties stop.** On a high-risk capability (`highRisk` terms or `risk: high`), freshness and the lexical key may not decide. When only they would, the result is a conflict (`tie`), not a random or alphabetical choice. A pin or an override resolves it. A mandatory skill that loses, or two winners that overlap, are conflicts too (`mandatory`, `winners-overlap`).
  - **Resolution record.** Each exclusive capability gets a `CanonicalResolution`: the winner, the level that decided it, every rejected alternative with its code, and the pins or overrides that were ignored. `canonicalStop` turns a conflict into one line that starts with `skills not resolved: ` and names no free text.
  - **Facts.** New `src/skills/canonical-facts.ts`: `canonicalFromCollision` and `canonicalFromRegistered` build candidates from the registry and the collision classes; `technologyCompatibility` compares declared versions with the project evidence.
  - **Run seam.** `runSkillCatalogue` takes an optional `canonical` dependency. When it is given, only the winners stay on offer, skills that need a loser are left out, and a conflict stops the step before any agent starts (`blocked`, which `runAgentStep` logs and fails). The log counts the skills left out as "not canonical". No run supplies it yet, so runs behave as before.
  - **Small changes.** `policyAllows` in `tool-policy.ts` and `hasTerm` in `unresolved.ts` are exported for reuse.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-canonical.test.ts` (every precedence level, the order of the levels, exclusion, high-risk ties, several capabilities, mandatory skills, determinism and limits), `tests/skill-canonical-facts.test.ts` (trust tiers, source status, gates, compatibility) and `tests/skill-canonical-stop.test.ts` (a blocked catalogue); additions to `tests/skill-run-catalogue.test.ts`.
  - **Not done.** Owner and project policy files, the publish step that fills the registry with approved candidates, and the evaluation producer are later stories.
- Skill deduplication 2 — collision classes and report (#209). Part of #196.
  - **What it does.** New `src/skills/collisions.ts` (pure, no file system, process or clock) and `src/skills/collision-rules.ts` (data only). `classifySkillCollisions` gives each collision one class, one reason code and a stable `col-<16 hex>` id, and gives the same result for any input order. The classes are `exact-mirror`, `version-reuse`, `id-conflict`, `fork` and `capability-overlap`; the nine reason codes are listed in `COLLISION_REASONS`.
  - **Merge.** Candidates with the same fingerprint become one `DedupedSkill`: the representative skill as it was given (package, identity and extra files unchanged), plus the provenance of every approved member, the count of unapproved members and the settings status. Candidates with other fingerprints are never merged. Input objects are not mutated.
  - **Held back.** `COLLISION_BLOCKING_CLASSES` (`version-reuse`, `id-conflict`) and `mirror.settings-differ` hold an entry back from `plannerPackages` when every approved member of its unit is in one of them. Forks and capability overlaps stay offered. An unapproved copy cannot hold back an approved skill.
  - **Approval.** `collisionCandidate` marks a candidate approved only when its source is enabled and its commit is the approved one. It throws `TypeError` when the skill's source id or repository key does not match the source record.
  - **Run seam.** `runSkillCatalogue` takes an optional `candidate` dependency (default: none, so a run behaves as before). When it is given, mirrors are offered once, held-back skills and the skills that depend on them are left out, and the log counts the merged and held-back skills. Mandatory includes are never removed. No run supplies it yet, and nothing reads collisions in live runs: the registry has no provenance to build candidates from.
  - **Limits.** At most 1,000 candidates (`RangeError` above that). Pair comparison is O(n²) over units.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-collisions.test.ts` (all five classes, all nine reasons, a non-collision control, precedence, order independence, input immutability, the report fields and the limits); additions to `tests/skill-run-catalogue.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
  - **Not done.** Canonical selection of a representative, the import-to-registry publish step, and `forkOf` and `evaluation` producers are later stories.
- Skill libraries safety 2a: static scanner (#472). Part 1 of 3 of #202. `scanSkill` in `src/skills/scan.ts` scans an imported skill without running it and returns `{ schema: 1, digest, findings }`.
  - **Findings.** Eleven categories; graded severities, with `high` only for clear-cut patterns; `digest` equals the package digest. Finding ids are stable (a hash of the scan header, the rule id, the raw path and the matched text, so renaming a rule changes its ids); every distinct match is reported; a secret value is never shown, and a file that holds a secret gets no excerpts.
  - **Credentials.** `credentialHits` in `credential-scan.ts` gives the position of every credential, and of a private key the whole key block. `credentialProblem` is unchanged.
  - **Code and tests.** Rules are in `scan-rules.ts`; tests are in `skill-scan`, `skill-no-network` and `skill-credential-scan`.
  - Nothing calls it yet: no command, config, run or flow format change.
- Flow variables 1 — flows describe their variables (`var_info`), and the shipped flows do (#497). Part 8 of 11 of #407.
  - **Section.** Optional top-level `var_info` in `src/flow/schema.ts` (`VarInfoSchema`, `varInfoProblems`): per variable `label`, `help`, `kind` (`number`, `yes_no`, `text` (default), `list`, `choice`), `min` and `max` for numbers, `choices` for a choice, `separator` for a list (`comma`, `space`, `line`; default `comma`) and `set_by_run`. `vars` is unchanged.
  - **Refused on save.** An entry for a variable not in `vars` (the error names it); `min`/`max`, `choices` or `separator` on a kind they do not fit; `min` above `max`; a duplicate choice; a default that does not fit its own entry.
  - **Runs.** Not checked against `var_info`: `--var`, `.claude-factory/config.yaml` and watchers are accepted as before. A flow without `var_info` loads and runs as before.
  - **Shipped flows.** `VAR_INFO` in `scripts/build-flows.mjs` describes every variable of every shipped flow, including all of `issue-gitflow`. `set_by_run` marks `github_repo` and `issue` (and the other variables a run sets). `review_twice_above_risk` is `text`, since it takes `off` or a number. Retired flows get no `var_info`. `flows/*.yaml` regenerated.
  - **API.** `GET /api/flows/<name>` returns `var_info` (`{}` when the flow has none or does not parse).
  - **Guards.** The hotfix check in `src/engine/guards.ts` ignores top-level `var_info`, so older copies and run snapshots of a shipped flow are still recognised.
  - **Editor.** `ui/editor.js` accepts `var_info` and keeps it in step when a variable is renamed or removed (`moveVarInfo`); `ui/flow-problems.js` opens the Variables section for a `var_info` problem.
  - **Docs.** `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` has the "Describing variables" section, with an example that the complete-flow check of `tests/guide.test.ts` parses. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (Variables) updated.
  - **Tests.** `tests/schema.test.ts` (the `var_info` rules and that every shipped variable has an entry), `tests/server.test.ts`, `tests/runner.test.ts`, `tests/hotfix-guards.test.ts`, `tests/ui-publish.test.ts`, `tests/ui-flow-problems.test.ts`, `tests/ui-step-types.test.ts`.
  - **Not yet.** No form reads `var_info`; that is #498.
- Flow variables 2 — a watcher's variables are fields made from what its flow declares (#498). Part 9 of 11 of #407.
  - **Form.** The watcher form (`repoWatcherDialog` in `ui/watcher-form.js`) shows one field per variable the chosen flow describes: a number field with its range, a switch, a text box, a list editor or a choice, each with label, help and the flow's default as placeholder. A flow is read again when the flow or the source changes, so a new described variable shows without new interface code. A `default` flow resolves to the flow of its source (`DEFAULT_FLOWS`). Save waits for a pending read of the flow.
  - **Stored values.** A field left at its default is not stored. Text and lists have a "Use default" box, so an empty value can still switch a default off. `agent_env` is a list of `KEY=value` pairs with the note "May contain secrets".
  - **Free-text box.** "One name=value per line" stays for variables the flow does not describe. A described variable typed there moves to its field when the form opens; a value with a line break gets its own text area.
  - **Server check.** `addRepoWatcher` and `updateRepoWatcher` (`src/repos/watchers.ts`) take a `describe` function and refuse a described variable whose value does not fit its kind or range (400, `vars.<name>: …`, naming the variable). `set_by_run` variables and undescribed ones are not checked. `updateRepoWatcher` checks only a change that names `vars`, `flow` or `source`, so enabling or disabling an old watcher is never refused. `api-repos.ts` reads the flow through the run's resolver (`resolveFlowPath`); a flow that cannot be read gives no check.
  - **Old values.** A stored value outside its range still runs. It is refused only when the watcher is saved again, and the form marks that field.
  - **Kinds and ranges.** `varValueProblem` in `src/flow/schema.ts` is the one check for a value against its kind; `varInfoProblems` uses it for a default.
  - **Flow API.** `GET /api/flows/<name>` also returns `vars` (the flow's defaults, `{}` when the flow does not parse).
  - **Code.** New `ui/var-fields.js` (`varFields`, `splitVars`, `mergeVars`, `listItems`, `joinItems`), which knows nothing about watchers.
  - **Not done.** Watchers in `config.yaml` keep their dialog: it edits the monitor only (id, interval, enabled), and `PUT /api/config` does not check their variables.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-var-fields.test.ts`; `tests/ui-watchers.test.ts`, `tests/repo-watchers.test.ts`, `tests/repo-watchers-api.test.ts`, `tests/schema.test.ts`, `tests/ui-a11y-admin.test.ts` extended; `tests/ui-inline-styles.test.ts` updated. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (Variables, Watchers) updated.
- Fix: self-update skips its test run when the daily release check already passed the same tests on the same code (#482). Part 3 of 3 of #463. `release-daily` writes a record of a green run on a clean tree to `<data>/tested/`; self-update skips its test step only when the test command is its own, the staged commit has the same tree and the record has exactly the checked form. Every other case runs the tests as before. The log says "is not tested again" and names the run; install and build still run.
  - **Writer.** The `verify` step of `release-daily` (`scripts/build-flows.mjs`) writes `tested/<key>` with `run <id>, <UTC time>, tree <sha>` only when the tests passed, the tree was clean before and after, and `HEAD^{tree}` did not change. Every git exit status is checked. A failed build keeps the record but still prints `CHECKS: no`. A record that cannot be written never fails the release.
  - **Reader.** `src/self-update-tests.ts` (`testedKey`, `passedBefore`, `detectTestCmd`, `pinsTests`, `sameTests`); `src/self-update.ts` (`testCmd` option for tests; the check sits before the test step). A `test_cmd` pinned in `.claude-factory/config.yaml` always runs the tests. The log prints only the run id and time, after they matched the pattern.
  - **Flow.** `flows/release-daily.yaml` regenerated; story flows unchanged.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/self-update-record.test.ts`, `tests/self-update-skip.test.ts` and `tests/release-verify.test.ts`; `tests/gitflow.test.ts` checks that the record exists after a green release run; `tests/helpers/self-update.ts` also holds `tools/detect-commands`.
  - **Limits.** Trust: a file in `tested/` lets merged code install without self-update's own test run, so anything that can write to the data folder can make self-update skip its tests. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Runs list 3d-4: compact density for the runs lists (#462). Part 4 of 4 of #379.
  - **The switch.** The bar has a **Density** group with **Comfortable** and **Compact** (`aria-pressed`). It is on the admin Runs list and on My runs, also in the read-only preview ("view as"). Closed sections, **Show all**, the selection and the focus stay when you switch, and so does the 30-second refresh.
  - **Compact.** Every section is a table (`table.table.compact.run-table`, labelled with the section title), one row per run. Columns: Select, Status, Run, Next, Task, Repository, Updated, and for admins Owner, Steps and Cost, then Actions. A row shows the same texts, links and buttons as the card.
  - **Remembered.** The choice is stored under `scf-runs-density` in `localStorage`. Any other stored value means comfortable; storage that throws is ignored.
  - **Code.** `DENSITY_KEY`, `DENSITIES`, `readDensity(store)` and `writeDensity(value, store)` in `ui/runs-list.js`; `renderSections` takes `density`; `buildRow` is split into `rowParts` (the parts of a row), `cardRow` and `tableRow`; `v.list` is the `ul` or the `tbody`. `runsBar` takes `density` and `onDensity`, and `renderRunsList` and `renderMyRuns` take `store`. `.run-table` styles in `ui/css/pages/runs.css`. Tests in `tests/ui-runs-density.test.ts`, with page cases in `tests/ui-filters.test.ts` and `tests/ui-user-runs.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated. No server or API change.
- Integrations shown the same way: provider, identity and scope (#384). Part 5 of 5 of #262.
  - **One block.** New `ui/integration.js` (pure, no DOM): `integrationOf` gives the provider (GitHub, or the host name of the address), the identity ("GitHub App <slug>", "token of <user name>", "fine-grained token", "deploy key <type and comment>", "the server's own access") and the scope (Read, Write, GitHub API, each "works", "failed" or "not checked" from the last test; "limited to this repository" for a deploy key or the app). `integrationBlock` turns it into the Provider, Identity and Scope items. No secret is part of it.
  - **Used in three places.** The Connection section of a repository view, the Authentication cell of the repository list ("Provider · Identity" with the scope below) and the Credentials page.
  - **Credentials page.** Columns are Owner, Provider, Identity, Scope, Fingerprint, Created and Last used. A credential `repo:<id>` is matched to its repository of the same owner through the repository list; when that list cannot be loaded the row says "Unavailable" and keeps its own data. Long fingerprints and keys wrap. The page is reached from the Repositories page as a third scope for admins (**Mine**, **All**, **Credentials**). No server change; a user never calls `GET /api/admin/credentials`.
  - **GitHub App.** Settings → GitHub App links to **Repositories that use the app** (`#/all-repos?method=github-app`). A repository whose app was removed shows "The administrator removed the GitHub App" as its attention state (was "GitHub App removed").
  - **Authentication filter.** The list takes `method` in the address as a filter with its own chip; removing one chip keeps the others, **Clear filters** clears all of them, and the repository switcher keeps `method` when it clears the repository. With no repository using that method it says "No repository uses this authentication.".
  - Tests: new `tests/ui-integration.test.ts`; extended `tests/ui-admin-credentials.test.ts`, `tests/ui-admin-repos.test.ts`, `tests/ui-repo-view.test.ts`, `tests/ui-repos.test.ts`, `tests/ui-filters.test.ts`, `tests/ui-context.test.ts`, `tests/ui-settings.test.ts` and `tests/ui-repo-status.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Fix: self-update runs the tests of a new commit through a test slot, like the tests of stories (#481). Part of #463. At most 2 test runs at once (`<data>/test-slots`); the tests wait at most 15 minutes for a free slot and then run anyway. The 15-minute limit of the tests starts when they start, so a hanging run ends on its own, and a run that hits it counts as a failed try (see #480). Install and build steps and going back to the previous version are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/self-update-tests.ts` (`markFinder`, `slotStep`) and `src/self-update-state.ts` (`runStep` takes `env`, `waitMs`, `startMark`); `src/self-update.ts` (`slot` option for tests, `stage()` wraps only the `test` step, with the running version's `tools/test-slot`). The start mark is never kept in the record, the failing test names or the log. Tests in `tests/self-update-tests.test.ts` and `tests/self-update-retry.test.ts`; `updater()` in `tests/helpers/self-update.ts` uses a short wait.
- Evidence through the API (#138): `GET /api/runs/:id/evidence` and `scf runs evidence <run-id>` answer the manifest of a run, whether its evidence still verifies, and the references of the files it names (path in the run folder, hash, size), never their content. The owner of a run and administrators may read it; a user gets no models, policies or account ids, and another account's run answers "run not found". A run without a manifest answers `state: "not_available"` with a reason.
- Evidence privacy and retention (#140): stored secrets are hidden in every evidence event before it is written, and the events and the manifest are checked for stored secrets before `evidence.json` is written; when the check cannot be done or finds a secret, the run gets no manifest. `scf clean --evidence-older-than <days>` (`evidenceOlderThanDays` in `POST /api/clean`) removes the evidence files of cleaned runs that ended at least that many days ago and keeps the run; without the option clean-up works as before. A run without evidence is reported as "no evidence" (made before evidence existed, or removed by a clean-up).
- AI help quality 1 — scenario evaluations for the answers and actions of Ask SCF (#244). Part of #226. New `src/server/help-evals.ts` and `src/server/help-eval-scenarios.ts`: 14 fixed scenarios (a product question, a status, a known and an unknown diagnosis, a state claim without evidence, records that disagree, an old record, a proposed action, an action "confirmed" in a later turn, an injected instruction, a forged page context, another account's state, a secret in a record, an administrator's page asked by a user). Each one goes through the real path of a turn and is checked on what is sent to the model and on what the contract check keeps. New command `scf eval help`: it calls no model (every scenario has a fixed answer), prints the verdict and the numbers, writes a report under `evals/help/` in the data folder and ends with 1 when a release is blocked. `scf eval help --live` asks the model of `help.model` instead; it is never run by a test. Thresholds: no secret leak, no leak of another account's data, no offered action, no failed safety case, every citation correct, grounding at least 90%, usefulness at least 80%, at most 10% needless refusals, a prompt of at most 20,000 characters. A report names the failed scenario, the check and ids and counts; it holds no prompt, no answer and no secret. The normal tests run all scenarios with the stub (`tests/help-evals.test.ts`). No new API route and no new setting.
- AI help diagnosis 1 — Ask SCF explains common problems from what the product itself says (#237). Part of #226. New `src/server/help-diagnosis.ts`: for the first two things of a question that have something to explain (a failed or waiting run, a queued run or a queue with waiting runs, a repository whose connection check failed, is old or is missing, and for administrators a board issue, a monitor finding and a server that reports a problem) the server adds a source "Diagnosis of …" with five fixed parts: the symptom, the cause, the supporting evidence, the missing evidence and the safest next check. There is no new diagnosis rule: the cause is the failure summary of the run's next-step record (`failure.ts`, worded by the table of `errors.ts`) or the "why" of a next-step record, for the asking account, or nothing. An error the product does not recognise says "not recognised" and gives the product's own safest next step; a cause written by a model, an old record and records that disagree are "a hypothesis, not confirmed". A reply that rests on such a diagnosis is shown as "likely" at most. No log, transcript or step output is read; each text passes the guard on its own. With diagnoses, at least 2 documentation sections still go with a question (4 without). No new API route and no new setting.
- AI help safety 2 — authorization, retention, audit and usage limits for Ask SCF (#243). Part of #226.
  - **Who may ask.** New settings under `help`: `audience` (`everyone`, or `admins`), `disabled_accounts` (account ids that may not ask) and `keep_days` (default 90, as before). An account that may not ask gets 403 with a plain sentence; an account over its daily questions, or whose help turns of today cost as much as its own daily budget, gets 429. Nothing is stored and no provider is called.
  - **Audit.** New audit actions `help-ask`, `help-delete` and `help-export`: the account, the conversation id, the result and the id of the turn. Never a question, an answer or a title. The Audit page lists them in its action filter.
  - **Export.** `GET /api/help/export` (`scf ask export`) gives an account all its own conversations as JSON.
  - **Deletion.** Deleting an account removes its conversations and its usage numbers (as before); the audit lines stay, without any text. There is no legal hold. The guide says so.
- AI help context 4 — operational answers from live state (#233). Part of #226. New `src/server/help-evidence.ts`: with every question Ask SCF gets what the server observes now for the asking account, as sources of the kind "current state": the things the page names (a run with its next-step record, a repository's connection check, a refinement session; for administrators a board issue and a monitor finding), the account's part of the queue, a user's newest waiting or failed runs, and for administrators the server health. At most 5, each with the kind and id of the thing, the page to open and the time it was observed. All of it comes from the help context service of #231, so nothing of another account is taken. A record that is known to be old says "(may be out of date)"; when records disagree the server adds a source that says so; a reply that rests on either is shown as "likely" at most, never as confirmed. The explanation keeps "Observed:", "So:" and "You could:" apart. No new API route and no new setting.
- AI help context 3 — product answers from the installed documentation (#232). Part of #226. New `src/help/docs.ts`: for every question Ask SCF looks in the documents of the install (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, and `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` for administrators) and hands at most 4 fitting sections to the model as sources, each with its document, heading and version (`docs/USER_GUIDE.md#the-page-context (SCF 0.1.0)`) and an excerpt of at most 1,200 characters. The search is a deterministic word match on headings and text: no model, no network. A user gets sections of the user chapters only. A reply can cite only sections that were handed over; when none fits, the reply is marked "could not check". The index keeps the version and a hash per file; an index that no longer fits is built again and the reason is logged, and a missing document is shown under `documentation` in `GET /api/help/usage` and `scf ask usage`. Documents of a repository that is worked on are never read. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is now part of the package. No new API route and no new setting.
- AI help context 2 — a permission-filtered help context service (#231). Part of #226. New `src/server/help-context.ts`: `helpSnapshot` gives a small, bounded snapshot of a run, the queue, a repository, a refinement session, a board issue, a monitor finding, the next steps or the server health for one account. It has no rules of its own: each adapter asks the permission table for the API call that reads the same thing and uses that call's reader and owner check. A thing of another account, a thing that does not exist and an unknown kind are all "not found". A snapshot never holds a cost, a model name, a folder, an owner or an admin setting, and records which fields were included, omitted, redacted, unavailable or stale. The page context of Ask SCF now finds repositories, refinement sessions, the queue, and (for administrators) board issues and monitor findings, not only runs. No new API route and no new setting.
- Tool isolation 1 — versioned tool profile and adapter capability contracts (#146). Part of #133. New `src/tool-profile/`: types, zod schemas, version numbers and pure helpers only. No agent is started differently, and no flow or setting changes.
  - **`ToolProfile`**: `version`, `profile` (`id` and `version`) and `grants`. A grant names a kind (`files`, `shell`, `git`, `github`, `web`, `mcp`, `builtin`), its operations and its targets, and declares data classification, credential, timeout, concurrency, output cap and approval; a `shell` grant also declares its sandbox. Kinds, operations and every value are closed lists. What no grant names is refused.
  - **`checkToolCall`** answers `allow`, `require_approval` or `deny` for one kind, operation and target, with the policy reason codes `tool.not-allowed`, `repository.not-allowed` and `approval.required`.
  - **Hash and reference.** `toolProfileSha256` is the hash of the canonical form; `toolProfileRef` gives the `id`, `version` and `sha256` a run keeps. Nothing writes it to a run yet.
  - **Least privilege.** `toolProfileExcess` and `isAtMostAsPermissive` compare two profiles; `resolveToolProfile` gives what several profiles all allow, under the strictest condition of any.
  - **`AdapterCapabilities`** says per kind which controls an agent program can hold, and which sandbox levels. `unenforceable(profile, capabilities)` lists what it cannot hold. The records for Claude Code and Codex are in `adapters.ts`: Codex cannot allow tools, commands or paths one by one.
  - **Flows of today.** `legacyToolProfile` turns the tool settings of an agent step into a generated profile and reports the rules it cannot express.
  - **Skills.** `tool_profile` in `skill.yaml` is unchanged: it is what a skill needs. `skillCapabilitiesOf` and `meetsSkillToolProfile` compare it with a `ToolProfile`.
  - Tests in `tests/tool-profile.test.ts`. `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Policy 1 — versioned policy contracts (#141). Part of #133. New `src/policy/`: types, zod schemas, version numbers and pure helpers only. Nothing in the engine asks a policy yet, and no safeguard is changed.
  - **`PolicyPack`**: `version` (of the format), `policy` (`id` and `version` of the contents, the pair evidence already carries), `mode` (`observe` or `enforce`), ordered `rules` and a `defaultOutcome` (`allow` or `deny`). A rule has an `id`, the `boundaries` it is about, `when` conditions, an `outcome` and a `reasonCode`.
  - **`PolicyInput`**: `version`, `boundary` (one of eight moments of a run), `runId` and `facts`: a closed list of short values such as repository, branch, changed paths, secrets found, output size, time, costs, sandbox, owner role and credential. No free text.
  - **`PolicyDecision`**: `version`, `policy`, `packSha256`, `mode`, `boundary`, `inputSha256`, `outcome` (`allow`, `warn`, `require_approval` or `deny`), `reasonCode`, the deciding `rule` and `evaluatedAt`.
  - **Reason codes** are a closed list in `src/policy/reasons.ts`, each with one sentence; a rule can only use a code of that list.
  - **`evaluatePolicy(pack, input, evaluatedAt)`** is pure: no file, network, model, clock or randomness. The first rule that applies decides. A rule that needs a fact the input does not have denies with `policy.input-incomplete`.
  - **Evidence.** `policyIdentityOf` and `policyDenial` turn a decision into the policy identity and the `step.denied` denial of #134. The evidence schemas are not changed.
  - Tests in `tests/policy-contracts.test.ts`. `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Context 2 — a repeatable selector for the context of a run (#152). Part of #133. New in `src/context/`: `select.ts`, `repository.ts`, `patterns.ts` and `store.ts`. **Not wired yet:** no run calls the selector and no run folder gets a `context.json`; that is #153.
  - **`selectContext`** makes a `ContextBundle` and the text of its sources from a repository at one commit, the task (issue text, comments and accepted refinement notes that the caller already fetched, and the paths the task is about), a budget and the policy profile. No model, no network, no clock: the same repository state and task give the same sources and hashes.
  - **Rules**, in a fixed order, each with an id that is recorded with the source: `task-issue`, `refinement-notes`, `root-instructions` (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`), `nested-instructions` (the same names in the folders above a named path), `ownership-file` (`CODEOWNERS` in its three places), `task-comments`, `named-in-task`, `api-schema-files` (OpenAPI, Swagger, AsyncAPI, GraphQL, Protocol Buffers, Avro) and `root-readme`.
  - **Left out, with a reason:** a missing instruction file, ownership file or README (`missing`), a file marked `linguist-generated` in `.gitattributes` or with a "Generated by" header (`generated`), a link, a submodule or a binary file (`unsupported`), and what does not fit the budget (`over-budget`; what a later rule found goes first).
  - **Only the declared commit.** `openRepositoryAt` reads through `git ls-tree` and `git cat-file` at a full commit id, never the working tree; a link is never followed, and the caller's git settings are not used.
  - **Owners** of a file come from `CODEOWNERS`; an owner given as an e-mail address is left out and counted.
  - **`writeContextBundle`** writes the source texts under `context/` and then `context.json` into a folder, and never replaces a file.
  - **Format change** (still version 1, nothing has been written with it): two more reasons for a source that is left out, `generated` and `unsupported`.
  - Tests in `tests/context-select.test.ts` and `tests/context-repository.test.ts`. `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Context 1 — a versioned format for the context bundle of a run (#151). Part of #133. New `src/context/`: types, zod schemas, a version number and pure helpers only. Nothing writes a bundle yet, and `run.json` is not changed.
  - **`ContextBundle`** (`runs/<id>/context.json`): `version`, `runId`, `createdAt`, the repository and commit, a `budget` (`maxSources`, `maxSourceBytes`, `maxTotalBytes`), `policies` (id and version only), `sources` and `omitted`.
  - **A source** records `kind`, `uri`, `revision` (commit or version), `content` (file under `context/`, SHA-256, bytes), `owners`, `classification`, `retrievedAt`, `freshness`, `selection` reason and `consumers`. Kinds: `repository-file`, `issue`, `issue-comment`, `refinement-notes`. Addresses are `repo:…`, `issue:…` and `refinement:…`; a path of the machine or a web address is refused.
  - **Text and policy stay apart.** The bundle holds no source text, and a policy is never a source.
  - **Hash.** `serializeContextBundle` gives one fixed text per bundle, `hashContextBundle` its SHA-256, and `contextBundleRef` the evidence `ArtifactRef` for `context.json`.
  - **Old runs.** `readContextBundle` gives `absent` for a run without the file and never throws; a newer version is refused with one sentence.
  - Not done here: writing `context.json` into the run folder and adding its reference to the evidence of a run. Nothing selects context yet (#152) and nothing writes evidence yet (#135).
  - Tests in `tests/context-bundle.test.ts`. `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Evidence 4 — verify the evidence of a run (#137). Part of #133.
  - **`scf evidence verify <run-id> [--json] [--runs-dir <dir>]`** checks a run folder and prints the result. Exit code 0: verified. 1: not verified. 2: nothing to verify (a run from before evidence, or a run that has not ended). It reads only: no server, no model, no network.
  - **What is checked, in this order:** the manifest (readable, a version this Foundry reads, of this run, no `evidence.incomplete` mark); the event file; the chain (version, place, link and hash of every line); the end (number of events and last hash as in the manifest); the content (the manifest says what the events say); the flow definition in `run.json` (its hash); every file the manifest names (hash and size).
  - **The result** (`VerifyResult`, also what `--json` prints): `status` (`verified`, `failed`, `not-finalized`, `absent`), `verified`, a short `explanation`, the first `failure` (`check`, `code`, `message`, and the `line` or `path`), the state of every check, the manifest's SHA-256, the number of events and the last hash, the state of every file (`ok`, `changed`, `missing`, `unreadable`) and `missingOptional`.
  - **Optional and required files.** A required file (a log, a transcript) that is gone fails the check. An optional file (`evidence.diff`) that is gone is listed under `missingOptional` and the run still verifies. A changed file always fails, optional or not. A link or a folder in place of a file is `unreadable`.
  - **Module:** `src/evidence/verify.ts` — `verifyEvidence` (pure: texts and a way to look at a file) and `verifyRunEvidence(runDir, runId)`. Neither throws or writes.
  - No API route yet: that is #138.
- Evidence 3 — the events of a run are a hash chain, and a run that ends gets a manifest (#136). Part of #133.
  - **Hash chain.** Every line of `evidence.jsonl` now has `prevHash` (the hash of the line before; `null` for the first) and `hash` (the SHA-256 of the event in its canonical form, without `hash` itself). A changed, added, removed or moved line breaks the chain. The event version stays 1.
  - **Canonical form** (`src/evidence/canonical.ts`, the rules of RFC 8785): names in order, no spaces, strings and numbers written one way, UTF-8. Every line is written in it, byte for byte. Fixed test vectors hold the texts and their hashes.
  - **Manifest.** When a run ends as succeeded, failed or cancelled, `runs/<id>/evidence.json` is written from the chain: status and times, the flow and the hash of its stored definition, the number of events and the hash of the last one, base and final commit, models, policies, approvals, test results, every log and transcript with its hash, the diff and the pull request. Only `finalizedAt` depends on when it is written.
  - **Diff.** For a run with a base and a final commit, `runs/<id>/evidence.diff` holds `git diff <base> <final>` with stored secrets hidden; the manifest has its hash. No diff when git fails, takes longer than 15 seconds or gives more than 20 MB.
  - **Resume.** A resumed run keeps all its events and goes on in the same chain. Its manifest and diff are removed when it starts again and written anew at the next end.
  - **A failure is visible.** If the manifest cannot be written, the live log says `! evidence: no manifest was written (…); this run cannot be verified`, and no manifest is left. The run itself is not changed.
  - **A stream that stopped stays stopped.** A write problem now leaves the mark `evidence.incomplete` in the run folder: no later start continues that stream and the run gets no manifest, so a stream with a hole can never look whole. A stream that is not an unbroken chain (also one written without hashes) is not continued.
  - The manifest holds ids, names, hashes and commit ids only: no task, output, transcript text, note or key. The OS sandbox of a user's run cannot write `evidence.json`, `evidence.diff` or `evidence.incomplete`.
- Evidence 2 — every run writes its lifecycle events to `runs/<id>/evidence.jsonl` (#135). Part of #133.
  - **The stream.** One JSON line per event, only appended, in the #134 format. `seq` counts 0, 1, 2, … and the id of an event follows from its place (`ev-000000`, `ev-000001`, …). Nothing is added to `run.json`.
  - **Run events:** `run.queued` (with the time the job was put in the queue), `run.created`, `run.started`, `run.stopped` (also when the run waits for approval), `run.resumed`, `run.cancelled`, `run.failed`, `run.completed`.
  - **Step events:** `step.started`, `step.routed` (the model the step was given to, and the model before it after a fallback), `step.retried` (`outage` or `limit`), `step.denied` (a refused tool, by name only, or a rejected approval), `step.approved`, `step.failed`, `step.completed`. Steps of a sub-flow and of a parallel step have their own events.
  - **Logs are named, not copied.** The end of a step names its log (for an agent step: its transcript) by path, SHA-256 and size. No output, prompt, error text, reason or note is in the stream.
  - **Never in the way of a run.** A write that fails is reported once in the live log (`! evidence: …`) and the run goes on; the stream then stops until the run is started again, so it never has a hole in the middle.
  - **Restarts.** A resume reads the file and goes on at the next place; earlier lines are never changed. A last line that was cut off by an interruption is removed (it never was an event). A file with a line that is not an event of this run is left alone and not continued.
  - **Who.** Start, resume, approval and cancel name who asked: an account id, the command line, a watcher or the Foundry. `Scheduler.cancel` and `cancelWaitingRun` take that as an extra argument.
  - A shell step whose id has the word `test` or `tests` (`run_tests`, `test_main`) is recorded as a test run with its outcome. A target without a model name (`codex:openai`) is recorded with the model `default`.
  - The OS sandbox of a user's run cannot write `evidence.jsonl` or `evidence.json`.
  - Runs without the file (older runs) work as before.
- Starter skill 2 — built-in `spring-boot` skill for Spring Boot application engineering (#187). New package `skills/spring-boot/` (`skill.yaml`, `SKILL.md`, `REVIEW.md`), version 1.0.0, category `platform`, roles coder and reviewer, with a dependency on the `java` skill (1.0.0 or newer), so `java` is loaded first whenever this skill is selected. It applies to a module with a Spring Boot dependency or plugin in its build, or sources that import `org.springframework.boot`; not to plain Java, plain Spring without Boot, or another module of the same repository. It covers following the application's own patterns, dependency injection (constructor injection, no field injection), configuration properties and profiles, controllers and validation, services and transactions, exception handling, observability and test slices, and tells the agent not to add or change starters, the Boot version or frameworks the task did not ask for. HTTP contract, messaging and database rules stay in their own skills. The review checks name transaction boundaries, configuration safety, validation and test coverage. Budgets: planner description 160 characters, coder 1700 tokens, reviewer 600 tokens. The existing `spring-boot` entry of `SKILL_RULES` does the triggering; the manifest detectors are declarative only. New `tests/skill-starter-spring-boot.test.ts` (manifest, description, budgets, MVC, service-only, unrelated-module, plain Java and plain Spring fixtures through catalogue, integrity, resolve with the dependency, payload and run evaluation); `tests/skill-registry.test.ts` checks that it loads as built-in. The behaviour checks do not run an agent. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` says when the skill is offered. No code or flow change.
- Starter skill 4 — Node.js service engineering (#189). New built-in skill `skills/nodejs` (`skill.yaml`, `SKILL.md`, `REVIEW.md`) for coder and reviewer: the supported Node.js version, the package manager of the existing lockfile and the scripts in `package.json`, no new dependency by default, ESM and CommonJS, blocking work on the event loop, unhandled rejections and other asynchronous failures, streams and backpressure, timeouts and cancellation, start-up and shutdown with resource cleanup, configuration, dependency boundaries, logging and tests. The review file checks lifecycle and asynchronous failure paths. It is selected from Node.js evidence only (`engines.node`, `@types/node` or a server package in `package.json`, imports of built-in modules), in JavaScript and TypeScript alike; browser-only TypeScript does not get it. No change to the candidate rules. New `tests/skill-starter-nodejs.test.ts` (JavaScript and TypeScript services, browser-only, Java, Python and Deno fixtures); `tests/skill-registry.test.ts` is extended. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` names the skill and its evidence.
- Starter skill 8 — Cassandra data modelling and access (#193). New built-in skill `skills/cassandra/` (`skill.yaml`, `SKILL.md`, `REVIEW.md`; version 1.0.0, category `database`, roles `coder` and `reviewer`, risk `low`). It applies when a repository or module has a Cassandra driver, a Cassandra mapping or a CQL file, or when the task names Cassandra or CQL, and not to other database work: its capabilities are all Cassandra names, so generic words such as "migration", "schema" or "repository" never select it. The coder instructions cover modelling from the queries, partition and clustering keys, consistency, prepared statements, paging, batches, lightweight transactions, TTL and tombstones, retries and schema changes with their rollout order, and tests. They reject `ALLOW FILTERING` as a design solution and say when it is acceptable (one bounded partition named in full, or a paged one-off job outside the request path, with a comment). The reviewer section checks hot partitions, unbounded partitions, tombstones, consistency and migration rollout. The skill grants no cluster access (no shell, no network, no connector) and assumes no topology. Budgets: planner description 160 characters, coder 2500 tokens, reviewer 800 tokens. No code change: the existing `cassandra` candidate rules do the triggering, and the server pins the built-in at start like the others. New `tests/skill-starter-cassandra.test.ts` (schema, integrity, selection fixtures for a schema, repository access, migrations and unrelated database modules, and behaviour evaluations); `tests/skill-registry.test.ts` checks that the package ships. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` lists it.
- Starter skill 7 — IBM MQ application integration (#192). New built-in skill `skills/ibm-mq/` (`skill.yaml`, `SKILL.md`, `REVIEW.md`; version 1.0.0, category `interface`, roles `coder` and `reviewer`, risk `low`, no connector, no shell, no network). It applies when a repository or the affected module uses an IBM MQ client (the `com.ibm.mq` Java and JMS libraries, `ibmmq`, `pymqi`, `mq-golang`) or holds MQSC files, and not to Kafka, RabbitMQ, ActiveMQ or AMQP work. The word "MQ", "JMS" or "queue" alone in a task does not name it: its capability names are `ibm-mq-jms`, `mqi`, `mqmd`, `mqsc` and `websphere-mq`. The coder instructions make the transaction and acknowledgement boundaries explicit (syncpoint options, JMS session modes, one commit after the work, MQ with a database) and cover MQMD fields, message and correlation ids, request/reply, persistence, expiry, encoding, poison messages and backout queues, duplicates and retries by reason code, reconnect, publish/subscribe and tests. The skill gives no access to a queue manager and asks for no credentials. The reviewer section checks boundaries, duplicates, poison messages, backout thresholds and reconnect safety. Budgets: planner description 160 characters, coder 2300 tokens, reviewer 650 tokens. No code change: the existing `ibm-mq` candidate rules do the triggering, and the server pins the built-in at start like the other built-in skills. New `tests/skill-starter-ibm-mq.test.ts` covers schema, budgets, selection (put/get, request/reply, pub/sub and unrelated-messaging fixtures), integrity, and behaviour evaluations; `tests/skill-registry.test.ts` checks that `ibm-mq` loads as built-in. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` lists the package.
- Evidence 1 — versioned formats for run evidence (#134). Part of #133. New `src/evidence/`: types, zod schemas, version numbers and pure helpers only. Nothing writes evidence yet, and `run.json` is not changed.
  - **`EvidenceEvent`** (a line of `runs/<id>/evidence.jsonl`): `version`, `id`, `seq`, `runId`, `time`, a `type` (eight run events, seven step events) and the fields of that type; `prevHash` and `hash` together, for the hash chain.
  - **`RunEvidence`** (`runs/<id>/evidence.json`, for a run that ended): flow and its hash, the event file with count and last hash, base and final commit, models, policies, approvals, test results, files, diff and pull request.
  - **Identities** for step, model, tool, policy, approval, test, commit, pull request and who acted. No free text and no unknown fields, so no secret has a place: a tool has no arguments, an account is an id, a pull-request address has no sign-in data or query.
  - **Files** are named by a path inside the run folder, SHA-256, size and `required`; their contents are never copied.
  - **Versions.** A version above the one this Foundry writes is refused with a clear sentence; a lower one is migrated step by step, then checked. A run without a manifest reads as `absent`.
  - Tests in `tests/evidence-schema.test.ts`. `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Starter skill 6 — Kafka producer and consumer engineering (#191). New built-in skill `skills/kafka/` (`skill.yaml`, `SKILL.md`, `REVIEW.md`; version 1.0.0, category `interface`, roles `coder` and `reviewer`, risk `high`). It applies when a Kafka dependency, import, client configuration file, schema registry client, or producer, consumer or Kafka Streams code is in the modules the work touches, or the issue names Kafka; not when documentation or code only mentions events, messages or queues. The coder instructions cover the delivery guarantee, producer acknowledgement, keys, partitions and ordering, consumer groups and offsets, duplicates and replay, retries, poison messages and dead letters, transactions, schema compatibility, stream processing, observable failures and tests, with short Java and TypeScript notes. They never assume exactly-once behaviour: it may be claimed only when the configuration is verified in the repository and every effect stays inside Kafka. The reviewer section checks ordering, replay, poison messages and schema compatibility. The skill gives no cluster access: no connectors, no shell, no network, and the instructions forbid connecting to a broker or registry. Budgets: planner description 160 characters, coder 2600 tokens, reviewer 700 tokens. One rule change: the npm packages `@kafkajs/confluent-schema-registry` and `@confluentinc/schemaregistry` now count as Kafka evidence (`src/skills/integration-rules.ts`). The server pins the built-in at start like the other built-in skills. New `tests/skill-starter-kafka.test.ts` covers schema, budgets, selection (producer, consumer, stream processing and unrelated-module fixtures), integrity and behaviour evaluations; `tests/integration-candidates.test.ts` covers the new packages. Docs: `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Skill catalogue", "Write a skill").
- Starter skill 9 — built-in `oracle` skill for Oracle database engineering (#194). New package `skills/oracle/` (`skill.yaml`, `SKILL.md`, `REVIEW.md`), version 1.0.0, category `database`, roles coder and reviewer. It covers what differs from PostgreSQL and MySQL (empty string is `NULL`, no `LIMIT` or `ON CONFLICT`, DDL commits), bind variables, transactions and isolation, locking, sequences and identity, dates and timestamps, LOBs, indexing, pagination, migrations and their rollback, PL/SQL boundaries, ORA error handling and tests. The review checks cover dialect, binding, transaction boundaries, locking, temporal semantics and migration reversibility. Selection uses the existing `oracle` candidate rules (Oracle driver, `oracle.jdbc` import, `.pks`/`.pkb`, `tnsnames.ora`); plain `.sql` files, other databases and generic SQL words in the task give no evidence, and a task that names Oracle or PL/SQL names the skill. The skill is text only: no shell, network, connector or credentials. Its `oracle-migrations` capability makes it high risk under the default `skills.unresolved.high_risk` list. Budgets: planner description 160 characters, coder 2300 tokens, reviewer 700 tokens. New `tests/skill-starter-oracle.test.ts` (JDBC, SQL migration, PL/SQL and unrelated-database fixtures through catalogue, integrity, resolve, payload and run evaluation; the behaviour checks do not run an agent); `tests/skill-registry.test.ts` checks that `oracle` loads as built-in. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated. No code or flow change.
- Fix the model analyst: findings of the code review.
  - **Reviews.** A review step that fails is handled as failing (higher level, or "look at this") before the analyst asks whether its reviews find anything, and a lower level is only advised for a review that works at least 97% of the time. A lowered review is put back when it leads to a change less than half as often as before (the old test, 15 points less, could never be true). No trial starts for a review none of whose reviews led to a change.
  - **Model trials need a published figure.** The trial model's name is made from the current one, so a model trial only starts when `reference.json` holds a figure for that model. The report lists advice that was not tried and why. Effort trials are unchanged.
  - **An override never changes the agent or the provider.** The engine resolves the step's own target and replaces only the model, so a step on a proxy or a local provider stays there. The model of a step that does not run on Claude Code is left alone. A router rule now wins for the effort too.
  - **Overrides follow the flow.** An override stores what the flow said for the step (`from: {model, effort}`) and applies only while the flow still says that. Overrides without `from` work as before.
  - **What a step runs on now.** Visits that ended on a router fallback do not count (a run on the budget fallback cannot be told apart). After a trial was put back and the step has not run since, no trial starts from the trial's own numbers; none starts back to what the flow itself says.
  - **Sub-flows.** Steps inside a sub-flow are classified by the last part of their id (`build/fix_tests`), and the analyst only advises for them.
  - **Counting.** The 20 visits needed for advice do not include visits stopped by a usage limit; time-outs are measured against the same visits in the advice, the trials and the report.
  - **The record owns the overrides.** `tuning.json` is saved before `overrides.json`; at every check an override that no running or kept trial owns is dropped and the override of a running trial is set again if it is missing. Trial entries are validated and a bad one is dropped instead of stopping every check. Running and kept trials are never cut from the record.
  - **Reset.** `scf analyst reset` also ends kept trials as "put back", so the same changes do not start again at the next check.
  - **Published figures.** The lookup session has the two web tools only (`--tools`), no MCP servers, no personal settings, no slash commands, and runs in an empty temporary folder that is removed. A failed or empty lookup is tried again a day later, not every 6 hours. Coding scores without a test name are not compared, values out of range (scores 0 to 100) are dropped, and a source is only printed when it is a web address, with `|` and line breaks made harmless.
  - Tests in `tests/analyst.test.ts` and `tests/analyst-tune.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Ask SCF: a question can carry a bounded page context that the server checks and looks up again (#230). Part of #226.
  - **The envelope.** With `page` and one selected thing (as before), a question may now name up to 5 things (`entities`), up to 8 visible filters (`filters`) and up to 2,000 characters of marked text (`selection`). The whole is at most 4,000 characters; what is too much is cut in a fixed order (page, things, filters, marked text) and the stored envelope says what was cut.
  - **Refused** (400): any other field (owner, role, permissions, a token, a copy of a record), markup, a file path as an id or filter value, and a filter named after ownership, permission or a secret.
  - **Looked up again.** When the turn runs, the server works out which page the address is for the asker's role and looks up every named thing with the rule of the API call that reads it. A thing of another account and a thing that does not exist are both left out and only counted. The model is told only what was found. In this version runs are looked up; other kinds count as not found.
  - **New call** `POST /api/help/context` (`scf ask context`): what would be taken from a context, without storing or asking anything.
  - **Changed for callers.** An id or filter that is a file path or markup is now refused where the first version stored it. The model no longer gets an id the account cannot see; it gets the page type instead.
  - Code in `src/help/context.ts`; "The page context" in chapter 14 of `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, "Ask SCF: the page context" in `docs/DESIGN.md`. Tests in `tests/help-context.test.ts` and `tests/help-context-api.test.ts`. The interface does not send the new parts yet.
- Ask SCF: a defence against orders hidden in copied text, and secrets are taken out (#242). Part of #226.
  - **Secrets.** Before a question is stored, before anything goes to the model and before a reply is stored, the server removes stored credentials (by value), private keys, authorization headers, cookies, sign-ins in addresses, common token shapes, values of names like `DB_PASSWORD=…`, and what matches the new optional setting `help.sensitive_patterns`. In its place stands a mark that names the kind, such as `[removed: a token]`; the message also lists the kinds (`redacted`). The server log names kinds only.
  - **Copied text is data.** A source can now carry a short excerpt (at most 1,200 characters, 8,000 for one turn). Excerpts stand in their own part of the request, apart from the rules and from the question. A source that reads like an order to the model is left out and cannot be cited.
  - **Fail closed.** When the stored credentials cannot be read or a pattern is broken, a new question is refused (503, "Ask SCF cannot check your question for secrets right now…") and nothing is stored; a waiting turn ends as "not set up" without a model call; a reply that cannot be checked is not kept. A page context that holds a secret is refused (400).
  - **Changed for callers.** The reply's texts are no longer cleaned with the stored credentials alone but with all rules above, so a reply can hold `[removed: …]` marks. No new route.
  - Code in `src/help/guard.ts`; "Secrets and copied text" in chapter 14 of `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, "Ask SCF: the guard" in `docs/DESIGN.md`. Tests in `tests/help-safety.test.ts`.
- Ask SCF: answers show their sources, how sure they are, links and suggestions (#236). Part of #226.
  - **In parts.** The short answer first, then how sure it is, what it rests on, the proposals as cards, and the sources and assumptions to unfold.
  - **Five states that look different**: checked, not fully confirmed, not checked (no source of the kind the question needs), the sources do not agree, and not answered. A statement about your work is shown with the lines of the current state it was read from and their times.
  - **Text stays text.** HTML and addresses in an answer are never run or linked. A link appears only for a page of the Foundry that your account has; it is checked in the browser again, after the server.
  - **Cards say what they do**: Read-only or Changes state. A suggested change is shown as a suggestion; Ask SCF does not do it and there is no button for it.
  - Tests in `tests/ui-ask-model.test.ts` and `tests/ui-ask.test.ts`.
- Ask SCF: history, progress, stop, retry and coming back, in the drawer (#235). Part of #226.
  - **Following a turn.** Question and reply stand under each other. The drawer says that Ask SCF is working, after 20 seconds that it takes longer than usual, and then what came of it: the answer, "you stopped it", or the fixed sentence of the failure.
  - **Controls with their consequence.** Stop, Try again, Archive, Take back from the archive and Delete. Each says what it does. Delete asks first. None can be pressed twice: while a call is on its way they are not offered, and Send sends nothing while a reply is written.
  - **Try again** uses the same two messages and names the try ("This is the second try"). It is not offered after a limit, where it cannot help.
  - **History and New conversation.** The list of your conversations with their state, the archived ones on request, and an empty conversation at any time.
  - **Late answers are dropped.** An answer for a conversation you left is not drawn over the one you are in.
  - **After a reload** the drawer shows the conversation you were in and follows a reply that was being written. The tab keeps only its id. Nothing is asked twice.
  - Tests in `tests/ui-ask-model.test.ts` and `tests/ui-ask.test.ts`.
- Ask SCF: a drawer on every page of both displays (#234). Part of #226. Only when `help.enabled` is on; off, no button is shown.
  - **Where.** An **Ask SCF** button in the top bar of the admin and the user display opens a panel at the right side. It is one implementation (`ui/ask.js`, `ui/ask-model.js`, `ui/ask-view.js`); the two shells only mount it. Not in "View as user".
  - **Your page stays.** The panel lies over the page and draws nothing of it again: typed text, open parts and the address stay. A started question is kept when you close the panel.
  - **Nothing is sent by opening.** A question goes out with **Send** (or Ctrl+Enter) only. Under the question box you read what goes with it: the address of the page, and nothing else. A checkbox sends the question alone.
  - **Suggested questions** fit the page. A suggestion fills the question box; you send it yourself.
  - **Keyboard and screen reader.** The panel is a named dialog. The focus goes in and comes back, Tab stays inside, Escape closes. A status line says that Ask SCF is working and when the answer is there. Full width on a narrow screen; no motion.
  - Tests in `tests/ui-ask-model.test.ts` and `tests/ui-ask.test.ts`.
- Ask SCF: help turns run through the configured provider (#229). Part of #226. **Off by default**: an admin sets `help.enabled: true`.
  - **A turn** is one question. `POST /api/help/conversations` (new) or `…/:id/messages` (next question) stores it, answers 202 and queues the turn; `…/retry` tries a failed or stopped answer again in the same messages, `…/cancel` stops one. On the command line: `scf ask new|more|retry|stop`.
  - **No tools.** A turn is one Claude Code call with no tools, no MCP servers, no permissions and no GitHub token, in an empty temporary folder that is removed afterwards. The answer is checked by the contract: no action, only fixed pages, only sources the server offered (none yet, so replies say "could not check"). Only a Claude Code model can answer; a Codex model gives a plain "not set up" answer.
  - **Limits** (`help` in `config.yaml`): time per turn (60 s), turns at once (1), queue length (10), turns per account per day (30), cost per turn ($0.10) and an optional daily budget for all help turns. One turn per account at a time; the others wait in one first-come line.
  - **Plain words.** Timeout, provider failure, provider limit, used-up budget, setup problem, unusable answer, restart and "you stopped it" each have a fixed sentence; so have "switched off", "still answering", "queue full" and "no questions left today". The text of a provider is never shown.
  - **Admins** see provider, limits and usage per account and day with `GET /api/help/usage` (`scf ask usage`), without any question or answer. Users get 403 there and never see a model, provider, cost or token count; the configured model and provider names are taken out of their replies.
  - `help.json` gets `usage` rows (kept 90 days); a file without them reads as before. The fake agent answers help turns (`FAKE_HELP`). Tests in `tests/help-turn.test.ts` and `tests/help-turn-api.test.ts`.
- Ask SCF: conversations and messages are stored (#228). Part of #226.
