  - **What is stored.** `help.json` in the data folder (mode 0600, written under the lock through a temporary file): conversations with stable ids, times, a state and messages. A question keeps the page it was asked on and at most one selected thing; a reply keeps its checked structure (sources, links, drafts, proposals) apart from the text a person reads.
  - **States.** active, failed, cancelled, completed, archived and deleted. A failed, stopped or interrupted reply is tried again in the same two messages; a late result of an earlier try, of a stopped turn or of a deleted conversation is not stored. A reply left unfinished by a restart is marked as interrupted when the server starts.
  - **Access.** Only the owner reads and changes a conversation. Another account's id answers like an unknown one. Administrators get no extra access, and "View as user" shows no conversations. Deleting an account removes its conversations.
  - **Retention.** Delete removes title and messages at once; an empty record stays 30 days. A conversation unchanged for 90 days expires the same way. The clean-up runs hourly, and expired content is never shown before it ran.
  - **Calls.** `GET /api/help/conversations`, `GET|DELETE /api/help/conversations/:id`, `POST …/complete`, `…/archive`, `…/restore`; on the command line `scf ask list|show|complete|archive|restore|delete`.
  - The data-folder move copies `help.json` unchanged. Tests in `tests/help-store.test.ts`, `tests/help-api.test.ts` and `tests/remote-help.test.ts`.
- Ask SCF, the help assistant inside the product: its contract (#227). Part of #226. No page, no route and no model call yet; this is the set of rules every later part goes through.
  - **What it is.** `src/help/contract.ts` names the four kinds of question (product help, status, troubleshooting, guided resolution) and the five parts of a reply (answer, recommendation, navigation, draft, action). Only an action changes something, and the list of allowed actions is empty: Ask SCF is read-only.
  - **Checked on the server.** `checkReply` keeps only evidence the server offered, links to fixed pages the role has, and actions on the server's list (none). Without evidence of the kind a question needs, a reply is marked "could not check"; the sentence is the server's.
  - **Requests it does not serve** get a fixed limitation and the safest next step (`limitationFor`). Backlog refinement goes to Refinement, "what happens next" to the Foundry's own next step, and monitor findings to the Problems page.
  - **Users** never get model names, providers, costs, token counts or anything of another account or of the administrators (`forRole`, `hideNames`).
  - Chapter 14 of `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and "Ask SCF: the contract" in `docs/DESIGN.md`. Tests in `tests/help-contract.test.ts`.
- Fix: the command line for the running Foundry, after a security review. What changes for you: `scf api` with `DELETE`, `PUT` or `PATCH` now asks (`--yes`), and sends a body only with `--data`; errors go to standard error; `scf whoami` exits with 4 when not signed in; `--data` cannot override what a command sends.
  - **Clear text.** `--server` with a host that is not this machine means `https://`; `http://` to another machine is refused, also from a stored session. Before, `scf login --server foundry.example.com` sent the password over plain http.
  - **`scf api`.** No body unless `--data` is given (before: `{}`, so `scf api PUT config` reset the settings). `DELETE`, `PUT` and `PATCH` ask for `yes`. `.` and `..` in the path are refused.
  - **`--data`.** It adds fields only. A field that is fixed by the command, an argument, or an option is refused, and so are the new locked fields: `role` on `users set`; `method`, `token`, `username`, `newKey` on the `repos add…`, `use-…`, `new-key` and `set-url` commands that do not set them; `hours` on the `monitor mute…` commands; `action` and `key` on the `turn` actions. Inline `token`, `password`, `secret`, `current`, `key` and `privateKey` are refused for every command. On a command that reads, `--data` is refused instead of dropped.
  - **Paths.** An argument that is part of the path cannot be empty, `.`, `..` or contain `/` or `\`, and is encoded once. Before, `scf repos remove ../users/<id>` deleted an account.
  - **Answers.** Redirects are not followed (the password went along). Below 400 only JSON counts (and CSV for `audit export`); anything else is "this does not look like a Foundry server". A plain-text refusal shows its first line. A call ends after 120 seconds (sign-in: 30), and a live stream is refused.
  - **Sign-in.** Standard input is read line by line, so `printf 'old\nnew\n' | scf account password` and a piped `scf login` without `--email` work. Only a cookie named `scf_session_<port>` is kept. The session file is written as a new file with mode 600 and moved in place. A wrong password no longer says "sign in first".
  - **Smaller.** `--older-than ""` is refused instead of read as 0. `flows save` and `blocks save` ask before they replace a file; `users set-app-repos` and `users add --role admin` ask. `scf flows delete x` (a mistyped action of `flows`, `blocks`, `monitor`) is an error that lists the commands; `scf flows --help` lists them. The usage error says that text starting with `-` goes after `--`. With `--json` a refusal is printed whole. A `--data @file` that is not JSON is not quoted in the error.
- Fix the helper processes: findings of the code review.
  - **Monitor.** A new run that waits only for a helper's cap is marked `capped` in the queue (the queue also names the `cap`), and the "queue not moving" detector leaves it out. Before, a cap raised a critical finding "N jobs could start … although M slots are free".
  - **Test cap.** `tools/test-slot` ignores a `test-slots/cap` that was not written for 4 minutes and reads the cap again while it waits. The governor writes the test cap also when the cap on runs cannot be written.
  - **`governor`.** Memory pressure "warning" lowers the runs only with a load above 1.5 per core. After 15 checks in a row without overload the cap goes one up, also when the machine is never quite calm. A state older than 4 minutes is forgotten (no cap of yesterday after a night), `sysctl` is called by its full path, no "6 → 5" line when the configured number was lowered, and `scf governor report` shows no limit that has ended.
  - **`flaky`.** The flows keep the log of a test run that failed and passed on the second run as `tests.first.log`; such tests count as "passed on a retry". The parser reads what Vitest prints with `--reporter=verbose` (the default reporter prints nothing for a retried test). A cause goes only to the test whose own error shows it. A run without an issue is no story. Tests, stories and what was filed are kept per repository. A story is filed only for a test file of which a test was also seen to pass, what cannot be filed no longer uses up the two places per check, the state is saved after each filing, and `gh` has a time limit of 60 seconds.
  - **`blockage`.** Waits "depends on" count for 6 hours, until the log shows that the story started or ended. Stories that wait for each other in a circle are reported. Stories are named with their repository. Only the last 512 kB of the service log are read.
  - **`limits`.** The cap is worked out from the runs allowed at that moment, and goes up by one per check at most. `scf limits set` reads a reset without a time zone as local time and refuses one more than 7 days ahead.
  - **Caps.** One file per helper in `governor/caps/` instead of one shared `governor/caps.json` (which is still read, so a cap that holds survives the upgrade).
  - **`digest`, `blockage`.** A run that says "running" while its process is gone is listed as interrupted. A result of another helper older than 26 hours is left out, one unreadable record no longer stops the digest, and the day's file is named by the local date.
  - **Login agents.** `install` waits until the old job is gone and tries `bootstrap` up to three times; `status` goes by what launchd runs. `uninstall` stops the helper before it clears its cap. The service and the analyst still install the old way.
  - **Daily release.** Its test run asks for a test slot again, with a wait of five minutes at most, so it is counted and the governor's test cap holds for it.
- Fix: a cap set by a helper process (the load governor, the limit forecaster) no longer holds back the resume of a run that already started. After a restart under a cap, the run that held the turn to merge could stay in the queue while the runs waiting for that turn filled every slot, so nothing was merged for hours. The cap now holds back new runs only; resumes are bound by the configured number of runs at once. Test in `tests/scheduler-fair-use.test.ts`.
- Fix: self-update runs the tests of a new commit up to 3 times when they fail, 20 minutes apart (#480). The Health line says which try it is and when the next one runs. After the third failure the record and the Health line name the failing tests (at most 20 are kept, redacted and cleaned; the Health line shows 3). A newer commit on main is tried at once. Build and install failures still fail after one try.
  - **Record.** `self-update.json` gets `retry` (`commit`, `tries`, `at`) while the wait runs; `failed` gets `tries` and `tests` for a test failure. Both are optional, so old records load. A `retry.at` that is not a time counts as due now.
  - **Restart.** The wait survives a server restart. The Health line shows the minutes left; the Foundry does not stage before the time is over.
  - **Code.** New `src/self-update-tests.ts` (`failingTests`); `src/self-update.ts` (`TEST_TRIES`, `retryWaitMs` and `now` for tests, reason `retry_wait`); tests `tests/self-update-retry.test.ts`, `tests/self-update-tests.test.ts`, and the shared helpers in `tests/helpers/self-update.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- UI quality 1f-1 — test helpers for usable and readable content (#475). Part 1 of 5 of #372, part of #264. `tests/browser/helpers.ts` has `expectUsable` (visible, enabled, in the viewport, not covered at its centre, 44 × 44 px with `touch`), `expectReadable` (not wider than the viewport, no cut-off text, ellipsis included; the last content can be scrolled into view and is not covered; scroll positions are restored), `clipped` (the elements whose text is cut off by a clipping ancestor; `strict` also counts ellipsis) and `lengthen` (makes visible text 1.4× as long and marks it with `data-lengthened`). Cases in `tests/browser/harness.spec.ts`. Test code only.
- A command line for the running Foundry: every call of the server's API is a command, `scf <area> <action> … [--json]`. 159 commands in 18 areas (`runs`, `turn`, `board`, `find`, `repos`, `refine`, `flows`, `blocks`, `settings`, `providers`, `monitor`, `users`, `admin`, `audit`, `account`, `secrets`, `watchers`, `server`).
  - **Sign-in.** `scf login`, `scf logout`, `scf whoami`. The same accounts and sessions as the web interface; no server change. The session is kept in `cli-session.json` in the data folder (mode 600); the password is asked without echo and never stored.
  - **Every command.** `--json`, `--data '{…}'` / `--data @file.json`, `--server`, `--as` (reading as a user, for an admin with a running view), `--help`. `scf <area>` lists the commands of an area. Exit code 4: not signed in or not allowed.
  - **Safety.** Commands that delete or replace ask for `yes` (`--yes` skips; without a terminal they stop). Secrets are asked for or read from standard input, never taken as an option, and refused inline in `--data`. `scf settings set` insists on `--data` because the server replaces the whole file.
  - **Any call.** `scf api routes [word]` lists all calls with who may make them; `scf api <METHOD> <path>` makes one.
  - **Kept complete by a test.** A command for an unknown route fails, and so does a route with neither a command nor a reason in `API_ONLY` (today one: the live event stream of a run). The file commands (`scf run`, `scf user`, `scf monitor on|off|status`, …) are unchanged.
  - New `src/remote/` (runner, table type, output, session, five area files) and `tests/remote*.test.ts` with the helper `tests/helpers/remote.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Fix: the helper processes and the analyst no longer run as macOS background processes. On an overloaded machine those got almost no processor time, so the load governor did not run exactly when it was needed. Install them again (`scf governor install` and so on) to get the new setting.
- Five helper processes, each with `run|report|install|uninstall|status` and a login agent of its own (macOS): `scf governor`, `scf flaky`, `scf limits`, `scf blockage`, `scf digest`. They read the data folder and write only in their own folder there.
  - **`governor`** (every minute). Lowers the runs at once by one per check while the load is above 2.5 per core or the memory is under pressure (not below half the configured number), raises it after five calm checks, and allows one test run at a time above 4 per core. Changes are logged in `governor/changes.log`; `scf governor reset` takes the limits away.
  - **`flaky`** (every 6 hours). Lists tests that failed, or passed only on a retry, in at least three different stories in 7 days, with a likely cause. `scf flaky auto on` makes one bug story per test file (at most 2 per check, never twice); off by default.
  - **`limits`** (every hour). From one reading (`scf limits set claude|codex <percent> <next reset>`) it estimates the weekly limit and says when it is reached at the pace of the last 24 hours. `scf limits auto on` lowers the runs at once so that it lasts; off by default.
  - **`blockage`** (every hour). Lists the stories that hold up the most others, through held files and "depends on", and says when run slots are empty while stories wait.
  - **`digest`** (daily 07:30). What was done, split, failed and what waits for you in the last 24 hours, with a few lines from the other helpers, and a desktop notification.
  - **Engine.** The scheduler takes the lowest cap that still holds from `governor/caps.json` (never below 1; a cap past its end time is ignored). `tools/test-slot` honours `test-slots/cap`.
  - New `src/ops/` and `tests/ops.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Test ports part 2 — the refinement publish, import and source-label API tests use the shared helper (#467). Part 2 of 6 of #465.
  - **Tests.** The nine `tests/refinement-publish*-api.test.ts` files, `tests/refinement-import-api.test.ts` and `tests/refinement-source-label-api.test.ts` start their server with `startTestServer`, so the system picks a free port. They are gone from the allowlist of the guard in `tests/test-ports.test.ts`. No test case is removed, and there is no product behaviour, API or config change.
- Test ports part 3 — the other refinement API tests use the shared helper (#468). Part 3 of 6 of #465.
  - **Tests.** `refinement-api`, `refinement-architect`, `refinement-backlog-api`, `refinement-draft-api`, `refinement-impact-api`, `refinement-judge-api`, `refinement-round`, `refinement-split-api` and `refinement-talk-api` start their server with `startTestServer`, so the system picks the port. `refinement-ready-api` already did (#466). They are removed from the allowlist of the guard in `tests/test-ports.test.ts`.
  - A test that restarts its server signs in again: the new port gives a new session cookie name. No test case removed; no product behaviour, API or config change.
- Test ports part 5 — the tests that retried on a taken port use the shared helper (#470). Part 5 of 6 of #465.
  - **Tests.** 13 test files (accounts, audit, limits, monitor, network, password links, runs, users) start their server with `startTestServer` and no longer pick a port or retry on `EADDRINUSE`. `tests/net-server.test.ts` uses `freePort` for the one port that must stay closed. These files are gone from the allowlist of the guard in `tests/test-ports.test.ts`. No test case removed; no product behaviour, API or config change.
- Test ports part 6 — the last tests use the shared helper and the guard has no exceptions left (#471). Part 6 of 6 of #465.
  - **Tests.** The last ten files (`auth`, `home-process`, `password-change`, `repo-key`, `repo-watchers-run`, `search-api`, `skill-cli`, `ui-gallery-server`, `user-cli` and `tests/browser/seed.ts`) start their server with `startTestServer`, or give a child process a port from `freePort`. `tests/auth.test.ts` restarts on the port of its first start, because the session cookie is named after the port.
  - **Guard.** The allowlist in `tests/test-ports.test.ts` is gone, with the code in `tests/helpers/port-scan.ts` that served it and its two test cases; one case that the scan has no exceptions takes their place. No file under `tests/` picks its own port any more. No other test case removed; no product behaviour, API or config change.
- Test ports part 1 — a shared free-port helper, and `startServer` gives back its port (#466). Part 1 of 6 of #465.
  - **Server.** `startServer` returns `port`, the port it really listens on. With `port: 0` the system picks a free port, and `url`, the session cookie name (`scf_session_<port>`) and the host check use it. A port other than 0 behaves as before. The CLI still takes `--port` 1-65535.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/helpers/port.ts`: `startTestServer` starts on port 0 and adds `base`; `freePort` returns a port that was free a moment ago (only for a child process that needs `--port`). New `tests/helpers/port-scan.ts` and the guard in `tests/test-ports.test.ts`, which fails on a self-chosen port in `tests/` outside an allowlist. The allowlist can only shrink; parts 2-6 of #465 move the rest.
  - The refinement tests and their helper use `startTestServer`. No product behaviour, API or config change.
- Test ports part 4 — API tests with a port constant at the top of the file use the shared helper (#469). Part 4 of 6 of #465.
  - **Tests.** Thirteen test files (repositories, watchers, credentials, key variables, permissions, view-as, monitor problems and the server tests) start with `startTestServer`, so the system picks the port. They are off the allowlist of the guard in `tests/test-ports.test.ts`. No product behaviour, API or config change.
- Large log and diff browser checks test what the page draws (#476). Part 2 of 5 of #372.
  - **Three repairs.** The large-seed log now has `LONG_TAIL` on line 5,000, so a kept line is long; the large-log check waits until the visible log holds its 2,000 kept lines and ends with line 5,000, then opens the Logs tab and times it; the large-diff check waits for 1,500 drawn lines, then clicks "more" and waits for 2,000.
  - **Measured.** `LARGE_LOG_MS` is now the time to a visible, capped log (2,000 kept lines, last line "line 5000 of the large log", note "3000 earlier lines are not shown"): median 567 ms, under the 3,000 ms limit, so its `KNOWN` entry is removed. `LARGE_DIFF_MS` measures 614 ms against its 1,000 ms limit.
  - **Not done.** The "UI quality 6 - draw a long live log in one step" follow-up is dropped with that entry, since its budget now passes. Budget limits are unchanged; only the `LARGE_LOG_MS` reason text names the new median.
  - **Tests.** `tests/browser-seed.test.ts` (the last log line is over 600 characters), `responsive.spec.ts` (log and diff stay in their own box), and `performance.spec.ts` (a harness case for the log readiness check). `Ready` in `tests/browser/perf.ts` gained `visible` and `last`. No product code or config change.
- UI quality 1f-3 — seed data and run clean-up for the journey and stress specs (#477). Part 3 of 5 of #372.
  - **Seed.** With `startSeeded({ large: true })` only, a published flow `note` (`SeedData.noteFlow`) writes the task into `note.txt` through `$FACTORY_TASK`, echoes `NOTE_WAITING` and stops at an approval. `seedStress` (`SeedData.stress`) adds a failed run with a 2,000 character reason and a 100 character `github_repo`, and a run whose diff in `stress-work` has added lines of at least 400 characters. Neither field is set without `large`.
  - **Journey 1.** "1 Start work" runs on the large server, with the flow list cut to `note`. The run id comes from the `POST /api/runs` answer; `cancelRuns` is the fallback.
  - **Helpers.** `tests/browser/helpers.ts`: `apiAs(role, "large")`, `startRun` (moved from `journeys.spec.ts`) and `cancelRuns`, which cancels every running or waiting run and every queued job with a task, also when a run has no record yet.
  - **Tests.** `tests/browser-seed.test.ts` checks the stress data, the `note` flow and a `note` run (waiting, diff, log, cancelled). `tests/browser/journeys.spec.ts` checks the clean-up in the failure paths.
  - **Not changed.** No product code. Task 1 keeps its counts; it has a different fixture from the other four tasks (see `docs/ui-redesign/measurement.md`).
- UI quality 1f-4 — the essential journeys work at 360, 768, 1024 and 1440 px (#478). Part 4 of 5 of #372.
  - **Cases.** New `tests/browser/essential.spec.ts`: the eight journeys in `ESSENTIAL` (`tests/browser/widths.ts`) at each width, 32 cases in all. Each step proves its control with `expectUsable` (`touch` at 360 and 768 px), acts, then proves arrival with `expectReadable` and `expectNoSidewaysScroll`. Journeys 1 and 2 are Sign in and Find what needs me; approval in journey 2 happens on the run page of a hand-started `gate` run, which is cancelled in `finally`. The other large-server journeys (Start work, Diagnose a failed run, Board) cancel their runs in `finally` too.
  - **Helpers.** `card`, `COLUMNS`, `expectBoardFits` and the run helpers moved from the board and journey specs into `tests/browser/helpers.ts`; `board.spec.ts` and `journeys.spec.ts` import them.
  - **CSS fixes.** In the coarse-pointer block of `ui/css/responsive.css` (copied to `tests/fixtures/ui-style-baseline.css`): `.run-link` is now 44 px and `inline-flex` like the other lists, and `.scf-tabs__tab` is 44 px, since the Refine journey showed it at 35 px. No new breakpoint. No screenshot was regenerated, so a screenshot that moves with these rows may need a new baseline.
  - **Follow-up.** "Ask SCF drawer at the four widths with help switched on" (`test.info()` annotation in `essential.spec.ts`). The seeded servers have Ask SCF switched off, so the drawer case is skipped. A live guard checks that `#ask-scf` is hidden and empty on both displays.
  - **Results.** `docs/ui-redesign/measurement.md` has a `## Responsive results` table, filled from a real chromium run. `tests/ui-redesign-audit.test.ts` checks its rows against `ESSENTIAL`, its header against the widths and the cell format.
  - **Not changed.** No product code, no new breakpoint and no `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` change; the CSS fixes only enlarge the touch targets that the 44 px rule already sets for coarse pointers.
- UI quality 1f-5 — long and large content stays readable at 360 px (#479). Part 5 of 5 of #372, which closes #372 as part 6 of 6 of #264.
  - **Stress items.** New `tests/browser/stress.spec.ts`: one test per item of `STRESS` (`tests/browser/widths.ts`) at 360 px with touch, each ending with `expectNoSidewaysScroll`. Repository name (100 characters) is shown in full with no strict clipping, on the run page, the Runs list and Repositories (`/api/repos` routed so the first repository carries the long name). Text 40 % longer (`lengthen`) on Home, Runs, Repositories, Refinement and Start work, for user and admin: nothing clipped, every drawer link and every visible `button, a.btn, select` usable. A status of 2,000 characters keeps its failure card readable; the raw details hold all 2,000 characters, and the API keeps the reason in full (no limit). A log of 5,000 lines shows exactly 2,000 lines, the note "3000 earlier lines are not shown", line 3001 first and line 5000 last. A diff with 400 character lines scrolls inside `pre.diff`. A big diff shows "Show all 2005 lines" and then 1,000 added and 1,000 removed lines. A board with 30 cards in one column fits and its last card link is usable. A tall Add repository dialog (`https-token`, 360 × 480) scrolls inside the viewport; its close button, "Add repository" and "Back" are usable; Escape closes it and adds nothing. The dialog height stays 480 px: the form overflows there, so it was not lowered to 400.
  - **CSS fixes.** In `ui/css/responsive.css` (copied to `tests/fixtures/ui-style-baseline.css`), no new breakpoint:
    - coarse pointers: board card issue links are 44 px and `inline-flex`;
    - below 768 px the top bar wraps instead of cutting with an ellipsis: page title, health chip, account name, repository context and the top actions;
    - the Runs "Show" button group (`.seg`) wraps instead of squeezing its buttons;
    - the big-diff file box no longer clips its scrollable `pre.diff`.
    No screenshot was regenerated. These rules can move `tests/browser/visual.spec.ts` baselines, so refresh them when those tests are run.
  - **Results.** `docs/ui-redesign/measurement.md` has `### Stress content at 360 px` under `## Responsive results`, with the seven rows filled from a real chromium run (all `pass`). The section names 360, 768, 1024 and 1440 px and says the Ask SCF drawer is not built. `tests/ui-redesign-audit.test.ts` checks the rows against `STRESS` and the "not built" sentence.
  - **Tests.** `stress.spec.ts` passes all seven items on chromium. `npm run build && npm test` passes.
  - **Known failures and follow-ups.**
    - The `npm run typecheck` errors in other test files (for example `tests/ui-work-board.test.ts`, `tests/browser/board.spec.ts`) were already there and are not fixed here. No error is reported in `stress.spec.ts`, `widths.ts` or the audit test.
    - 11 cases in the neighbouring browser specs fail on chromium (admin `navigation.spec.ts`, several in `tables.spec.ts`). They look like missing seeded text ("ann@example.com", "Seeded failed run", a long repository name), not layout. Not yet checked against a clean checkout.
    - The big diff is checked for sideways scroll only after it is opened and expanded, not before.
    - The dialog's address step is not checked for sideways scroll after the dialog opens.
    - Screenshot baselines for the CSS fixes above are not regenerated.
- Skill deduplication 1 — canonical identity and content fingerprints (#208). Part of #196.
  - **What it does.** New `src/skills/identity.ts` with `skillFingerprint` (a SHA-256 over the skill's content that ignores provenance, `skill.yaml`, `.DS_Store`, the folder name, line endings and a BOM in `.md`/`.txt` documents, frontmatter layout and the Unicode form of paths), `buildSkillIdentity` (`SkillIdentity`: `id`, `upstream`, `name`, `version`, `fingerprint`, `digest`) and `relateSkills` (`same-artifact`, `same-content`, `version-conflict`, `other-version`, `distinct`). `normalizeSkill` returns the `identity`, and the normalization report lists a `fingerprint` per skill.
  - **Scripts and assets are exact.** Files under `scripts/` and `assets/`, and files that are not `.md`/`.txt` documents (for example a root `run.sh`), are hashed byte for byte, so a line-ending change there is a different artifact. A `SKILL.md` whose frontmatter does not parse is hashed as text and never equals a valid one.
  - **Body kept.** The `SKILL.md` body is hashed as written (only line endings change); indentation and trailing blank lines count.
  - **Unchanged.** `SkillPackage`, `skillDigest`, the registry, pins, run locks, `id@version` and the `digest` field. Nothing reads the fingerprint yet. No command, config, run or flow format change.
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-identity.test.ts` (fingerprint, relations and fixtures in `tests/fixtures/skill-identity/`) and additions to `tests/skill-normalize.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Skill libraries safety 1 — enforce the open-source licence and provenance policy (#201). Part of #196.
  - **What it does.** `evaluateLicence` in `src/skills/licence.ts` judges the licence evidence of one skill: the licence files of its nearest folder (or of the skill itself), the licence files inside the skill, and the notice files above it. Each licence must be identified and accepted; the result is `allowed`, `review`, `rejected`, `missing` or `ambiguous`. Fails closed: text that is not recognised exactly is `ambiguous`, never accepted. Repository-wide and per-directory licence files both count; a source-available or missing-licence folder is refused even when other folders are open source.
  - **Policy.** `src/skills/licence-rules.ts`. Allowed: the source licences (`SOURCE_LICENCES`). Review: MPL-2.0, EPL-2.0, LGPL, GPL, AGPL, CC-BY-SA-4.0. Rejected: BUSL-1.1, SSPL-1.0, Elastic-2.0, PolyForm, Commons-Clause, CC-BY-NC, CC-BY-ND. The source's `licence.allow` list must also name each licence found. Short licences (MIT, ISC, 0BSD, BSD-2/3, Unlicense) and Apache-2.0 are matched by their full text; a changed word means "not recognised".
  - **Declared licence.** A `license:` in SKILL.md must name the licence files (or point to one); any other term is `ambiguous`. A source-available term anywhere is `rejected`.
  - **Promotion.** `promoteImport` in `src/skills/promotion.ts` refuses a skill whose evidence is not `allowed` and lists it in `refused` with its reasons. It throws when the input does not belong together (another source, commit or repository; a disabled source; a skill outside the approved paths; a bad `retrievedAt`).
  - **Manifest.** `manifestText` (schema 1) lists every promoted package with its repository, repository key, immutable commit, upstream path and name, retrieval time, licences, declared licence, licence files (path, SHA-256, bytes) and author notice lines. `attributionProblem` checks that the attribution files are still kept byte for byte.
  - **Attribution.** Licence and notice files are returned byte for byte in `PromotedPackage.attribution`, so the approved artifact can keep them.
  - **Code.** `isUtcTime` is now exported from `src/skills/release.ts` (same behaviour) and used for `retrievedAt`. Nothing calls `promoteImport` yet; the story that writes approved artifacts must call it and keep the attribution files. No command, config, run or flow format change, so `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` is unchanged.
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-licence.test.ts` and `tests/skill-promotion.test.ts`, with the Apache-2.0 texts in `tests/fixtures/licences/`; `tests/skill-release.test.ts` covers `isUtcTime`.
- Skills.sh metadata discovery without automatic trust (#213). Part of #196.
  - **Command.** `scf skills discover [<export-file> [<file>]]` reads a saved Skills.sh export (no scraping, no site calls), resolves each GitHub https repository to its current `HEAD` commit with `git ls-remote`, and stores it as a `proposed` candidate in `<data folder>/skill-source-candidates.json`. It prints `PROPOSED`, `KNOWN` (already in `skill-sources.yaml`, any status), `UNRESOLVED` and `PROBLEM` lines. With no operand it lists the stored candidates.
  - **No trust.** The command never writes the allowlist, the lock, the quarantine or releases. Trust, licence, popularity and safety claims from the export are not copied; credential-like entries are dropped. It is optional: runs never read the list.
  - **Safety.** One detached `git ls-remote` per repository, in its own process group, killed on timeout (20 s), too much output or Ctrl-C. Git's text is never shown. Ctrl-C leaves the list unchanged; a broken list is never overwritten.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/discovery.ts`, `src/skill-import/ls-remote.ts`, `src/skill-import/discover-cli.ts`; `OPTIONS` is exported from `git-tree.ts`. Tests: `skill-discovery`, `skill-discover-cli`, `skill-ls-remote`, and cases in `skill-no-network` and `cli`. No config, run or flow format change. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Skill libraries foundation 4 — normalize external skills into `SkillPackage` (#200).
  - **What it does.** `normalizeImport` and `normalizeSkill` in `src/skills/normalize.ts` turn the skills of a prepared import into `SkillPackage` values, plus a provenance record and a `NormalizationReport` (`normalized` and `quarantined`). Pure: no file system, process or clock, so the same input gives the same output on every machine. Nothing calls it yet, so no command, config, run or flow format changes.
  - **Layouts.** Four upstream layouts are read: `plain` (Agent Skills frontmatter), `metadata` (extra fields under `metadata:`), `extended` (extra frontmatter keys such as `tags`, `depends-on`, `requires`) and `foundry` (a `skill.yaml` next to `SKILL.md`). The layout is recorded in the provenance.
  - **IDs.** `sourceQualifiedId` builds `<source>-<name>-<hash>`; the hash covers source, repository and name, so unrelated repositories cannot shadow each other. The upstream name is kept in the provenance, never overwritten.
  - **Normalized fields.** Capabilities, technology versions, required tools (with their effects), dependencies, entry points (instructions, scripts, documents) and context-size estimates. Versions such as `v1.2` become `1.2.0`.
  - **Provenance.** Unknown upstream metadata is kept as `frontmatter.<key>`, `metadata.<key>` or `manifest.<key>` (canonical JSON, size-bounded) instead of being dropped.
  - **Quarantine.** A skill with a missing or invalid required field is listed in `quarantined` with its reasons and produces no package.
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-normalize.test.ts`, with fixtures for the four layouts in `tests/fixtures/skill-layouts/`. The rules and value helpers are in `normalize-rules.ts` and `normalize-values.ts`.
- Runs list 3d-3: the Archived view and bulk archive (#461). Part 3 of 4 of #379.
  - **Archived view.** **Show** gains **Archived** (`view=archived`). It asks `GET /api/runs?archived=1` with the other filters and draws one section "Archived (n)", newest first. "Only what needs me" and the repository filter apply there too. Admin and My runs share it.
  - **Checkboxes.** Rows in Failed, Completed and Archived have a checkbox "Select run <flow> <work or id>", and each of these sections has **Select all** (the rows shown, after **Show all**). Other sections and the read-only preview have none.
  - **Bulk bar.** With a selection, "N selected", **Archive N runs** (or **Archive 1 run**, or **Take out of the archive** in Archived) and **Clear selection**. Archiving asks once ("Archive N runs? They stay available under Archived."); taking out does not ask. Runs are sent one after the other; while a batch runs (question included) the buttons do nothing and nothing is drawn after the page was left.
  - **Result.** A toast "Archived 10 runs." or "Took 10 runs out of the archive.", with " 2 were refused: <the server's sentences>" when some were refused. The list then reloads. The selection survives the 30-second refresh, a run that leaves a selectable section is dropped, and changing to or from Archived clears it.
  - **Code.** `archiveRun(id)` and `unarchiveRun(id)` in `ui/api.js`; `ARCHIVED` and `archivedSections` in `ui/runs-list.js`; `renderSections` takes `select` and returns the selectable ids; `bulkBar`, `bulkArchive` and `bulkText` in `ui/runs-bar.js`; `ask` option on `renderRunsList`. The user page sets `asking` around the question. `.bulk-bar` and `.run-select` styles in `ui/css/pages/runs.css`. Tests in `tests/ui-runs-bulk.test.ts` and the runs list, bar, filter and user runs tests. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated. No server or API change.
- Fix: the daily release no longer queues for a test slot. Its check step could wait up to 90 minutes for a free slot while the step itself is stopped after 60, so on a busy machine the run failed with "timed out" and no release pull request was opened. `verify` in `release-daily` now starts its tests at once (`FACTORY_VAR_TEST_SLOTS=0`), as the story that holds the merge turn does. Test in `tests/gitflow.test.ts`.
- The model analyst: `scf analyst run|report|install|uninstall|status`. A process of its own that, every 6 hours, reads the runs of the last 14 days and writes `analyst/report.md` and `analyst/latest.json` in the data folder.
  - **Per step and model:** how often the step worked, timed out, was repaired (`fix_*` came next) or was sent back by a review (`address_*` / `revise_*`), minutes, tokens and its share of all tokens.
  - **Advice:** try a lower level (effort first, then the next smaller model), try a higher level, time limit rather than model, look at this (worse in the last two days), or keep. 20 visits are needed; visits stopped by a usage limit do not count against a model.
  - **Published figures:** once a week a Claude Code session with web search only fetches scores, prices and speeds into `analyst/reference.json`, each with its source and who measured it. Independent figures are used as they are; makers' and unattributed figures are lowered by a tenth. They can hold back advice for a much weaker model and never outweigh your own runs. `--no-web` skips the lookup.
  - **Schedule:** `scf analyst install` adds the login agent `com.spaghetti-code-foundry.analyst` (macOS). It is separate from the server's agent.
  - **Self-tuning** (`scf analyst auto on|off`, off by default; `scf analyst reset` puts everything back). The analyst sets the advised model or effort for a step in `analyst/overrides.json`, one trial per flow at a time. After 20 visits a lower level is kept unless the step does worse, a higher level only when it does better; a far worse trial stops after 8 visits; a put-back change is not retried for 30 days. `analyst/tuning.json` records every trial and the report shows them.
  - **Engine.** Every agent step reads `analyst/overrides.json` at its start (a router rule wins; a missing or broken file changes nothing) and logs "set by the analyst". The step record has the new field `effort`. No flow file is changed.
  - New `src/analyst/`, `tests/analyst.test.ts` and `tests/analyst-tune.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Search and filters on My runs (#460). Part 2 of 4 of #379.
  - **The bar.** My runs shows the bar of #459 (search, Repository, Flow, Status, Time, "Only what needs me", **Show**, **Clear filters**) without the Owner select. `owner` in the address is ignored. The address, the count announcement, "No runs match your search and filters.", "Only the newest 200 matches are shown." and the 300 ms debounce work as on the admin list.
  - **Server calls.** `a.runs` gets the parameters of the filter, and no argument when no filter is set. The canonical repository name is read from `runFilters` first. A missing or failing `runFilters` still draws the list, with empty menus.
  - **Redraw and errors.** A filter change always redraws, also when the answer equals the old one. A failed reload shows the "Could not refresh" note with **Retry**; nothing is rolled back. The read-only preview ("view as") shows the bar and can filter.
  - **Leaving the page.** Nothing is asked or drawn after the page was left; the cleanup stops the bar's timer.
  - **Code.** `renderMyRuns` in `ui/user/runs.js` has the new options `filter` and `alive`; `ui/user/app.js` passes `filter: parseFilter(<hash>)`. `askRuns(ask, f, names)` moved out of `renderRunsList` into `ui/runs.js` and is exported, so both lists share it. No server change. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Skill libraries foundation 3b — fetch an approved commit into quarantine (#458).
  - **Command.** `scf skills import <source-id> [<file>]` fetches the approved commit of an enabled source and stages it with the #457 staging step. It prints `REJECTED` lines, `OK … (staged)` or `(unchanged)`, and `Quarantine: <path>`. Exit 0 when all skills are staged, 1 for a rejection or a `PROBLEM`; usage errors exit 1 with the usage text.
  - **Isolation.** `src/skill-import/git-tree.ts` fetches one commit into a bare temporary repository and reads it only with `ls-tree` and `cat-file`. No checkout. https only, no redirects. Git runs with an empty private `HOME` and none of your git config, credential helper, `~/.netrc` or prompts. Errors carry a fixed code, never git's text.
  - **Limits.** 120 s, 256 MiB during the fetch, 8 MiB file list. The fetch runs in its own process group and is killed on timeout, size or interrupt. Ctrl-C or SIGTERM before writing stages nothing.
  - **Code.** The writing part of `stageImport` is now the exported `publishImport`; `stageImport` behaves as before.
  - **Tests.** Real-git, stub-git and real-signal tests, plus static checks that only four git commands are started and that only `src/cli.ts` imports `skill-import/`. No config, run or flow format change.
- One Repositories page for admins (#383). Part 4 of 5 of #262.
  - **Scope switch.** `#/repos` shows **Mine** and **All** links (`.scope-switch`, `aria-current` on the open one) for an admin. `#/all-repos` is the same list with the scope **All**. `renderRepos` in `ui/repos.js` takes `scope` (`MINE` by default) and `switcher`; the user display and the default output are unchanged. `sortByAttention` and `attentionCount` in `ui/repo-status.js` take an optional attention function.
  - **All rows.** The All list has an Owner column (with the "blocked" pill and "Unknown account"), is sorted by attention, then owner, then address, and links each row with **Open** to `#/repos/<id>`. It has no **+ Add repository**, no deploy-key banner and no methods-failed banner. The next step promotes only **Test connection**. New `allAttention` in `ui/admin-repos.js` adds two states: "Not on the app list" (bad) and "Watcher problem" (warn). A failed test wins over both.
  - **Admin sections.** New `ui/repo-admin-sections.js` (`adminSource`, `adminSections`) adds **Branches**, **Run settings** (with the Definition of Ready) and **Watchers** to the repository view, and **Transfer** to Dangerous actions. Settings, Definition of Ready and Transfer moved off the list rows. `settingsDialog` takes `{ only, title }` and saves the stored values of the other fields unchanged. Watchers can be added, edited, enabled, disabled and deleted from the view; a failed call shows the server's sentence.
  - **Foreign repositories.** For a repository of another account, `renderRepo` takes `source` and `attention` and hides **Change authentication**, **Remove** and **Generate a new key**. It shows "Only the owner can remove this repository or generate a new key." The back link goes to `#/all-repos`. **Test connection** stays.
  - **Watchers link.** In `ui/watchers.js` the heading of a repository group links to the repository view. A small CSS rule marks the open scope link.
  - No server, API or permission change; old addresses keep working.
- Guided connect and re-authentication with verification and read-back (#382). Part 3 of 5 of #262.
  - **`ui/repo-connect.js` (new).** Holds `repoDialog` (moved from `ui/repos.js`, which re-exports it, so callers do not change) and the pure helpers `storesToken`, `saveUnknown`, `replaceLines`, `verdict` and `readBack`.
  - **Steps.** **Add repository** runs in steps: 1. Address (with **Next** and **Back**; an empty address is refused on the spot), then 2. Method and 3. Details. The focus moves to the first field of each step.
  - **Automatic test.** After a save the dialog tests the connection by itself (not for the method "none"). It stays open and locked while the test runs.
  - **Read-back.** The result shows the address, the method, the user name, the public key of a deploy key, the checks, and "A token is stored. It is never shown again." No secret is shown or kept; typed tokens are cleared after a save. A failed or missing test offers **Test again** and **Change details**. The repository is kept. Callers reload after **Done**.
  - **Confirm gate.** **Change authentication** ends in a step that says what is replaced or deleted and that watchers may stop. The button **Replace the sign-in** stays disabled until you tick "Replace the current sign-in". **Back** keeps what you typed.
  - **Uncertain save.** When a change fails with a server error or no answer, the dialog reads the repository again and shows what is stored now. If that fails too, the confirm step says what is stored is not known, and asks for the token again. "Nothing changed" no longer closes the dialog until a save works.
  - **Close rules and `modal` fix.** The dialog closes only after its last request ends. `modal()` in `ui/dom.js` notices when `#modal-root` holds other content: it removes its key listener and leaves the newer content alone (no Tab trap, no Escape that empties the root).
  - The GitHub App help line now says the connection is tested right after you save. No server, API, permission or storage change.
- Search and filters on the admin Runs list (#459). Part 1 of 4 of #379.
  - **The bar.** A bar above the list has a search box ("Search runs", placeholder "Run id, #7 or text", 200 characters at most), selects for Repository, Flow, Status, Time (Today, 7 days, 30 days) and Owner, the checkbox "Only what needs me", the **Show** buttons (All, Needs you, Running, Queued, Failed, Completed) and **Clear filters** (hidden when nothing is active). Every control has an accessible name and a `data-focus` name. The owner select moved from the page header into the bar.
  - **Server and browser.** Search, repository, flow, status, time and owner go to `GET /api/runs` as `q`, `repo`, `flow`, `status`, `since` and `owner`, so a match older than the newest 200 runs is found. "Only what needs me" and **Show** filter in the browser and ask nothing new. A repository name is matched in any letter case: the page loads `/api/run-filters` first and sends the exact name; an unknown name keeps the browser filter and its note. With a server-side filter and 200 runs back, the list says "Only the newest 200 matches are shown." No server change.
  - **The address.** `#/runs?q=…&repo=…&flow=…&status=…&time=…&owner=…&needs=1&view=…`, always in this order. Wrong or unknown values are dropped. `#/runs` with only `repo` and `owner` works as before; the repository context keeps working, and a saved view still stores repository and owner only.
  - **Typing.** The server is asked once, 300 ms after the last key. The search box keeps its node, text and focus over the 30-second refresh, a refresh during the 300 ms and a change of another control (the pending text is part of that change). No request and no address change happen after the page was left.
  - **Redraw and errors.** A filter change always redraws, also when the answer equals the old one. When a reload fails, the address, the bar and the list go back to the last filter that was drawn (also when a newer change overtook an older one) and a toast shows the error. Queued jobs with no run of their own follow the repository, flow, time, search and owner filters and are hidden under a status filter.
  - **Messages.** A polite status region says "1 run matches." or "N runs match." while a filter is active. With no match the list says "No runs match your search and filters." with **Clear filters**, which restores `#/runs`.
  - **Code.** New `ui/runs-filter.js` (pure: `parseFilter`, `filterHash`, `filterQuery`, `sinceOf`, `jobMatches`, `isActive`, `countText`) and `ui/runs-bar.js` (`runsBar`, `noMatch`; the debounce timer lives in the bar and `stop()` clears it). `ui/api.js` `runs(params)` takes a string (owner) or an object, and has the new `runFilters()`. `ui/context.js` keeps the new keys on the Runs page. `.runs-bar` styles are in `ui/css/pages/runs.css`. New tests `tests/ui-runs-filter.test.ts` and `tests/ui-runs-bar.test.ts`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- A repository view with sections: overview, connection and dangerous actions (#381). Part 2 of 5 of #262.
  - **`#/repos/<id>` (new page).** Opens one repository in both displays; `#/repos` is unchanged. An unknown id, or one of another account, shows "No such repository." with a link back. `ui/ia.js` has the detail page `repo`; routed in `ui/app.js` and `ui/user/app.js` (the breadcrumb names the repository); `USER_HASH` in `ui/auth.js` accepts the address. No new API route. Both repository pages in the admin router draw into their own box, so a late list answer cannot paint over the view.
  - **`ui/repo-view.js` (new).** `renderRepo(main, { id, admin, readOnly })` draws the sections from a `SECTIONS` array `{ id, title, show(ctx), draw(ctx) }` (part 4 can add admin sections before Dangerous actions): **Overview** (name, address, attention state and next action, active runs, added date, Definition of Ready), **Connection** (method, user name, public key with Copy and hint or the app install link, the last test with every check, Test connection, Change authentication) and **Dangerous actions** (Generate a new key for deploy keys, Remove; both ask first and name the repository and what is deleted). While an action is pending, every action button of the view is disabled. Read-only mode shows the sections with no buttons.
  - **`ui/repos.js`.** `keyBlock`, `appBlock`, `test`, `newKey`, `remove`, `removeQuestion`, `newKeyQuestion`, `repoHref`, `loadActiveRuns`, `fillRuns` and `installLink` are shared exports; `connectionLines` takes `{ all: true }`. A list row keeps only its next action and an **Open** link; the Authentication cell shows the method label only. The deploy-key banner says to open the repository for the key. The Remove question says the repository's settings and watchers are deleted, and names a stored key or token only for a method that has one. The list's "Try again" for a failed removal moved to the view.
  - **Docs.** `docs/ui-redesign/inventory.md`, `dialogs.md` and `prototypes.md` list the new route and the moved `confirm` lines. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Flow editor and refinement session use the page templates (#449). Part 5c of #341.
  - **Flow editor.** The page is built with `editorPage`, and the header with the kit `pageHeader`. The class `flow-head` is now `page-head`, and `.editor-body` is gone. `with-overview` is set on the page root (`ui.page`). A flow name of only spaces falls back to the saved name, then to "flow", so the header does not throw while typing.
  - **Editor grid.** Moved from `layout.css` to `templates.css` under `.page-editor`. The region wrappers use `display: contents`; the Overview pane (`.page-editor .page-side > *`) is sticky and becomes one static column at 1100 px and below.
  - **Refinement session.** The page is `conversationPage({ head, thread: [note, waiting, workspace] })` and the toolbar is `pageHeader`.
  - **Retry focus.** When the "Could not refresh" note goes away while focus is inside it (Retry or the automatic poll), the session title takes the focus, also after the title is redrawn. Focus is not moved if the person went elsewhere.
  - **Not done yet.** The `admin-flow` visual case and its baseline images are not added; they need Playwright on darwin.

- Skill libraries foundation 3a — stage portable SKILL.md folders into quarantine (#457).
  - **Layout.** `stageImport` writes `<data folder>/skill-quarantine/<source>/<commit>/` with `import.json` (the record) and `files/<upstream path>`. Folders have mode 0700, files 0600. It writes under `skill-quarantine.lock`: a temp folder, a check, then one rename.
  - **Plan.** `planImport` (no file system) finds skills, and `prepareImport` checks content and `SKILL.md` frontmatter (`name`, `description` must be strings). A skill with a link, submodule, unsafe or colliding path (case or Unicode form), or over a limit is rejected with a short reason; the other skills are still staged. Limits: 200 files and 2 MiB per skill, `SKILL.md` 64 KiB, 256 KiB per file, 64 MiB and 500 skills per import. Results do not depend on the listing order.
  - **Repeat imports.** The same source and commit returns `unchanged` after the folder is fully verified; any difference is a `conflict` and nothing is changed. Disk errors become `cannot-write` with only the error code.
  - **Registry.** `discoverSkills` never scans the quarantine, also through a link, a different letter case or `allow_unreleased`.
  - **Data-folder move.** `VERBATIM_DIRS` makes the move copy `skill-quarantine/` unchanged (no path rewrite).
  - No command yet; it comes in 3b. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Repository rows show health, sign-in, last check, active work and the next action (#380). Part 1 of 5 of #262.
  - **`ui/repo-status.js` (new).** Pure functions, no DOM: `repoName`, `repoAttention(repo, { admin, options })` → `{ level, text, action }`, `sortByAttention`, `attentionCount`, `attentionSummary`, `activeRuns(runs, repo)` (running and waiting runs matched on `github`), `matchesText` and `readCompact` / `writeCompact`.
  - **My repositories.** Each row shows the short name (full text in `title`), the sign-in method, the connection state, the last check, the active runs and at most one attention state with one next-step button. Order: needs authentication (method `none`, non-admin), GitHub App removed, last test failed (first failed message), never tested, nothing to do. Repositories that need attention come first; a line above the table counts them. The button runs the existing action (Change authentication, Test connection, the install link) and is not repeated in the Actions cell. A read-only preview shows no buttons.
  - **Filter and compact rows.** A text filter on the address appears from 8 repositories; typing redraws only the results. **Compact rows** is remembered in `localStorage` (`scf.repos.compact`).
  - **Active runs.** Loaded in the background from `/api/runs`; "…" while loading, "—" if the call fails. No new API route. Styles are in `ui/css/components.css`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Runs list in sections, with stable polling (#378). Part 3 of 4 of #258.
  - **Sections.** The admin Runs page and My runs show **Needs you**, **Running**, **Queued**, **Failed** and **Completed**, in that order, each with a count; empty sections are left out and a run is in exactly one. The section comes from the server's record only (`next.who === "You"`, the `pending` entries of `GET /api/queue`, the run status). Completed is collapsed above 10 runs, with **Show all**.
  - **Rows.** One row for both pages: status, next action, task summary, repository and issue, updated time; the admin adds owner, steps and cost. Queued rows show their place (`1 of 4`), "goes first" and, for a user, "N runs ahead of you"; the admin Queued heading shows `2/3 running`. The Queue card, the two tables and the toolbar count are gone. The `readOnly` preview shows no Remove button.
  - **Stable refresh.** New `ui/runs-list.js` (`sectionOf`, `runSections`, `renderSections`, `listRow`, `activityAt`) updates rows keyed by run id; the 30-second refresh keeps focus, scroll, open sections and the chosen owner, and unchanged data changes no DOM node. A late older answer is dropped (`seq`, also on the admin list), and nothing is drawn after the page was left. `runCard` in `ui/user/runs.js` is now an alias of the new row.
  - **Styles and tests.** Section styles in `ui/css/pages/runs.css` and `ui/css/responsive.css`; new `tests/ui-runs-list.test.ts`; UI and Playwright tests updated (Runs screenshot baselines need review). `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Runs") updated. No server change.
- Named personal views for lists and boards (#353). Part 4 of 4 of #255.
  - **`ui/views.js` (new).** `viewStore(accountId, store)` keeps `{ v: 1, views: [{ id, page, name, filters }] }` under `scf.views.<accountId>` in `localStorage` (`list`, `add`, `rename`, `move`, `remove`). Names are 1–40 characters, trimmed, unique per page; at most 20 per account. `filters` goes through `parseQuery`/`formatQuery`. Garbage is read as no views and replaced on the next save; unreadable storage refuses writes and leaves the bytes alone. Also `viewsRow`, `manageViewsDialog` and `isStale(view, { repos, owners })`. It never calls the API.
  - **Pages.** Runs, Board, the redesigned Work page, Refinement and My runs show the row and **Save view**; `filterBar` takes `onSave` and `row`, `filterEmpty` and `filterGone` take `onDelete`. Each page takes a `views` option; a "view as user" preview has none.
  - **Stale views.** A view whose repository is not in the account's list, or whose owner is not among the run owners, is marked "out of date" and opens the empty state with **Clear filters** and **Delete this view**. The Board loads owners from `/api/run-owners` (admin only); if unknown, no view is marked for its owner.
  - **`ui/context.js`** adds `account()`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Saved views") and `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` updated. No server change.
- Repository context 4 — the Work page follows the address (#446). Part 4 of 4 of #352.
  - **Address first.** `renderWork(main, arg, { user, query, go })` keeps `repo` and `owner` in local variables set from `#/board/<name>?owner=<id>`. A saved repository or owner is ignored on load, also one that conflicts with the address. The repository matches the poll answer without regard to case; the address is not rewritten.
  - **Changes.** The repository select writes `#/board/<name>` or `#/board` and keeps `owner`; the owner select writes `?owner=`; **Clear filters** writes `#/board`. All go through `go`, so through `context.keep`. The page no longer adds `repo=all` itself.
  - **Polling.** A changed poll answer no longer calls `cleanPrefs`, so A → B and A → All keep the new selection and the address.
  - **Unknown repository or owner.** Both are kept. An unknown repository shows the "not in your list" empty state with **Show all repositories** (`data-focus="work-all"`) and does not widen the scope. An owner without stories shows "No story matches the filters." and **Clear filters**.
  - **Use as my context.** With a registered context offer the toolbar shows **Use as my context** (`data-focus="work-context"`); it stays on an empty board when a scope is chosen or a context is offered.
  - **`ui/app.js`** passes `query` and `go` to `renderWork`. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` updated.
- Repository context 3 — Clear, "Use as my context" and the Board's unknown repository (#445).
  - **Clear filters.** With an active context, **Clear filters** and removing the repository chip write `?repo=all` (`owner` stays), so the context is not put back. `clearedHash(role, hash, ctx)` leaves addresses that name a repository, `#/board/<name>?owner=…`, pages without `scope`, an inactive context and unknown addresses unchanged.
  - **Use as my context.** The filter bar offers it when the page shows another known repository, or all repositories while a context is set. `setContextOffer` / `contextOffer` in `ui/filters.js` hold the offer; `filterBar(…, { onContext: null })` suppresses it, an explicit `onContext` wins, and `stop()` clears it. With an offer and no chips the bar names the scope.
  - **Classic Board.** `pickRepo` ignores case and returns undefined for an unknown name. `#/board/<unknown>` shows the "not in your list" text, all repository buttons and a button to `#/board?repo=all`. Old `#/board/<unknown>` links now show this empty state instead of the first repository.
  - **Routers.** The user router passes `go` to `renderRefinement`, `renderRepos` and `renderMyRuns`; both routers send filter changes through `context.keep`.
- Small screens 1e-2 — read-only Watchers, monitor lists and Overview (#453). Part 2 of 3 of #371. Below 768 px: the watchers list has no "+ Add watcher", "+ Add the monitor" or empty-state action (↻ stays); one watcher has no Check now, Edit, Enable/Disable or Delete; the monitor's page has no "Switch bug stories…" button, "Mute a detector" row, Actions column or "End mute"; the Overview shows no "Cancel run" for a `closed_elsewhere` problem. `renderWatchers`, `renderWatcherDetail`, `storiesRow`, `monitorLists`, `mutesTable`, `renderHealth` and `renderOperations` take `readOnly` (default `false`); the header health panel and the Problems page do not pass it. `operations`, `watchers` and `watcher` leave `NOT_YET` in `ui/app.js`, so the notice shows and a crossing of 768 px redraws them. The monitor list redraw no longer shows a literal "null" for absent children. At 768 px and wider nothing changes. No server change. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- Skill libraries foundation 2 — allowlisted upstream skill sources and owner onboarding (#198).
  - **Source manifest.** `skill-sources.yaml` (`schema: 1`) lists sources with `id`, `repository`, `status`, `reason`, `ref`, `paths`, `adapter`, `trust`, `licence.allow`, `update.cadence` and `owner`. `src/skills/sources.ts` parses it (`parseSources`, `readSources`, `usableSources`); only `enabled` sources are usable. Nothing reads it in a run yet, and nothing is fetched.
  - **Refs.** An approved release needs a full commit id in `ref.approved`; a branch or tag is allowed only in `ref.track`, for update discovery.
  - **Status.** `disabled`, `quarantined` and `retired` are explicit and need a `reason`. An enabled source needs an owner, an approved ref and an update cadence.
  - **CLI.** `scf skills sources check [<file>]` checks the file offline, without choosing or moving the data folder. Each problem names the field and says how to fix it.
  - **Docs.** "Skill library sources" in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` describes the format and the pull-request workflow.
- Repository context 2b — the switcher is live in both routers and after repository changes (#451). Part 2 of 2 of #444.
  - **Start and stop.** Both displays start the context after sign-in (`initContext` in `ui/app.js` and `ui/user/app.js`); the first page draws after the list has loaded or failed. In the read-only preview (`?as=`) it starts with `readOnly`, so storage is never read or written. When the preview ends the context is stopped; when the view cannot be shown none is started.
  - **Addresses.** With two or more repositories a bare `#/runs`, `#/refinement`, `#/repos`, `#/all-repos` or `#/board` is rewritten with `history.replaceState` before the page draws (`?repo=<name>`, `#/board/<name>` or `?repo=all`). An address that names a repository or has any query wins. A redirected address is not scoped. With fewer than two repositories nothing is rewritten.
  - **Choosing.** On a page with `scope` a choice changes the address and the page draws again; `owner` is kept. On other pages only the label changes.
  - **After a change.** Adding or removing a repository (My repositories, the Add dialog from **Start work**) and a transfer (All repositories) call `reposChanged()`, and the switcher loads again: none, one, many changes it from text to label to button. One → many on `#/repos` rewrites the address to `#/repos?repo=all` without drawing twice.
  - **Docs and tests.** New "Repository context" section in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`. Router, user, Start work, My repositories and All repositories tests extended; `tests/helpers/flow-app.ts` stubs `context.js`.

- Small screens 1e-3 — the flow builder is view-and-run below 1024 px (#454). Part 3 of 3 of #371. At 1024 px and wider nothing changes.
  - **Flow pages.** `flows`, `flow` and `new-flow` leave `NOT_YET`. Below 1024 px the header has no Save and no More menu, and "Test run" is the primary action; the list has no "Draft flow" or "Blank flow"; Visual mode has no add-step bar and the settings card and step inspector are locked with `lockReadOnly`; YAML mode is `readonly`; variable lists show name and value in two columns (`.kv` rule in `ui/css/pages/editor.css`). `renderHeader`, `renderNavigator` and `renderEditor` take `readOnly`; `createFlowPage` takes `isReadOnly`.
  - **Asked at every change.** `save`, `remove`, `newBlank`, `generateDialog` and Ctrl/Cmd+S do nothing when the window is narrow. A delete or rename clean-up that fails after the window shrank offers no Retry, and its callback does nothing.
  - **`#/new` and `#/flows`.** `#/new` shows "needs a wider screen" unless an unsaved draft exists; then the draft is shown read-only. The welcome page offers no create buttons.
  - **Resize.** Crossing 1024 px redraws the flow page; a dirty flow keeps its edits in both directions and the notice says "unsaved changes are kept". On `#/library` the flow list in the sidebar redraws at the crossing, although the Library page stays full.
  - Tests: unit tests in `ui-flow-page`, `ui-editor`, `ui-flow-states`, `ui-flow-save`, `ui-library-states` and `ui-editor-styles`; the browser spec `support-levels.spec.ts` covers `#/flows/gate` at 768 and 1024 px and a resize from 1024 to 800 px.
- Page templates 5b — the runs list, the board and the run page (#448). Part 2 of 3 of #341. Nothing changes for the eye; focus and scroll behave as before.
  - **Runs list.** `renderRunsList` mounts one `collectionPage` in every state (loading, first-load error, 403, data). With data the slots are `actions` (queue text, owner filter, spacer, note, Refresh), `notices`, `filters`, `list` and `empty`.
  - **Board.** `boardView` returns one node, a `boardPage`, on all three return paths; `data-scroll="board"` sits in `.page-columns`. `renderBoard` still mounts the banner, the body and the note into `main` in that order. `.board` moved from `ui/css/pages/board.css` to `ui/css/templates.css` as `.page-board .board`, with the same declarations.
  - **Run page.** `createRunHeader` builds its bar with `pageHeader({ layout: "toolbar", … })`. `renderRunDetail` mounts `detailPage` with the head holder, the stream banner, the alert, the status and the tabs. When the focused Reconnect button goes away, focus moves to the run title.
  - **Docs and tests.** "Who scrolls" in `docs/DESIGN.md` updated; style baseline and the tests for the list, board, run header, run page and templates extended.
- Skill libraries foundation 1 — administrator skill folders load only as an approved catalogue release (#197).
  - **Behaviour change.** A folder in the data folder or in `skills.roots` is refused (`root-refused`) unless its `catalogue-release.json` matches an entry of `skills.releases.approved` and its packages match the manifest exactly. After this update, existing folders stay refused until they are an approved release or `skills.releases.allow_unreleased: true` is set (development only, default `false`).
  - **Release manifest.** `catalogue-release.json` has `schema`, `catalogue`, `version`, `createdAt`, `source` (`repository`, `commit`, optional `ref`) and `skills` (`id`, `version`, `digest`). The release digest covers every field and every skill digest, not key order or whitespace. The reader never opens links or special files and refuses a manifest with a credential-like value.
  - **No network.** Skills are never fetched from a public network while planning, coding, reviewing or resuming. A test checks the imports.
  - **CLI.** `scf skills` prints a `RELEASE` line per release and a `NOTE` for each folder loaded without an approved release.
  - A refused source does not use up the package or byte limits of later sources. `readRegular` opens files with `O_NONBLOCK`.

- UI redesign workspace 5d — The refinement session page as one workspace with named parts (#357). Last part of #260. `#/refinement/:id` keeps the title, state, repository and **Rename**, **Drop** and **Restore** on top and shows one of five parts at a time: **Idea and brief**, **Conversation**, **Story drafts**, **Hand off** and **Log**. Each tab shows a short state ("none", "made" or "working"; "n questions open"; "n drafts, m ready"; "n on GitHub" or "nothing ready"). A "Next:" line (ask the architect, answer the questions, add a draft, check readiness, publish) is advice only and switches to its part. The chosen part is kept per session in page memory through polling and reloads; a new session opens on "Idea and brief", one with drafts on "Story drafts". Panels are hidden, never rebuilt, so typed text and pending saves survive a switch. The architect's status line shows in every part while it works. The tabs are a tab list (`role="tablist"`, `aria-selected`, arrow keys, focus kept with `data-focus`). New `ui/refinement-workspace.js` (`workspace`, `partSummary`, `nextStep`, `defaultPart`); `briefSection`, `briefParts` and `briefMeta` moved there and `ui/refinement.js` re-exports them; `publishable` is now exported from `ui/refinement-next.js`. Rules and API calls are unchanged. New "The session page" in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`; tests in `tests/ui-refinement-workspace.test.ts`.
- Small screens 1e-1 — support levels, the notice, and read-only Users and Settings (#452). `supportLevel(pageId, width)` in `ui/viewport.js` returns `"full"` or `"read-only"`: administration pages are read-only below 768 px, `flows`, `flow` and `new-flow` below 1024 px, every other page (the Library too) is full at every width; an unknown id or a missing or `NaN` width gives `"full"`. `levelNotice(pageId, { dirty })` gives the notice text or `null`. `route()` fills one `#level-note` element above `#main` (hidden on full pages and on pages in the `NOT_YET` set in `ui/app.js`, which keep their controls for now). New helper `lockReadOnly(root)` in `ui/fields.js`: text fields become read-only, selects and checkboxes are disabled, buttons are removed. Wired in this part: Users (no "+ Add user", "Default limits" or empty-state action), one user (only "View as user"; the Account card stays) and Settings (no Save button or note); Credentials, Audit and Dashboard get the notice only. Crossing 768 px redraws the page only when its level changes; a crossing while a dialog is open is kept and the page is redrawn when the dialog closes. Unsaved Settings fields are dropped when the width crosses 768 px. At 768 px and wider nothing changes. No server change. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Small screens") and `docs/DESIGN.md` updated.
- UI redesign workspace 5c — Entry choices: a task, an idea, an existing issue, or a session you already have (#356). `#/start` opens with "How do you want to begin?": **Start a task now** (the default; with a kept draft it says so and opens it), **Refine an idea first**, **Refine an existing issue** and **Continue a refinement session** (goes to `#/refinement`). The new-session dialog moves to `ui/refinement-new.js` (`newSessionDialog(repos, onMade, { kind })`; `sessionProblem` and `sessionBody` are re-exported from `ui/refinement.js`) and has two kinds: "An idea" (`{ repo, idea, title? }`) and "An existing issue" (`{ repo, issue }`; a blank, zero or non-number issue is refused with "Give the issue number."). The server's refusals show in the form, and the typed repository, kind, title, idea and issue number are kept in page memory until the session is made. The separate import dialog is gone. The preview shows no entry that makes a session. No server change. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/ui-redesign/` updated.
- Skill safety 1d — every Claude session checks the locked skills against the policy and the real step, and records what it was given (#431).
  - **Behaviour change.** A skill that needs a shell is no longer loaded into a Claude step without one, for example a read-only step. The step is not refused; the log says `· skill tools: <key> not loaded in this step [tool-not-in-step]`.
  - **Policy changes after the lock.** If the policy no longer allows a locked skill, or a locked version needs `network` and the gate never saw it, the session is refused with `skill tools: <key> [tool-not-approved]: …`. With `skills.tools.missing: degrade` the skill is left out (`! skill tools: <key> left out [tool-not-approved]`). A dependant is left out with its dependency. Every call checks again, so retries and resume decide again.
  - **Record.** The step record has `skills.grant`: mode, approved rules, sandbox, capabilities with their sources, `connectors: []`, `met` and `leftOut`. The log has a line `· skill tools: mode …; … approved rule(s) …; sandbox …; shell …, network …, files …; connectors: none`.
  - **Isolation and resume.** A session stays isolated when a skill was left out. A step does not resume an earlier session when a skill was left out of it.
  - **`allowed-tools`.** `allowed-tools` in a skill's SKILL.md is never passed on to Claude.
  - **Failure hint.** A `skill tools: …` failure says: allow it under `skills.tools`, or set `skills.tools.missing: degrade`, then resume the run.
  - **Not changed.** Codex sessions, reviewer steps, `--allowedTools` and the permission mode behave as before. The capabilities are an estimate; they decide only which skills are loaded, never what a session gets.
- Repository context 2a — the repository switcher view and its controller (#450). Part 1 of 2 of #444.
  - **Not visible yet.** Both displays get an empty `<span id="context" class="context">` as the first child of `#top-actions`. It takes no space while empty. Nothing calls `initContext` yet, so the top bar looks the same; part 2b turns it on.
  - **View.** New `ui/context-view.js` (`switcherView`) shows no repository, a label for one, or a button with a panel for two or more. With eight or more there is a search box. Rows have a star button; the panel closes with Escape or when you choose.
  - **Controller.** `ui/context.js` adds `initContext`, `switcherSections`, `reposChanged` and `goneText`. A user's list comes from `api.repos()`, an admin's from `api.allRepos()`, grouped by owner. A failed first load shows "Repositories could not be loaded." with **Retry**.
  - **Compact screens.** At 767 px and below the panel is fixed under the top bar and rows are at least 44 px high.

- Skill safety 1c — a run stops at the plan gate when a skill needs a tool or connector it may not use (#430).
  - **Behaviour change.** A pinned skill that needs `network`, or more than the flow gives, now stops the run until `skills.tools` allows it. Recover with the config and a resume.
  - **Record.** `run.skillPlan.tools` has `action`, `met`, `missing`, `leftOut` and `warnings`. It is absent on older runs, when the resolver already stopped, when no skill was selected and when the check failed (which fails closed with `skills not resolved: the skills could not be checked`).
  - **Codes.** `tool-not-approved`, `tool-not-in-flow` and `connector-unavailable`.
  - **`degrade`.** The run goes on with a warning. A mandatory skill, or one a mandatory skill needs, still stops.
  - **Log.** `· skill tools: <key> <need> — <sources>` and `⚠ skill … [code]`.
  - **Not filtered yet.** Under `degrade` a left-out skill is only marked and warned about; it stays in the run's lock and its text still reaches sessions until a later part.
  - **Modes.** Planned, explicit and `plan_check` runs are checked, also on resume. Older run files stay valid.
  - **Docs and tests.** New "Skills, tools and connectors" in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, one line in `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md`; new cases in `tests/skill-gate-run.test.ts` and `tests/skill-gate-tools.test.ts`.
- UI redesign workspace 5b — Start work: review before start, and the draft is kept (#355). The form button is now **Review**: it runs the required-field check and shows a review page (flow, repository or "No repository", full task, every value with "set by the flow" for fixed ones, safety notes, **Start** and **Back**). Only **Start** calls `POST /api/runs`, once, with exactly the body shown; a refused or failed start returns to the form with the server's sentence and every value. New `ui/user/start-draft.js` keeps flow, repository, task and inputs in page memory (never web storage) across page changes, with "Your draft from before" and a **Clear** button; it is cleared after a successful start, a stale flow or repository falls back to the default, and the `?as=` preview never reads or writes it. Start work journey 1 now counts 4 clicks (was 3). New "Review before start" in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`; counts in `docs/ui-redesign/` updated.
- Repository context 1 — store, address rules and `repo=all` (#443). Part 1 of 4 of #352; nothing new is drawn yet. New `ui/context.js`: a store `scf.context.<account id>` with the current repository (empty means All), favourites and recents (last 5, no duplicates ignoring case), `prune`, and a memory fallback when storage is missing or fails. It also has `scopedHash` and `chosenHash` (addresses for scoped pages and for a choice in the switcher) and `repoList` / `switcherRows` (the rows of the switcher). `ui/ia.js` exports `ALL_REPOS`, marks six pages with `scope` and accepts `repo=all` in the address; `resolve` drops it from the page's query, so `#/runs?repo=all` shows the unfiltered list. `GET /api/admin/repos` rows now carry `github` ("owner/name", lower case) for GitHub repositories. "Filters in the address" in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` mentions `repo=all`.

- UI quality 1d — a narrow-screen board: one column at a time (#370). Below 768 px the board shows one column, chosen with a labelled **Column** picker that lists every column with its card count and marks the columns that hold cards of a highlighted chain. The first non-empty column shows by default; the choice is kept when the board refreshes and falls back to the first non-empty column when it disappears. `boardView` takes `column` and `onColumn`; the picker and all columns are always rendered and CSS in `ui/css/pages/board.css` hides them by width, so from 768 px up the board is unchanged. No server change. New unit tests in `tests/ui-board.test.ts` and browser tests in `tests/browser/board.spec.ts`. See "The board" in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`.
- Page templates 5a — the template module, the toolbar layout of `pageHeader`, the guard, and the dashboard and settings pages (#447). Part 1 of 3 of #341. Nothing changes for the eye.
  - **Templates.** New `ui/templates.js` exports `overviewPage`, `collectionPage`, `boardPage`, `masterDetailPage`, `editorPage`, `settingsPage`, `conversationPage` and `detailPage`. Each returns `div.page.page-<name>` with its regions as plain `div`s in a fixed order; an empty slot (`null`, `false`, `[]`) draws no region. The head is `pageHeader` in its toolbar layout (options `title`, `back`, `status`, `actions`), or a ready node in `head` (not together with `title`). It imports only `./dom.js` and `./kit/product.js`.
  - **`pageHeader` option `layout`.** `"header"` (default, unchanged) or `"toolbar"`; any other value throws. The toolbar layout draws the flat `div.toolbar.page-head` of the existing pages (back link `a.btn.ghost` with "←", `h1`, meta, actions). It uses the old classes on purpose, until the pages move to the `scf-` header. Documented in `docs/UI_COMPONENTS.md`; a variant in the gallery.
  - **CSS.** New `ui/css/templates.css`, imported right after `layout.css`. `.dash-grid` moved there from `layout.css`, scoped under `.page-overview`.
  - **Pages.** `renderDashboard` and `renderSettings` (loading and loaded) mount one `overviewPage` and one `settingsPage`. Order and content of the nodes are as before.
  - **`mount()`.** When the focused control is gone, the fallback heading is looked for in the target, then its parent, then the nearest `.page` ancestor, then the target itself.
  - **Guard.** New `competingSelectors(cssText, shared)` in `tests/helpers/ui-css.ts`; the test "keeps shared component classes out of the page files" now also checks `templates.css`.
  - **Tests and docs.** New `tests/ui-templates.test.ts`; cases in the kit, dashboard, settings, dom and css tests; new baseline CSS order. Visual cases `admin-dashboard` and `admin-settings` are added to the browser matrix; their baselines still need `npm run test:ui` on a machine with Chromium. "UI styles" and the new "Page templates" in `docs/DESIGN.md`.

- UI quality 1c — critical tables become cards on narrow screens (#369). Part 3 of #264.
  - **Cards.** Below 768 px the Runs (admin and My runs), Repositories and Refinement lists show each row as a labelled card, main link first and actions last (Restore in Refinement).
