  - **Not done.** The open findings are not filed as GitHub issues yet (`gh` needed approval in this step). The table says "not filed yet"; SR-O2 and SR-O3 have no title or severity until their issue numbers are found.
- Refinement 6c — the view compares the size with the build limits and recommends a plan review (server) (#287).
  - **Behaviour.** The view of a draft carries `fit`: "likely fits in one story" when the estimated files and lines are within `max_files` and `max_code_lines` of the repository's build flow, else "likely too big — consider splitting" (`over` lists `files` and/or `lines`); the text names both limits. With no watcher, or a flow without both limits, the verdict is `unknown` ("the build limits are not known"). It is worked out on every read of the session (the flow is loaded each time, no cache), so a changed limit shows at once. With at least one `sensitive` topic the view carries `planReview`: a recommendation for a plan review by a person, with the topics and the repository's `review_plan_label`, or a note that there is no review label. `PUT /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/review-label` with `{ "add": true | false }` stores `addReviewLabel` with the draft (owner only, also when no review is recommended); nothing is sent to GitHub, no run sets it, and neither the view nor the choice blocks a change or a publish.
  - **Which watcher.** The first enabled watcher of the repository whose flow has both limits; the watcher's `vars` win over the flow's. Order: the owner's own stored watcher, then `config.yaml`, then any other. Only the two numbers and the label name are used. A failed lookup gives no limits and never fails a read.
  - **Old installs.** `addReviewLabel` is optional in `DraftSchema` (only `true` is stored), so stored sessions still load.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/build-limits.ts` (`buildLimitsOf`); `src/refinement/draft-impact.ts` (`fitOf`, `planReviewOf`, `setReviewLabel`; `impactView` takes limits), `draft.ts`, `store.ts` (`setReviewLabelOf`), `src/server/api-refinement.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts` (a rule for the route). Tests: `tests/refinement-build-limits.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-impact.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-impact-api.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Size and plan review" and a row in the API table. The flow format did not change.
- Refinement 5b — check readiness by code, "accepted anyway", and the ready state (server) (#291).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/ready-check` checks a draft against the Definition of Ready of its repository (no AI call) and answers 200 with the session. The draft shows `readiness` (`{ at, items: [{ id, text, result, reason, by }] }`, result `met`, `not-met` or `unsure`, `by` `code`) and `state` (`drafting` or `ready`). Code checks by rule: `no-open-questions`, `out-of-scope`, `checkable`, `value`, `standalone`, `no-plan`; `small` and items without a rule are unsure. The check changes no field of the draft.
  - **Accepted anyway.** `POST …/ready/:item/accept` with a reason (1 to 300 characters) marks an item; `DELETE` removes the mark. `no-plan` cannot be accepted (409); an item not in the list is 404. Reasons are stored with the draft and published as `### Accepted anyway` in `preview.body` (left out when empty). A mark counts only for the wording it was given for, and a mark on an item that is now `met` shows as "not needed" and is left out of the preview.
  - **State.** A draft is ready when it has a check and every item of the current list is `met` or accepted anyway; a new or reworded item counts as not checked. The session is `ready` when it has a draft and all drafts are ready, else `drafting`. A change of a draft's text, a move to notes, an accepted suggestion, a removed draft it depended on, a change of the open questions or an Epic change that alters the preview removes the check results; the reasons stay. The stored state is corrected on a read. Publishing (#81) must call `isReady` again.
  - **Log.** New kinds `ready-checked` (with counts), `ready-accepted`, `ready-unaccepted`; a check and an accept need room in the log.
  - **Old installs.** `readiness` and `acceptedAnyway` are optional in `DraftSchema`, so an old `refinements.json` loads unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/draft-ready.ts` (`checkReady`, `acceptAnyway`, `removeAccepted`, `isReady`, `readinessView`, `sessionState`); `src/refinement/draft.ts` (schema, log kinds, `preview()` takes the list), `store.ts` (`readyList` option, `correctReadyState`), `src/server/api-refinement.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts` (three rules). Tests: `tests/refinement-ready.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-ready-api.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`; one old test in `tests/refinement-draft.test.ts` changed (a new draft has no check, so a `ready` session goes back to `drafting`).
  - **Docs.** User guide: permission rows and the "Ready check", "Accepted anyway" and "Ready" paragraphs.
  - **Not included.** No page yet; no architect judgement of `unsure` items. A test that a mark survives every kind of edit covers text edits only.
- Fair use 1 — admin settings for per-user limits: defaults and overrides (#295). Part 1 of 4 of #19.
  - **Behaviour.** Admins set default limits for all accounts and override them per account: `maxConcurrent` (runs at the same time), `maxRunsPerDay` and `dailyBudgetUsd`. A field can be empty ("no limit"). The Users page has a Limits column (own values marked "(own)"), a "Limits" button per account and a "Default limits" button. Limits are stored and shown only; nothing is enforced yet. Limits are per account only, not per repository. Admins are accounts like any other. If the limits call fails, the page still lists users and the column says "not available".
  - **Store.** New `src/auth/limits.ts`: `limits.json` (`{ version: 1, defaults, users }`, strict zod schema) in the data folder; a missing file means no limits. `getLimits`, `effectiveLimits` (never throws; an unreadable file means no limits and is logged once), `setDefaultLimits`, `setUserLimits`, `removeLimitsLocked`. Updates are patches: a number sets a field, `null` clears it, an omitted field stays; an account entry is dropped when its last override is cleared. `deleteUser` removes the override and stops with the new error code `bad-limits` (400) if `limits.json` is broken. `UserSchema` is unchanged, so older versions still read `users.json`.
  - **API.** `GET /api/users/limits`, `PUT /api/users/limits` and `PUT /api/users/:id/limits`, admin only (a user gets 403). The fixed `limits` path is matched before the `:id` routes. Rules in `src/server/permissions.ts`.
  - **Audit.** New action `limits-change` (`src/auth/audit.ts`, `ui/audit.js`): who, the account or "defaults", the field names; no amounts. A failed audit write is logged, not fatal.
  - **Code.** `src/auth/limits.ts`, `src/auth/users.ts`, `src/server/api-users.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts`, `ui/api.js`, `ui/users.js`, `ui/audit.js`. Tests: `tests/limits.test.ts`, `tests/limits-api.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/ui-users.test.ts`, `tests/ui-audit.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: Users page ("Limits") and the API table.
- Repositories 4e — existing watchers move from config.yaml to their repositories (#273).
  - **Behaviour.** At server start, every watcher in `config.yaml` except the monitor moves into the repository store with the same id, options and enabled state. A repository not yet connected gets a connection for the first admin with the method `none` (shown as "legacy" on the admin pages); an already connected repository keeps its owner and method. If that method cannot run a watcher (deploy key, or `none` owned by a non-admin), the watcher moves disabled and the log and Watchers page say why. The old `owner` option is dropped (the log says when it named another account). `config.yaml` is first copied to `config.yaml.before-watcher-move-<stamp>`, then rewritten without the moved watchers (through a checked temp file and a rename). Nothing moves without an admin account; at `REPO_LIMIT` the rest stay in the file and are logged. Safe to repeat: the store is written first, so a crash leaves a copy in both places and the next start drops the file's copy. Only an exactly equal stored copy counts as moved, and an id used twice in the file stays. A store error is logged and startup continues. `scf watch` is unchanged.
  - **API.** `PUT /api/config` refuses a new or changed watcher other than the monitor, with a sentence pointing to the Watchers page; an unchanged leftover still saves. A stored watcher can carry `repoName` to keep a mixed-case spelling (`Acme/App`), so status comments and run history keep matching.
  - **Code.** New `src/repos/migrate-watchers.ts`, run in `startServer` before the watchers are read; `src/repos/watchers.ts` (`fileWatcherProblem` replaces `watcherOwnerProblem`; `repoName`), `src/server/api-admin.ts`, `src/auth/run-owner.ts`; `methodLabel` in `ui/repos.js` adds "legacy"; new wording of the file-watcher note in `ui/admin.js`. Tests: `tests/watcher-move.test.ts`, `tests/watcher-move-server.test.ts`, and extended watcher, server, run-owner and UI tests (file watchers there now come from writing the file and `reloadConfig()`).
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Watchers move from config.yaml to their repositories", with the backup and how to go back.
  - **Not included.** One lock around the whole move; two starts at the same moment could interleave, but a repeat gives the same result.
- Refinement 6b — ask for the architect's view of a draft and keep it with the draft (server) (#286).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/impact` starts (or resumes) an architect run of kind `impact` and answers 202 with the session; the rules of a review apply (owner only, a brief must exist, the draft must not be empty, one architect run per account). When the run ends, the checked view is stored with the draft as `impact` and replaces an older one; no field and no session state changes. The session answer shows each draft's `impact` with every statement's `basis` (`found` or `estimate`) and `outOfDate: true` when the title, who, what, why, a criterion (its id or text), out of scope or depends-on changed since the view was asked. `D1`-style draft numbers are stored as draft ids; a statement about a draft that was not in the task is left out. Only draft references are supported, not criterion references (the answer form of #285 has none). A wrong form fails the run with "The architect's answer did not have the agreed form" and the old view stays. The view is hidden while the repository is not in My repositories. The log has `impact-asked` and `architect-impact`; an orphan `impact` run is adopted like a review.
  - **Cost.** An impact run keeps the flow's $3 limit, not the $1 of suggestions and reviews (`SUGGEST_MAX_COST_USD`), because it reads the code and up to 50 issues.
  - **Old installs.** `impact` is optional in `DraftSchema`, so stored sessions still load.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/draft-impact.ts` (schema, `setImpact`, `impactView`) and `src/refinement/impact-text.ts` (`impactText`, `impactOf`); `src/refinement/store.ts`, `architect.ts`, `draft.ts`, `talk-text.ts`, `src/server/api-refinement.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts` (a rule for the route). Tests: `tests/refinement-impact.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-impact-api.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "The architect's view of a draft" and a row in the API table. The flow format did not change.
- Closing an issue keeps its waiting run; reopening brings it back (#282).
  - **Behaviour change.** The watcher no longer sets a waiting or stopped run of a closed issue to `cancelled`; `endWaitsOfClosedIssues` and its per-issue `gh issue view` calls are gone. A closed issue with a waiting run gets its status labels tidied and no `closed_elsewhere` record; that record is now only for working (active or queued) runs. Runs that older versions already cancelled stay cancelled.
  - **Reopen.** When an issue without a status label was `closed` in the stored state and its newest run is failed, stopped or waiting, the watcher sets the label that fits the run (failed, waiting or needs-info) and writes `open`; no new run starts and the run is listed in Your turn again. If the label call fails the state stays `closed` and the next check tries again. A run older than the newest 1,000 is found through the full run index.
  - **Code.** `src/queue/watcher.ts` (`tidyClosed`, reopen check, `olderRun`), `src/words.ts` (`closed_elsewhere` text). Tests: `tests/watch.test.ts` (close and reopen for waiting for answers, waiting for approval and failed runs; failing label call; more than 1,000 newer runs; run cancelled by an older version).
  - **Docs.** User guide: the problems list and the status table.
- Runs of a retired flow get no Retry (#283). Part 5 of #268.
  - **Behaviour.** A run is retired when a watcher started it (or it has no source) and its flow name is in none of the three flow folders, looked up with the run's own `repo` folder. A hand-started run is never retired; a flow name with unsafe characters counts as retired. `POST /api/runs/:id/resume`, `/approve` and `/reject` answer 409 `The flow "<name>" is retired — this run cannot be resumed. Start the work again with a current flow.` When the issue is established closed, the closed message wins. Both run pages show no Approve, Reject, Resume or Retry and say "This run's flow is retired — it cannot be resumed." Your turn offers no `retry` or `retry_hint` for such an item; a stale page gets the existing "that is not possible for this item" 409. The record of a retired failed run says to start over; stopped, cancelled and interrupted runs say to start a new run. The open run page updates when a flow file appears or disappears.
  - **CLI.** `scf resume|approve|reject` refuse with the retired message plus " Use --force to resume it anyway."
  - **Code.** `src/run-gate.ts` (`flowRetired`, reason `flow_retired`), `src/next-step.ts` (`retired`), `src/server/next.ts` ("flow file exists" cached per repo folder and flow name for one request), `src/your-turn.ts` (`actsFor`), `src/queue/watcher.ts`, `src/server/api-runs.ts`, `ui/runs.js`, `ui/user/runs.js`. No new route, no change in `permissions.ts`. Tests: `tests/run-gate.test.ts`, `tests/next-step.test.ts`, `tests/server.test.ts`, `tests/server-turn-actions.test.ts`, `tests/server-your-turn.test.ts`, `tests/ui-user-runs.test.ts`, `tests/cli.test.ts`; new helper `tests/helpers/flow-repo.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: Your turn section.
- Refinement 6a — the architect writes its view of a draft: `ask=impact` in the round flow (read-only) (#285).
  - **Behaviour.** `scf run refine-round --var ask=impact` gives a checked answer with `areas`, `dependsOn`, `dependents`, `risks`, `size`, `overlaps` and `sensitive`; each statement has a `basis` of `found` or `estimate`. Same charter and same read-only tools as the other asks. `size.files` is all files changed, `size.lines` is production code only (as the build plan's `SIZE:` line); the check sets the size word from the numbers. Nothing is written to GitHub.
  - **Flow.** New step `list_issues` (only `ask=impact`): the newest 50 open issues, titles, texts and labels, no comments, into `issues.md`; for other asks it prints one line and reads nothing. It is in the `refine-round` entry of `src/engine/guards.ts`, so it gets a token. `tools/issue-digest` has optional `--max N` (default 200) and `--no-comments`; `refine-brief` is unchanged.
  - **Check.** `checkImpact` in `tools/refine-round-check` fails the run for hours, days or weeks in a text, a passed sentence limit, a wrong form, or questions, proposals, suggestions or remarks; the sentence never holds text of the answer. An area marked `found` without a file becomes `estimate`; every overlap is `estimate`, and an overlap must name an issue that was read. `ROUND_CHECK_MAX_OUTPUT` is now 150,000.
  - **Code.** `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (regenerated `flows/refine-round.yaml`), `src/engine/guards.ts`, `src/engine/repo-access.ts`, `tools/issue-digest`, `tools/refine-round-check`. Tests: `tests/refine-round.test.ts` (small, large, overlap, sensitive, hours, `found` without a file, 50 issues only for `impact`), `tests/refine-brief.test.ts`, `tests/refine-access.test.ts`; fake `claude` extended.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "The architect's question round". The flow format did not change.
- Your turn and the other lists no longer show runs of closed issues (#280).
  - **Behaviour.** A failed, stopped, waiting or cancelled run of an issue known closed on GitHub gets the record `issue_closed` (who "Foundry", action "Nothing — the issue is closed", link to the issue). It is not in Your turn or its count, not a card on the Board, not in the failed list of "since", and the server notifier sends nothing. `notify.command` does not run for it (also not for succeeded runs); it still runs when the state is unknown or open. A run that closed its own issue is exempt, and `closed_elsewhere` stays for queued and running runs. When the last check failed and nothing is stored, the item stays listed with the note "The state of the issue on GitHub could not be checked". When the issue is open again, the run is listed again. Both run pages show no Approve, Reject, Resume or Retry for it.
  - **Code.** `src/next-step.ts` (kind, `issueClosed`, `issueUnchecked`), `src/issue-states.ts` (`runIssueState`), `src/server/next.ts`, `src/server/your-turn.ts`, `src/board.ts`, `src/since.ts`, `src/notify.ts`, `src/words.ts` (glossary), `ui/next.js`, `ui/turn.js`, `ui/runs.js`, `ui/user/runs.js`. No new route. Tests extended in the matching test files.
  - **Docs.** User guide: Your turn section, notifications and the status table.
- A run of a closed issue cannot be retried (#281). Part 3 of #268.
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/runs/:id/resume`, `/approve` and `/reject` answer 409 "The issue is closed — nothing to retry. Reopen the issue if the work is still wanted." when a live check says the issue is closed; after a reopen they answer 202. The Your turn actions `retry` and `retry_hint` are refused with the same 409 before any label or comment call. The live check uses the run owner's stored token or GitHub App, otherwise the server's `gh`; for a deploy key or refused access it counts as "GitHub cannot be reached". When GitHub cannot be reached, a stored `closed` refuses and anything else lets the resume through. The live result is stored only for a repository with an enabled issues watcher. A run without an issue makes no GitHub call.
  - **CLI.** `scf resume|approve|reject` refuse with the message plus " Use --force to resume it anyway."; `--force` runs them. A check that could not be made prints one line and continues.
  - **Code.** New `src/run-gate.ts` (`CLOSED_MESSAGE`, `runIssue`, `resumeGate`, `gateRun`); used in `src/server/api-runs.ts`, `src/server/turn-actions.ts` and `src/cli.ts`. No new route, no change in `permissions.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: API table (note "Closed issues") and CLI reference.
- Workspace 5d-2 — "View as user" in the admin display: the read-only preview (#278).
  - **Behaviour.** The Users page has a **View as user** button on every account with the role user (not on admins). It starts the view and opens `/user/?as=<id>`, which shows that user's My runs, run page, My repositories, Refinement and Start work. A bar at the top says "You are viewing as <name>. Nothing can be changed here." with **Back to the admin display**, which ends the view and goes to `/#/users`. Every call carries `as=<id>`; no changing call leaves the browser, and a button that would change something shows "This is a preview. Nothing can be changed here." Buttons that only change things are not shown. When the view has ended (403), the page says so and offers **View again** and **Back to the admin display**; it never shows the admin's own data. `/user/` without `as` still sends an admin to `/`; `as` opened by a user is ignored.
  - **Safe.** The guard in `ui/api.js` throws before `fetch` for any non-GET call in a view; the server (5d-1) stays the real rule. No new route and no change in `permissions.ts`.
  - **Code.** New `ui/view-as.js` (bar, "view ended" card, `beginView`); `ui/api.js` (`setViewAs`, `startViewAs`, `stopViewAs`, `as=` on GETs and on `events`); `ui/auth.js` (`otherDisplay` and `enterDisplay` take `viewAs`); a `readOnly` option in `renderStart`, `renderMyRuns`, `renderMyRun`, `renderRepos` and `renderRefinement`; `ui/users.js`, `ui/user/app.js`, `ui/user/index.html` and `ui/style.css` extended. Tests: new `tests/ui-view-as.test.ts`; `tests/ui-auth.test.ts`, `tests/ui-users.test.ts` and `tests/ui-user.test.ts` extended.
  - **Docs.** User guide: new "View as user" part in chapter 7.
- Refinement 5a — Definition of Ready per repository (#290).
  - **Behaviour.** Every repository has a Definition of Ready: an ordered list of items, by default seven. An admin changes it in a **Definition of Ready** dialog on the Repositories page (reword, add, remove, move, add a default item back, **Back to the default**). The My repositories page shows it read-only. It is only stored and shown for now.
  - **API.** `PUT /api/admin/repos/:id/ready` (admin), `GET /api/repos/:id/ready` (owner or admin); `GET /api/admin/repos` rows have `ready`. The change is audited as `repo-change`.
  - **Safe.** Texts are one line, at most 200 characters, set as text and never put in the audit line. A new optional `definitionOfReady` field in `repos.json`; existing files load unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/ready-list.ts`, `setRepoReady` and `findOwnedRepo` in `src/auth/repos.ts`.
- Refinement 4f — remarks on the draft page: checks, review, move to notes (#129).
  - **Behaviour.** Under each field and criterion of an open draft the page shows the remarks of the code checks (updated from the save answer, without redrawing the field in use) and of the architect's review, with the kind in words ("Cannot be checked", "Vague", "Contradicts", "Describes how to build", "An implementation plan"), "written before your last change" for a stale remark, and when the review was made. **Review draft** asks for a review; a line shows while the architect works, **Ask again** or **Try again** after a pause or failure. A plan or how-to-build remark says it belongs in the build step and has **Move to notes for the builder**; after a confirmation the text moves to the notes as a "Wish:" line. Unsaved text is saved first (Suggest does this too now). No text changes unless the person types or presses a button. The log tells a review asked for, made and a text moved; the Context brief part does not show a review run's line.
  - **Safe.** No new route and no change in `permissions.ts`. Every text is set as text, never as HTML. Remarks and buttons show only where the draft fields show.
  - **Code.** New `ui/refinement-remarks.js`; two new calls in `ui/api.js` (`reviewDraft`, `moveToNotes`, on the routes of #128); `ui/refinement-draft.js`, `ui/refinement.js`, `ui/refinement-talk.js` (the kind `review`) and `ui/style.css` extended. Tests: `tests/ui-refinement-remarks.test.ts` and extended refinement UI tests.
  - **Docs.** Section 12 of the user guide, new "Remarks on the page" part.
- Workspace 5b — owner on the board, Your turn and the queue; Dashboard cost per user (#275).
  - **Behaviour.** For admins, Board cards, Your turn items and the rows of the Runs queue card show the owner's name (`deleted user` when the account is gone; nothing without a run or owner). The Board has an **All owners** filter that hides other owners' cards in the page without a new call. The Dashboard has a **By user** card (Name, Runs, Cost; last 30 days, highest cost first; "no owner" and "deleted user" lines).
  - **API.** `GET /api/stats` has `byUser: { owner, name, runs, costUsd }[]`; the costs add up to `totals.costUsd`. `BoardCard` and `TurnItem` have optional `owner` and `ownerName`; pending jobs in an admin's `GET /api/queue` have `ownerName`.
  - **Users.** `userRun`, `ownQueue` and the user display show no owner name and no cost.
  - **Code.** `computeStats` (`src/stats.ts`) adds `byUser`; names are added in the `stats` route (`src/server/api-admin.ts`); `src/board.ts`, `src/server/board.ts`, `src/your-turn.ts`, `src/server/your-turn.ts`, `src/server/next.ts`; `ownerLabel` of `ui/runs.js` is reused in `ui/board.js`, `ui/turn.js` and `ui/dashboard.js`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "The board", "The runs list", "Your turn" and "Dashboard".
  - **Not included.** An owner filter on Your turn; a cost-per-user chart over time.
- Workspace 5d-1 — "View as user" on the server: read-only and in the audit log (#277).
  - **Behaviour.** New `POST /api/admin/view-as` (`{userId}`, admins only) starts a 30-minute view for the caller's session, writes one audit line (`view-as`) first and answers `{id, name}`; 404 for an unknown account, 400 for an admin account or the caller's own. `DELETE /api/admin/view-as` ends it. While it runs, a `GET` with `as=<userId>` is answered exactly as for that user (same rules, same cut-down views) for flows, queue, runs, run events and diff, repos, credentials and refinement. `as=` without a view, from a user, or on anything but a `GET` is 403. Calls without `as=` and `GET /api/session` are unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `src/server/view-as.ts` (in-memory `ViewStore` keyed by a hash of the session, clock argument for tests); `api()` in `server.ts` swaps in the target user; credential and repo routes use the route's `user`; `view-as` added to the audit actions, `ui/audit.js` and the CSV export; two rules in `permissions.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "View as user (server)" in chapter 7 and two permission-table rows; a row in the safety table of `docs/DESIGN.md`.
- Closed issues — remember the state of each issue, with one batched GitHub call per watcher check (#279). Part 1 of #268. Nothing a user sees changes yet; `endWaitsOfClosedIssues` is as before.
  - **Behaviour.** Each check of an issues watcher stores `open` or `closed` for every distinct issue of its repository that has a run with status failed, stopped, waiting or cancelled (any flow, any age, no cap). Issues in the open list of the check are stored as open without asking GitHub; the others are asked in one `gh api graphql` call, and none is made when there is no candidate. More than 500 candidates are sent in sequential chunks. Each check replaces the repository's entry, so old issues are pruned. A failed or rejected call changes no state, sets `failedAt` and shows as the watcher error `checking closed issues: …` (it takes precedence over the tidy error). A deleted issue or pull-request number is not stored as closed. A check that timed out can no longer commit.
  - **Store.** New `src/issue-states.ts`: one file per repository in `issue-states/` in the data folder, written to a temporary file and renamed, so two repositories or two processes never overwrite each other. A missing or broken file reads as empty; reads are cached by mtime and size. `knownIssueState(repo, issue)` returns `open`, `closed`, `unknown` (the last check failed and nothing is stored) or `undefined` (no enabled issues watcher for the repository). The entry is deleted when the last enabled issues watcher of a repository is disabled or removed.
  - **Code.** `issueStates()` and `issueState()` in `src/github.ts`; `checkIssueStates` in `src/queue/watcher.ts`; the sweep in `src/queue/watchers.ts`. Tests: `tests/issue-states.test.ts`, extended `tests/watch.test.ts`; the fake `gh` answers `api graphql`.
- An old run replaced by a newer run is now always left out of Your turn (#284). Before, only the newest 200 runs were compared, so with more runs in between the old run stayed listed.
  - **Rule.** A run is replaced when a newer run exists for the same repository and issue, pull request or CI run, however many runs lie between them and also when the newer run is of another flow.
  - **Fast.** `nextFor` builds one index of the newest run per work item from the run briefs, once per page, so a page does not read every `run.json`. `RunBrief` has two new fields, `pr` and `ciRun`. The statistics (`supersededRuns` in `src/stats.ts`) are unchanged.
  - **Known gap.** The `superseded` flag on the Runs list still uses the loaded runs only, so it can differ from Your turn for very old runs.

- Repositories 4d — the Watchers page works per repository (#272).
  - **Behaviour.** The page groups watchers by repository and shows each owner (with a "blocked" mark). **Add watcher** picks a connected repository from a list; repositories that cannot have a watcher are listed as not available, with the reason. Add, edit, enable/disable and delete use the repository watcher API (#269), not `PUT /api/config`; API errors show as the server wrote them. Edit sends only the form's fields, so other options are kept. The dialogs block a double submit. The `owner` field is gone from the form. The monitor stays on the page and in `config.yaml`. Watchers still in `config.yaml` show read-only with "moves to its repository at the next update" and **Check now**; a watcher whose repository is gone can only be deleted.
  - **Users.** A user sees no Watchers page, watcher name or watcher status; runs a watcher started on their repository are in "My runs". Tests check that a user's run, queue and run list never name the watcher.
  - **Code.** `GET /api/admin/repos` rows have an optional `watcherProblem`. New `ui/watcher-form.js`; `addRepoWatcher`, `saveRepoWatcher`, `removeRepoWatcher` in `ui/api.js`; `ui/admin.js` changed (`editWatcher`, `watcherConfig` and the owner field removed). Tests: new `tests/ui-watchers.test.ts`; extended `repos-admin-api`, `user-view`, `repo-watchers-run`, `ui-monitor`, `ui-next` and `server` tests.
  - **Docs.** User guide section 6, "The Watchers page (admins)".
  - **Not included.** New screenshots (`images/watchers.png` and `images/watcher-form.png` are out of date); a test that a watcher-started run shows in "My runs" from start to end.
- Repositories 4b — a stored watcher's GitHub calls use its repository's credential (#270).
  - **Behaviour.** Every GitHub call of a stored watcher (issues, labels, comments, the status comment, the `/approve` permission check, CI runs, pull requests) uses the credential of its repository: the stored token (`github-token`), a GitHub App installation token limited to that repository (`github-app`), or the host login for the method `none` with an admin owner. With a token or the app, `gh` gets an empty settings folder and no inherited `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` or `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`. An app token is renewed before it ends, not on every call. The admin actions on a watcher's item under "Your turn" use the same credential; without the watcher they are refused with a sentence and never fall back to the host login. Watchers in `config.yaml` keep the host login.
  - **When it fails.** If the credential is missing, unreadable or refused (for example the owner is no admin and the method is `none`), the check makes no GitHub call and the watcher's status shows a plain sentence. It works again at the next check once the credential is fixed.
  - **Status comments.** A repository with its own token or app has its own status-comment board. A comment written earlier by another account (for example the host login) is not "ours" any more: the watcher posts a new one and leaves the old one as it is (answer (a) to the question on the issue).
  - **Safe.** No token appears in logs, status, API answers or error text; errors of a call made with a token have it replaced by `[redacted]`. The app has no user, so it only treats comments that `gh` says it wrote itself as its own.
  - **Code.** `withGhEnv` / `currentGhSession` (an `AsyncLocalStorage`) in `src/github.ts`, read by `gh()`; new `src/queue/gh-identity.ts`; `Watcher`, `StatusComments`, `WatcherManager` (`identityOf`) and `src/server/turn-actions.ts` run a whole check or action as one identity. `noteRateLimit()` is unchanged. Tests: `tests/gh-identity.test.ts`, `tests/github-env.test.ts`, `tests/repo-watchers-gh.test.ts` (two repositories, two tokens), and extended `redact`, `status-comment` and `server-turn-actions` tests; the fake `gh` records the token it was called with.
  - **Docs.** User guide, "Watchers of a repository".
- Workspace 5c — admins start runs for themselves like a user (#276).
  - **Behaviour.** The admin display has a **Start work** page (`#/start`, link before Runs). It is the user's page: published flows, My repositories, task and inputs. After Start the admin lands on the admin run page. The run is owned by the admin, shows in Runs with the admin as owner and writes the `run-start` audit line. `/user/#/start` opened by an admin now leads to `/#/start` (the hash was dropped). The ▶ Run button of the flow editor is unchanged.
  - **Server.** `GET /api/flows?published=1` gives an admin the same list a user gets (only the value `1` counts; without it the admin's answer is unchanged, and a user's answer never changes). `POST /api/runs` with `"likeUser": true` from an admin follows the user rules: a published, saved flow only, `yaml` and `repo` refused, only published inputs, `github_repo` must be one of the admin's own repositories, variables frozen, same answers and status codes. `likeUser` must be a boolean, else 400 (checked right after the body is read). No new route; the notes in `permissions.ts` and the permission table in the user guide say what is new.
  - **Code.** `src/server/api-flows.ts`, `src/server/api-runs.ts` (`asUser`); `renderStart` in `ui/user/start.js` has the option `admin` (asks for `flows(true)`, sends `likeUser: true`, keeps the method "none" in the repository dialog, `NO_FLOWS_ADMIN`); `flows(published)` in `ui/api.js`; the `start` route in `ui/app.js`; the link in `ui/index.html`; `otherDisplay` in `ui/auth.js` no longer has the `#/start` exception.
  - **Docs.** User guide chapter 2, "Start work as an admin".
- Workspace 5a — Credentials page for admins, metadata only (#274).
  - **Behaviour.** New admin page **Credentials** (`#/credentials`, nav link after Repositories): a table with Owner, Name, Type, Fingerprint, Created and Last used ("never" when null), sorted by owner name and then name. "deleted account" shows when the owner is gone. No add, change or remove button.
  - **Safe.** New call `GET /api/admin/credentials` (rule in `permissions.ts`, `user: "no"`; a user gets 403). The answer holds only `id`, `type`, `name`, `created`, `lastUsed`, `fingerprint`, `owner`, `ownerName`; never a secret, key id, `iv`, `tag` or `data`. An unreadable store answers 500 with the sentence `GET /api/credentials` uses and logs only the file name and kind. Every text is set as text, never as HTML.
  - **Code.** `listAllCredentials()` in `src/credentials/store.ts`; the route in `src/server/api-credentials.ts`; new `ui/admin-credentials.js`, `api.allCredentials`, the `credentials` branch in `ui/app.js`.
  - **Docs.** Section 7 of the user guide has a "The Credentials page (admin)" part.
- Repositories 4c — blocking a user pauses their watchers; deleting a repository removes them (#271).
  - **Pause.** A stored watcher whose owner is blocked does not run: it checks nothing and starts nothing. `GET /api/watchers` shows the state `paused` and "paused: the owner is blocked". A blocked owner shows before other repository problems, but after an id clash with `config.yaml`, a missing repository or a missing owner. Unblocking starts it again without a restart. Other watchers are not restarted. Runs that are already going are not touched.
  - **CLI.** The account sweep (`src/server/account-work.ts`) calls `sync()` when an account's status, role or e-mail changes, or when the watcher file changes, so `scf user block` is seen by the server.
  - **Removal.** Removing a repository (by id or by name) or deleting an account removes its watchers in the same step, under the same lock; they are written back if the later steps fail. With `by` given, one `repo-change` audit line per repository tells "watchers removed: …", before the removal line. A transfer keeps the watchers. At start the server removes watchers whose repository is gone and logs each one; if that fails it logs the file and the reason and goes on.
  - **API.** The repository `DELETE` routes and the account `DELETE` call `ctx.watchers.sync()`, even when the delete fails half way.
  - **Code.** `src/repos/watchers.ts`, `src/auth/repos.ts`, `src/auth/users.ts`, `src/words.ts` (new watcher state `paused`), `src/server/api-repos.ts`, `src/server/api-users.ts`, `src/server/account-work.ts`, `src/server/server.ts`. Tests: extended `account-work`, `repo-watchers*`, `repos-admin`, `user-delete` and `words` tests.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the `paused` state and "Watchers of a repository".
- Refinement 4d — suggestions on the draft page: accept, edit and accept, reject (#127).
  - **Behaviour.** Every field of an open draft has **Suggest**, with the hints "ask the architect to look at the code first" (no brief) and "accept a rule or an example first" (criteria without a map entry). While the architect works a line says so and no Suggest button shows; a paused run shows **Ask again**, a failed one **Try again**. A suggestion shows next to its field, marked "Suggested", with **Accept**, **Edit and accept** (a dialog; not for Depends on) and **Reject** (a dialog with an optional reason). Criteria show their rule or example and are decided one by one. Accept on a text field that shows text asks before it replaces it. Unsaved text in other fields is kept. Fields, criteria and depends-on items show "typed", "accepted" or "accepted, then edited". The log tells suggestions asked for, made, accepted and rejected in words; the Context brief part does not show a suggestion run's line. A suggestion run for a draft that is not open shows its line at the top of Story drafts.
  - **Safe.** No new route and no change in `permissions.ts`. Every text is set as text, never as HTML. Suggest saves typed text first and sends nothing if that save fails. Dropped sessions, admins and missing repositories see waiting suggestions and the marks without buttons.
  - **Code.** New `ui/refinement-suggest.js`; three new calls in `ui/api.js` (they use `stay`); the kind `suggest` in `kindOf` (`ui/refinement-talk.js`); its words in `ui/refinement.js`; `ui/refinement-draft.js` and `ui/style.css` extended; `send` skips its page reload on a 401 when text is unsaved.
  - **Docs.** Section 12 of the user guide, "Suggestions from the architect", has a new "On the page" part.
- Workspace 4d-3 — answer the questions on the run page (#132).
  - **Behaviour.** On the user display, a run with `questions` and `canAnswer` shows **Your answer**, a text box and **Send answer** (`POST /api/runs/:id/answer`; Ctrl/⌘+Enter sends too). The button is off while the call is out; a refusal shows the server's sentence and keeps the text; updates keep the text and focus; the form comes back by itself when the run stops with questions again. **Answers given** lists the answers, oldest first, as plain text.
  - **Next step.** `nextStep("planner_questions", …)` has a new option `answerHere` (set by `nextFor()` for a user's record that no watcher follows and whose flow reads the task). The sentence then reads "… answer the questions on the run page and it continues" and links to the run page. Watched runs, runs that cannot read an answer and admin records keep "… on the issue". `commentText("planner_questions")` is unchanged.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Answer a run's questions" and "Your own runs".
  - **Not included.** A box on the admin page; posting the answer on GitHub.
- Refinement 4e — the architect points out problems in a draft (server) (#128). No page yet.
  - **Code checks, no AI call.** Every draft in the session view has `remarks`, computed from its text on each read and save (new module `src/refinement/draft-check.ts`). They find vague words (a fixed list, for example "fast", "easy", "etc") and text that reads like an implementation plan (a path with a file extension, a code block, a call like `save()`, build steps like "first add a table, then …"), in every field but the notes for the builder. Text in backticks and web addresses are never flagged, so a story may name an API route on purpose. A remark has the field (and the item), its kind (`vague` or `plan`), the word found and a plain sentence; a plan remark says it belongs in the build step. Remarks are advice only and block nothing.
  - **Review.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/review` starts an architect run (`refine-round`, `ask=review`) and answers 202 with the session. The rules of the other architect runs apply (owner only, one run per session and per account, a paused run is resumed, a failed run changes nothing). The result is stored with the draft as `review` and no field changes; a new review replaces the old one. At most 20 remarks, each with a field (and item), a kind (`uncheckable`, `vague`, `contradiction`, `how`, `plan`) and one or two sentences (at most 300 characters). A remark about a text that changed or moved after the review is marked `stale`. The architect only points out; it proposes no new text.
  - **Move to notes.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/move-to-notes` with `{ "field": "…", "item": "…" }` adds the text to the notes for the builder as a line "Wish: <text>" and removes it from the field. Only a text with a `plan` or `how` remark (a `plan` remark from the code checks, or either from the review) can be moved; otherwise it is refused. Nothing moves by itself. Typed text added to typed notes stays `typed`; any mix with accepted text is `accepted-edited`.
  - **Check tool and log.** `tools/refine-round-check` checks the form and limits of a review and fails with one plain sentence that holds no text of the answer. The log tells that a review was asked for, that the architect reviewed (with the number of remarks) and that a text was moved to the notes. No save starts an AI call.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/draft-review.ts` (store the review, `reviewView`, `moveToNotes`), `ask=review` in `scripts/build-flows.mjs` and `flows/refine-round.yaml`, `review` in `ARCHITECT_KINDS` and `endArchitectRun` (`src/refinement/store.ts`), `src/refinement/architect.ts`, `src/refinement/talk-text.ts`, two routes in `src/server/api-refinement.ts` with rules in `src/server/permissions.ts`. Tests: `tests/refinement-draft-check.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-review.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-review-api.test.ts`, and extended `refine-round`, `talk-text` and `permissions` tests; the fake `claude` knows `ask=review`.
- Repositories 4a — watchers in the repository store, with an admin API (#269).
  - **Behaviour.** A watcher can be stored for one repository (by its id) in the new `repo-watchers.json` (`src/repos/watchers.ts`). It has the options of a `config.yaml` watcher except `github_repo` and `owner`, which come from the repository; source `monitor` is not allowed. Admin routes `GET/POST /api/admin/repos/:id/watchers` and `PUT/DELETE /api/admin/repos/:id/watchers/:wid` list (with status and holds), add, change (also enable and disable) and delete. A user gets 403; changes are audited and start, stop or restart the watcher at once. Only GitHub repositories with the sign-in `none`, `github-token` or `github-app` are accepted. A watcher id is unique over the store and `config.yaml`; a duplicate is refused with a sentence, and `PUT /api/config` now refuses new duplicates and new collisions with stored ids (duplicates already in the file still load and save).
  - **Runs and lists.** Runs of a stored watcher belong to the repository's owner (no fallback to the first admin) and show in that user's "My runs"; a stored watcher with no owner queues nothing. `GET /api/watchers` also lists stored watchers with their `repoId`; one that cannot run shows a `problem` sentence and an error state. Status, holds, locks and questions of two repositories do not mix. `config.yaml` watchers and the monitor work as before.
  - **Safe.** A changed or removed watcher starts nothing more, even in the middle of a check (`Watcher.stop(true)`). If `repo-watchers.json` cannot be read, no stored watcher runs, the log names the file once, and the server retries on `watcherRetryMs`. Removing or transferring a repository, deleting a user, and changing a user or their sign-in call `sync()`; removing a repository or deleting a user also removes their stored watchers, with an audit line.
  - **Code.** `WatcherSchema` in `src/config.ts` is split so the store reuses the options. `ctx.config()` returns `config.yaml` plus the stored watchers that can run; new `ctx.fileConfig()` (the file only, used by `GET`/`PUT /api/config`) and `ctx.blockedWatchers()`. New permission rules and examples; the fake `gh` gets opt-in `FAKE_GH_ISSUES_BY_REPO` and `FAKE_GH_COMMENTS_BY_ISSUE`. Tests: `tests/repo-watchers.test.ts`, `tests/repo-watchers-api.test.ts`, `tests/repo-watchers-run.test.ts`.
  - **Not included.** Checks, labels and status comments still use the server's own `gh` sign-in, so a private repository that only its own token can read shows a watcher error. In the Watchers page, Edit, Disable and Delete work only on `config.yaml` watchers. No tests for lock-held cases of the store, or for a deleted user's watchers being cleared.
- Gitflow: when `develop` got a commit the run has not fetched yet, the secret scan of the push failed on the unknown commit and the run failed. That case is now read as "develop moved": merge again and test again.
- Gitflow, models matched to the work: one Codex review per code change (a second round only when `review_twice_above_risk` is set; default `off`); the plan is made at high effort and only plans with a risk above 75 are rewritten after the review (`revise_above_risk`, was 50 — Codex scores risk higher than the planner, so three of four plans were rewritten); the docs step and merge-conflict step each start a fresh, small session instead of continuing the whole coding conversation.
- Self-repair 9 — proof with real incidents, and the admin chapter (#93).
  - **Changed.** The detector "Label and run disagree" (`label-mismatch`) is now *major*, not minor. Its bug story comes after 2 checks in a row instead of after 3 different days. This also holds for existing installs that set `report_to`: a mismatch that lasts 2 checks now gets a story. It is one word in `src/monitor/work-detectors.ts`.
  - **Tests.** `tests/self-repair-incidents.test.ts` replays four real incidents (a restart loop, GitHub's request limit used up, a step that fails for every story, a label and a run that disagree) with the real monitor, reporter, watcher and flows and a fake `gh`, `claude` and git remote: the story is made no later than the second check, goes first, takes the hotfix path to `main` and `develop`, and ends as *fixed* after 24 hours of normal work. `tests/self-repair-rules.test.ts` proves the five rules (nothing private in a story, one open story per problem, the circuit breaker stops a flood, a feature run cannot reach `main` and fails when resumed at `push_main`, with the monitor watcher removed or disabled nothing is created). New helper `tests/helpers/self-repair.ts` with one movable clock; the fake `gh` gets the opt-in switch `FAKE_GH_STORIES=1` so `issue list`, `view` and `close` know the stories made over REST. `tests/guide.test.ts` now compares `FLOW_AUTHORING.md` with the flow schemas section by section, and checks chapter 13 of the user guide (all eight sections in order, detector names, story headings, links).
  - **Docs.** New chapter "13. Self-repair (for admins)" in the user guide; two sentences about "waiting — a bug story goes first" in "Why is nothing happening?"; the severity in the detector table; the fix-commit lines `COMMIT:` and `MAIN:` in `FLOW_AUTHORING.md`; `DESIGN.md` has a new §17 Self-repair and `LESSONS_LEARNED.md` no longer lists self-repair as open.
  - **Not included.** A story template for each work detector (stories for `same-step-failing` and `label-mismatch` use the general text); a replay of "came back → second story → needs you" (`tests/monitor-fix.test.ts` and `tests/monitor-tries.test.ts` cover it).
- Workspace 4d-1 — the planning flows print their questions and read the task (#130).
  - **Behaviour.** `send_back` (`issue-plan`, `issue-gitflow`) and `ask_for_info` (block `plan`) post the same comment as before. Their output is now the planner's text (without the `PLAN_STATUS:` and `ROUTE:` lines) instead of the comment link, so a user's run page shows the questions under **Questions**. The page shows the first 4,000 characters; the whole text is in the comment on the issue. When the comment cannot be posted the step fails, as before.
  - **Task.** The prompts of `plan`, `plan_review` and `revise_plan` read the task under one fixed line; it may be empty and comes on top of the issue. `usesTask` is true for both flows, so Start work shows the Task box for a published copy. A watcher run has an empty task and plans as before.
  - **Upgrade.** A run started before the update, and a published copy of an older flow (or a flow built from the old `plan` block), keep the link: save the flow again from the built-in one.
  - **Not included.** Answering on the run page; the Jira and Linear flows (they already print their questions).
- Fix: an issue kept `Factory_needs_info` after its questions were answered while it waited for another story, the release pull request, the budget, the per-check limit or a busy one-at-a-time watcher (#225). The label now follows the same record as the status comment.
  - **Answered, not started (no run yet):** the needs-info label is removed at the next check (one edit, nothing added). Issues already in this state are corrected at the first check after the update. The label is only edited when it is wrong; a failed edit is the check's error ("correcting labels") while the holds and status comments are still written. The label comes back only when there are new, unanswered questions.
  - **A run that stopped for questions, was answered and cannot continue yet** (per-check limit, or another story running under `one_at_a_time`): the label says `working` and the run resumes when it can, instead of keeping needs-info. The same holds for a run that waits for `/approve` and was approved while another story runs. It never flips back to needs-info or waiting.
  - **Release pull request:** the issue has no status label while the release pull request holds it; the app shows the thing to do (merge it) in Your turn.
  - **Not covered:** a second stray needs-info or waiting label next to the right one, and a waiting label on an issue without any run, are left as they are.
  - Code: `Watcher.dropAnsweredLabels` and the answered-run arms in `tickIssues` (`src/queue/watcher.ts`). Tests: `tests/watch-labels.test.ts`, `tests/watch-labels-limit.test.ts`.
- Refinement 4b — the draft page: write, save as you type, preview (#125).
  - **Behaviour.** The **Story drafts** part of the session page lists the drafts by title ("Untitled draft" when empty) and has **New draft**; **Open** / **Close** shows one draft on the session page (no new address), **Remove draft** asks first. An open draft has fields for the Title, "As …", "I want …", "so that …", Acceptance criteria (type in the last empty field to add one; change and Remove one by one; an emptied one keeps its text), Out of scope, Depends on (an issue number, or another draft of the session; Remove) and Notes for the builder. The Epic is set and cleared in the same part. Typing is saved about a second after you stop and when you leave the field; the page says "Saving…", "Saved" or the server's sentence. A failed save keeps the text and the next change tries again; a field that failed keeps its sentence on the page even when another field saves. Text whose draft or criterion was removed elsewhere, or typed just before the session could no longer be changed, shows in a **Not saved** card until you discard it; the browser asks before you leave the page with text that is not saved. **Preview** shows the story as the server wrote it (title, Epic line, sentence, criteria with check boxes that cannot be ticked, Out of scope, Notes for the builder, Depends on); **Show as Markdown** shows the exact text. Fields and buttons show only on your own, open session whose repository is in My repositories; a dropped session and another account's session show the drafts as text, and a hidden list says that it is not shown. The log tells an added draft, a removed draft and the Epic.
  - **Safe.** No new route and no change in `permissions.ts`. Every text is set as text, never as HTML; typed lines that look like headings stay inside their own part of the preview. A save or a poll never redraws the field in use and never throws unsaved text away. Changes go to the server one after the other, and a poll answer that arrives while a change is on its way is not drawn. A save after the session ended (401) keeps the page and the text.
  - **Code.** New `ui/refinement-draft.js` (`draftSection`, `mayChange`, `draftTitle`, `issueNumber`, `previewParts`, `previewNodes`, `beforeLeave`, `unsaved`, `opened`); four calls in `ui/api.js` (`addDraft`, `saveDraft`, `removeDraft`, `setEpic`) and a `stay` parameter on `req` (no reload on a 401; only `saveDraft` uses it); in `ui/refinement.js` the page is drawn in three parts (above the drafts, the drafts, the log), with `save` and a turn queue shared with `send`, and a reload now hands its cleanup on; `.drafts` rules in `ui/style.css`; `parentNode`, `remove` and `insertBefore` in the fake DOM.
  - **Not included.** Suggestions from the architect, the Definition of Ready, publishing, a Markdown renderer for typed text, an address per draft, keeping unsaved text over a new sign-in.
- Workspace 4d-2 — the server takes an answer to a run's questions and continues the run (#131).
  - **Behaviour.** New call `POST /api/runs/:id/answer` with `{ "text": "…" }` for the owner of the run or an admin. It saves the answer with the run, queues a resume at the step the run would resume at and answers 202 with `{ "runId" }`. After that every step reads the task followed by all answers, oldest first, under the heading `## Answers to the questions of this run (oldest first)` (`{{task}}`, `$FACTORY_TASK`, `$SCF_TASK`). The `task` of the run stays as typed. The text is saved as sent. The answer stays when the resume is cancelled or dropped, for a blocked account and over a restart.
  - **Limits.** 4000 characters per answer; 100000 bytes for the task with all answers.
  - **Refused.** 404 (unknown run, or another account's run, same body); 400 for a missing, non-string, empty or blank `text`, a NUL character, more than 4000 characters, more than 100000 bytes, or a run that is queued or working; 409 when the run did not stop with questions, when a watcher follows it (a run started by hand can be answered), when its flow has no step that reads the task, and for an architect run; 500 when the stored secrets cannot be read. No refusal writes the answer, a job or an audit line.
  - **Safe.** Stored secrets in the text become `[redacted]`. The text is never in `queue.json`, the audit log or a server log line. The queue file is now written to a temporary file and renamed, so a failed write leaves the old file whole. If the queue file cannot be written, the call fails and the answer is taken out of `run.json` again; if that write fails too, the answer stays saved without a job and the next resume reads it. Once the job is in the queue file, the answer is kept and the call is accepted, also when a later write of that file fails.
  - **Format.** Optional `answers` (`at`, `text`, `by`) in `run.json`; `answers` (no `by`) and `canAnswer` in the user run view and its `update` events; new audit action `run-answer`, also in the Audit page filter. A queued answer follows the bug-label priority like other run-page jobs.
  - **Code.** `RunAnswer`, `taskWithAnswers()`, `answerRoom()` (`state.ts`); `saveAnswer()`, `AnswerRefused` (`runner.ts`); `Scheduler.answer()`, `JobMeta`; `answerBlock()`, `questionsOf()` exported (`user-view.ts`). `Scheduler.submit()` is split into a private `put()` (check, queue, write the file) and `pump()`.
  - **Not included.** The answer box on the run page (4d-3), posting answers on GitHub, an admin page, and a lock between the server and `scf resume` in a terminal: the two do not see each other, so do not resume a run in a terminal while it is answered — the answer can be lost and the run can run twice.
  - **Not included.** The box on the run page (4d-3), posting the answer on GitHub, a box on the admin page.

- Fix a test that failed on a busy machine (one status comment for two watchers): it waits for the comment itself instead of a fixed ten seconds. It failed the tests before the change for two stories. A failing test is now also tried again twice before it counts (`retry` in `vitest.config.ts`), because several stories run the suite at the same time.
- Refinement 4c — suggestions for a field of the draft (#126).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/suggest` with `{ "field": "…" }` (`title`, `who`, `what`, `why`, `criteria`, `outOfScope`, `dependsOn` or `notes`) starts an architect run (kind `suggest`) and answers 202 with the session. The rules of a round apply: owner only (404 another user, 403 an admin), a brief is needed, one architect run per session and per account, a paused run is resumed by the same call (same draft and field; anything else is 409), and a failed or cancelled run changes nothing. `criteria` with no rule and no example in the map answers 409. The suggestion is stored beside the draft (`suggestions`), never in a field and not in `preview`. `POST …/suggestions/:sid/accept` with `{}` puts it in as `accepted`; with `{ "text": "…" }` the given text goes in as `accepted-edited` (not for depends on: 400). `POST …/suggestions/:sid/reject` with an optional `{ "reason": "…" }` removes it and keeps its text and reason with the draft; the next suggestion run of the session gets them. Nothing is kept per repository or per user.
  - **Criteria and depends on.** Each criterion names a rule or example of the map (`tie`); one whose rule or example is not in the map is left out. A criterion keeps its tie through every edit; removing the map entry drops the tie and the waiting suggestions from it, quietly. Depends-on suggestions are an issue number or another draft of the session; anything else is left out.
  - **Limits.** One suggestion for a text field, up to 10 for criteria and depends on; a new run replaces the waiting ones of its field; at most 20 wait per draft; a reason has at most 300 characters; the newest 30 rejected suggestions are kept per draft. A suggestion run costs at most $1.
  - **Flow.** `refine-round` takes `ask=suggest` with `--var field=…`. The architect works from the task (idea, brief, map with numbers R1/E1, draft, other drafts D1, rejected suggestions) and opens a file only to check a claim; it never writes an implementation plan. `tools/refine-round-check` checks the form and limits and fails with one plain sentence without any text of the answer. The task is at most 90,000 bytes: the brief is cut first, then map lines and other drafts, the draft last, with a "Left out" notice.
  - **Format.** Optional `suggestions` and `rejected` in a draft, `tie` in a criterion, `draft` and `field` in `architect`, and the log values `suggestion-asked`, `architect-suggested`, `suggestion-accepted` and `suggestion-rejected`. An older build cannot read a file that holds them.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/draft.ts` (schema and pure functions), `talk-text.ts` (`suggestText`, `suggestOf`), `store.ts`, `architect.ts`, `api-refinement.ts`, `permissions.ts`, `tools/refine-round-check`, `scripts/build-flows.mjs` and the generated `flows/refine-round.yaml`.
  - **Not included.** The page (a running suggestion shows as a brief read until then) and the review of #128.

- Gitflow: when a branch rule on GitHub refuses the push to `develop`, the run stops at once and says so. Before, it read the refusal as a develop that had moved, merged and tested three more times, and then failed without the real reason.
- Refinement 4a — the story draft in the session (server) (#124).
  - **Behaviour.** A session holds story drafts (at most 20) and one optional Epic. Four calls, for the owner only (404 another user, 403 an admin, 409 a dropped session or a repository that is not in My repositories): `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts` adds an empty draft (201; nothing is filled in from the idea, the brief or the map), `PUT …/drafts/:did` saves what was typed, `DELETE …/drafts/:did` removes a draft and takes it out of the depends-on lists of the others, and `PUT …/epic` with `{ "issue": 73 }` sets the Epic, `{ "issue": null }` clears it. A draft has `title`, `who`, `what`, `why`, `criteria` (a list), `outOfScope`, `dependsOn` (a list) and `notes`. Only the fields in a save change; a list is sent whole (a known `id` with the same text stays, a known `id` with a new text is changed, no `id` is new, also when its text is already in the list, so send the `id` of every item you keep; a missing item is removed; an unknown or repeated `id` is 400). An emptied field (`""`, spaces, `null`) is removed.
  - **Record.** Every text field and list item has `from`: `typed`, `accepted` or `accepted-edited`. The server sets it and ignores a value from the client. New text is `typed`; changing an `accepted` text makes it `accepted-edited`; the same text keeps its record; an emptied field has none. Nothing in this part writes `accepted` (#80–#83 do).
  - **Depends on.** Issue numbers (whole numbers from 1) and other drafts of the same session; not itself, not twice. The Epic is a whole number from 1. Neither is checked against GitHub here (publishing, #81, does that).
  - **Preview.** Every draft in the session view has `preview` (`{ title, body }`): `**Epic:** #n` when there is one, "As …, I want …, so that ….", `### Acceptance criteria` with `- [ ]` lines, `### Out of scope` and `### Notes for the builder` only with text, and `### Depends on` (issue numbers, drafts as "Title (draft)", or "None (can be built on its own)."). An empty part is "…"; a full stop is added when the why has none; line breaks in the sentence and in criteria become spaces. Show it as text, never as HTML.
  - **State.** The first draft moves an *exploring* session to *drafting*; removing the last one moves *drafting* back to *exploring*. Epic changes and saves do not change the state.
  - **Hidden.** While the repository is not in My repositories the view has `draftsHidden: true` instead of `drafts`, and the log lines of drafts and the Epic have no `detail`. `epic` stays.
  - **Limits.** Title 120 characters on one line; who, what and why 500; 50 criteria of 500; out of scope and notes 5,000; 20 depends-on items; 20 drafts. No control characters (a title also refuses tab, and U+2028 and U+2029 as line breaks). A limit answers 400 with a plain sentence; nothing is removed to make room.
  - **Log.** Adding and removing a draft and setting or clearing the Epic write a line (`draft-added`, `draft-removed` with the title, `epic-set` with `#n`, `epic-cleared`); a save writes none. These need room in the log of 1,000; a save does not.
  - **Format.** `refinements.json` stays version 1 and gets `drafts[]` (a full check on load: unique ids, targets that exist, no self-reference) and the optional `epic`; the four log values. An old file loads unchanged; an older build cannot read a file that holds drafts or an Epic.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/draft.ts` (`DraftsSchema`, `EpicSchema`, `newDraft`, `saveTyped`, `dropDraft`, `changeEpic`, `preview`); `addDraft`, `saveDraft`, `removeDraft`, `setEpic` and `mustBeOpen` in `store.ts`; error codes `bad-draft` and `bad-epic`; `HAS_CONTROL` exported from `talk.ts`; four rules in `permissions.ts`; the session page lists drafts by title and shows four new log sentences (`ui/refinement.js`).
  - **Not included.** The page for writing drafts, suggestions from the architect, the Definition of Ready, publishing, checking the Epic and issues on GitHub, a cycle check between drafts, reading dependencies back from the preview text.
- Repositories 3e — user runs never use the machine's login; commits carry the right name (#118).
  - **Behaviour.** In every run owned by a user, and in an admin's run on a repository with a stored sign-in (token, deploy key or GitHub App), no step acts with the machine's login. Every step, agent steps and shell steps without `repo_access` too, runs without `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN` and the bot's token (`bot.gh_token_env`, the bot installation), with an empty `gh` folder of its own, without the machine's git settings and credential helpers, without the SSH agent and ssh (unflagged steps allow https and local paths only), and without prompts. A step marked `repo_access` still gets only the repository's credential. An admin's run on a repository with the method "none", an unlisted one or a local folder is unchanged. The bot's token is not asked for in the isolated runs, so a wrong `bot.gh_token_env` no longer stops them.
  - **Commits.** Isolated runs commit with the bot name and e-mail from Settings, each field on its own, else the name and e-mail of the run's owner account. The Mac's own git identity is never used (`user.useConfigOnly`). When the owner's account is gone and the bot name and e-mail are not both set, the step fails before it runs with "the account that owns this run could not be found, so its commits have no name"; set them in Settings and resume the run.
  - **Agents.** In these runs `agent_env` cannot set `GH_*`, `GITHUB_*`, `GIT_*`, `SSH_*`, `XDG_*` or `LC_ALL`, Claude Code agents always skip the personal setup (`--strict-mcp-config`, also with `isolate_agents: false`), and a GitHub token variable is never used as the key of an anthropic-compatible provider. The failure explanation call removes every token variable, the bot's included.
  - **After the upgrade.** Unflagged steps and agents in these runs can no longer call `gh` or the remote. The machine's git settings (signing, aliases, proxy) are not used: set `HTTPS_PROXY` or `GIT_SSL_CAINFO` in the server's environment. Runs of a deleted account stop at their next step unless the bot name and e-mail are set.
  - **Limits.** This is done through the environment of the steps. It is not an operating-system sandbox: a step runs as the server's macOS account and can still read that account's files and Keychain (`~/.ssh`, `~/.netrc`, a `gh` login kept in the Keychain), start `ssh` itself or set the variables again. A Codex agent still loads the account's Codex settings (`~/.codex/config.toml`); keep GitHub tokens and GitHub MCP servers out of that file on a server with users. A step marked **Run in Docker** gets none of the machine, only the commit name; as before, the push hook does not run inside the container, and such a step has no credential. The watchers' own GitHub calls are not runs and keep the machine's login. A separate account or container per run is not included.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/isolation.ts` (`stepIsolated`, `commitIdentity`, `tokenVarNames`, `isolationEnv`, `stepIsolation`); `hasStoredSignIn` in `src/auth/repos.ts`; `NO_COMMIT_IDENTITY` in `guards.ts`; `engine.botEnv` (bot identity asked once per run, only for steps that keep the machine's login) in `runner.ts` and `execute.ts`; `agentEnv(spec, isolated)` and one shared environment for both agents in `src/agents/run.ts`; `claudeProviderEnv(target, neverSend)`; `failure-explain.ts` removes the token names; one entry each in `failure.ts` and `errors.ts`. No new route, no format change, no new dependency.
- Refinement 3d — the session page: questions, answers and the map (#122).
  - **Behaviour.** The session page has a **Questions** part and a **Map** part after the Context brief. Ask for a round, answer each question (an option, your own text or "I don't know yet"), then ask for another round. Ask the architect a question of your own. The map lists Rules, Examples and Open questions: Accept or Reject proposals, Edit or Remove entries. A line counts the open questions ("a story with open questions is not ready"). While the architect is queued or running, the page says what it does by kind (reading the code, writing its questions, answering your question) and polls every 5 seconds; paused and failed runs show the reason and "Ask again" or "Try again". The log shows questions, answers and entries in words. A dropped session, another account's session and a gone repository show the texts without buttons; a hidden talk says it is not shown. A queued brief now says "Then it reads the code."; a paused or failed round or question no longer shows Refresh or Try again in the Context brief.
  - **Safe.** No new route and no change in `permissions.ts`; the server still decides. Texts are shown as text, never as HTML. No run id, cost, model or folder is shown. One change at a time; a poll that began before a change never draws over it.
  - **Code.** New `ui/refinement-talk.js` (`talkSection`, `kindOf`, `mayChange`, `answerText`, `openLine`, `doneText`, `roundLabel`, `pendingQuestion`, `againLabel`, `canAsk`, `talkLogText`); seven calls in `ui/api.js` (`askRound`, `askOwnQuestion`, `answerQuestion`, `acceptProposal`, `rejectProposal`, `changeMapEntry`, `removeMapEntry`); `activityText(doing, kind)`; `.talk` rules in `ui/style.css`.
  - **Not included.** Story drafts, the Definition of Ready, changing the state of the session, changing an answer, adding an entry by hand.
- Self-repair 7 — the Problems page (#91).
  - **Behaviour.** New admin page **Problems** (`#/problems`, after Watchers). One sentence at the top: running or not, bug stories on/off/quiet/stopped, last check, stories made today and the daily limit, circuit breaker with its reason. Findings are listed most severe and newest first (100, then "Show N more") with a state: seen, waiting, building, fixed-watching, came-back, needs-you, muted, gone. **Details** shows the evidence, the bug stories and the runs. Actions: switch the monitor on/off, mute, end mute, try again, **This is not a problem** (mute for good with a reason), **Make a story now**. Each detector is listed with its description, thresholds (editable, saved through the config), last found and mute. **Health** links to the page when findings are stored. A findings file that cannot be read is shown as a warning, not as "No findings".
  - **Make a story now.** Skips the severity and duration rules, the daily and per-check limits, the quiet time and an open circuit breaker. Never skips the off switch, an unreadable guard file, mutes, "only once" (a story found by its marker is taken up and counts as a try) or the two tries; cleaning applies. It makes only that one story: the check it runs does no other reporting. Status codes: 200, 400 (bad or unknown finding, unreadable file), 409 (off, not running, exists, muted, two tries, gone, no `report_to`), 502 (GitHub).
  - **Safe.** Admin only (users get 403 everywhere, including `GET /api/health`). No answer holds a fingerprint. The wish is kept in memory only. The story is saved onto the newest findings file (other changes are kept, an old story of another repository goes to `earlier`), and never over a file that cannot be read.
  - **Formats.** `GET /api/monitor` adds `running`, `lastCheck`, `perDay`, `madeToday` (from the monitor's log, can be above `perDay`), `breaker` (`open`, `why`), `findingsUnreadable`, `state` per finding, and `key`, `thresholds`, `lastFound` per detector. New `GET /api/monitor/findings/:id` (`evidence`, `stories`, `runs`) and `POST /api/monitor/story` (`{ "finding": "<hash>" }`). `GET /api/health` adds `monitorFindings` (`open`, `total`, `unreadable`). The log line `story-made` has `by` for a story made on request.
  - **Code.** New `src/monitor/state.ts` (`findingState`, `buildingStories`, `byUrgency`, `madeToday`, `withStory`); `storiesByMarker`, `adoptIssue` and `Reporter.storyNow` in `src/monitor/report.ts`; `Monitor.storyNow`; `WatcherManager.monitorRunning`, `monitorLastCheck`, `storyNow`; new `ui/problems.js`; `mutesTable` and `muteForm(…, { notProblem })` in `ui/monitor.js`.
  - **Not included.** Removing the lists from the Watchers card; auto-refresh; a story for a gone or muted finding; a marker lookup beyond the newest 100 `bug` issues; a lock that serializes the checks of a replaced monitor; a daily limit that counts replaced stories; a last-check time that survives a restart.
- Refinement 3c — ask for a round, and ask the architect a question, from a session (#121).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/round` starts `refine-round` (`ask=round`) and `POST /api/refinement/:id/ask` with `{ "question": "…" }` starts it with `ask=question`; both answer 202 with the session, for the owner only (404 another user, 403 an admin, 409 a dropped session or a repository that is not in My repositories). A round needs a brief and an answer to every question of the last round (409). The run gets the talk as its task. A succeeded run stores the round (at most 5 questions, proposals, `done`) or the answer; a failed or cancelled run changes nothing in the talk. One architect run per session and per account, counted with the brief read; a paused run is resumed by the same call. The `architect` view has `kind`. A user sees one line of the task in the Runs list, on the run page and in the queue.
  - **Limits.** The talk text is at most 90,000 bytes (oldest rounds, then the brief, then the end are left out, with a notice); an own question has 1–2,000 characters, at most 50 are kept; a round needs 9 log lines (start and end), a question 2, and other changes may not use the lines a running run still needs.
  - **Format.** Optional `architect.kind` and `architect.question` in `refinements.json`; log values `round-started`, `architect-round` and `round-done`; `kind` in the `architect` view. An older build cannot read a file with the new fields.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/talk-text.ts` (`talkText`, `questionOf`, `cutBytes`); `askArchitect` takes a kind; `askOfJob`, `kindOfRun` and the zod check of `check_round` in `architect.ts`; `ownQuestion` in `talk.ts`; `architectLogRoom` and the new ends of `endArchitectRun` in `store.ts`; `userTask` in `user-view.ts`; rules for both routes in `permissions.ts`.
  - **Not included.** The page (part 3d).
- Self-repair 6d-2 — two tries, then a person (#109).
  - **Behaviour.** After 2 bug stories for one finding that did not fix it, the monitor makes no third: the finding *needs you* (`needsYou`), the log gets one `story-skipped` line (reason `two_tries`), and Your turn shows one item per finding (kind `monitor_needs_you`, cannot be dismissed) with the sentence, the evidence and links to the two newest stories. A story from before the upgrade counts as one. The "fixed" verdict starts a new count and ends "needs you". Nothing is marked for a story GitHub could not be asked about in that check (the call budget ran out); it is decided at a later check. Nothing becomes "needs you" while stories are off, quiet, stopped by the breaker, muted, or the story is closed as not planned; GitHub is asked before the mark is set. The item is hidden while the finding is gone, its story is open or fixed, or a mute applies, and is back when that ends. The findings list shows "needs you" and a **Try again** button.
  - **Try again.** `POST /api/monitor/retry` with `{ "finding": "<hash>" }`, admin only. It waits for a check in flight (also of a monitor that was just replaced), then starts the count anew and logs `try-again` with `by`. 400 for an unknown finding or a broken findings file, 409 for a finding that does not wait for a person. The fingerprint is in no answer.
  - **Safe.** A finding that needs a person is never pruned. The evidence goes only to the admin-only `GET /api/your-turn`, never into a notification, a story or the log. A story that was made but whose save was lost counts as a try when it is adopted.
  - **Formats.** Optional `tries` and `needsYou` on findings in `monitor-findings.json` (an old file loads); log event `try-again` and skip reason `two_tries`; `needsYou` in `GET /api/monitor`; `evidence` and `stories` on the Your turn record.
  - **Code.** `MAX_TRIES`, `triesOf`, `waitsForPerson`, `tryAgain` in `src/monitor/fix.ts`; `evidenceLines` in `src/monitor/findings.ts`; `Monitor.busy()` and `WatcherManager.monitorIdle()` (retired monitors are kept only while their check runs); `NextKind` `monitor_needs_you`.
  - **Not included.** A Try again button on the Your turn item itself, and a command-line "try again".
- Refinement 3b — the session keeps the talk (#120).
  - **Behaviour.** A session holds a talk: rounds of questions (at most 5 per round), your answer to each question, proposed entries that wait for you, and the map with three lists: rules, examples and open questions. Five calls change it, all for the owner only: `POST /api/refinement/:id/questions/:qid/answer` (`{ "option": n }` from 1, `{ "text": "…" }` of 1–2,000 characters, or `{ "unknown": true }`; a question is answered once, a second answer gets 409), `POST …/proposals/:pid/accept` (the entry goes to its list), `POST …/proposals/:pid/reject` (the proposal is removed), `PUT …/map/:eid` (`{ "text": "…" }`, 1–500 characters) and `DELETE …/map/:eid`. "I don't know yet" adds the question to the open questions at once. Nothing else enters the map without your accept. Every call that returns a session has `talk` (`rounds`, `proposals`, `map` with `rules`, `examples` and `open`, and `asked`). The log has every question, answer, and accepted, rejected, changed and removed entry, with its text cut at 2,000 characters. Another user gets 404, an admin 403 (an admin may read), a dropped session 409. While the repository is not in My repositories, the session has `talkHidden: true` instead of `talk`, the log lines of the talk have no text, and the five calls answer 409.
  - **Limits.** 100 entries per list, 50 waiting proposals, 50 own questions and the 1,000 log entries of a session. A full list or log answers 400 with a plain sentence; nothing is removed to make room. Characters are counted as in part 3a (an emoji is one). A round or an own question that does not fit in the log is refused whole. A round that brings more proposals than there is room for keeps the questions and the proposals that fit; the log line `proposals-left-out` says how many were left out.
  - **Format.** As for #101: `refinements.json` stays version 1 and gets the optional field `talk`, the new log values (`question`, `answered`, `open-added`, `entry-accepted`, `entry-rejected`, `entry-changed`, `entry-removed`, `asked`, `architect-answered`, `proposals-left-out`), `detail` up to 2,000 characters for them (the old values keep 120), and the optional `list` on a log line. Old files load unchanged. An older build cannot read a file that holds the new fields.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/talk.ts` (schemas, limits and pure functions) and `src/refinement/errors.ts` (`RefinementError`, re-exported by `store.ts`, with the new codes `bad-answer`, `bad-text` and `bad-round`). `store.ts` exports `answerQuestion`, `acceptProposal`, `rejectProposal`, `changeEntry`, `removeEntry`, and, without an actor, `recordRound` and `recordAsked` with `ROUND_LOG_LINES` and `ASKED_LOG_LINES` for #121.
