Five new rules in the permission table.
  - **Not included.** Starting a round, changing an answer, adding an entry by hand, the page.
- Repositories 3d — deploy key and GitHub App in runs (#117).
  - **Behaviour.** A shell step with `repo_access: true` now also signs in with a deploy key or the GitHub App. **Deploy key:** the key is a file `sign-in/key` (mode 0600) in a folder `sign-in` (mode 0700) of the run folder, outside the workspace. It is written when the step starts and deleted when it ends. git uses only that key (no ssh agent, no ssh files of the account, host keys in `known_hosts` of the data folder) and speaks ssh only; `FACTORY_REPO_URL` is the stored ssh address. There is no `GH_TOKEN`: a step that calls `gh` by name fails with "a deploy key gives git access only; choose a token or the GitHub App under My repositories". **GitHub App:** the step gets a new installation token limited to the repository, as `GH_TOKEN` and as git's credential; it is never cached or stored and is hidden in output and logs.
  - **Cleanup.** The `sign-in` folder is removed when the step ends and when the run ends (succeeded, failed, stopped, cancelled, and a cancelled waiting run). A leftover from a crash is removed when the run is resumed and when the server starts (a folder whose holder, recorded as pid and start time, still runs is kept). A folder that cannot be removed fails the step or the resume with a fixed sentence; `on_failure` is not followed.
  - **Failures.** Fixed sentences, with no text from GitHub and no key path: key missing or unreadable, key or app token refused ("reconnect the repository under My repositories"), app not set up, not installed on the repository or not working, GitHub not reachable, request limit used up, token could not be made, and "the app's token ran out during the step". The run ends there and `on_failure` is not followed. They are also known to the failure classes (`failure.ts`), the plain-words errors (`errors.ts`) and the architect's reasons.
  - **Safe.** The key and the app token reach only the marked step: not an agent step, not a step without the flag. A step that holds a credential runs in its own process group, which is killed when the step ends. Protected branches and the secret scan still apply.
  - **After the upgrade.** An admin's deploy-key or app repository no longer uses the server's own access in marked steps; a deploy-key repository cannot run steps that call `gh` (the built-in issue flows fail at their first `gh` step).
  - **Limits.** An app step must finish within one hour (the life of the token); resume gets a new token. The `gh` stand-in catches `gh` called by name, not by an absolute path. A background process that leaves its process group is not stopped. A refusal printed on stdout of a step that passes does not fail it.
  - **Code.** `repoAccess()` returns `key` and `app` access. `appTokenAccess`, `prepareKeyStep`, `repoKeyEnv`, `removeSignInDir`, `sweepSignInDirs`, `processStart` in `src/engine/repo-access.ts`; `keyRefused` and the sentences in `src/engine/guards.ts`; `sshKeyEnv` in `src/repos/connect.ts`; `installationTokenInfo` and `limited` in `src/github-app.ts`; `ownGroup` in `runProcess`/`runShell`. Tests: `tests/repo-key.test.ts`, `tests/repo-app-run.test.ts`, `tests/fixtures/fake-ssh.sh`.
- Workspace 4c — My runs and the run page for users (#112).
  - **Behaviour.** **My runs** lists your runs as cards, newest first, with the ones that need you on top. A card shows the status with its "?", the flow, the first line of the task, the repository and issue, the sentence of what happens next and when it started. The flow name is a real link (Tab and Enter). A queued run shows "n runs ahead of you" and a **Remove** button. The list refreshes every 30 seconds. With no runs it says so and offers **Start work**. The run page shows the status, **Now** (the next-step sentence, and the current step with its description), the task, the repository, the branch and the flow version, and three parts: the live **Log**, **Steps** (no output, cannot be opened) and **Changes** (the diff, or a clear line when there is none). Buttons: **Approve** and **Reject** (a dialog with a note), **Retry** (continues at the step where it stopped) and **Cancel** (after a confirmation). A run that is queued only has **Cancel**; an architect run has no Approve, Reject or Retry (it is continued from its session). A refusal shows the server's sentence and the page stays usable; the same goes for a cancel that cancelled nothing. A failed run shows the plain explanation card, without raw details. Dialogs cannot be closed while their call is out.
  - **Safe.** No new route and no change in `permissions.ts`; the server still decides. The pages draw no cost, token count, model, agent name, folder, transcript, "Open flow" link or "Retry from step…" list.
  - **Formats.** The queued jobs of `GET /api/queue` (user view) have `flow`, the name of the flow of a queued run.
  - **Code.** New `ui/user/runs.js` (`renderMyRuns`, `renderMyRun`, `myRunsEntries`, `runActions`, `runCard`, `confirmDialog`, `decisionDialog`, `firstLine`, `workText`). `logLine` and `diffView(d, { none })` are exported from `ui/runs.js`. `modal()` in `ui/dom.js` takes an optional `busy` guard. `ui/user/app.js` routes to the new pages. `Scheduler.queue()` returns `flow`.
  - **Not included.** "Retry from step"; removing the unused `admin: false` branches of `ui/runs.js`; a change of the record's wording "Resume the run on its page". The 360 px layout and keyboard use are checked by tests of the CSS and the fake DOM only.
- Refinement 3a — the question-round flow (#119).
  - **New flow.** `refine-round`: `clone` (the same step as `refine-brief`) → `round` (the architect) → `check_round`. By hand: `scf run refine-round --task "<the talk so far>" --var github_repo=owner/name [--var ask=question]`. With `ask=round` (the default) the architect answers with `questions` (at most 5: `view` need, build or test; `text`; `why`; 2–4 `options` with `text` and `tradeoff`; `recommended`, counted from 1), `proposals` (at most 20: `list` rule, example or open; `text`) and `done` (one sentence, required when there are no questions). With `ask=question` the answer is `{ "answer": "…" }`, with no questions and no proposals.
  - **Read-only.** Same as `refine-brief`: tools `Read`, `Glob`, `Grep`, mode `dontAsk`, the architect charter, the code in `repo/`, model `claude-opus-5-5`, at most $3 and 30 minutes a run. No step writes to GitHub; the open issues are not read again. The talk is `{{task}}` in the agent prompt only, between markers that make it material to read.
  - **Form and limits.** `tools/refine-round-check` takes the JSON from the first `{` to the last `}`, keeps known fields only, keeps the first 5 questions and 20 proposals, and cuts texts: question `text` and `why` 500 characters, option `text` and `tradeoff` 300, proposal `text` 500, `done` 500, `answer` 8,000. A list that is present but not a list fails; a missing list is empty.
  - **Failures.** One plain sentence, never holding text of the answer, when the answer is not a JSON object, a question has no `why`, not 2–4 options, an option without a trade-off, a `recommended` outside its options or a `view` outside the three, when there are no questions and no `done`, when `ask=question` has no answer or holds questions or proposals, or when `ask` is not `round` or `question`.
  - **Token.** The grant of the stored token is per flow: `refine-brief` → `clone`, `list_issues`; `refine-round` → `clone`. The agent step and `check_round` never get it. The clone has `repo_access`, so a run by hand follows the same rules as the clone of `refine-brief`.
  - **Output limit.** A shell step still keeps 20,000 characters of output; `check_round` of `refine-round` keeps 100,000 (`stepMaxOutput()` in `src/engine/guards.ts`, `maxOutput` of `runShell`), room for a full checked round.
  - **Not for users.** `POST /api/runs` answers 404 for a user (also when an admin published a copy), `GET /api/flows` does not list it for a user, and it can't be deleted (403 built-in, 409 for a copy).
  - **Code.** `REFINE_CLONE` in `scripts/build-flows.mjs`; `REFINE_ROUND_FLOW`, `REFINEMENT_FLOWS`, `isRefinementFlow()` in `src/flow/usage.ts`; `REPO_READ_FLOW` is gone, `REPO_READ_STEPS` is a map per flow.
  - **Not included.** Starting the flow from a session; a second try when the answer has the wrong form.

- Workspace 4b — Start work (#111).
  - **Behaviour.** The user display has **Start work** (`#/start`), first in the top bar. With no hash (or `#`, `#/`) a user with no runs, nothing queued and nothing running lands on Start work; anyone else lands on My runs (also when the lookup fails). Step 1 lists the published flows with title and description as radio buttons (the first is chosen); with none the page says "No flows yet. Ask your administrator to publish one." Step 2 appears when the flow has the input `github_repo`: a list of your GitHub repositories by `owner/name` with their connection status, and **Add repository**, which opens the usual dialog; afterwards the list is loaded again and the repository you just added is chosen. With no repository the page says so. A fixed `github_repo` is shown as text. Step 3 has a **Task** box (when the flow uses the task; never required), every input with label, help, default and a "(required)" mark, and every fixed field as read-only text. Each flow keeps what was typed and its own repository choice while you switch flows. **Start** sends `flow`, `task` and the inputs; an empty required field shows a message and sends nothing; the server's sentence for a refusal is shown and the typed values stay; the button is off while the call runs; on success the run page opens. A repository is only sent when one is chosen from the list. Ctrl/⌘+Enter in the Task box sends. The form is one column and uses labels, so it works at 360 px and with the keyboard alone.
  - **Safe.** No new API route and no change in `permissions.ts`. The server still checks the published flow, the inputs and the ownership of the repository; the page only shows the answer. It shows nothing about costs, models, agents or settings.
  - **Formats.** `GET /api/flows` (user view) has `usesTask` (true when a step reads `{{task}}`, `FACTORY_TASK` or `SCF_TASK`, or when a step starts a sub-flow). `GET /api/repos`, and the answers of adding and changing a repository, have `github` (`owner/name`, lower case, no `.git`) for a GitHub record; `GET /api/admin/repos` does not. New fields only, so older clients keep working.
  - **Code.** New `ui/user/start.js` (`renderStart`, `homeHash`, `startProblem`, `startBody`, `githubRepos`, `pickRepo`, `addedRepo`; relative imports so a test can load it). `githubNameOf` in `src/auth/repo-url.ts` (also used by Refinement); `usesTask` in `src/flow/publish.ts`; `isNoHash` and `#/start` in `ui/auth.js`; `repoDialog` resolves with the record it added; `route()` in `ui/user/app.js` decides the empty address behind its generation guard.
  - **Not included.** A Start work page for admins. A required Task: a flow that needs a task can be started with an empty box, as the server allows today. A flow that reads the task only in a script under `tools/` shows no Task box.

- Repositories 3b — the block library sets `repo_access` (#115).
  - **Behaviour.** The built-in blocks that call `gh` or the remote are marked, so a flow made from them has **Needs repository access** ticked on those steps. Nothing changes in a run yet (#116).
  - **Marked.** `github-repo`, `pull-repo`, `push`, `open-pr`, `pr-comments` (both steps), `ci` (both steps), `merge`, `push-plan`, `push-result`, `request-approval`, `triage` (`split_ticket`) and `plan` (`ask_for_info`, because it comments on the issue), besides `pull-ticket`. **Not marked.** `run-tests`, `commit`, `secret-scan`, `learn`, and the Jira and Linear blocks.
  - **Clone.** `pull-repo` clones `$FACTORY_REPO_URL` with plain git when it is set (nothing sets it yet), else with `gh` as before. An existing `.git` still only fetches.
  - **Upgrade.** A flow made from blocks earlier holds its own copy of the steps, without the flag: tick the box on those steps, or insert the block again.
  - **Code.** `blocks/*.yaml`, the inline steps of the retired flows in `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (the Jira and Linear `ask_for_info` drops the flag, as it does not call `gh`), the regenerated `tests/fixtures/flows/`, and `scanRepoAccess`, which now also reads `blocks/` and `tests/fixtures/flows/`. `flows/` is unchanged.
  - **Not included.** The engine does not use the flag yet (#116).
- Repositories 3c — steps with `repo_access` use the repository's token (#116).
  - **Behaviour.** In a run with an owner, every shell step with `repo_access: true` (at any sub-flow depth) signs in with the stored token of the repository named by `github_repo` (`github-token` or `https-token`): `gh` gets it as `GH_TOKEN`, git gets it through a credential helper, and `FACTORY_REPO_URL` is the stored address. The token is looked up when the step starts, so a changed token is used by the next step, and its "last used" time is set.
  - **Which access.** Method `none`, a repository that is not in the owner's list, a deploy key or the GitHub App: an admin's run keeps the server's own access (for a deploy key or the app, with one line in the run log); a user's run fails at the step with "set a token for this repository under My repositories". A run without an owner that is not a refinement run works as before. A missing or unreadable token fails with its sentence. A refusal by GitHub (expired, revoked, no access) fails the run with "GitHub refused the token of this repository; reconnect the repository under My repositories" and counts as an environment failure; a rate limit is not a refusal. In a marked step a refusal on stderr fails the step even when the script goes on, and a failed sign-in ends the run: `on_failure` is not followed.
  - **Safe.** A token step ignores the bot's `GH_TOKEN`, the machine's `gh` sign-in and settings (an empty `gh` folder of its own, removed afterwards), git's global and system configuration, the workspace's credential helpers and extra headers, and `LC_ALL` is `C`. It speaks https only and never prompts. The token is in the step's environment only (also `SCF_GIT_PASSWORD`), never in a command line, an address, `.git/config` or a file, and it stays hidden in output and logs when it is changed or removed while the step runs. Steps without the flag and agent steps never get it. Protected branches and the secret scan still apply: the credential helper is added after the engine's `core.hooksPath` entry.
  - **Refinement.** The refinement flow works as before, also for runs saved before the upgrade (the grant by flow name stays for runs without the flag).
  - **After the upgrade.** Runs from watchers, the Run button and the command line use the stored token as soon as their owner has one. A token made for reading only fails at the first push or comment: give it Contents, Issues and Pull requests, read and write, then press Test connection. A user's run on a repository without a token now fails at its first marked step. GitHub shows the token's account, not the bot, for those steps. Token steps ignore the server's git and `gh` settings; set `HTTPS_PROXY` or `GIT_SSL_CAINFO` in the server's environment. A private flow that pushes in an unmarked step still uses the server's access.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/repo-access.ts` (`stepRepoAccess()`, `repoTokenEnv()`, `ghConfigDir()`); `repoAccess()` in `src/auth/repos.ts` returns the address and user name and the admin cases; `repoReadAccess()` and `repoReadEnv()` are gone from `src/engine/guards.ts`, which gains `TOKEN_REFUSED_RUN`; `runShell()` has a `scan` option that checks the whole output; `Engine.accessFailed` in the runner. Two rows in `src/errors.ts` and the Troubleshooting rows. The fakes have `fakeGit()` and `tests/fixtures/fake-git.sh`.
  - **Not included.** The user-specific "Your turn" and next-step wording for these failures (a user still reads "ask the administrator" there), and an override of an SSH origin written into the workspace's `.git/config`.
- Workspace 4a — the user display gets its own entry (#110).
  - **Behaviour.** Users work on their own page at `/user/` (`/user` works too): **My runs**, **My repositories**, **Refinement**, the account name, **Change password** and **Sign out**. It has no admin links, folder name, sidebar, health line or "since you last looked" line. A user who opens `/` is sent to `/user/` before any admin page is drawn; the hash is kept for `#/runs`, `#/runs/<id>`, `#/repos`, `#/refinement` and `#/refinement/<id>`, any other hash is dropped. An admin who opens `/user/` is sent to `/`. Someone without a session gets the sign-in form where they are, and stays there after signing in as a user. A set-password link still works. An unknown hash on `/user/` goes to `#/runs`. At 360 px the page does not scroll sideways: the top bar wraps, a long account name breaks, and a wide table scrolls in its own box. Every link and button can be reached with Tab and shows a focus mark. A dialog takes the focus when it opens, keeps Tab inside, closes with Escape and gives the focus back to the button that opened it (this holds for the admin pages too). A page that draws itself again keeps the focus on the same button.
  - **Safe.** No new API route; `permissions.ts` is unchanged and the server still decides what a call may do. The redirect is for comfort only: the admin scripts are still served as static files.
  - **Code.** New `ui/user/index.html` and `ui/user/app.js` (each hash change draws into its own box, so a slow page cannot overwrite a newer one). `ui/auth.js` has `isUserHash`, `userPage`, `otherDisplay` and `enterDisplay`; `allowedHash`, `startApp` and the `.role-user` CSS rules are removed. `ui/dom.js` has `tabStops` and `trapTarget`; `modal()` can be closed only once. `src/server/server.ts` maps `/user` and `/user/`. A test checks that `ui/user/app.js` imports no admin module.
  - **Changed.** Run rows and Refinement titles are real links. Tables sit in `.table-box`. The admin top bar shows its links only once the account is known (`<body class="signed-out">` until then).
  - **Not included.** New pages for users. The old screenshot `user-home.png` was taken out of the guide and the design document; a new one is not taken yet.
- Self-repair 6d-1 — did the fix work? (#108).
  - **Behaviour.** A story closed as completed is *waiting for the update*; sightings then are not "came back" and make no story. The 24-hour clock starts when the running Foundry contains the fix commit (the later of the server start and the close), else at the first server start after the close (not a git checkout, fix commit unknown, or `report_to` is not the Foundry's own repository), and anyway `monitor.fix_wait_days` after the close (logged as `waited`). Not seen for 24 hours of normal work (time without a check, such as a sleep or a failed check, does not count): the finding is *fixed* and the story gets one comment "Not seen since the fix.", saved before the post, found again by its marker after a lost answer, and held back while stories are off, quiet, stopped by the breaker or the finding is muted. No comment when the close was `fix_wait_days` or more before the clock. A sighting with new proof (first seen, a miss, or evidence time after the clock) cancels "fixed"; the "came back" story is made by the usual rule (2 checks in a row; minor: 3 different days after the clock) and links the first. This replaces "24 hours after the close". A reopened story, or one closed at another time, forgets its clock and verdict. Stories of findings that are not seen are asked about on GitHub at most every 6 hours (open and unsettled ones also one by one, within the 6 calls). Stories closed before the upgrade start as waiting.
  - **Settings.** `monitor.fix_wait_days` (1–365, default 7).
  - **Formats.** Optional story fields in `monitor-findings.json`: `fixCommit`, `clockAt`, `clockWhy` (`update`, `restart`, `waited`), `workedMs`, `seenAfter`, `fixedAt`, `fixNote` (`due`, `tried`), `notedAt`; `earlier` entries may have `url` and `closedAt`. Log events `clock-started` (with `reason` and `count`), `fixed`, `came-back`. `GET /api/monitor`: `story.fix` is `waiting`, `watched` or `fixed` for a closed story in `report_to`. The feature path (`push_develop` of `issue-gitflow`) prints `COMMIT: <full sha>` after the unchanged `PUSHED:` line. An older build rejects `fix_wait_days` once the config is saved.
  - **Code.** New `src/monitor/fix.ts` (`checkFixes`, `cameBack`, `fixCommitOf`, `fixRunsOf`, `fixState`, `forgetFix`, all pure); `selfContains()` and `selfDir()` in `src/engine/guards.ts` (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` without a shell; only a "yes" is cached); `fixedComment()`, `isFixedComment()` in `src/monitor/story.ts`; `MonitorDeps.self`; `storyKey` exported from `src/monitor/breaker.ts`.
  - **Not included.** The count of tries, "needs you", Your turn and "try again" (#109). A clock that started only by waiting never gets the comment. Fixed stories outside GitHub's newest 100 `bug` issues are not read one by one.
- Refinement 2d — the session page: ask the architect, watch it work, read the brief (#102).
  - **Behaviour.** The session page has a "Context brief" part. The owner of an open session sees one button: "Ask the architect to look at the code", "Refresh" (with a brief), "Try again" (after a failure) or "Ask again" (paused). It is not shown for a dropped session, another account's session or a repository that is not in My repositories. While the architect is queued or running the page shows what it is doing and updates every 5 s until the run ends; there is no button then. Paused and failed reads show the reason in plain words. The brief shows its five parts, the date and the branch; Refresh keeps the old brief until the new one is done. The log says when the architect was asked, wrote the brief or could not finish. In the Runs list and the Queue an architect run has the mark "refinement", which opens its session.
  - **Safe.** The brief is shown as text, never as HTML. No run id, cost, model or folder is shown; the step description is turned into plain words before it is shown. Nothing new on the server.
  - **Code.** `askArchitect` in `ui/api.js`; `architectStatus`, `activityText`, `askLabel`, `briefParts`, `briefMeta`, `POLL_MS` in `ui/refinement.js`; `refinementMark` in `ui/runs.js`; a pill rule in `ui/style.css`.
  - **Not included.** Story drafts; hiding the Resume button on the run page of an architect run.
- Repositories 3a — flow format: a shell step asks for repository access (#114).
  - **Behaviour.** A shell step can have `repo_access: true`, and the flow editor has a **Needs repository access** checkbox for it. The engine reads it since #116.
  - **Rules.** Shell steps only. Not with `sandbox: true`, and not listed in a `parallel` step. Both are errors with a plain sentence when a flow or block is loaded or saved.
  - **Built-in flows.** Every shell step that calls `gh`, uses the remote with git or calls `daily-branch`, `post-questions` or `create-split` is marked. No step that runs the tests or build is. `issue-gitflow` has a new step `fetch_main`, and `baseline_main` no longer fetches. The clone steps clone `$FACTORY_REPO_URL` with plain git when it is set (nothing sets it yet), else they use `gh` as before.
  - **Upgrade.** Flows, blocks and saved runs without the key load unchanged. Private flows that call `gh` or push will need the flag; they will fail for users (not admins) without it. An older build rejects a flow that has the key. A hotfix run of `issue-gitflow` started before the upgrade and resumed after it counts as a changed flow (`FACTORY_HOTFIX=other`).
  - **Code.** `ShellStepSchema` and `checkStepRefs` in `src/flow/schema.ts`, `ui/step-types.js`, `scripts/build-flows.mjs`, `blocks/pull-ticket.yaml`.
  - **Not included.** The engine does not use the flag (#116). The other library blocks and the retired flows are not marked.
- Self-repair 6c — mute a detector or a finding (#106).
  - **Behaviour.** An admin mutes one detector (by name) or one finding, with a reason (1–200 characters), for a time (up to 8760 hours) or for good. A muted finding is still recorded (count, last seen, seen-again count), but no story is owed or made and no comment is written; a story owed before the mute waits while the mute lasts. A mute for a time ends by itself (removed at the next check, or when the next mute is made; each logged as `mute-ended` with `reason: "expired"`). An admin can end any mute. Muted findings, and the failed runs of their stories, do not count for the circuit breaker. The monitor's card lists the findings (detector, sentence, severity, first and last seen, story link, muted or not) and the mutes (reason, since, until); `scf monitor status` prints `mutes: N` and one line per mute. A finding mute wins over a detector mute; one mute per detector and one per finding; at most 200.
  - **Safe.** `POST /api/monitor/mutes` and `DELETE /api/monitor/mutes/:id` are admin only (rules in the permission table). The reason is validated at the API (no control characters, checked before trimming) and again when the file is read. Who muted is the account id only. A finding is named by `markerHash(fingerprint)`; the raw fingerprint is in no answer and not in `status`. `hours: null` means for good.
  - **Formats.** `mutes: [{ id, kind: "detector" | "finding", detector, fingerprint?, reason, since, until?, by }]` in `monitor-guard.json` (kept over off and on; a malformed list makes the file unreadable, like `off`). Log events `mute-made` and `mute-ended`; skip reason `muted`; log fields `mute`, `text` and `until`. A finding's `skipped` list holds `muted:<id>`, so a mute that takes over is logged again. `GET /api/monitor` also answers `findings`, `mutes` and `detectors`. An older build ignores `mutes`, so muted findings would get stories again.
  - **Code.** New `src/monitor/mutes.ts` (`addMute`, `endMute`, `expireMutes`, `activeMutes`, `muteFor`, `muteLine`, `MuteError`) and `ui/monitor.js`; `Mute`, `validReason`, `MAX_MUTES`, `MAX_MUTE_HOURS` and the `recheck` option of `openBreaker` in `src/monitor/guard.ts`; `readFindings` in `src/monitor/findings.ts` (reads without renaming); the `mutes` dependency of `Reporter`, which reads the mutes at the top of a check and again before a story and before a comment.
  - **Not included.** Mute commands on the command line, changing a mute, a story that already exists is still built.
- Self-repair 6b — no circles and the circuit breaker (#105).
  - **No circles.** Runs that build a bug story of the monitor (`vars.github_repo` and `vars.issue` match the `report` or an `earlier` story of a stored finding; the repository ignores case) are never a finding: they are taken out of the runs, the active runs, the tracked issues of the watchers, the area locks and the queued jobs the detectors see. Active jobs stay in the queue, they fill the slots. This holds on the hotfix path or not, and for a story that a newer one replaced.
  - **The fix failed.** Each failed finish of a story run (status `failed`; stopped, waiting, cancelled and interrupted runs do not count) adds to `fixFailed` on its finding (`count` and `at`, the finish time of the newest run) and writes a `fix-failed` log line and a line in the card's activity. Runs that failed before the upgrade are counted once.
  - **Circuit breaker.** It opens when more than `new_findings` (default 5) different findings first appear within `within_minutes` (60), or when the newest `failed_fixes` (3) finished story runs all failed (a succeeded run resets the count; cancelled and interrupted runs neither count nor reset). Every new finding counts, but only when `report_to` is set and bug stories are not off. Findings first seen in the quiet time after a restart (`quietStart`) do not count. While open: no story, as with the off switch; findings are still recorded; one `story-skipped` line with reason `breaker` per owed finding (again each time the breaker opens). The reason and time survive a restart. Your turn shows one item for the admin ("The monitor stopped making bug stories: …", `monitor_stopped`, cannot be dismissed), also while the off switch is set; it goes when the breaker is closed.
  - **Closing.** Only an admin: the "on" button or `scf monitor on` (`changed` and `closed` in the answer, the log gets `on` and `breaker-closed`). Every switch-on sets `breaker.from`: findings and failed runs from before do not count again. `scf monitor status` and `GET /api/monitor` show the state `breaker` with `why`.
  - **Settings.** `monitor.breaker: { new_findings: 5 (1–499), within_minutes: 60, failed_fixes: 3 }`. An older build rejects this key once the config is saved.
  - **Formats.** `breaker: { from?, open?: { since, reason: "findings" | "failed_fixes", count, minutes? } }` in `monitor-guard.json`; optional `fixFailed`, `quietStart` and `earlier` (at most 10 replaced stories) in `monitor-findings.json`; log events `breaker-open`, `breaker-closed`, `fix-failed` (with `count`, `minutes`) and the skip reason `breaker`. All are optional keys, so an older build ignores them.
  - **Code.** New `src/monitor/breaker.ts` (`storyKeys`, `isStoryRun`, `withoutStoryRuns`, `keepEarlier`, `forgetSkip`, `failedFixes`, `decideBreaker`, all pure); `openBreaker()`, `breakerNow()`, `breakerWhy()`, `inQuietTime()` in `src/monitor/guard.ts`; `MonitorDeps.guard`; `RunBrief.githubRepo`, `issue` and `interrupted`; `NextKind` `monitor_stopped`.
  - **Not included.** A story that was replaced before this version (it is not in `earlier`), mutes (#106). After the upgrade the breaker opens at the first check if the newest 3 finished story runs already failed; one "on" resets it.
- The Audit page (#97).
  - **Behaviour.** Admins get **Audit** in the top bar (`#/audit`): a table with time, who, action, target and result, newest first. Filters: user (a list of the accounts), action, from date and to date; changing one reloads the list. The dates are days in the browser's time zone, both included; a day that does not exist (such as 31 February) is ignored. **Export CSV** downloads every line that matches the filters shown. When there are more lines than the 500 shown, the page says so and points to the export. An empty result says "No entries." An error, also from the account list, shows on the page. While a new list loads, the old rows are replaced by a loading line.
  - **Safe.** Nothing new on the server: the page uses `GET /api/users`, `GET /api/audit` and `GET /api/audit/export`, all for admins only. A user never sees the link, and `#/audit` goes to `#/runs`.
  - **Code.** New `ui/audit.js`; `api.audit` and `api.auditExportUrl` in `ui/api.js`; the route in `ui/app.js` and the link in `ui/index.html`. `renderAudit` draws and returns its cleanup at once.
  - **Not included.** Paging beyond the first 500 on the page, a filter for a deleted account, automatic refresh.
- **Audit log: retention setting (#98).**
  - **Behaviour.** New setting `audit.retention_days` in `config.yaml` (a whole number from 1 to 3650, default 180) and the field "Keep the audit log for … days" under Settings → Safety. The server removes lines of `audit.jsonl` older than that when it starts and once a day. A changed setting counts at the next round, without a restart. There is no "keep for ever".
  - **Safe.** Only a line that can be read and is too old is removed. Lines that cannot be read stay (also a line with bytes that are not valid UTF-8), the order stays, the mode stays `0600`. The file is read in small parts, so a large log does not fill memory. It is replaced in one step (`audit.jsonl.tmp` and a rename, under `auth.lock`) and is not touched when no line is old enough. If the clean-up fails or the lock is busy, the server keeps running, the log says `audit: <file> <kind> (clean-up)`, and the next round tries again. Nothing else removes or changes a line.
  - **Upgrade.** A `config.yaml` without the key loads unchanged. An older build rejects a `config.yaml` that has the `audit` key (it is written when Settings are saved). **Changed:** the first start removes lines older than 180 days; to keep more, set `audit.retention_days` before the first start.
  - **Code.** `purgeAudit`, `auditSweeper`, `AUDIT_SWEEP_MS` in `src/auth/audit.ts`; `replaceFileLocked` in `src/auth/store.ts`; `auditSweepMs` in `ServerOptions`.
- Repositories 2e — GitHub App installation (#69).
  - **Behaviour.** New method `github-app` for a GitHub repository with an https address, so a user needs no personal token. It is offered only when the admin has set up the app (app ID, key file and name); otherwise the page says so and the API answers 400. When the method is saved, and at every connection test, the server looks up the installation (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/installation`, signed with the app) and keeps its ID in the record as `installationId`. Not installed: 409 with a sentence. App not working: 500. GitHub not reachable: 502. A request the server refuses itself makes no call to GitHub. Test connection clones, checks the push and calls the API with a new short-lived token limited to that repository. New codes: `app-not-set-up`, `app-not-installed`, `app-broken`. New route `GET /api/repos/methods` (the methods a user may choose and the install link). My repositories shows the link `https://github.com/apps/<name>/installations/new` and what to do next. Choosing another method wipes the old token or key; a transfer keeps the method and the ID.
  - **Settings.** Settings → GitHub App has App ID, App name (slug), Private key file and Installation ID (optional). The installation ID is only for the bot identity, which is unchanged: an existing configuration with an installation ID works as before.
  - **Safe.** No token, JWT or key appears in an answer, a log line or a message; tokens go to git and `gh` through the environment only. The installation ID is never taken from a request. A user cannot read the app settings. A run that cannot get the app's token now fails with a fixed sentence (the HTTP status, or "the private key file cannot be read…"): GitHub's answer and the key file path are no longer in the reason. Any user can connect any repository the app is installed on; install the app only on repositories every user may work in (see the guide).
  - **Formats.** New `slug` in `github_app` of `config.yaml`; `installation_id` may be missing. New `installationId` and method `github-app` in `repos.json`. After the first save with a slug, an older build rejects the config, and a `repos.json` with the new method (as noted for #20). Old files load unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `src/github-app.ts` (`installationToken` per installation and repository, `repoInstallation`, `repoApp`, `installUrl`); `checkNewRepo` and `checkRepoAuth` in `src/auth/repos.ts`; `blockedResult` in `src/repos/connect.ts`; `addRepo`, `setRepoAuth` and `setRepoConnection` take optional installation arguments.
  - **Not included.** Runs and the architect's read do not use the app yet; no approval per repository.
  - **Check by hand once.** A real app against GitHub (the answers are from GitHub's documentation, tested here with fakes only).
- Refinement 2c — ask the architect from a session (#101).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/architect` starts a run of the shipped flow `refine-brief` for the session (the idea is the task, the session's repository is `github_repo`) through the normal scheduler, answers 202 with the session, and needs no published flow. Only the owner may call it (another user gets 404, an admin 403). A dropped session, or a repository that is not in My repositories any more, answers 409. When the read succeeds, the brief (text, time, branch, run id) is stored with the session. A refresh keeps the old brief until the new run has succeeded; a failed or cancelled run leaves it unchanged. `GET /api/refinement/:id` (and every call that returns a session) now has `architect` (`state` idle, queued, running, paused or failed, the `runId`, `doing` while it runs, `reason` when failed or paused), `brief`, and `briefHidden: true` while a brief exists but the repository is not in My repositories. A paused read (usage limit, sign-out, daily budget) is resumed by the same call, in the same run. Dropping the session cancels its read, also a queued or paused one. Deleting the account cancels its reads.
  - **Limits.** One read per session and one per account at a time (queued, running or paused); a second start answers 409 with a plain sentence. $3 per read and the daily budget apply as for any run.
  - **Settling.** The end of a run is taken into the session by the scheduler's `onFinished` hook and again whenever a session is read, so a run that ended while the server was down is stored on the next read. A run that was queued but never recorded in the session (a stop in between) is adopted on the next read. Failure reasons are fixed sentences without folder, cost, model or command.
  - **Where it shows.** The Runs list (a user's runs carry `refinement: <session id>`; an admin's too, with the cost), costs and statistics. The queue shows `refinement` on a queued read (an admin also sees `source`). Not on the board, not in "Your turn", "since", clarity, notifications, the health page or the monitor's findings; a failure shows in the session only.
  - **Changed.** `POST /api/runs/:id/resume`, `approve` and `reject` answer 409 for an architect run, for every role: it is continued from its session. A user cannot start `refine-brief` through `POST /api/runs`, and `GET /api/flows` does not list a published copy of it.
  - **Format.** `refinements.json` (version 1) gets the optional fields `brief` and `architect`, and the log values `architect-started`, `architect-resumed`, `architect-brief` and `architect-failed`. Old files still load; an older version cannot read a file that holds the new fields. A brief is cut at 60,000 characters.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/architect.ts` (`askArchitect`, `settleSession`, `settleFinished`, `stopArchitect`, `cancelReads`, `architectView`, `architectReason`); `setArchitectRun`, `noteArchitectResumed`, `endArchitectRun` in `src/refinement/store.ts`; `refinementSessionOf` in `src/server/user-view.ts`; the `refinement` origin in `src/your-turn.ts`.
  - **Not included.** The Refinement page (#102).
- Audit API: read with filters, export as CSV (#96).
  - **Behaviour.** `GET /api/audit` answers `{entries, more}`, newest first (by the order of the lines), at most 500 entries. Each entry has `time`, `actor`, `action`, `target`, `result` and, when set, `detail`. Actor and target accounts carry the current name, or "deleted user"; the file keeps ids only. Old account lines show `result: "ok"` and the account as target. Filters: `user` (an account id, matches actor or target), `action`, `from`, `to` (ISO times, both included). Unknown, empty and repeated filters answer 400. `GET /api/audit/export` takes the same filters and downloads all matching lines as `audit.csv` with a header row, oldest first.
  - **Safe.** Admin only (a user gets 403). Answers pass the same redaction of stored secrets as every other answer; in the CSV each row is checked again after quoting, and a row that would show a secret is hidden whole. When the stored credentials cannot be read, nothing is shown. A cell that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` gets a `'` in front. Lines that do not parse are skipped. An unreadable log answers a plain 500 and the server log says `audit: audit.jsonl unreadable`. The log is read in chunks, so a long log does not hold the server.
  - **Code.** `scanAudit`, `latestAudit`, `auditRecord`, `AUDIT_ACTIONS`, `isAccountId`, `auditTime` in `src/auth/audit.ts`; `sendCsv`, `csvLine`, `csvCell`, `redactedCsvLine` in `src/server/http.ts`; new `src/server/api-audit.ts`.
  - **Not included.** The audit page, paging beyond the first 500.
- Refinement 2b — the architect reads with the repository's own token (#100).
  - **Which steps get the token.** In a run the server started for a refinement session (source `refinement <id>`, set by server code only), the shell steps `clone` and `list_issues` of the top-level flow `refine-brief` get the token stored for the repository, looked up for the owner of the run when the step starts, as `GH_TOKEN`. No other step and no agent step gets it.
  - **Only that token.** It replaces the bot's `GH_TOKEN`. The two steps ignore the server's git settings (a rewrite to ssh, a credential helper, a header), allow only https, never ask for a password and switch off git tracing. The token stays hidden in output even if it is changed or removed while the step runs, also in a sandboxed step.
  - **Method "none".** The server's own access is used only when the owner of the run is an admin; any other account fails with "set a token for this repository under My repositories".
  - **Failures.** A missing, unreadable or refused token, a repository that is not the owner's, or a run without an owner fails with one plain sentence (an environment failure). A rate limit is not a refusal.
  - **Never stored or shown.** The token is not written to `run.json`, `queue.json`, a log, a transcript or an API answer.
  - **Runs without an owner.** A refinement run without an owner is no longer given to the first admin; it fails at the clone, and `adoptRuns()` leaves it alone.
  - **Clone.** `refine-brief` now clones an https address with `gh` as git's only credential helper, also in runs by hand, so no token is in a command line or in `.git/config`.
  - **Code.** `repoAccess()` in `src/auth/repos.ts`; `isRefinementRun()` in `src/auth/run-owner.ts`; `repoReadAccess()`, `repoReadEnv()` and `tokenRefused()` in `src/engine/guards.ts`; `combineRedactors()` and the `pinnedSecrets` and `dockerEnv` options of `runShell()`; `FAKE_GH_EXPECT_TOKEN` and `SHOWGH` in the fakes.
  - **Check by hand once.** The real `gh` and git credential path, including a fork, could not be tried here.
- Repositories 2d — Test connection (#68). `POST /api/repos/<id>/test` (the owner or an admin) runs a shallow clone, a push dry run on a scratch branch name (nothing is pushed) and, for GitHub repositories, a read of the repository, its issues and its pull requests. Works for `github-token`, `https-token`, SSH deploy key (the API check is skipped) and `none`. Fixed messages say what to do. The result is saved as `connection` on the repository and cleared when the token, key, method or address changes, on transfer, and when the stored token or key is deleted. 60 s limit; 409 for a running test or a repository changed during it (also when its token or key was deleted meanwhile), 404 if it went away. The method `none` (the server's own access) may only be tested by an admin, whoever owns the repository; for a user it answers 409. In an empty repository, or one whose `HEAD` points nowhere, the push check uses a local commit that is never sent, so write access is still tested. git and `gh` run with `LC_ALL=C`, so messages are recognised in any server language; GitLab, Gitea and Azure DevOps wording is recognised too. Host-key messages name the right `known_hosts` file (the data folder for a deploy key, `~/.ssh/known_hosts` for `none`). The page shows the status and a **Test connection** button. Safety: token through `GIT_ASKPASS`, key in a 0600 temporary file, no secret in arguments, URLs, answers or logs, `--hostname github.com`, an empty `gh` configuration folder for stored tokens. Formats: new optional `connection` in `repos.json` (an older build rejects such a file) and `known_hosts` in the data folder. Code: `src/repos/connect.ts`, `src/auth/repo-connection.ts`. Not included: runs that use the sign-in, a button on the admin page.

  - **Not included.** Paging beyond the first 500. The audit page came in #97.
- Self-repair 6a — off switch, quiet time after a restart, and the monitor's log (#104).
  - **Behaviour.** An admin stops all bug-story making with a button on the monitor's card (Watchers page) or `scf monitor off`; `scf monitor on` and the button switch it on; `scf monitor status` prints the state. The card shows one line ("Bug stories: off since 14:05", "quiet until 14:15 after the restart"). While off, findings are still recorded but no story, comment or GitHub call is made and nothing new becomes owed; owed stories stay owed. The state is read at every check and before each label, issue and comment call. Off does **not** stop the building of stories that already exist: pause the issue watcher or remove the label. For `monitor.cooldown_minutes` (default 10, 0 = none) after the server started, no story is made and none becomes owed. A state file that cannot be read stops stories; "on" keeps it as `monitor-guard.json.broken` (older: `.broken.1`, …), starts a fresh file and the card and log say so. "Off" on such a file does nothing. "Off" takes `monitor.lock` over from a stuck holder and a paused "on" cannot overwrite it.
  - **Formats.** `monitor-guard.json` (`version: 1`; `off: { since, by }`, `reset`; unknown keys are kept; `by` is `cli` or an account id) and its `.broken` backups; `monitor.lock` (a folder with a `token`); `monitor-log.jsonl` and `monitor-log.1.jsonl` (one JSON line per event, each file at most 512 KiB; a rotation lock `monitor-log.lock`, hard ceiling 1 MiB); optional `skipped` (reasons already logged) in `monitor-findings.json`; new key `monitor.cooldown_minutes`, which an older build rejects once the config is saved.
  - **API.** `GET /api/monitor`, `POST /api/monitor/off`, `POST /api/monitor/on` (admin only, in the permission table).
  - **Code.** New `src/monitor/guard.ts` (state, lock, log, `switchStories()`, `storiesVerdict()`), `src/monitor/cli.ts`, `src/server/api-monitor.ts`; `ReporterDeps.guard` and `record`; `WatcherManagerOptions.startedAt`, `WatcherManager.monitorAct()`; `Monitor.act()` is public; `storiesLine()` and `storiesRow()` in `ui/admin.js`.
  - **Not included.** Stopping runs of existing bug stories, mutes (#106), an audit-log line for the switch.

- Refinement 2a — the architect charter and the context-brief flow (#99).
  - **New flow `refine-brief`.** Takes `github_repo` and an idea (the task). Clones the repository (`develop` when the remote has it, the default branch otherwise), reads the open issues with their comments, and the architect writes a context brief with five parts: what already exists, code the idea would touch, open issues that overlap, rules that apply, could not find out. A brief that misses a part, leaves one empty, has the headings in another order or holds a code block fails the run. Every claim must name its file or issue.
  - **Read-only.** The architect step has the tools `Read`, `Glob` and `Grep`, mode `dontAsk`. No step pushes, comments or labels. The repository is cloned into `repo/`, so its `.claude/` is not loaded. The idea is only `{{task}}` in the prompt; the repository name only `$FACTORY_VAR_GITHUB_REPO`; issues go to `issues.md`.
  - **Backlog limits.** Up to 200 open issues, newest first. Bodies are cut at 2,000 and comments at 600 characters (every comment is kept). When the backlog is larger, a step `check_brief` fails the run unless the brief says so under "Could not find out". The branch is chosen from the local clone, so a network error never passes for "no `develop`".
  - **Charter.** The architect's role is one text in the new block `blocks/architect-charter.yaml`; the generator reads it from there.
  - **Cost and model.** $3 per run (`limits.max_cost_usd`), model `claude-opus-5-5`; change it with a routing rule for the flow `refine-brief`.
  - **Cannot be deleted.** `DELETE /api/flows/refine-brief` is refused: 403 for the built-in flow, 409 for a saved copy ("in use by refinement (the architect)").
  - **Code.** New `tools/issue-digest`; `REFINE_BRIEF_FLOW` and the extra user in `flowUsers()` in `src/flow/usage.ts`; `FAKE_BRIEF` in the fake `claude`.
  - **Not included.** Starting the flow from a refinement session.

- Audit log: runs, repositories, credentials, flows and settings (#95).
  - **Behaviour.** Actions in the web interface add a line with `result: "ok"` and `by` set to the account that made the call: `run-start`, `run-cancel`, `run-approve`, `run-reject`, `run-resume` (target: run id); `repo-add`, `repo-change`, `repo-remove` (target: repository id, detail: stored address); admin `repo-change` (detail: `settings:` and the changed field names) and `repo-transfer` (detail: new owner id); `credential-add`, `credential-remove` (target: id, detail: type); `flow-publish` (target: flow name, detail: version); `settings-change` (target `config.yaml`, detail: names of the changed top-level settings); `turn-answer`, `turn-approve`, `turn-reject`, `turn-retry` from `POST /api/your-turn/act` (target: `owner/repo#issue`).
  - **No line** for a call that is refused or fails before a change, or that changes nothing (a cancel that cancelled nothing, the same settings again, a flow saved without a change, an unpublished flow). A change that was made is logged even when the answer is then an error for an old key left in the Keychain.
  - **Safe.** Best effort: a line that cannot be written does not fail or undo the action, and it never waits for `auth.lock`. The server log says `audit: <file> <kind> (<action>)`.
  - **Never logged.** Tokens, passwords, keys, notes, task text, credential names and setting values.
  - **Format.** New action names in `EVENT_ACTIONS`; a `target` may now be 255 characters (was 200). Old lines are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `auditAction`, `changedKeys` and `EventAction` in `src/auth/audit.ts`; `setRepoAuth` returns `changed`, `setRepoSettings` returns `{ repo, changed }`, `transferRepo` returns `moved`, `removeRepo` and `removeGithubRepo` return `removed`.
  - **Not included.** Runs a watcher starts, `scf run`, `scf approve` and `scf reject`, evals, deleting or unpublishing a flow, blocks, clean-up, running a watcher now, the audit page (#96).
- Passwords (#8).
  - **Behaviour.** Everyone can change their own password (**Change password** in the top bar; the other sessions of the account end). An admin can **Reset password** (removes the password, ends the sessions, shows a one-time link) and **Unlock** an account. The sign-in form says what to do after a forgotten password. The Users page shows a "locked" status.
  - **Calls.** New `POST /api/password`, `POST /api/users/:id/reset` and `POST /api/users/:id/unlock`. Sign-in and set-password answers 429 carry `Retry-After`.
  - **Code.** `resetPassword()` and `changePassword()` in `src/auth/users.ts`; `src/auth/common-passwords.ts`; `src/server/sign-in-throttle.ts` (in-memory waits and locks; the tries are counted before the check, given back on success, and kept for stored accounts); `signInClock` server option; `HttpError` can carry headers.
  - **Changed.**
    - New passwords need 12 characters (was 10) and must not be common.
    - Links last 24 hours (was 7 days); earlier links keep their end time.
    - Limits are waits from the 5th wrong try and a 30-minute lock at 20 (was 429 after 10 per e-mail and 60 per client in 15 minutes); a right password no longer counts for the address.
    - `GET /api/users` has `lockedUntil`.
    - New audit action `reset`.
    - The export `SignInLimiter` is gone.

- Self-repair 2 — more monitor detectors (#86).
  - **New detectors** (each has a threshold under `monitor:` in `config.yaml`): **stuck run** (nothing written to the log for longer than the step's timeout plus 10 minutes; a step without a timeout counts 120 minutes), **same step keeps failing** (the same step of a flow ended runs as failed for 3 different issues in 24 hours), **label and run disagree** (more than 3 checks), **lock without owner** (a code-area lock or a run lock held by a run that is not running, more than 10 minutes), **queue not moving** (jobs queued, slots free, nothing started for 15 minutes), **restart overdue** (a new version waits more than 2 hours), **develop is red** (the tests after a merge into develop failed 2 times in a row, looking back 24 hours) and **slow step** (more than 3 times the usual time, 3 times in 24 hours).
  - **Normal is never a finding.** A run that waits for a person, a usage limit that resets by itself, a sign-out, a story that waits for a dependency or a release, and a `done` label are left out. Interrupted runs, rejected approvals and the run budget do not count as a failing step. A run inside a sub-flow step is not judged as stuck.
  - **Descriptions.** Every detector has a two-sentence `description`; `detectorInfo()` lists them (and `detector-failed`) for the app.
  - **The monitor keeps running while a new version waits** (`WatcherManager.drain()`), so a restart that takes too long is seen. `stopAll()` still stops it.
  - **Formats.** New `monitor:` keys (`stuck_run`, `same_step_failing`, `label_mismatch`, `orphan_lock`, `queue_stalled`, `restart_overdue`, `develop_red`, `slow_step`) that an older build rejects once the config is saved. The findings file and `GET /api/queue` are unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `src/monitor/work-detectors.ts`; `slowVisits()` in `src/estimate.ts`; `labelLies()` and `TrackedIssue.labelOff` in `src/queue/watcher.ts`; `Scheduler.lastStart()`; `lastLogWrite()`, `readAreaLocks()` and `areaLockDir()` in `src/monitor/monitor.ts`; `pidAlive` is exported from `src/engine/state.ts`.
  - **Not included.** Showing findings or descriptions in the app.
- Audit log: sign-ins (#94).
  - **Behaviour.** Every sign-in adds a line to `audit.jsonl`: `action: "sign-in"` with `result` `ok` (`by` and `userId` are the account id) or `failed` (`by` is `anonymous`; `userId` only when the e-mail belongs to an account). Wrong password, unknown e-mail and blocked account each write one line. No line holds the typed e-mail or a password. Nothing is written for a 429, a bad request, an over-long e-mail or a server error.
  - **Safe.** A line that cannot be written does not stop the sign-in; the server log says `auth: audit.jsonl cannot-write`. A failed try never waits for `auth.lock`.
  - **Changed.** Setup writes a `create` line with `by` set to the new admin's id, and stops with a 500 when `audit.jsonl` cannot be opened.
  - **Format.** New event line: `time`, `by`, `action`, `result`, and `userId` or `target` (up to 200 characters; 255 since #95), with `detail` (up to 500) only next to a `target`. `by` may be `anonymous`. Old account lines are unchanged and still parse.
  - **Code.** `writeAudit`, `appendAuditLocked`, `EVENT_ACTIONS`, `createUser(…, { bySelf })`, `startSession(…, { audit })`.
  - **Not included.** Other events (#95), an audit page (#96), the client address.

- SSH deploy key (#67).
  - **Method.** New method `ssh-deploy-key` for a repository. It works only with an SSH address (`git@host:path` or `ssh://…`); an https address, a token or a user name gives 400 (the message names the "SSH address").
  - **Key.** The pair is made with `/usr/bin/ssh-keygen` (ed25519, no passphrase, no comment) in a 0700 temporary folder that is removed afterwards; if the folder cannot be removed, nothing is stored. The private key is the credential `repo:<id>` of type `ssh-key` and is never returned. A failed ssh-keygen answers 500 "the SSH key could not be made; see the server log" and changes nothing.
  - **API.** The record and `GET /api/repos` have `publicKey`. `PUT …/auth {"newKey": true}` makes a new key and removes the old one. Choosing the method again repairs a key that is missing or is not the record's own, and cleans old Keychain keys left by an earlier try.
  - **Redaction.** The public key stays readable in the answers of `/api/repos`, also when a stored secret is part of it (such as a token `ssh-ed25519`). Only a whole public-key line as a complete JSON string value is kept; the rest of the answer is hidden as before, and so is a stored secret that reaches outside the key.
  - **The page.** A deploy-key row shows the public key with **Copy**, a hint to add it as a deploy key with write access, and **Generate a new key** (with a confirmation; the old key stops working). The change dialog asks for the **SSH address** (or, for a token method, the **HTTPS address**) when the stored address has the other form.
  - **Also fixed.** A log file of a step that cannot be written (folder gone, disk full) now fails the step and stops the process; before, it was an unhandled error.
  - **Formats.** New optional `publicKey` and the new method in `repos.json`; old files load unchanged; an older build rejects a file that has such a record.
  - **Code.** New `src/credentials/ssh-keygen.ts` (`generateKeyPair`, `KeygenError`, `PUBLIC_KEY_RE`), `redactKeeping` in `redact.ts`, a fourth argument of `send` and a second one of `redactedJson` (the public keys to keep), and `SCF_SSH_KEYGEN_BIN` (for tests).
  - **Transfer.** An SSH deploy key is repository-bound (`REPO_BOUND_METHODS`): an admin's transfer moves the key to the new owner with the record and its `publicKey`; a missing key refuses the transfer (409 `no-credential`). The admin repository answers also keep the public key whole.
  - **Not included.** The connection test, runs that use the key, the GitHub App.
- Bug stories from the monitor (#87).
  - **Behaviour.** With `monitor.report_to: owner/repo` set, the monitor writes one GitHub issue for a finding that lasts: critical and major after 2 checks in a row; minor after it was seen on 3 different days. The issue has the labels `bug` and the build label of that repository's issue watcher, so the normal flow builds it. Missing labels are made (never with `--force`). Without `report_to` (the default) nothing leaves the machine and findings are only shown. No watcher for the repository: the story gets only `bug` and the watcher card says so; with several watchers the one with flow `issue-gitflow` (or `default`) is used, else the first.
  - **The story.** Written from a fixed template per detector (no AI): title that names the symptom ("Runs that step aside are restarted in a loop"), what happened, since when and how often, effect on work, evidence, what should happen instead, how to see it again, where to look in the code, acceptance criteria (the symptom no longer occurs, a test covers it) and a hidden marker `<!-- claude-factory monitor=<hash> -->` (a hash of the fingerprint, not the fingerprint). Title and text go to `gh` on standard input as JSON, never in a command line.
  - **Cleaning.** Other repositories become "another repository" / "an issue"; user names, e-mail addresses, home-folder paths, watcher ids and other links are removed; tokens and keys are removed with the secret scan and the stored credentials. Log lines are cut to the part that says what went wrong and only words of a fixed list stay (others become `…`; a name of any person, watcher or other repository is never kept). A story about another repository carries no log lines. If a line cannot be cleaned with certainty the evidence is left out and the story says so.
  - **Only once.** An open story with the same marker means no new story. A comment "Seen again: N times since …" is added at most once per 6 hours per story. A story closed as completed that comes back (24 hours after the close, with proof from after it: a missed check, a reopened finding or new evidence) gets a new story that links the old one and says "Came back after the fix". A story closed as not planned is muted: no new story until it is reopened (noticed within 6 hours).
  - **Limits.** `monitor.report_limits: { per_day: 3, per_check: 1 }` (`per_check` at most 3). The most severe goes first; the rest waits and the monitor card on the Watchers page says so. At most 6 GitHub calls per check. If GitHub cannot be reached or its request limit is used up, the stories stay owed and are made at a later check, also when the problem has gone away.
  - **Formats.** New `config.yaml` keys `monitor.report_to` and `monitor.report_limits` (an older build rejects them). Optional `streak`, `days`, `due`, `report` and `missedAt` fields in `monitor-findings.json` (an old file loads). Findings with a story or an owed story are kept for good and counted apart from the 500 other findings (at most 1,000 of them; only archived ones go). `notes` in the watcher status (shown on the monitor's card). `tools/secret-scan --text` replaces secrets in text from standard input.
  - **Code.** `src/monitor/report.ts`, `clean.ts`, `story.ts`; `listIssuesByLabel`, `restIssue`, `createIssue`, `createLabelIfMissing` in `src/github.ts`; `watcherNotes` in `ui/admin.js`.
  - **Not included.** A findings page, an unmute button (reopen the story instead), evidence lines from other repositories. A story older than the newest 100 `bug` issues whose local record is lost can be written again.
  - **Tests.** A test of the watcher status comment waited too short on a busy machine; it now waits for the comment.

- Self-update (#92).
  - **Behaviour.** Off by default. When on, the running Foundry looks every 5 minutes at `main` of the repository named in the setting. When `main` has new commits it builds and tests them in a separate folder (`self-update-stage` in the data folder); only a version that built and passed its tests is installed. It installs only when the checkout runs from a git checkout, is on `main`, has no local changes (untracked files count) and can fast-forward, and the checkout's `origin` is the named repository. Otherwise it does nothing and the Health line says why. The install waits for the active runs: no new runs start (queued runs stay in `queue.json` and start after the restart), then the server restarts on the new version. If the build or the tests fail, the old version keeps running and the monitor gets a finding. If the new server does not answer within 3 minutes or stops, the supervisor puts the previous version back once and reports it. A cut-off install is finished or undone at the next start. The tip of `main` is checked again right before the install.
  - **Option.** `self_update: { enabled: false, repo: owner/name }` in `config.yaml` (`repo` is required when `enabled` is true; an older build rejects the key). Settings → Safety has a switch and a field. Switching it off while an update waits restarts on the unchanged version when the runs are done.
  - **Files.** `self-update.json` in the data folder is the record (`pending`, `tested`, `failed`, `updated`). A record that cannot be read stops self-update until it is deleted.
  - **In the app.** The Health line shows the running version (commit and date) and "An update is waiting (…)" with the reason, or why self-update does nothing. `GET /api/health` has the new fields `version` and `update`; they do not change `ok`, `summary` or `problems`. New route `GET /api/ready` (no session, only for a program on the same machine, answers `{ok:true}`): the supervisor uses it to see that a new version is healthy.
  - **Monitor.** New detector "Self-update failed": major when the old version keeps running, critical when the new version did not start healthy, when the Foundry could not go back, or when the record is broken.
  - **Code.** `src/self-update.ts`, `src/self-update-state.ts`; `supervise()` takes an optional start guard; `Scheduler.drain()` stops queued jobs from starting while the server waits to restart (also for a new build and a moved data folder).
  - **Limits.** Stop and start the Foundry once after upgrading, so the supervisor has the new code. After a failed go-back or a broken record, self-update stays off until a person repairs the checkout and deletes `self-update.json`. A machine that stops while `npm ci` or the build rewrites `dist/` needs `npm ci && npm run build` by hand. Going back does not undo data-folder changes the bad version made.
  - **Not included.** Other branches, origins that are not on GitHub, installs that are not git checkouts.
- Fix a test that failed on a busy machine: the clean-up after the failure-explanation tests retries while a stopped model process still writes its log (it failed the tests before the change for two stories).
- Docs: new `docs/DESIGN.md` (how the Foundry is built, with diagrams) and `docs/LESSONS_LEARNED.md`; the user guide and README have pictures of the current interface (Your turn, Board, sign-in, My repositories, the user display) and every older picture was retaken. The README is rewritten around the current way of working (one label, Your turn, gitflow, accounts) and its quick start no longer uses a flow that is not shipped.
- Users page (#65).
  - **The page.** Admins get **Users** in the top bar (`#/users`). It lists name, e-mail, role, status (active, blocked, no password yet), last sign-in and the number of runs; your own row says "(you)". Buttons: **Add user**, **Edit**, **New link**, **Block**, **Unblock**, **Delete**. An account with the role `user` never sees the page or the link; `#/users` goes back to `#/runs`.
  - **Links.** **Add user** (name, e-mail, role) and **New link** show the set-password link once, with **Copy** and a read-only field to select (Copy needs HTTPS or localhost). The dialog says the link works once, for 7 days, and that the admin must send it. The link is built from the address in the admin's browser. **New link** is also offered for a blocked account without a password; the dialog says the link works only after an unblock.
  - **Block, Unblock, Delete.** Block says what happens (signed out, queued runs cancelled, running runs finish) and has **Also stop all their work now**; afterwards the page says how many runs were cancelled. Unblock says cancelled runs are not restarted. Delete names the account and says stored credentials are wiped and runs are kept.
  - **Refusals.** A refusal (last admin, e-mail taken, bad input) shows in the dialog and the list does not change. The list is read again when the dialog closes, so an error that came after the change (a 500) is not hidden. A change, block or delete of your own account reloads the page.
  - **Changed.** In the admin's Runs list the Owner column says `deleted user` (was `deleted account`) and is empty (was `—`) for a run without an owner; the same in the owner filter. The admin's run page has an **Owner** row (one extra `GET /api/users`; if it fails, the row is left out). The API's `ownerName` is unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `ui/users.js`; `ownerLabel`, `ownerText`, `ownerNames`, `ownerRow` in `ui/runs.js`; seven calls in `ui/api.js`. No server change.

- Bug stories go first (#88).
  - **Behaviour.** An issue with a bug label is handled before all others in every check (questions up front, starting, resuming, answering). It gets the next free slot before any other story, also before older queued ones and before other repositories on the same Foundry. Among bug stories the oldest issue goes first. When several stories wait for the same code area, the bug story gets it first. `max_per_tick` does not apply to bug stories. Running work is never stopped. A bug story still waits for its dependencies, its answers, the daily budget, usage limits and an open release pull request. Taking the label off puts the story back in line. Resume, approve and reject on the run page keep a bug story's priority; a run started by hand does not get it.
  - **Option.** Watcher option `priority_labels` (default `[bug]`, not case sensitive; `[]` turns it off). A cut issue list (100 or more open issues) gets a second search for the priority labels, so no bug story is missed.
  - **Changed.** Issues with a `bug` label on watched repositories now go first without any setting.
  - **In the app.** Board cards and queue lines of a bug story show "goes first". Another story's "what happens next" says "waits: a bug story goes first" (new record kind `bug_first`, in the Waiting column).
  - **Formats.** Optional `priority` and `storyAt` in `queue.json`; a new `config.yaml` key `priority_labels` that an older build rejects; `priority` and `behindPriority` in `GET /api/queue`; `goesFirst` on board cards. No new route, no flow change.
  - **Code.** `Scheduler.setPriority()`, `goesFirst()` in `src/queue/watcher.ts`, `peers` in `WatcherDeps`.
  - **Limits.** A story that reaches its first code-area claim just after the holder finishes can still take the area. Across watchers the rule uses the other watcher's last finished check. A label added after a question check was queued does not move that batch.
  - **Not included.** Skipping the release pause for bug stories (that comes with the hotfix path, #89) and a form field for the setting.
- Admin: all repositories, settings and transfer (#70).
  - **Page.** Admin page **Repositories** (`#/all-repos`): every account's repositories with owner (name, e-mail), URL, authentication method and connection status ("Not tested yet"), with **Settings** and **Transfer**. A user never gets it.
  - **Calls (admin only, user: `no`).** `GET /api/admin/repos`, `PUT /api/admin/repos/:id/settings`, `POST /api/admin/repos/:id/transfer {email}`.
  - **Settings.** Test command, docs to update, protected branch patterns, main and develop branch name. Checked on the server: lengths, no control characters, git's branch name rules (at most 200 characters, not `@`), patterns with `*` (any text) and `?` (one character), no `[…]`. `{}` clears them. They are never in `GET /api/repos` or on My repositories.
  - **Only stored and shown.** Runs still use the flow var `test_cmd` and `protected_branches` in `config.yaml`.
  - **Transfer.** By e-mail, checked like at account creation. Refused for a missing or invalid e-mail (400), unknown account (404), blocked account (409), or an account with 50 repositories (400). Personal tokens are wiped: the method becomes `none`, which reads "Needs authentication" for a user and "The server's own access" for an admin. Keeping a deploy key or a GitHub App installation is prepared (`moveCredentialLocked` in `src/credentials/store.ts`, `PERSONAL_METHODS` and `REPO_BOUND_METHODS` in `src/auth/repos.ts`) and starts to work with the parts that add those methods.
  - **Format.** `repos.json` records may hold `settings`; an older build cannot read a file that holds them.
  - **Code.** `src/auth/repo-settings.ts`, `listAllRepos`, `setRepoSettings`, `transferRepo`, `ui/admin-repos.js`.
  - **Not included.** Use of the settings in runs, an audit-log line, a list of accounts.
- The monitor (#85): a watcher that finds problems of the Foundry itself. Add a watcher with `source: monitor` (admin only; `id`, `every`, `enabled`; no repository, flow, label or owner; only one per install; not there until added). Each check runs five detectors with thresholds in the new `monitor:` section of `config.yaml`: restart loop (more than 5 resumes in 10 minutes; `resumes` at most 49), watcher error (more than 3 failed checks in a row, grouped by kind), GitHub request limit (hit, or more than 80% used; read from `gh api rate_limit` at most once a minute), watcher silent (no check for 5 intervals), unexplained failure (no rule explains it; minor). A detector that crashes becomes a finding. Findings (fingerprint, severity, summary, evidence, first/last seen, count) are stored in `monitor-findings.json` in the data folder; not seen for 24 hours means gone. The monitor only writes findings. Also: `run.json` gains `resumeLog` (the last 50 resumes), the status in `GET /api/watchers` gains `errorCount`, a failing monitor check shows in Health, and `scf watch --source monitor` is refused (it only runs in the server). Not included: a page or API for findings, reporting to GitHub, mutes.
- Hotfix path for bug stories (#89).
  - **Behaviour.** In `issue-gitflow`, an issue with a label in `hotfix_labels` (default `bug`, any case) is built as a hotfix: branch `hotfix/<issue>-<title>` from the `main` commit the tests passed on, the same plan, checks, build, tests, reviews and docs as a feature. After the reviews the fix is merged into `main` (no fast-forward), tested, and pushed only when green; if `main` moved, it merges and tests again; red tests or a conflict push nothing and fail with a clear message. Then `main` is merged into `develop` with the usual conflict handling and tests. The issue is closed when the fix is on `main`. The merged branch is deleted (`delete_merged_branches`), unless it got new commits after the merge. A hotfix is never split by itself; a risky plan still waits for a person. No pull request, tag or release note.
  - **Develop behind.** If `develop` cannot take the fix, the fix stays on `main` and the comment says so (first line `Merge main into develop…`) and what happens next. The comment on a finished hotfix says whether the running Foundry has the fix (only for the Foundry's own repository).
  - **Option.** New `hotfix_to_main` in `config.yaml` (default `false`; **Hotfixes** in Settings → Safety). **Changed for existing installs:** nothing, until an admin switches it on; a bug story is built as a feature, and its report has one line saying why. An older build rejects the new key. New flow variables `hotfix_labels` and `hotfix_prefix`.
  - **Protection of `main`.** The one exception to **Protected branches**: only `push_main` of the unchanged built-in `issue-gitflow` may push `main`, with a one-time token the engine writes for that step; setting `FACTORY_PUSH_ALLOW` by hand gives nothing. It is not a hard wall (a command that skips git hooks still gets through): use GitHub branch protection for that. If GitHub only accepts pull requests on `main`, the run fails and says so.
  - **Formats.** New first lines `_FIXED` and `_MERGE_BACK`; new step variables `$FACTORY_HOTFIX`, `$FACTORY_SELF_SHA`, `$FACTORY_SELF_REPO`.
  - **Code.** `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (steps `baseline_main` … `hotfix_done`, `report`), `hotfixState`, `pushAllowEnv`, `grantPush` and `selfBuild` in `src/engine/guards.ts`, `src/engine/execute.ts`, `runClosedIssue` in `src/next-step.ts`, `ui/admin.js`.
- Refinement sessions (#74).
  - **Page.** New page **Refinement** (`#/refinement`, `#/refinement/<id>`) for every account. The list shows title, repository, state and last change (an admin also sees the owner), with **Open sessions** and **Dropped** filters and **New session**. The session page shows the idea, the story drafts (empty for now) and the log (who, what, when), with **Rename**, **Drop** and **Restore**. A user now sees **Refinement**, **Runs** and **My repositories**.
  - **Calls.** `GET /api/refinement` (200 `{sessions, repos}`; a short summary per session, `repos` are the account's GitHub repositories). `POST /api/refinement {repo, idea, title?}` (201; 400 bad input or the 200-session limit, 403 repository not yours, 404 no account). `GET /api/refinement/<id>` (200 with idea, drafts and log; 404). `PUT /api/refinement/<id> {title}` (200; 400, 403 not the owner, 404, 409 dropped). `POST /api/refinement/<id>/drop` (200; 404, 409 already dropped). `POST /api/refinement/<id>/restore` (200; 403 not the owner, 404, 409 not dropped). A missing session answers `no such refinement session`. A store problem answers a plain 500; the log names the file and the kind only.
  - **File.** New `refinements.json` (version 1, mode 0600) in the data folder, copied unchanged by the data-folder move. States: exploring, drafting, ready, published, dropped. A new session is *exploring*; for now only Drop and Restore change the state.
  - **Limits.** 200 sessions per account (dropped ones count until removed), idea 10,000 characters, title 120, 1,000 log entries per session (after that Rename and Restore are refused and the session can only be dropped; nothing is cut).
  - **30 days and who may do what.** A dropped session is removed after 30 days (on read, at start and on a timer). An owner sees and changes only their own sessions. An admin sees all sessions and the owners, and may drop; rename and restore stay with the owner. The owner sees an admin's log entry as "an administrator".
  - **Account and repository.** Deleting an account deletes its refinement sessions, and stops with nothing changed when `refinements.json` or `repos.json` cannot be read. Removing a repository keeps its sessions; the page says the repository is gone. Only GitHub repositories can be used.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/store.ts`, `src/server/api-refinement.ts`, `ui/refinement.js`; rules in `src/server/permissions.ts`.
  - **Not included.** The architect steps, story drafts and publishing to the backlog. Nothing here calls an AI agent or GitHub.
- My repositories page (#66). New page **My repositories** (`#/repos`) for every account: it lists your repositories with the URL, the authentication method and the connection status ("Not tested yet" until the connection test exists). **Add repository** asks for the URL and the method: a GitHub fine-grained personal access token (repository permissions Contents, Issues and Pull requests, each "Read and write") or an HTTPS user name + token. The token is typed in a password field and never shown again. A repository can be removed (with a confirmation) and its authentication changed. When a removal reports that an old key is still in the Keychain, the message stays on the page with **Try again**. An admin can also choose "The server's own access". A user now sees **Runs** and **My repositories**. Code: new `ui/repos.js`; errors thrown by `ui/api.js` now carry the HTTP status; no API change. Not included: the connection test, SSH deploy keys, the GitHub App, per-repository settings. The dialog fields are spaced like the other dialogs, and an admin who opens a longer address such as `#/repos/x` gets the list.
- Users API for admins (#63).
  - **Calls.** `GET /api/users` (200, a list of accounts with `id`, `name`, `email`, `role`, `status`, `created`, `lastSignIn`, `runs`, `hasPassword`; never a hash or a token). `POST /api/users {name, email, role}` (201 `{user, token, expires}`; 400 for bad input, 409 for a taken e-mail). `PUT /api/users/<id> {name?, email?, role?}` (200 `{user}`; 404, 409 for the last admin or a taken e-mail). `POST /api/users/<id>/block {stopWork?}` (200 `{user, cancelled}`; 409 for the last admin). `POST /api/users/<id>/unblock` (200 `{user}`). `POST /api/users/<id>/link` (200 `{user, token, expires}`; 409 when the account has a password). `DELETE /api/users/<id>` (200 `{ok, credentials, cancelled}`; 409 for the last admin). All are admin-only; a user gets `403 {"error":"not allowed for your role"}`. A field that is in the body must be valid; `null` is a bad value. An admin may act on their own account within the last-admin rule; blocking or deleting yourself signs you out.
  - **At once.** Block and delete cancel queued jobs before they answer; `stopWork` also cancels running and waiting runs and returns the numbers (`cancelled: {queued, running, waiting}`). The server log says `account <id> blocked|deleted|stop-work: cancelled …`, with the account id only.
  - **Audit.** `by` is the admin's account id; new action `edit` for a name or e-mail change. **Changed:** such a change wrote no line before. A call that fails writes no line, so a block or delete that stops with an error may already have signed the account out or removed its repositories and stored credentials.
  - **Old key.** Delete answers 500 with `the account was deleted, but ` and the credentials sentence when an old key stays in the Keychain; the account is gone.
  - **Errors.** A store problem answers a plain 500; the log names the file and the kind (`users: audit.jsonl cannot-write`), never a value or a path.
